# Rules: Coding sessions and terminals

**Covers:** Chat › Code, Build › Code, Build terminals, the harness, the night shift, the benchmark: `harness/`, `hosted/`, `terminals/`, `harness_bench/`, `codehost.py`, `daemon/routes/build*.py`, `dashboard/components/harness/`, `dashboard/components/buildCode/`, `dashboard/components/terminal/`.

How to use this file: read the contents list, then read only the entries your change touches (each line gives the line number where the full text starts). The rules that apply to every change are in the root `CLAUDE.md`. To add a rule, see "Adding a rule" there.

<!-- contents:start (generated by scripts/gen_agent_rules.py; do not edit) -->

## Rules

- **Coding work from the phone goes through ONE door (`harness/phone_code`): only folders the user has used, every check BEFORE the card, nothing queued but by the sender's own Start, a night task that starts NOW inside the night shift's limits, and one ending read from its record** (next leaps 12-a, master plan R2.5, D-12.1, D-12.2; the night shift plan's decision 5).
  THE DOOR. Two ways in, one module: `/code <folder>: <words>` (`comm/inbound._handle_code_command`, beside `/answer`, because only the poller knows who sent it; `reflex/commands._cmd_code` is the no-door fallback and `/help` lists it) and the phone chat's `code_start` tool (`settings/records_build.CodeStartTool`, allow tier, NOT on `LOW_TRUST_DENY`: it writes no task). The asking sender is `phone_code.Asking`, set ONLY by the poller (`set_asking` around `_handle_chat`'s turn, after the allowlist, the proof and the private-chat guard); `phone_code.door_open()` (the phone surface AND that sender) arms `code_start` in `records_build.build_tools_for`, the one table both lanes read (`post_record_tools` gives it to the phone's POST lane; the PC's chat, a desktop reply into a phone chat and an agent run never get it, and a call there refuses `NOT_ON_PHONE`). Cards the tool asked ride `who.cards` and the poller sends them AFTER the reply (`_send_asked_cards`; `skip_code` keeps the reminder off that reply), buttons plus "Reply 1 …" (10-b's rule). `night_task_add` stays refused at low trust.
  THE FOLDERS. `used_folders`: coding sessions' PICKED folders (`branches.facts_of(...).checkout`, never a worktree), active project roots, earlier night tasks, newest first, named by `sessions.folder_names`. `find_folder` matches the display name or the folder's own name ignoring case; a typed path only when its `folder_key` is one of them (a network spelling never). None: `none_words` names eight. Two or more: a `code_folder` prompt (numbered; `after_pick` leads to the card).
  THE CARD. `propose` runs `problem()` first (words, `MAX_PHONE_WORDS`, Emergency stop, `routes/night._check_folder`, a Local only phone chat's model before the pay check, `pay_problem`) and registers a `code_start` prompt (`ref_id` JSON: path, name, the EXACT words, when) only when all pass; `card_words` names the folder and its last two parts, the branch rule, the task limit, any wait for room, and `uses_words` (Iron-Proxy's CACHED usage only: "Uses your Claude plan (42% of this 5-hour window used)."; read for the account the work is PINNED to, the vendor's Default via `build_accounts.default_for` as `routes/build._with_initial_account` picks it, never `usage_for` with no account, which skips an account at its limit: at its limit the card says the reset time, or the group partner `build_accounts.takeover` names for a `MOVE_KINDS` limit; needing sign-in is said; with `build_auto_resume` OFF (`routes/build.auto_wait` arms no wait and no move) it says the work stops at the limit, never a wait or a partner; the words after "at its usage limit" come from `limit_wait_words(when, room_wait)`, which says what BOUNDS the wait: tonight, the coming night window (a reset after it closes: "stops when the window closes"; before it opens: "which is before it starts"; none known: "if it has not reset, it waits until the night window closes"); now, the task's time limit (a reset past now + `task_minutes`: "past its 90-minute limit, so it stops at the limit"; none known, or a wait for room first: "only up to its 90-minute limit, then stops"); never "it waits for the reset" for a wait the code ends first; pinned by `test_the_card_reads_the_account_the_work_runs_on_and_says_its_limit`, `test_with_carry_on_by_itself_off_the_card_says_the_work_stops_at_the_limit` and `test_the_cards_limit_words_say_what_bounds_the_wait`). A card waits `CARD_TTL_S` (an hour, `_code_prompt_fresh`). The answer goes through `_resolve_prompt` → `_resolve_code_prompt`: CLAIM first (first answer wins), Not now keeps nothing, Start runs `problem()` AGAIN and makes ONE `night.add_task(start_now=True, asked_from="comm:<channel>", report_json={channel, sender_id, thread_id, reply_to, name})`; a press says Starting / Queued / Not started on the button (`_PRESS_QUIET` holds the plain "→ Starting" reply).
  THE RUN. `NightTaskRecord.start_now`, `asked_from`, `report_json` (additive). `night.may_run_now`: a `start_now` task runs outside the window (the night pass's queue filter, the account wait in `settle_running`, the carry-on gate in `routes/build.start_build_turn`). By day nothing else ends its ACCOUNT WAIT (`minutes_left` counts ended turns only; the window bounds a night task's), so `night.wait_over` bounds it: held only until `armed_at` (else the task's start) + what was left of its limit, and never for a known reset after that; then `settle_running` clears the wait and ends it STOPPED with `WAIT_PAST_LIMIT` (its slot and `awake_needed` freed; pinned by `test_a_phone_tasks_wait_on_its_account_never_outlasts_its_time_limit`, `test_only_a_phone_tasks_wait_is_bounded_by_its_time_limit`). While the wait loop may still move it within its group (`routes/build.may_move_in_group`, `resume_due`'s own test, passed as `movable`), a later reset ends nothing, only the time limit: the card's "…, in the same group, takes over" holds whichever loop looks first (`test_the_partner_the_card_names_takes_over_whichever_loop_looks_first`). Every other limit is the night's (at once, one per folder, the task's time limit, pay, Emergency stop). Its first turn is `N.BY_PHONE` (`TASK_STARTERS` with `BY_NIGHT`: the night posture and wait, Emergency stop's note, the evidence rule). `awake_needed` holds for a queued `start_now` task. `report_json` is never in `night.view` (the page reads `from_phone` / `start_now`; Tonight says **From your phone**).
  THE WORDS HOME. `build.phone_code {task_id, step}` (no words in the event): `start_task` rings `started` after the turn starts, `night.report_ended` rings `ended` for a phone task INSTEAD of a chat report, and `daemon/app`'s handler calls `phone_code.deliver`, which re-reads the record, fails closed on a destination gone, no longer two-way, or a sender no longer allowed, appends to the chat that asked (`report_json.thread_id` while it exists; else, ONLY with the destination's chat on, the sender's phone chat: a command-only destination keeps no chat on the PC, `_onto_phone_chat`) and sends. `report_json` + `deliver` are a stopgap second "where it reports" field and writer beside 39-a's `chat_thread_id` / `chat_delivery.report_to_chat` (39-a refuses phone chats until 39-c): 39-c folds both into the one writer, and its phone ending becomes a fenced report like 39-a's. `started_words` / `ending_words` come from `night_morning.shared_row(summary_row(task))` (masked; a Local only task's branch and notes left out), never the model's words; a task the user stopped themselves says nothing. `morning_pass` leaves phone tasks out of every night summary (and they never hold one back): one notice per outcome.
  Not built (12-b, 12-c): `/code` listing, stop and carry on from the phone, work on a paired computer; test counts ("12 of 12") are not in the morning facts, so the ending says "tests passed". The task does not carry Local only itself: a tonight card (or one that waits for room) from a Local only phone chat starts later on the default model of THAT moment, as `night_task_add` from a Local only chat always has (`start_task` re-checks pay only; a later fix stores Local only on the task and re-checks `egress.stays_on_this_network` there). Not driven against a real Telegram, Slack or email account: Slack's client may swallow `/code` as its own slash command (the Handbook says to use plain words or a leading space there). Pins: `tests/test_leap12a_*.py`, `dashboard/__tests__/leap-12a-tonight-from-phone.test.tsx`.

- **A coding session's model on Jarvis's OWN tool names writes bash on Windows too, a short file is shown whole after a missed edit, and another tool's argument names are renamed at the translation seam** (v1.573.0; the owner's yes to the three harness fixes the glm benchmark pointed to)
  - **Why**: glm-5.3-flash on v1.570.0 (48 tasks x 3 repeats, product path) lost NO task to a tool mistake, and still spent about 6% of its tool calls on them: about 90 Linux commands on cmd.exe (31 of 48 tasks), 42 of 468 edits refused, 4 calls with a wrong argument name. A Claude lead's `Bash` ran in Git for Windows' bash since v1.386.0 and every helper's `shell` since v1.421.0; the LEAD on the generic tool set was the one command writer left on cmd.
  - **The shell** (`dialects.own_command_tools`: a dialect none of whose tools reaches `shell` / `process_start`, today GENERIC only): the one harness build both lanes share (`chat_turn`, MIRROR NOTE there) asks `command_env.helper_command_facts` ONCE per turn for a Build session's turn and hands `mode.build(own_shell_syntax=, start_note=)`; `shell_description` is that same call's `shell_words`, so the words and the shell that runs them cannot disagree. `HarnessTurn.shell_syntax_of` answers the turn's own language for a command tool called under its own name; `ctx_for` now puts the language on the context whether or not the name was translated. A call the HARNESS makes (the finish check, `mark_called_as`) keeps ONLY the language it was marked with: a folder's own check command stays cmd.exe. `finish.earlier_command_syntax` gives a carried-on reply's earlier own-name commands the turn's language. `build()` drops the decision for a dialect with a command tool of its own (Claude, Codex, Qwen keep theirs byte for byte), and a start note without a language. No bash found, another OS, a turn that is not a coding session's: exactly as before. The benchmark's `harness()` and `_command_shell` build the same facts, so its fingerprint names bash.
  - **The missed edit** (`textedit._not_found_message`): when NO line is close and the file is at most `SHORT_FILE_LINES` (60) lines and `SHORT_FILE_CHARS` (4,000) characters and not blank, the refusal is `NOTHING_CLOSE_SHOWN` plus the whole file numbered (no empty last line); past either bound, `NOTHING_CLOSE` as before. The engine still refuses; the session's note (`edit_memory`) still follows on its own line and the three-refusals gate is untouched.
  - **The names** (`dialects.OWN_ARGUMENT_ALIASES`, `own_arguments`): `file_path` → `path` on `read_file`, `write_file`, `edit_file`, `multi_edit`; `old_string` / `new_string` → `old` / `new` on `edit_file` and inside each of `multi_edit`'s `edits`. ONLY when the tool's own name is absent (both sent: nothing is renamed and the registry says what it got). Applied in `HarnessTurn.translate_calls`, BEFORE any check reads the call: a rename inside the registry would let the approval rules judge `file_path`-less arguments and the tool write somewhere they never looked at. A hallucinated name (`new_type`) and a missing argument still get the registry's sentence.
  - **MEASURED** (glm-5.3-flash, the same benchmark home, 48 tasks x 3 repeats, product path; v1.570.0 / v1.571.0's change / this): passed 128 / 129 / 126 of 144; model rounds per run 14.6 / 15.5 / 11.3; tool calls 2,239 / 2,399 / 1,748; failed tool calls 251 / 299 / 106; wrong-shell failures 90 / 80 / 1; input tokens 41.4M / 43.3M / 29.8M. The whole pass difference is `hard-scheduler-ordering` (2, 3, then 0 of 3): no run failed on a tool, each missed the same hidden rule, and seven more repeats on this version passed 6 (6 of 10 in all, against 5 of 6 before). Not shown to be a loss, not shown not to be. A recorded replay of a cmd.exe run (`test_finish_replay_v1386.py`) pins `git_bash.find` to None: two of its commands that failed in cmd run in bash.
  - **OPEN**: an AGENT RUN's own calls (a helper) do not pass `translate_calls`, so a helper's `new_string` still gets "missing required"; Codex's and Qwen Code's leads keep cmd.exe (not measured); the helper's Environment line (`host_os_line(bash=)`) has no twin in a coding turn's prompt (the tool descriptions carry it).
  - **Pin**: `tests/test_local_model_fit_v1573.py` (the real Build turn with the real Git bash; every listed revert red). Moved on purpose: `test_bash_dialect_v1386.py` (the generic shell follows bash when found; `daemon/chat_turn.py` is a named writer of `own_shell_syntax=`), `test_harness_bench_build_session_v1378.py`, `test_helper_standard_v14210.py` (the lead's words), `test_missed_edit_words_v1462.py`.

- **The harness's built-in words say how to take care, and a rule every worker must hold goes in the guardrail FLOOR, never in "How you work"** (v1.571.0)
  - **What the built-in layer gained** (`harness/components.py`): `HONEST_SUMMARY` right after the base's "what you changed and how you checked it" and `PROJECT_CARE` after `ASK_POLICY`, both in `DEFAULT_SYSTEM_PROMPT` and `DEFAULT_PLAN_PROMPT`; `SECRET_RULE` and `READ_IS_NOT_AN_ORDER` at the end of `DEFAULT_GUARDRAILS` (six rules). `FLOOR_CLOSING` says the count in a word; a test ties that word to `len(DEFAULT_GUARDRAILS)`, so a rule added without it goes red. 919 characters a prompt, 1,107 on the generic tool set.
  - **Why the floor**: a helper and a team worker get the floor through `harness/standard` and never see the lead's "How you work"; a folder's own `system_prompt.md` replaces "How you work" WHOLE (the two lines there go with it) and can never remove the floor.
  - **A floor rule never argues with the rest of the prompt**: `READ_IS_NOT_AN_ORDER` names its exceptions (the folder's instruction files, a skill, a helper brief), because the Memory, Skills and Helper-brief sections of the same prompt say "follow". `PROJECT_CARE` has a new dependency ADDED AND REPORTED, never asked about: `ASK_POLICY` stands.
  - **The generic tool set has hints** (`dialects.GENERIC.hints`; glm and every unrecognised local model speak it): `read_file` before `edit_file` (copy `old` exactly), `glob` and `grep` over shell commands, `plan_update` on multi-step work. Each only when the turn shows its tools (`dialect_hints`): no edit hint in plan mode.
  - **A change to these words regenerates `tests/fixtures/team_first_off_golden_v1400.json`** (`IJ_REGEN_TEAM_FIRST_OFF=1`), CHECKED FIRST by taking the additions back out of the new capture and comparing with the old one (system prompt and tool specs); `tests/test_team_first_prompt_v1400.py`'s docstring records each regeneration.
  - **Measured** (2026-11-24 to 25; glm-5.3-flash, generic tool set, product path, 48 tasks x 3 repeats, the same benchmark home, master `7519620e1` first and then the branch): 128 then 129 of 144 passed (core 60 and 60, hard 26 and 27, frontier 33 and 32, team 9 and 10); reply time mean 92 s both; model calls 2,098 then 2,230 (+6.3%), input tokens 41.4M then 43.3M (+4.7%), inside the first run's own spread between repeats (9.6M to 19.8M). No gain and no loss this run can show. NOT measured on a Claude or GPT model.
  - **A fresh benchmark home has no key for the GLM gateway** (401 "No api key passed in"; every run "did not run"). The home `C:\ijb` holds it; never copy it to another home without the owner's yes.
  - **Pinned by** `tests/test_harness_prompt_care.py` (10 reverts, each red).

- **Two writers never meet: a change of a Build › Code session's model, effort or mode is never checked against one coding tool and saved on another's row (one under way when a switch starts lands on the EARLIER tool first; one sent while the switch holds its claim is refused at once, in words), and outside any git repository a write of a folder's files waits for, or is refused in words beside, a write of a folder above or below it** (v1.565.1; items 3b and 3c of `docs/BUILD-CODE-FOLLOW-UP-PLAN-2026-11-20.md`).
  - **3b, the tool gate**: `HostedService._tool_gate(sid)`, an `asyncio.Lock` in the weak map `_tool_gates` (kept only while someone holds it). `patch` with any of `service.TOOL_FIELDS` (`model`, `effort`, `mode`, `load_folder_resources`; a new field the tool owns goes in it) refuses with `W.CLAIMED.format(what=TS.CLAIM)` while `live.claim == TS.CLAIM`, then holds the gate through the old body (`_patch_now`: its row read, the tool's live change, its row write); no wait between that look and the lock. `tool_switch.switch` takes the gate right after its claim and holds it through `_switch_claimed` (the plan under the claim, the stop, the park, the row update), letting it go before the claim with no wait between. A name or the Capabilities boxes take no gate (the session's, not the tool's).
  - **Why refuse rather than wait**: a change that waited for the switch's end would be checked against the NEW row, and `check_model` checks a name's shape only, so a Claude Code model would be saved on a Codex row. A change already holding the gate is safe: the switch reads the row after it and parks it with the earlier tool (a stricter mode picked then is what the new tool's mode is worked out from).
  - **3c, nested plain folders**: every write of a folder's files goes through `checkpoints.folder_locked(folder, fn)` (the four rewind routes' `_lock`, the Changes tab's Revert, the git routes' `locked`, the delete's `_move_own_copy`), inside the session's own `app:<sid>` lock. In a repository it is `build_git._locked(lock_key)` as before (nested folders share the repository's lock). Outside one it is `_locked(lock_key)` and THEN `_apart`: the folder (`_plain_path`: absolute, `normcase`, no separator at its end) waits on `_PLAIN_FREED` while any `_PLAIN_HELD` path is it, above it or below it (`_nested`; `work-2` is not inside `work`), at most `build_git.GIT_LOCK_WAIT_S`, then `GitRefused(NESTED_BUSY, 409)` (the rewind routes say `FOLDER_BUSY`). Always in that order, never the other way round. A new write of a session's folder takes `folder_locked`, never `_locked(lock_key(...))` by itself.
  - **Pinned by** `tests/test_tool_switch_patch_race_fu3b.py` (the switch held in its stop: every tool field refused at once, a name still saved; a change held in its row write: the switch is SEEN waiting for the gate, the new row has the plan's values and the parked record the change) and `tests/test_build_code_nested_folders_fu3c.py` (two real sessions, the first rewind held INSIDE its write: the second refused in words both ways, then it goes; the second seen WAITING and writing only after the first; folders beside it, `work-2` included, never wait). Mutation counts are in the commit message.
  - **KNOWN**: a switch pressed while a change is still being made LIVE in the running tool waits for it (bounded by that tool's own control wait: Claude Code's `CONTROL_TIMEOUT_S`, 30 s a request); a change that reaches the daemon after the switch has ENDED, from a window that still shows the old tool, is checked against the new tool (a mode or effort it lacks is refused; a model name is not checked against a list); the nesting check compares spellings (`abspath`), so a junction or a mapped drive to the same folder is not seen as it (as `lock_key` never did); a plain folder's write does not wait for a repository's write inside it (measured: its checkpoint holds a repository with a commit as ONE entry and lists none of its files).
- **A pull request's REVIEW COMMENTS are read by the checks watcher on their OWN clock with the user's `gh`, kept as Iron Jarvis's record, and reach a model only inside ONE fence when the user presses Answer these; "answered" comes from the turn's journal, and nothing is ever sent to GitHub** (v1.562.0; 26-a, R2.16 of `docs/BUILD-OUT-PLAN-2026-11-20.md`; idea 26 of `docs/next-leaps/07-coding-github-evidence-scoreboard-sealed-room.md`).
  - **Where**: `harness/pr_comments.py` (`PrCommentRecord`, a late model; `read` = `gh api repos/<o>/<r>/pulls/<n>` then `--paginate …/comments` and `…/reviews`, the owner, repository and number parsed from the session's OWN `pr_url` by `pr_ref` and checked to GitHub's characters; `_pages` reads a newer gh's one array AND an older gh's arrays back to back; `rows_from` keeps the newest `MAX_KEPT` 200, a review with no words or still PENDING is not a comment, an outdated comment has `outdated` and its original line; it sorts GitHub's items newest first WITHOUT reading their words (`_newest_first`, each id once) and makes only the kept 200 plain and masked — a mask runs every rule, 1500 comments blocked the loop 3.4 s — and the watcher calls it through `asyncio.to_thread`). Every word is made plain (`code_comments.plain`), MASKED and only then cut (`_cut`). Author kinds from `author_association` + `user.type`: owner, member, collaborator are TICKED (D-26.3's writers); outside and bot are shown unticked with their label.
  - **The clock** (`daemon/routes/ci_watch._read_comments`, in `_tend` for every state but FIXING, REVIEWING and CLOSED, and while `ci_autofix` is off — that setting is about fixing checks; while it is off a WATCHING record's `watch_since` is cleared and set again when fixing is turned on, so a record made for the comments never ends the checks' watch as "still running after 6 hours"): `CiWatchRecord.comments_*` (additive, NULL on an old row: every reader treats NULL as the default). First read `READ_FIRST_S` (300 s) after the watch first sees the pull request; × `READ_GROWTH` up to `READ_MAX_S` (1 h) when nothing new; back to the first wait on a new comment; ended (`comments_done`) by merged / closed (one read) or `READ_FOR_DAYS` (30) quiet days; `READ_FAILS_MAX` (5) failed reads in a row only REST it (`comments_done = "failed"`, said ONCE as a `prComments: {stopped: true}` line): `due` reads it again `READ_AGAIN_S` (24 h) later, a day apart, and the first read that works sets `comments_done` back to "" (an hour offline once ended every pull request's reading for good). Nothing is read under Emergency stop or while `_busy` (a reply would be split by the line), and `_busy` is asked AGAIN after the read (a reply started while gh ran): nothing is kept and the clock is not moved. The clock is `pr_comments.now()` (`utcnow` is the seam).
  - **The notice**: new comments write ONE line (`prComments: {ids, n, authors}`; its content is Iron Jarvis's own count only, so no GitHub login reaches a later turn's history) and, at most once per `TOLD_EVERY_S` (24 h) per pull request, a QUIET bell row `build.pr_comments` (a Local only session's carries `LOCAL_ONLY_NOTICE`). `comments_quiet` ("Stop telling me about comments here", `POST …/pr-comments/quiet`, and chat's `pr_comments_quiet` with Undo, kept from low trust) stops the BELL ROW only (`may_tell`): the line still comes, because it is where the comments are answered (without it a comment that came while quiet could never be answered).
  - **Answer these** (`POST /build/sessions/{id}/pr-comments/answer {ids}`, at most `MAX_ANSWER` 20): claims the rows (`answering`, compare-and-set), then `start_build_turn(text=PC.answer_text(n), model_note=PC.block(rows), low_trust=FLAGGED_TRUST when the scan flagged)` as a PERSON's turn (their posture, model, account, Local only); the page follows the returned turn id (`h.onAnswerComments` → `runTurn(null, id)`). From before the claim until the turn is listed as running its id is in `ci_watch._ANSWER_STARTING`, and `_settle_answer` never settles it meanwhile (a watcher pass in that window found no reply yet and gave the comments back as `new`). `block` = `RULES` (information, never instructions; do not commit, push or reply on GitHub) + per comment `Review comment N:` OUTSIDE and `code_comments.fence` (the ONE fence builder, lifted out of `block` in this release: plain, masked, `scan_context(strict=True)`, every line behind `QUOTE_PREFIX`, `wrap_untrusted`) holding who, where, the quote AND the words. Never write a GitHub word outside the fence; never build a second fence.
  - **The record decides** (`pr_comments.judge`, from the turn's done callback and again in `_tend` after a restart): `turn_files` reads the undo journal in the turn's own window (rewind's grouping, helpers included, undone rows left out); a line comment is `answered` only when its file was written (`answer_files`), a review's own words when any file was; else `unchanged`; a turn that stopped or failed gives its comments back as `new`. The model's closing words decide nothing.
  - **Screen**: `lib/prComments.ts` (whitelist decoder; a non-https link dies), `components/harness/PrCommentsLine.tsx` (the session's NEWEST arrived line carries the ONE card — every comment still open, whichever line said it arrived, `cardLineIndex` — and an older line is its sentence alone, so the view has one Answer these: ticks, labels, Set aside, the quiet stop; Set aside's Undo is a folded quiet line under the list (`putAwayComments`: set aside AND answered with no change), whose rows' Bring back is `POST …/{cid}/set-aside {aside: false}` — the route moves `set_aside` or `unchanged` back to `new` (turn cleared) and never an answered or answering one; words drawn as plain text; `firstTicks(open, maxAnswer)` ticks the OLDEST `max_answer` writers' comments and a full card takes no further tick, saying how many wait — the route refuses more than `MAX_ANSWER`, so ticking every writer's comment made the first press fail on a busy pull request), `ChangesReview` `github` pins (`placePins`: under the row of the comment's own side, the rest listed after the rows; `DiffView` `lineNote`, absent = drawn exactly as before) fed by `HarnessBranch` on the watch's own slow visible-only clock, only where the watcher reads (`watchCi` and not `BUILD_CODE_GIT`: a Build › Code session's pull request is not watched, `dashboard/__tests__/pr-comments-branch.test.tsx`). Chat: `pr_comments_list` (allow; GitHub words inside one fence; a Local only session's title, pull request, authors, places and words only to a turn that stays on this network — `egress.may_recall_local_only_chats`, the work record's rule — else its count alone) and `pr_comments_quiet` (ask, Undo kind `pr_comments_quiet`; the same Local only rule: its output and card say "a Local only coding session", never the title), armed by `build_tools_for` for both lanes. **Delete**: `chat.delete_thread` (both doors: `DELETE /build/sessions/{id}` and `DELETE /chat/threads/{id}`) calls `pr_comments.forget_session`, which drops the session's `PrCommentRecord` rows AND its `CiWatchRecord`; a new per-session table of this feature is dropped there too.
  - **Left open**: Chat › Code only (the watcher's `_candidates` reads Chat › Code rows; the pull request a Build › Code session opens since v1.561.0 brings no comments yet); a file changed only by a command is not counted as answering a comment; an edited comment keeps its state; the signed-in user's own comments are listed like anyone's; not driven against the real GitHub (a fake `gh` pins our reading of its answer shapes) or on the packaged build.
  - **Pins**: `tests/test_pr_comments_read.py`, `tests/test_pr_comments_fence.py`, `tests/test_pr_comments_watch.py`, `tests/test_records_pr_comments.py`, `dashboard/__tests__/pr-comments.test.tsx`, `dashboard/__tests__/pr-comments-session.test.tsx`, `dashboard/__tests__/pr-comments-branch.test.tsx`.

- **Build › Code commits and opens a pull request in ONE press each, with a message Iron Jarvis wrote from its OWN record; it keeps working in the folder, its own branch is offered once per folder, and a folder session commits ONLY the ticked files** (v1.561.0; 22-b, R3.4 of `docs/BUILD-OUT-PLAN-2026-11-20.md`; idea 22 of `docs/next-leaps/06-coding-proof-council-board-landing.md`; choice D-22.1 as the master plan settled it).
  - **The row**: `HostedSessionRecord.git_json`, written ONLY by `service.create` and `routes/build_app_git` (`store.merge_git`, onto the facts as stored now). A session on its OWN branch keeps exactly the facts `branches.start` returns (`session_git.facts_of` = `branches.clean_git_record`); its `folder` IS its own copy of the folder, and `session_git.checkout_of` (the picked folder) is what the list's `?folder=`, the screen's folder groups (`lib/buildApp.groupByFolder`: `checkout || folder`) and the check consent (`proof.decide(key=)`, `routes/build_app_proof`) are keyed on. A folder session keeps only its pull request (`{"direct": "1", branch, pr_url, pr_number}`), which never reads as a branch (`facts_of` is None).
  - **The start**: `POST /build/app/sessions {branch: true}` (StrictBool) runs `branches.start` off the loop; refused in words on the folder chip for no folder, a saved conversation or a folder that cannot branch; a row that could not be written takes the fresh copy back (`branches._undo_start`). The tool runs in the copy with the main folder's `.venv` / `node_modules` (`session_git.tool_env` in `_start_process`). The start box asks "Work on a new branch in this folder?" ONCE per folder that can branch (`folder_defaults.VIEW_BRANCH`, `{"work": "branch"|"folder"}`; `save` and `save_checks` keep it); after the answer a quiet switch changes one session. Chat: `build_defaults_set view=build_code new_branch=true|false` (Undo puts the folder's answer back, or none: asked again).
  - **The presses** (`routes/build_app_git.py`, thin over the SAME `branches` functions as `routes/build_git.py`): refused while the reply runs (`service._claim`), while Iron Jarvis checks it (`proof.checking`), while the tool still works or the conversation is open in a terminal (`branching._files_free`), and re-checked inside the session's git lock and the folder's lock (`branching._still_free`); the claim holds the session so no message starts meanwhile. Merge and Discard are a branch session's only; Discard stops the tool first (it works in that copy), then archives.
  - **A folder session's commit** (`session_git.folder_commit`): the ticked paths must each be one of the repository's changed files NOW (`git status`), `:(literal)` pathspecs, `git commit --only -F - --` (what the user staged for any other path stays staged); a new file is `git add --intent-to-add` first and reset when the commit fails. Ticked by default = what differs from the session's FIRST message's checkpoint tree (`session_git.ours`), else the tool's own file steps; never everything. A pull request only off the repository's main branch (`main_branch`: the remote's HEAD, else `main`, else `master`), its branch pushed without force and without writing an upstream into the user's config.
  - **The draft** (`session_git.draft_message`): the first user message (never a carry-on or a steer), masked, summary ≤ 72 characters, and the files. NO model call (`POST …/git/draft {paths}` writes it again for the ticks; the screen asks only while the user has not edited it).
  - **Screen**: `components/buildCode/ReplyGit` under the newest SETTLED reply (not while it runs, a card waits, the check runs or its first-time card asks), read once per settled reply; Commit is the one main button; Open a pull request appears once there are commits the main branch lacks. The row's pull request link is the folder's OWN read for a folder session (`session_git.folder_status` sends `pr_url` only while the folder is on that pull request's branch; `buildAppGit.prLinkFor` never takes the view's `pull_request` for one), and once one is open, commits the remote's branch lacks (`unpushed`) get one quiet Push (`POST …/git/push`, never a force). Inside Build › Code the Branch view says "Open a pull request" too (`root=BUILD_CODE_GIT`). A branch session's Changes tab has a Branch scope: `HarnessBranch.BranchView` with `root=BUILD_CODE_GIT`, `watchCi={false}` and `commentsKey=buildCodeCommentsKey(id)` (the message box's own key; the bare id was a comment no message ever took). A folder session's row is drawn only for the session's OWN changes (a ticked file), a pull request to open, or one open (`buildAppGit.hasGitWork`, G3): the user's own changes alone draw nothing. A start with the branch question unanswered saves the folder's "No, in the folder" (offered ONCE). `CodePanes` is memo'd: every prop SessionPane hands it is the same value frame after frame (`archivedFromPanes`); a new function there drew the panes on every streamed word (`build-app-view-v1409`, `code-comments-v1544-0` went red). The git paths are written out whole for each root (`buildGit.at(root, chat, app)`): `tests/test_wave6_dead_code_v14390.py` reads callers statically and sees no `${root}/…` template. Chat cannot press either: the page table's Build row (`daemon/pages.py`) says where they are, so the feature lookup finds it for "commit what Codex did".
  - **The copy and the sessions in it** (review 3): Delete of a branch session MOVES its copy to the trash and keeps the branch (`service._move_own_copy` → `branches.remove_worktree`, under the session's git lock and the folder's, after the tool stops; a copy that cannot move refuses the delete in words, `session_git.NOT_DELETED`, nothing deleted), as Chat › Code's delete does. A session made IN a branch session's copy carries its facts (`session_git.shared_json`): Branch from here (`branching._make_row`) and a helper (`handoff`), so each is grouped under the picked folder, gets the main folder's environment and commits on that branch. While ANOTHER session (archived too; never this session's own helper) has the copy as its folder (`session_git.others_in_copy`), Discard is refused in words (`COPY_SHARED`, asked again inside the locks) and a delete leaves the copy to it (`worktree.moved: false`).
  - **Left open**: a pull request's failing checks are not fixed for Build › Code yet (22-c); a branch session's copy is a new folder to the tool's own per-project memory and trust (Pi's trust); a session opened from the tool's saved conversation finds its first message only in Iron Jarvis's own log; the draft's first message and the fallback ticks are read from the newest `events.MAX_LINES` events (a longer session's draft starts from a later message); two sessions in one copy each record only their OWN Merge, push and pull request on their row (the other reads the branch's pull request from GitHub when asked); Delete does not wait for this session's OWN helpers to stop (`handoff.stop_helpers_soon`) before it moves the copy, and their rows still name it (a copy one holds open on Windows refuses the delete in words); not driven with a real coding tool, a real GitHub or on the packaged build.
  - **Pins**: `tests/test_build_app_git_v1.py` (the check, the re-check inside the locks, the taken-back copy, the work record and project row, the lookup, the delete's move, the shared copy, the check's PATH), `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-branch-git.test.tsx`, `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-reply-git-mount.test.tsx` (the real view: drawn under a settled reply, never while it runs, a card waits, it checks or the check's card asks), the Branch-view comment in `code-comments-v1544-0.test.tsx`.

- **A copy of a DELETED session's files never outlives it: every own checkpoint store of a deleted session is swept at the daemon's start and again after a delete that could not remove it; a store whose row exists, archived or not, or whose row is only MISSING (a backup restored), is never touched** (v1.559.2; item 3a of `docs/BUILD-CODE-FOLLOW-UP-PLAN-2026-11-20.md`, privacy).
  - **Why**: `forget_own_store` could not remove a store a file of which another program held open (an antivirus scan, a `git gc` still packing; Windows cannot delete an open file), said nothing and never tried again: a copy of the user's folder (a `.env` included) stayed in `workspaces/code-checkpoints/` with no session. It now logs a warning, and `own_store_left` tells the delete.
  - **Deleted is not missing** (the review): a backup restored leaves out the rows of the sessions made since (the stores are in no backup, and a restore removes nothing the archive does not carry), and undoing it (the before-restore archive) brings the rows back. A store is a DELETED session's only when its row is gone AND either its delete's mark is beside it (`<safe>.git.deleted`, `checkpoints.OWN_DELETED`: `HostedService.delete` writes it BEFORE the row goes, `checkpoints.mark_own_store_deleted`, and takes it back when the row could not be removed; `forget_own_store` writes it again before the removal and drops it once the store is gone; so a delete cut short, even right after the row went, is still known) or the session's own folder (`events.session_dir`, `hosted/<sid>`: its log and its checkpoints' record; the delete moves it to the trash, a restore keeps it) is gone too. Stores left by v1.546.0–v1.559.1 have no mark, and their folders went to the trash: still swept. Pinned with a real `create_backup` → `restore_backup_live` → sweep → restore of the before-restore archive.
  - **The sweep**: `checkpoints.sweep_own_stores(home, row_ids)` removes every `<safe>.git` (and a half-made `<safe>.git.<8 hex>.new`) under `OWN_DIR` of a deleted session (above), matched by `own_store(home, id).name` of each row and of each name under `hosted/`; nothing else in that folder, and never a junction (a link is no directory there). It drops a mark whose store is gone, and one beside a store whose row AND session folder are both there (that session is whole; a later restore leaving the row out must not read the leftover as a delete). It LISTS the folder FIRST and reads the rows AFTER (a store is made only for a session whose row exists), so a session made meanwhile is never taken for a deleted one. Rows or session folders that cannot be read remove NOTHING (`Swept.unread`; `store.all_ids` raises and never makes the table: a missing table is not "no sessions"), and `service.sweep_left_stores` removes nothing while the database is SET ASIDE and unanswered (`core.db.read_set_aside_marker`; the rows are in the database set aside): it says so once and nothing is tried again until the next start. It logs how many it removed and how many are left, and only "tried again later" when another sweep follows.
  - **When**: once at start (`bg_tasks["build_code_stores"]` → `service.sweep_stores_until_gone`, off the loop; boot never waits; no `loop_health` row, so no words in `loopWords.ts`), and after a delete whose store is still there (`HostedService._sweep_stores_later`): one sweep after each wait of `STORE_RETRY_S` (1 min, 10 min, 1 h; none at once, the delete just failed) until none is left (and the rows were read), then the next start. A start that leaves one, or could not read the rows, goes through the same waits. The deletes have one round of waits at a time (a later failed delete starts it again); the start's round is apart from it, so the two may sweep at the same moment (harmless: a removal re-reads the disk). `shutdown` cancels the deletes' round. The wait is `HostedService._sleep` (a test replaces it).
  - **`_writable_then_again` ADDS the write bit** (`S_IMODE(lstat) | S_IWRITE`) and swallows any failure of its retry: setting the mode to `S_IWRITE` took a POSIX folder's read and search bits, and nothing could remove it after. `_remove_store` is shared by the delete and the sweep and re-reads the disk for its answer.
  - **Pinned by** `tests/test_build_code_left_stores_fu3a.py` (real git, real routes, the real lifespan; "held open" is a real open file on Windows; elsewhere, where an open file can be deleted, `os.unlink` refuses that one file each time, as Windows does — a read-only folder would not hold, because the removal makes a folder writable when its own removal fails). Its helper `_rows` removes each row's session folder, so only the ROWS keep those stores (the second review: with the folders there, a sweep that read no row, or no archived one, still passed). Mutation counts in the commit message.
  - **KNOWN**: the write-bit rule of `_writable_then_again` can go red only off Windows (Windows' chmod sets only the read-only flag), so only the Linux run of the suite pins it; a store held open through all three waits stays until the next start; only the count is logged, not which session; a store whose delete could write no mark AND could not move the session's folder to the trash is kept as a missing row's, and so is one whose early mark a sweep running at that very moment dropped (it read the row before it went) when the daemon then died before `forget_own_store` wrote the mark again.

- **Iron Jarvis checks every Build › Code reply by running the folder's tests ITSELF, only after a clean reply that changed files, only a command the user said yes to EXACTLY, and says what happened from its own record; a failing run is check-again's third reason** (v1.551.0; 22-a, R1.9 of `docs/BUILD-OUT-PLAN-2026-11-20.md`; idea 22 of `docs/next-leaps/06-coding-proof-council-board-landing.md`; choices D-22.2, D-22.3).
  - **Where**: `hosted/proof.check(svc, sid, turn_id, events, row=, force=)` runs FIRST inside check-again's judging task (`check_again._judge_in`), so the judge reads its record; the tool's `done` is already out (the reply is never held). Same gates as check-again (`_after_turn`), plus: the message CHANGED files (`proof.turn_changes`: `panes.changes_view(scope="turn")` from git when the message's start is known, else the tool's own finished edit/write steps of THAT message only; a follow-up sent back for failing tests is checked again even when it changed nothing: `Chain.tests_failing`). The command is decided FIRST: a folder with no test command, or one the user turned off, costs no git read and writes no record (every clean reply's end runs this; keep it cheap).
  - **The command** (`proof.decide`): the folder's `finish.toml` `enabled = false` / `tests = false` is off (`why: folder_file`); else the folder's kept answer (`folder_defaults.read_checks`, the `checks` key of `BuildFolderRecord.defaults_json`, written only by `save_checks`; `save` keeps it): off, or the command kept as is; a card's yes is asked AGAIN when the folder now proposes another command; else a proposal (`proof.propose`: `plugins.toml` `[[checks]]`, `finish.doc_test_commands`, `package.json` `scripts.test` by lock file, a pytest section, `Cargo.toml`, `go.mod`) is a CARD (`tests: "ask"`) and runs only after `POST /build/app/sessions/{sid}/checks {command, run, turn}` (`routes/build_app_proof.py`): the route recomputes the proposal and refuses (409) any command that is not exactly it, or that the screen could not show whole (`proof.shown_command(c) != c`); then `check_again.recheck` checks that reply. A yes that starts no check (the reply is no longer the newest, a reply runs, Emergency stop) still answers the card: `_note_kept` writes a `why: "kept"` record under that reply when its newest record is still the card (never over a check that began). NOTHING reads the coding tool's steps for a command.
  - **The run** (`proof.run_checks`, BLOCKING, for ideas 23 and 25 too): `SandboxManager.run_check` → `NativeSandbox.run(env=build_env(...), keep_windows=False)` (the new `env` / `keep_windows` keywords; omitted = exactly as before), `main_env.find` for a branch folder's `checkout`, `procs.background_lane()` (below-normal) and `procs.owned_by("app:<sid>")`; one run per folder (ONE thread lock per folder inside `run_checks`, for every caller; a run waiting for it looks at its `stop` every `LOOK_S`, so any stop below ends the wait at once, and `busy_wait_s` gives `error="busy"`); `TIMEOUT_S` 300 (a kept `timeout_s` up to 1800). Stop (`service.interrupt` with no turn running → `proof.stop`), Emergency stop (`Orchestrator.stop_unattended` → `proof.stop_all`), a new message and Iron Jarvis closing (`_unwanted`, read right before the command starts and every `LOOK_S`; it reads Emergency stop ITSELF, so a switch turned on while the plan, the log or git were read never lets the command start) kill the tree, on a thread of its own (`_stop_run` is called on the loop; a tree with no job falls back to a `taskkill`), at once when closing; a Stop, Emergency stop or closing sends NOTHING back afterwards.
  - **The count** (`harness/test_counts.read_counts`): ONE run of ONE runner's own summary or None: two runners' summaries, a one-per-run summary seen twice (`vitest run && vitest run -c e2e`, `pytest a; pytest b`, `npm test --workspaces`) and any summary in the part before the parsed tail (searched by `_HEAD`, up to `HEAD_CHARS`; a cut line goes with it) give None; only go / cargo / dotnet's per-part lines are added up. No number is ever guessed; `from_run` runs off the loop. A program not on PATH (9009 / 127 + "not recognized"/"not found"), a time-out and a run with no tests are `not_run` / `none`, never `failed`: the tool is never sent back for the PC's own setup. "No tests" is a count of 0 with exit 0 or the runner's own none-collected exit (`proof.NO_TESTS_EXIT`: pytest / unittest 5) ONLY; 0 counted with any other exit is a suite that broke before a test ran (jest "Test suite failed to run … Tests: 0 total" exits 1, pytest's usage error 4): `failed`, sent back. Only `passed` gets the check mark (`proofTone`).
  - **The record**: one `proof` event, `live.log.append` ONLY (never streamed into a running reply), keyed `for_turn`, `state` checking → done, `by: "iron_jarvis"`, `line` built by `proof.line_for` from the record alone; `night.finish_tests` / `finish_needs_you` read it as they read a finish record (the same `tests` key; no second reading). Check-again's `tests_failed` reason sends `proof.tests_failed_note` (the run's output tail masked, scanned by `detect_injection`, fenced by `wrap_untrusted`; flagged = not sendable, `withheld` on the line); stuck = the same output signature twice; the end rewrites the record with `ended`, `origin_at` and `verdict` (one line, no second notice). `proof.after_reply(listener)` is the seam for 27-c and 28.
  - **Screen**: `lib/buildAppProof` decodes; `lib/buildApp` folds one row per `for_turn` (newest in place, `proofAt`); `components/buildCode/ProofLine` draws the line, the card (only the NEWEST check row may show it), Stop while checking, Rewind to before the message (the preview) when `ended`; the session view's `checking` makes `backgroundRereadMs` read again while it holds. "verified" is retired on Build › Code (`isBuildCodeSurface`).
  - **Chat**: `build_checks_set` (ask, Undo, kind `build_checks`) and `coding_reply_check` (allow) in `settings/records_build`, armed by `build_tools_for` for both lanes. Chat keeps ONLY a command the user named, and its card says that command and what it costs (`spend_check` → `chat_checks_words`, `proof.COST_WORDS`); with none named it never keeps the folder's proposal (its card showed none): a kept command stays as it is, a folder that was off is forgotten so the first-time card asks under the next changed reply (`proof.proposal` names it in the answer). `coding_reply_check` says a record is the NEWEST reply's only when its `for_turn` is that reply's (and it is that reply's newest record); "running" and "checking now" are asked of the running app (`live.turn`, `proof.checking`), never read from the log, so a log Iron Jarvis left mid-reply or mid-check says "did not finish"; otherwise "no check of its newest reply" and the earlier one as an earlier one's. `change_claims` kind `coding_checks` ("I turned off the tests after each reply"). Their words on the card, the toast and the run log (a new built-in needs ALL of these, or the toast says "use build checks set"): `mission.ASK_WORDS` (asking tools) + `_RUN_TOOL_WORDS`, `lib/toolWords`, `lib/runWords`, `lib/trustWords` (a low-trust-denied tool), `desktop/main.js` `TOOL_WORDS` and the `desktop-tool-words-v13580.json` row, then `scripts/gen_tool_words.py` and the fixtures (`IJ_REGEN_TOOL_WORDS_EVERYWHERE=1`, `IJ_REGEN_TOOL_LINES=1`).
  - **Left open**: a turn whose start was not recorded and a folder with no git count only the tool's own file steps as changes; a command the user said yes to (`npm test`) runs whatever the folder's script says at the time; a slow suite holds the line for up to 5 minutes; the run's whole output is read into the daemon's memory (`procs.run` reads both pipes; only its end is kept), as the shell tool's is; not driven with a real coding tool or on the packaged build.
  - **Pins**: `tests/test_build_app_proof_v1.py`, `tests/test_test_counts.py`, `tests/test_build_app_check_again_v1497.py` (22-a cases), `tests/test_records_build_checks_v1.py`, `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-proof.test.tsx`.

- **The Timeline tab opens on the GRAPH of the models; a node is drawn only for a model that ran and an edge only for work that changed hands, both from the Timeline's own data** (v1.550.0, the owner's "model-centric node view" brief).
  - **Data**: `dashboard/lib/buildGraph.ts` `replyGraph(TimelineReply)` — no new frame, field or route; the live fold, a rejoin and a saved reply draw one graph. The LEAD is the whole reply (`waiting` while a helper works and the lead has no call in flight and no own step running). Helpers group into WAVES by overlapping times (`helperWaves`: a running helper reaches to now). A BACK node (`back:<wave>`, the lead after the wave) exists ONLY when a model call starts at or after the wave's end (5 ms clock slack), or while the reply runs (`queued` with helpers still working, `waiting` with the reports in and no call yet); a reply that ended before the lead asked again draws NO back node and NO report edge. Edges: `handoff` (its words are the helper's `task`, plus `reason`) and `report` (the helper's `result`). `edgeFresh` lights an edge for `FRESH_MS` (4 s) after its `at`, live only.
  - **Layout** (`graphLayout`, from the panel's width alone): a node's box depends only on its wave and its index in it, so nothing moves while a reply runs; a wave wider than `graphColumns(width)` wraps, left-aligned, and its edges run down the column gaps, never across a node.
  - **Screen**: `components/harness/HarnessGraph.tsx`, mounted by `HarnessTimeline` only with `graph` (HarnessPanes passes it; without it the tab is the v1.390.0 axis, which the older tests render directly). State is told by a static tone-token glow AND words ("Waiting for helpers", "Reading app.py"); hover OR focus shows a card (a node's numbers, an edge's task/report); a press sets `focus` (`lead` / `back:n` = every row, `helper:<id>` = that helper's own bar and steps only), "All models" or Esc (after closing a picked card) clears it; the live read keeps running underneath. The only motion is `.graph-edge-flow` in globals.css (four dash runs, off under reduced motion). Words shared by both views live in `lib/timelineWords.ts` (re-exported by HarnessTimeline).
  - **Pinned by** `dashboard/__tests__/timeline-graph-v1550.test.tsx` (the recorded v1390 fixture + hand-made parallel, sequential, ended-early, failed replies; mutation-checked: the back-node gate, live-only lighting, the lead's wait, the overlap rule) and `dashboard/e2e/timeline-graph.spec.ts` (a real session, the reply stubbed in the browser; 1440 and 390 wide). The v1390 pane tests press the lead's node first.
  - **OPEN**: a helper's own helpers are not drawn (the daemon does not send them); the lead's thinking level is the session's setting, said only when the reply ran on the session's own model.

- **A folder in no git repository keeps its checkpoints in a store of Iron Jarvis's OWN, one per session, OUTSIDE the folder; every git call about that folder is told where it is, and the rest of the checkpoint code is the same** (v1.546.0; Release D of `docs/BUILD-CODE-T3-PLAN-2026-11-19.md`, phase 1d, decision D3).
  - **Where**: `checkpoints.own_store(home, sid)` = `<home>/workspaces/code-checkpoints/<sid>.git` (under `workspaces/`: a backup leaves it out, beside the kept indexes). Made by `make_own_store` (`git init --bare` into a temp name, then renamed; `core.bare=false`, `core.autocrlf=false`, `core.safecrlf=false`, `core.fileMode=false`, `core.longpaths=true`, the user's global `core.excludesFile` / `core.attributesFile` cleared, `gc.autoDetach=false`). `_own_info` writes `info/exclude` = `OWN_LEFT_OUT` (ONLY what a tool makes and remakes: `node_modules`, `.venv`, `__pycache__`, caches, `.hg`, `.svn`; the review: `build`, `dist`, `target`, `venv` from `_SKIP_DIRS` matched a client's "Target" folder at any depth and in any case, so it was in no checkpoint and the rewind said "already as they were") and `info/attributes` = `OWN_ATTRIBUTES` (`* -text -filter -ident -working-tree-encoding`: the folder's own `.gitattributes` never converts what is kept; the review measured `text=auto` turning LF into CRLF on the way back), and brings an older store up to date at its next use. NOTHING is written into the user's folder (a test checks there is no `.git` after a checkpoint, a rewind and an Undo).
  - **When**: `panes.record_turn_start` tries the folder's own repository first; ONLY when git says "not a git repository" (`GitUnreadable.not_a_repository`; a time-out or "dubious ownership" is NOT that) AND there is no `.git` in the folder or above it (`panes._git_on_disk`: a worktree whose main repository is away also says "not a git repository") is the store made. `has_own_store` (its `HEAD`) then decides for good: a folder that becomes a repository later goes on with the store.
  - **How a call finds the store**: `in_own_store(home, sid, folder)` sets a context variable for that ONE folder; `store_env(folder)` (read by `panes._run_git`, which `_git` and `_g_write` now go through, by `panes._write_tree` and by `checkpoints._restore_paths`) adds `GIT_DIR` + `GIT_WORK_TREE` for that folder only, inside the block only. `plan`, `restore`, `undo` and `forget_turns` are wrapped (`@_stored`); `repo_of` answers the store for it and never caches that as the folder's repository; `_delete_refs` goes through `_run_git`. A new git call about a session's folder in this module must go through `PN._git` / `PN._g_write` / `PN._run_git`, never `branches._g` directly, or it will miss the store.
  - **Bounded** before EVERY copy (`own_fits`, git's own listing with its ignore rules): at most `OWN_MAX_FILES` (5,000, the restore's own limit) and `OWN_MAX_BYTES` (256 MB) — at a message, and (`_own_limits`) before a preview, a rewind or an Undo copies the folder as it is now (the review: a preview of a big folder hashed it all into the store, and a rewind's `before` ref kept it). Past either, when git did not answer in time (`OWN_SLOW`), when it refused for another reason (`OWN_NOT_READ` with git's own reason, `unread_note`: a file another program holds open, a nested repository with no commit), or when the message stopped waiting (`OWN_LATE`), the message gets no checkpoint and the reason is kept beside the store (`ij-note.txt`, `own_note`); the Changes tab adds `own_words` (`OWN_WORDS`, or the reason). `gc --auto` after each kept copy (`tidy_own_store`).
  - **Words**: what names git where git is not the user's (`WHY_IGNORED`, `WHY_OLDER`, `DONE_STAYED`, `TOO_MANY`, `WHY_GIT_SLOW`) is said through `_say`, which gives the `*_OWN` sentence inside the store; a refusal because git did not read the folder is `OWN_UNREAD` (`_unread_words`); the preview carries `own: true` (its `too_many` then names no git) and the dialog's footnote is `REWIND_FOOT_OWN`. `_endings` is "" inside the store (it converts nothing; it used to walk up into parent folders for a `.gitattributes`), and there is no "commits since" (it holds none).
  - **A message with no checkpoint in a folder with no repository** is refused as `NO_CHECKPOINT` ("no checkpoint of the folder for this message"), no longer `NOT_REPO`: the folder can have checkpoints now; git missing altogether is `NO_GIT`.
  - **Two sessions in one plain folder** have a store each and share the folder's lock (`lock_key` is the folder's own path there, never the store's).
  - **A repository the folder became later is never written into**: `note_seen` (the Changes tab's idle read) does nothing for a session with its own store, and "This message" does not look for git checkpoints there (the review: the idle read wrote `refs/ironjarvis/…/seen-*` into a repository the tool had just `git init`-ed, or into a parent's, and the delete never swept it).
  - **The session's end**: `forget_session` removes the store (`forget_own_store`, read-only object files made writable first) AND still sweeps the folder and every repository its rows name; archive keeps it. A copy of files that are still in the folder is not trash.
  - **Pinned by** `tests/test_build_code_own_checkpoints_v1546.py` (real git, real routes; mutation counts in the commit message).
  - **KNOWN**: "This message" on the Changes tab still needs git (the whole session's list is shown); the per-file Revert of the Changes tab still needs git; the copies hold whatever the folder holds (a `.env` with keys included, unless a `.gitignore` leaves it out; this PC's global git ignore file is NOT read), on this PC only; a message that waits more than 3 s for its copy has none (as in a git folder, said as `OWN_LATE`); two sessions in NESTED plain folders (`plain` and `plain/sub`) take different folder locks (each write also waits for the other's since v1.565.1, above); a store whose files are held open when the session is deleted stays behind until a later sweep (v1.559.2, above); a folder inside a repository whose `.gitignore` ignores it gets no checkpoint at all (older than this release).

- **A Build › Code session can switch its coding tool: the SAME row goes on, the old tool's conversation is parked (and still held), and a hand-off written from our own record rides the user's NEXT message** (v1.545.0; Release C of `docs/BUILD-CODE-T3-PLAN-2026-11-19.md`, phases 2a to 2c).
  - **One module**: `hosted/tool_switch.py`. `POST /build/app/sessions/{id}/switch-tool {tool, model?, effort?, prefer_model?, prefer_effort?, confirm, expect?}` (`daemon/routes/build_app.py`, `HostedService.switch_tool`). Without `confirm` it answers `{switched: false, plan}` and CHANGES NOTHING (`_plan` is the whole decision and is run again under the claim); the plan's `lines` are the sentences the screen shows before the second press, and its `token` comes back as `expect`: a second press that would now do something else is refused (`CHANGED`, `code: changed`), never done silently. With `confirm` the answer is `{switched: true, session, plan, note, notice}`: `notice` is the conversation's own new line, so the screen shows it at once. The session view says `switches_tool` (false for a helper), `switched_tools` (true once the session EVER switched, whatever part of the conversation the view holds) and `handoff_pending` while a hand-off waits (`tool_switch.view_facts`).
  - **Refused before anything changes** (`_plan`): the same tool, an unknown or not-installed tool, an archived session, a helper session (`handed_from`), a running reply, a start in flight, an open card, another claim, background work (`keeps_working` / `working_alone` / `replying_alone` / `helpers_working`), a working helper of this session, a conversation open in a terminal, and (Claude Code) a branch or rewind it has not made yet (`branch.json`). "Signed out on this PC" refuses ONLY when the tool would run on this PC's own sign-in (the review: an Iron-Proxy account can run a tool this PC is signed out of).
  - **The account never leaves its group**: `handoff.helper_account` decides (its two refusals said in the switch's words through `not_read=` / `none_free=`), then `_same_group` checks what `_resolve_account` really picked. The group is the ANCHOR's: the row's account, else `anchor` in `tool_sessions.json` (the last account the session ran on, written at every `_park`, never removed). The review found the first cut read it from the parked records, which do not exist for a tool that had no conversation yet: Work account, no message, Pi, then Codex took any group's account.
  - **The mode is never looser**: `handoff.helper_mode(old, mode, want, words=…)`; Full access is never carried. On a switch BACK the parked mode is restored only when it is not `full` and is rated at most as loose as the session's mode NOW; a mode the table does not know (the user's own agent) is rated `DOES_NOT_ASK` there, as `helper_mode` rates it (the review: `strictness()` says ASKS for an unknown mode, which let `agent:yolo` back over an "ask" session).
  - **Model and effort**: what was picked for this switch (refused with `field` when the tool cannot take it); else the parked ones (a resume); else `prefer_*` (the screen's "folder default for that tool, else last pick", `lib/buildAppSwitch.toolStartWants`: never a refusal, an unusable preference is the tool's default); else the tool's default. The plan says which in a line.
  - **What a switch does, in order** (`_switch_claimed`, under `LiveSession.claim`): wait for a late id save; plan again; stop the process (programs it started are read first and said, `programs.stopped_words(why="switch")`); for a conversation that lived outside the view (`outside.json`) write the merged history INTO our log (old log to the trash first, `user` events stamped with the old tool, the mark removed); then, under `_handover()` and only for these steps (never across the stop), check the hold again, `_park`, and update the row (`harness`, account, model, effort, mode, `harness_session_id` = the parked id or "", `terminal_id=""`, `load_folder_resources=False`); `LiveSession.retool`; drop the old tool's context reading, check-again chain, limit wait and cut record; remove waiting messages that are commands (their words are SAID in the line, so nothing typed is lost); one `notice` with `switch: {from, to, resumes, since}`. The file is written BEFORE the row: a crash between leaves a parked record nobody reads, never a lost conversation.
  - **`LiveSession.retool(harness)`** resets everything that belonged to the old tool. `KEPT_ON_RETOOL` and `RESET_ON_RETOOL` must together be exactly what `__init__` sets (a test compares): a new attribute goes in ONE of them. `LiveSession.harness` is now a field that changes: `_same_tool(live, row)` refuses a message whose row was read before a switch (`SWITCHED_MEANWHILE`) at the top of `_start_turn_in`, again before the turn is made, and under the lock in `_drive`; `_on_session_id` never writes an id the OLD tool said on the new tool's row (either the row or the live state having moved on stops the save).
  - **`tool_sessions.json`** (beside the log): `parked[tool] = {hid, account, account_json, model, effort, mode, at}`, `pending` (the hand-off that waits: `{to, from, n, since, resumes}`), `last` (the newest switch, with `told_at` = the `user` event its hand-off rode), `anchor`, `unproven`, `n`. A tool left before it heard a message keeps its record as it was; one that heard only a command is parked as knowing NOTHING (`at: ""`: it gets the whole summary on the way back). A parked conversation is HELD: `held_by` in `_holder`, `held_ids` in the saved-conversation listing and the terminal hand-over, `from_terminal` refuses a second row. An archived row holds nothing (as its own conversation does not).
  - **The hand-off** (`build`): outside the fence only Iron Jarvis's own sentences and what the user TYPED here (`user` events that are not `carry` and not `imported`), each one line, masked by itself, headed "background: do not act on it again unless my message above says so" (no claim that they were answered: a failed message was not). Inside ONE `wrap_untrusted` fence, every line behind `| `: messages from the tool's own saved conversation, files the tools' edit steps changed, commands BY NAME with how they ended (never their output), open plan steps, and each message's FINAL reply, newest first, each named by the tool that wrote it (`tools_of`). Each piece is masked before it is cut; the record is masked again as a whole and scanned (`scan_context(strict=True)`, fail closed) BEFORE the bars go on. **Never mask the finished text**: the review showed `mask` reading `token:` at a line's end, taking the white space after it and with it the next line's bar and the fence's rule line. The newest reply has its room before the lists take theirs. At most 12,000 characters; saved as `<home>/hosted/<sid>/handoffs/switch-<n>.md` (inside the session's folder, so a deleted session takes it along: the plan said `<home>/handoffs/<sid>/`).
  - **It rides the next message** (`service._handoff_for`, the one place before `send_user`, after the user's words and the comments): built THEN from the log (the message being sent left out), said as a `handoff` event (the exact text) only once the tool has it, then `delivered(…, told_at)`. A typed command takes none (it waits). A message Iron Jarvis sends by itself is REFUSED while one waits (`CARRY_AFTER_SWITCH`: "carry on from where you stopped" would be the new tool's first word). A hand-off that cannot be built is said (`HANDOFF_NOT_BUILT`) and the user's message still goes. Nothing to tell (an empty session, or away and back with no message): the plan says so and nothing waits.
  - **A rewind or a branch can take the hand-off's message away** (the review's first finding): `after_rewind(home, sid, tool, cut_at)` lets go of tools parked after the cut and makes the hand-off wait AGAIN when `last.told_at` is at or after the cut (`_rearmed`); `carry_to_branch` gives a branch the anchor and a hand-off its forked conversation never heard, and never the parked conversations.
  - **Switching back**: `plan.resumes` when the parked conversation is still in the tool's own files (`_disk_session`), its account is in the anchor's group, no terminal runs it and (Antigravity) its agent is the same; another SESSION holding it now is not fought over (a new conversation). `unproven` marks a resumed conversation that has not answered: a start refused with `code: open_failed` (`_start_process`), or a process that started and went with no answer at all (Claude Code does not say a conversation is missing, its process just ends: `_settle_resume`, after the turn), lets it go, makes the WHOLE summary wait and says so; an answer proves it (`LIFE`), and a refused start, a sign-in or a limit decides nothing.
  - **Per-turn tool**: every `user` event carries `tool`; the fold (`dashboard/lib/buildApp.ts`) names each turn by it, else by the turn's `session` event, else the tool current at that point, else the first switch line's `from`, else the session's. `panes.since_switch` keeps what a tool REPORTED about itself (usage windows, context, MCP servers) off the next tool; the archive summary names the earlier tools.
  - **The mask learned three key names** (`private_key`, `secret_key`, `account_key`, any of `_`/`-`/nothing between) and a PEM body whose line breaks are written as `\n`; `dashboard/lib/stepDetail.ts` mirrors the rules (a test compares them).
  - **Also in this release**: `tests/test_base_health_v1173.py::test_a_probe_is_handed_the_time_the_page_budget_has_left` passed only on CI's retry in the v1.544.0 release run; the probe budget now reads `routes/knowledge._monotonic` and the test moves the time itself.
  - **Pinned by** `tests/test_tool_switch_v1545.py`, `tests/test_tool_switch_session_v1545.py` (two recorded tools in one session through `tests/fixtures/fake_two_tools_v1545.py`) and `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-switch-tool-v1545-0.test.tsx`; mutation counts are in the commit message (133 on the daemon side, all red; one redundant branch and two redundant guards were removed because a mutation of them changed nothing). Live on a scratch daemon with the real Claude Code and Codex: Codex named the file and a code word that existed only in Claude Code's reply; back on Claude Code it recalled its own word from its own conversation and learned Codex's from the "since" hand-off; Codex then reopened its own thread.
  - **KNOWN**: a summary is not the conversation, and a secret in a shape the mask does not know (a password in prose, a webhook address) goes to the other vendor inside it (the plan line says where the summary is sent, every time); the three call sites of `_same_tool` share one mutation (the helper is pinned, not each site); `_pane_facts`' use of `held_ids` has no test of its own; `patch` did not take the claim, so a model or mode change that raced a switch was validated against the row it read (v1.565.1, above); after a files-only rewind the hand-off says files were put back but still lists the earlier edits; the work map leaves out a session between a switch and its first message (its row has no conversation id then); Pi, OpenCode, Grok and Antigravity were not run live (Pi asks before nothing, so an "ask" session cannot switch to it until its mode is changed).

- **A comment on changed code rides a message as a structured field; the user's words go outside the fence, the path and the quoted code inside it** (v1.544.0; Release B of `docs/BUILD-CODE-T3-PLAN-2026-11-19.md`, all three phases).
  - **One daemon module for both Code views**: `harness/code_comments.py`. `clean(folder, raw)` decides each path from its TEXT (inside the session's folder; no stat, so a deleted file is fine and a network spelling is never touched), bounds every field (20 comments, 4,000 characters of quote, 2,000 of words) and refuses a comment with no words: a refusal is one sentence and NOTHING of the message is sent. `block()` writes what the tool reads: `Comment N:` and the user's words outside, then ONE `wrap_untrusted` fence holding `path, lines, side` and the quote, MASKED (`detections.redact.mask`) and scanned (`scan_context(strict=True)`; a part that cannot be scanned is left out and said, never sent unscanned). Never write a file name or a quoted line outside the fence, and never build a fence by hand.
  - **A FILE controls what is in a quote and in a file name, so both are made plain before anything else** (the independent review measured it): a line holding lone carriage returns, U+2028 or U+0085, with a zero-width space inside a look-alike of the closing marker, reads to a model that treats those as line breaks as a CLOSED fence followed by a "Comment 2:" of the file's own. `_plain` turns every line-break look-alike into a space and drops control and format characters (categories Cc and Cf); `_rel` refuses a file name holding any of them (`_odd`); and after the scan EVERY line inside the fence gets the bar `| ` (`QUOTE_PREFIX`), so no line of file content can start with a marker or with "Comment". The quote is masked BEFORE it is cut (a key that straddled the 4,000 cut kept most of itself) and cut at a whole line, with `CUT_LINE` said inside the fence. A refusal names the file (`_named`: plain, masked, short).
  - **Nothing may be added to a command.** `looks_like_command` (`/word`, not a path): Codex and OpenCode match `/compact` by its exact text, so a comment block after it made it plain words and spent the chips. `_start_turn_in` refuses any such text with comments (`NOT_WITH_COMMAND`), and the screen leaves the chips. "Try on several models" refuses comments in words (`build_attempts.WORDS_ONLY`): its check listed files, skill and command, not comments, and the attempts started without them.
  - **Pi's rewind match** (`branching._pi_entry`): the record is cut at 400 characters with an ellipsis; the match is now "cut, and Pi's text starts with the kept head". The old test ("400 characters AFTER white space is folded") was false for any cut text with a line break, and one such message made every message of the conversation unfindable.
  - **Comments alone are a message**: `sessions.history_of` gives a later turn `[sent N comments on changed code: names]` (the vanishing-question bug once fixed for pictures), and a first message of comments alone titles the session from the first comment's words.
  - **Build › Code**: `AppTurn.comments` / `AppQueue.comments`; `HostedService._comments_of` checks them in `start_turn` and `queue_add`; the block is added in `_drive` at the ONE place every tool's message passes (just before `live.driver.send_user`), so Claude Code, Codex, Pi and the ACP tools get the same text. `_note_sent` is given the text AS SENT: Pi's rewind finds a message by matching the recorded text against Pi's own list. The `user` event keeps `text` as typed and `comments` beside it (`for_event`: masked). A command of the tool's own takes none (`NOT_WITH_COMMAND`), as it takes no files. A waiting message keeps them (`queued.SessionQueue.add`, and `read`'s whitelist); a rewind returns them.
  - **Chat › Code**: `BuildTurnBody.comments`; `start_build_turn` checks them right after the "@" files and adds the block to `model_text` for THAT message only. The saved question keeps `content` as typed and `comments` beside it (`LINE_KEYS`), so later turns do not replay the block. `chat_thread_queue` keeps a waiting message's comments in their own shape, masked; `MAX_THREAD_QUEUE_CHARS` is 1,000,000 and the size is measured with `ensure_ascii=False` (escaped, one waiting message of Chinese or Cyrillic comments was 720,000 "characters"), so three full messages fit in any language (the page ignores a refused save, and they were then lost on a reload). The blocked-instruction event carries `S.session_id_for(thread_id)`, as every other seam in that file. More than twenty is refused in words by `clean`, not by the request model (a bare 422 says nothing).
  - **The screen offers comments only when the session view says `takes_comments`** (both views), so an older daemon never silently drops one. One store per session in `localStorage` (`lib/codeComments.ts`, shared by the Changes pane and the composer through `useSyncExternalStore`): the Changes rows get no new prop per streamed word (the draw-count pins hold: zero). ONE dialog owned by `ChangesReview` (the Wide view draws each file up to three times). `DiffView` takes the line press as an optional prop and draws exactly as before for its other user.
  - **Every way a message leaves the box either takes the comments or leaves them as chips, never loses them**: a refused send, a refused queue, a take-back and a rewind put them back; a note leaves them; a handed send (first message, Retry, the panes' own sends) does not take the waiting ones.
  - **Pins:** `tests/test_code_comments_v1544.py` (reads what the tool's PROCESS was sent), `tests/test_code_comments_chat_code_v1544.py` (reads what the MODEL was sent), `dashboard/__tests__/code-comments-v1544-0.test.tsx`. Also `tests/test_code_comments_try_on_v1544.py`. Mutation-checked: 54 daemon-side, all red, and every screen-side guard (the builder's count is in the commit messages).
  - **Live, on a scratch daemon**: real Codex and real Claude Code each repeated the quoted line back when the comment asked for it; in the real window the chip cleared on send and showed under the sent message. Pi was not signed in on that PC and was not run.
  - **KNOWN**: a range of lines needs two steps (press, close the box, Shift+press); a range that spans two blocks quotes only the lines the diff draws; a comment handed back by a rewind has no change mark, so it never says "changed since"; Enter and Space on a line number rely on the browser turning them into a press (not simulated in the tests); a look-alike of the fence marker made of full-width or Cyrillic letters stays in a quote as text (it sits behind the bar, on a line of its own data); Pi was not run live; on Linux a file name with `:` cannot be commented on.

- **A Build › Code checkpoint is a git TREE kept by a hidden ref, and the whole-folder restore removes or writes over NOTHING without a copy first** (v1.543.0; Release A of `docs/BUILD-CODE-T3-PLAN-2026-11-19.md`; the second half of this entry is what an independent review of the first version measured).
  - **The record alone kept nothing.** `panes.record_turn_start` wrote the folder's tree id into `turn_trees.json`, but no ref named the tree, so `git gc` could delete it (measured: with no ref it is gone after `git gc --prune=now`). `panes._record` now points `refs/ironjarvis/checkpoints/<sid>/<kind>-<stamp>` STRAIGHT AT THE TREE (`checkpoints.keep`, `git update-ref <ref> <tree>`): no commit is made, so nothing shows in `git log --all`, `git branch -a` or `git tag`. Never make a commit for a checkpoint and never put the ref under `refs/heads` or `refs/tags`. Measured: a plain `push`, `push --all`, `fetch` and `clone` do not carry the refs; `push --mirror`, `clone --mirror` and `bundle create --all` DO (the Handbook says so; never write "never pushed").
  - **Every row that leaves the record loses its ref**: the per-kind limits (`checkpoints.trim`: 300 `start`, 30 `seen`, 10 `before_rewind`; one shared limit of 60 let idle reads push a message's checkpoint out), a deleted session (`checkpoints.forget_session`, called in `HostedService.delete` BEFORE `_drop_own_folder` and before the record is moved away), and the messages a conversation rewind drops (`checkpoints.forget_turns`). Each row also names its repository's own git directory (`repo`, from `rev-parse --git-common-dir`): a session whose folder is already gone (a removed worktree, a deleted subfolder) still loses its refs through it. A new way for a row to leave must drop its ref too (`checkpoints.drop_rows`).
  - **A tree git no longer holds is said, never read as "the file was not there".** `cat-file -e <tree>:./<rel>` is false for a missing tree as well as a missing file: `revert_file` then refused a tracked file as "staged" and MOVED an untracked one to the trash. `panes._tree_there` is checked first (`REVERT_NO_CHECKPOINT`, `TURN_CHECKPOINT_GONE`, `checkpoints.CHECKPOINT_GONE`). The view offers a file rewind only for rows that carry `ref` and `uid` (`branching.view_block` → `files_at`).
  - **git does not keep the no-copy rule, so the code around it must.** Measured on git 2.50: `git restore --worktree` DELETES a whole folder that stands where a file must go (with the ignored files, the cloned repository or the junction inside it: a junction's OUTSIDE target was emptied), deletes a file that stands where a folder must go, and writes over a file it ignores. `checkpoints._apply` therefore runs five steps, and a change there keeps all five: (1) `_plan` puts such a path in `problems` (`WHY_FOLDER`, `WHY_PARENT`) and nothing changes, and LEAVES a file git ignores now (`WHY_IGNORED`: status `D` with the file on disk) and a case-only rename (`WHY_CASE`); (2) the files made since MOVE to `<trash>/made-since/`; (3) `_still_clear` looks at the folder again as it now is; (4) every file about to be written over is COPIED to `<trash>/replaced/` (no copy, no restore); (5) only then git writes.
  - **git writes through a PRIVATE index** (`_restore_paths`: `GIT_INDEX_FILE` under `<home>/workspaces/code-index/restore/`, `RESTORE_S` = 300 s). With the user's own index, git holds `.git/index.lock` for the whole write and a git stopped half way leaves it there (measured: their next `git add` fails). Never `git checkout`, `reset` or `stash` here.
  - **When `.gitignore` files are among what changes they go back FIRST**, and the "made since" files are then asked of `git check-ignore --no-index` (`_ignored_now`): one the checkpoint's own rules ignore stays where it is (it is in no checkpoint; a `.env` filled in after the tool un-ignored it was moved to the trash by the undo).
