  - **A failure comes back from the copies, and "whole" is read from the folder** (`_roll_back`: exact bytes, no git; `_is_as_it_was`: the folder's tree against the plan's `now`). `FAILED_ROLLED_BACK` (409) only when that read agrees; otherwise `FAILED_PARTIAL` (500) names the trash folder.
  - **Undo** (`checkpoints.undo`) goes through the same five steps, so what IT replaces is kept; `_exact_endings` then gives a file the rewind's own copy of it when the two differ only in line endings (so Undo is byte for byte under `core.autocrlf`). It is closed by the SEND path (`checkpoints.close_undo`, called in `HostedService._drive` before the message's checkpoint is recorded), never inferred from the message's own row: that row is missing when the folder could not be read in 3 s, and a later conversation rewind deletes it (Undo came back and wrote over a message's work). A rolled-back attempt is marked `void` and skipped, so it does not hide an earlier rewind's Undo.
  - **Line endings: restyle ONLY where the checkpoint cannot say.** A snapshot is the normalised tree the Changes pane compares; under `core.autocrlf` or a `text` attribute the original endings are not in it and git writes its own (measured live on the owner's PC, `core.autocrlf=input`: a CRLF `run.bat` came back LF). After git's write `_keep_endings` rewrites a file that is uniformly one kind where its replaced version (`<trash>/replaced/`) was uniformly the other, and ONLY when all three hold: git's conversion is on for it (`core.autocrlf` true/input, or `text` set/auto); no `eol=` names the kind and the file is not `-text`/`binary`; and the checkpoint's blob IS the LF form of the file (`_blob_id(_flat(now)) == blob`), so `git status` stays clean. The first cut ran on every restore: under `core.autocrlf=false` it re-applied endings a tool had flipped and never converged, and it changed a byte in a binary file with no NUL in it. In an undo the rewind's own exact copy is settled FIRST and is never restyled. `_endings` puts the note in the preview whenever a file goes back; a test that pins bytes sets `core.autocrlf` in its own scratch repository. Exact bytes in every case would need a second, raw snapshot per message: an owner decision, not built.
  - **"Made since" is not "absent from the tree".** A file MADE before the checkpoint (`_born`: `st_birthtime`, Windows' creation time) that the tree does not hold was ignored then: it goes to `left` (`WHY_OLDER`), never the trash. This is what covers a rule that lived outside the folder's own `.gitignore` files (the repository's root, `.git/info/exclude`, a global file). NOT used in an undo: a name deleted and made again within seconds keeps its old creation time on Windows, so the files a rewind made again look old. Files with `assume-unchanged` or `skip-worktree` are in no checkpoint either (`git add` skips them): `_unwatched` lists them in `left`.
  - **`.gitattributes` goes back in the first phase with `.gitignore`** (`_is_rules`): files that sort before it were written under the tool's rules. In that phase a "made since" file that stands where a rules file's folder must go moves out too, and an ignored-again file inside a folder that must become a file moves to the trash instead of staying. `_drop_empty_folders` removes a folder that holds no file at all from a file's place. Each of these was a state where the preview said yes and the press refused.
  - **The user's hooks stay out** (`-c core.hooksPath=<empty folder>` on the restore's one git call: `post-checkout` ran inside it and saw the private index as the repository's state). **`_copy_replaced` raises on a file it cannot stat** (it used to skip it and git wrote over it).
  - **A rewind that changed nothing is `void`** (no Undo), and **the send path closes Undo for every session of the folder** (`close_undo_in_folder`: this one's, and any whose folder is the same, inside it or around it).
  - **The per-file Revert had the same git behaviour since v1.420.0**: `revert_file` now calls `checkpoints._still_clear` before each `git restore` (`REVERT_IN_THE_WAY`), and its route takes the folder's lock too.
  - **The folder's lock is per REPOSITORY** (`checkpoints.lock_key`: the common git directory), and the who-may-write check runs AGAIN once it is held (`branching._still_free`: the wait can be minutes). A lock that could not be had answers `FOLDER_BUSY`, not the branch words.
  - **KNOWN LIMITS, said in the Handbook**: a message whose folder cannot be read in `TURN_START_WAIT_S` (3 s) has no checkpoint; another session's SEND does not take the folder's lock, so its tool can start writing during a restore (the copies in the trash are what protects it); Undo has no preview of its own; a nested repository with no commit yet stops the snapshot (`git add` refuses it).
  - **Order in `branching.rewind(files=True)`**: the cheap checks, `_keep_log_first`, then `files_step()`, then the tool's own lasting request. A files refusal therefore leaves the conversation alone; a tool refusal after it runs `checkpoints.undo` and says `FILES_BACK_AGAIN`. `files` in that function is also the message's attachment list: the flag is read into `with_files` first (the first version shadowed it and silently restored nothing).
  - **The preview's id is REQUIRED** for every press that writes the folder (`branching._need_expect` → 400 `READ_FIRST`): it is what proves the user saw this list. Tests post it too.
  - **Two locks, passed in by the ROUTE** (`build_app_branch._lock` → `lock(fn, folder)`): the session's `app:<sid>` (the Changes pane's Revert takes it too) and `code-folder:<folder>`, so two sessions in one folder never write it at once. **Who may write the folder**: `branching._files_free` refuses while a reply runs, the tool works alone or with helpers, a hand-off helper runs, the session is archived, the conversation is open in a terminal, or ANOTHER Code-view session has a reply running in the same folder (or one inside it, or one that holds it). KNOWN LIMIT: a Chat › Code session, a terminal or an agent working in that folder is not seen; the copies in the trash are what protects their work.
  - **Routes**: `GET …/rewind/preview?to=`, `POST …/rewind {to, files, expect}` (without `files` byte-identical to v1.441.0), `POST …/rewind/files {to, expect}` (every tool), `POST …/rewind/undo`. The dashboard calls each by a literal path (the route-caller check reads paths: a path built from a variable is not seen).
  - **Pins:** `tests/test_build_code_checkpoints_v1543.py` and `tests/test_build_code_checkpoints_safety_v1543.py` (real git every time; the second file is one test per review finding), `tests/test_build_app_rewind_files_v1543.py` (routes, the recorded fakes), `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-rewind-files-v1543-0.test.tsx`. Mutation-checked: every guard above was broken once and a test went red (the link test needs a symbolic-link privilege and is skipped without one).
  - **Not built (the plan's later releases)**: checkpoints for a folder that is not a git repository, comments on a changed line, switching the tool inside one session.

- **A tool's saved conversation written to in the last ten minutes is never continued here unasked: it may still be running in the tool's own app** (v1.530.0; found when the owner asked for Claude Code and Codex to be watched from Chat › Code).
  - `HostedService._holder` sees only Iron Jarvis's own rows and terminals; a conversation still open in Claude desktop, a terminal Iron Jarvis does not know or the Codex app was opened anyway, and two programs then wrote one conversation. `hosted/service.live_elsewhere_quiet(found, wall_clock())`: the saved file's own `mtime` (from `disk_sessions`) under `LIVE_ELSEWHERE_S` (600 s: a tool runs a long command without writing) refuses `POST /build/app/sessions {harness_session_id}` with 409 `W.LIVE_ELSEWHERE` ("{label} wrote to this conversation {ago} ago …"), unless the body says `elsewhere_closed: true` (`AppSessionBody.elsewhere_closed: StrictBool`: only a real JSON true, the owner's second press; "yes" is 422). It holds for every caller: Build › Code's own "saved by …" rows and Chat › Code's Other tools.
  - `wall_clock` is module-level wall time (`self.clock` is monotonic and cannot be compared with a file time). `tests/conftest.py` runs it an hour ahead for the whole session, because a test writes its saved conversation a moment before opening it; `tests/test_running_elsewhere_v1530.py` sets it back to the real time to pin the rule (30 s, 9 min refused; 11 min and the word allowed).
  - **Chat › Code's list shows the coding tools running elsewhere**: `HarnessSidebar` mounts `components/chat/OtherToolChats` with `mode="code"` (Claude Code and Codex only) above Terminals. A session written to within `outside.WORKING_S` (90 s) is "working", with its newest step (`outside._steps` over the end of its file, through the history reader's `map_events`, masked by `hosted.events.clip`; a connector tool reads "browser batch (Claude Browser)", `outside.tool_words`); while one works the list is read every 15 s, never while none does. A press opens the watch view (`GET /work-record/other-tools/{tool}/{id}/steps`, the session found among the LISTED ones by id, read again every 4 s while it works). "Continue here" is plain only after `LIVE_ELSEWHERE_S` of quiet; before that only "It's closed in …, continue here", which sends `elsewhere_closed`.
  - **Pins:** `tests/test_running_elsewhere_v1530.py`, `dashboard/__tests__/other-tool-running-v1530.test.tsx`.

- **An injected value must be injected, and a field must have a way IN**
  (v1.217.0). Two halves of the same lesson, both found by DRIVING the product
  rather than reading it. (a) The pane identity (`IRONJARVIS_BUILD`,
  `_PANE_ID`, `_PANE_CWD`, `_PANE_NAME`, `_PANE_CLI`) was set on the session
  AFTER the shell was spawned, with a comment claiming it applied to "anything
  the pane starts next" — nothing applied it, `pane_env()` had no caller in the
  codebase, and every test passed because every test asked the DICT what it
  held. The id is now minted before the spawn and the variables are merged into
  the child environment (`_with_pane_env` — the backends REPLACE the
  environment when handed one, so the merge must start from the base they would
  have used or the shell loses its PATH), and
  `tests/test_pane_identity_v1217.py` asserts the env the BACKEND RECEIVED.
  (b) `name` was settable only at creation and the New-terminal button never
  asks; `agent_cli` was known only to the browser, because `launchCli` types
  into an already-running shell. Both were reachable from `curl` and from
  nowhere else, which is the Raster Studio lesson restated: a green suite
  proves the library works, not that a user can reach it. `PATCH
  /terminals/{id}` is PARTIAL (omitted = keep, `""` = clear) for the same
  reason the remote-agent update is — a re-post silently destroys the field it
  does not carry.

- **One PTY, many attaches: only the PRIMARY attach resizes it** (v1.232.0,
  audit Wave 6). Every TerminalPane sent `resize` on open and on every
  ResizeObserver tick, and the daemon keeps last-writer semantics, so a
  phone attaching over Tailscale reflowed the desktop's running session into
  40 columns and the desktop's next tick reflowed it back. `sendResize` now
  consults `components/terminal/resizeGate.resizeAllowed` — the pane is
  visible AND `document.hasFocus()` — and a window gaining focus claims the
  size. Keep the gate on the CLIENT: the daemon cannot tell a phone from the
  desktop, and "the window the user is looking at" is a fact only the
  window knows. Same wave: `GET /sessions/{id}/review` answers `200
  {"review": null}` for the normal no-review state (it 404'd on every
  detail visit), and the session page reads that shape.

- **A Build terminal OUTLIVES the Build page** (v1.243.0). The user's report:
  leave Build, come back, and the panes "disconnect or show a strange string
  of characters" and take a while to return. Every TerminalPane owned its
  xterm AND its socket inside a mount effect, so a module switch (or a Rail ⇄
  Canvas flip, which swaps the wrapper element) disposed both, and the return
  rebuilt them from a full replay plus the repaint wiggle. The replay
  re-parsed old QUERIES (a live pane's scrollback held Codex's OSC 10/11
  colour queries), and the 800 ms `TERM_REPORT_RE` knew only CSI `c/n/R/t`, so
  xterm's colour answers were typed into the shell. `components/terminal/
  paneHost.ts` now holds each pane's terminal + socket in a module registry:
  an unmount PARKS (the wrapper moves to an off-screen, inert lot — never
  `display:none`; xterm's renderer pauses itself off-screen), a mount ADOPTS
  (`acquirePaneHost` → `adopt` → `waitForStableSize` → `settle` → `start`).
  Only `closeTerminal` → `disposePaneHost` and the page's load-time
  `retainPaneHosts` end a host — never put `term.dispose()` or `new WebSocket`
  back in the pane. A REAL re-attach (reload, restart, lost link) is
  DELIMITED: the daemon ends the replay with one EMPTY binary frame, and the
  host drops every `isTerminalReport` answer until xterm's `write("", cb)`
  says the parser has passed it. Daemon half: `TerminalSession.read()` fans
  each chunk out to every `subscribe()`d attach (a read used to hand a chunk
  to ONE caller, so two attaches — or the drain thread at the instant of
  attach — tore escape sequences), `subscribe` snapshots the replay under the
  same lock, and the last `unsubscribe` starts the background drain, because
  a PTY nobody reads fills its pipe and the Claude in it BLOCKS until someone
  looks. `tests/test_terminal_attach_v1243.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/terminal-host-v1243.test.ts`.

- **Build daily-driver pass, B1** (v1.245.0; the 2026-09-11 reliability audit
  measured each of these). (1) ConPTY sends a DA1 query at shell start and
  holds ALL output until answered (first byte 3.05 s vs 0.06 s): only an
  attached xterm answered, so `TerminalSession.read` now answers `ESC[?1;2c`
  itself when no pane is subscribed. (2) The WS input handler closed with
  4000 ("shell exited" — the one close a pane never reconnects from) on ANY
  exception, so a malformed resize killed a live pane for good: 4000 only
  when `not session.alive`. (3) Same-size resizes reflowed ConPTY and
  repainted the TUI: the route skips them after the attach's first (wiggle)
  resize, and the pane debounces its ResizeObserver (`RESIZE_SETTLE_MS`) and
  skips an unchanged `host.sentSize` unless forced (open, window focus).
  (4) The snapshot was written only on graceful paths: a lifespan loop calls
  `snapshot_if_changed` every 30 s (`output_seq` + identity key). (5) A
  restored pane kept `agent_cli` and its chip claimed a CLI that died with the
  old daemon: restore sets `resume_cli` instead and the pane's `ResumeStrip`
  types `RESUME_COMMANDS[cli]` (`claude --continue`, `codex resume --last`;
  others get no button). (6) `activity()` is cached per output change, the
  2.5 s poll left the access log, per-message deflate is off, a loop-lag
  heartbeat logs stalls > 250 ms, and Ctrl+C with a selection copies.
  `tests/test_build_stability_v1245.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/build-stability-v1245.test.tsx`.

- **The Build terminal path is BYTES, PUSHED and paced; only the pane on
  screen draws** (v1.248.0, B2). DAEMON: pywinpty handed output over as TEXT
  (a character split across two reads became U+FFFD) and could only be
  polled, so every attached pane's pump ran read/take/sleep(10 ms) forever —
  8 idle panes measured a whole CPU core (99.7% → 0.08% after). `ConPtyBackend`
  (ctypes) delivers raw bytes from a reader thread through
  `TerminalSession._ingest` → `_record_locked`, the ONE output path shared
  with `read()`; the handler is installed BEFORE `start`; a pushing session
  starts no drain; the route's pump sleeps until the subscription's waker
  fires (5 s safety net). `OutputSubscription._push` and `take` share the
  session's read lock — that is what makes a reader thread safe; never give
  a subscription its own lock. The pseudoconsole calls come from pywinpty's
  bundled `conpty.dll` + `OpenConsole.exe` when both exist (echo 0.5 ms vs
  14.8 ms on the inbox conhost), else kernel32; `IRONJARVIS_PTY_BACKEND=
  pywinpty|pipe` and `IRONJARVIS_CONPTY_HOST=inbox` force the others, and a
  pseudoconsole that cannot be made marks ConPTY broken for the process and
  the manager retries on the next backend. FLOW CONTROL: the pane counts bytes
  written to xterm and not yet parsed and sends `{"type":"flow","paused":
  <bool>}` at 512/128 KiB; the daemon consumes ONLY that exact shape and holds
  the SEND, never the READ (the 8 MB bound → 1013 still applies). An OLDER
  daemon TYPES every non-resize text frame into the shell, so the pane sends
  flow frames only when `/health` reports ≥ `FLOW_MIN_DAEMON` (1.248.0) — any
  new client→daemon text frame needs the same gate. DASHBOARD: `paneHost`
  loads `@xterm/addon-webgl` only while a terminal is on screen and releases
  it on park/release (browsers cap live WebGL contexts at ~16); a lost
  context falls back to the DOM renderer on the same buffer; `ij.build.webgl`
  = "off" disables it. A rail pane behind the focused one is `parked`: its
  terminal moves to the lot but its view and socket stay (badges live, no
  replay); it parks only after fitting and starting at its true size and
  never fits or resizes while parked. `tests/test_terminal_datapath_v1248.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/terminal-render-v1248.test.ts`. SAME RELEASE, found by
  its own Handbook bullet: the Guide's `_chunk` cut an oversize paragraph (a
  bullet list has no blank lines) at FIXED 1600-char offsets, so any edit above
  a bullet moved every later cut — "Restart to update" fell off the chunk that
  answers "how do updates install". `_line_pieces` now cuts at line
  boundaries, before the last list item that began in the piece;
  `tests/test_guide_chunk_v1248.py`. And the browser check found one U+FFFD
  in 4.3 MB: `output_tail` decoded a window cut at a BYTE offset, so its
  first (and, with bytes recorded as they arrive, last) character could be
  half a code point. `_utf8_window` trims only those edges — invalid bytes
  inside still show; `tests/test_terminal_tail_utf8_v1248.py`.

- **A Build pane outside any project can make one in one press** (v1.281.0,
  /goal surfaces wave 2). `PaneChat` shows `#pane-chat-make-project` beside
  the folder name when `projectForCwd` finds nothing; `makeProject` POSTs
  `/projects {name: <folder>, root: cwd}` (the chat page's own promotion)
  and sets the chip, so the next turn carries `project_id` and the assist
  route's `project_for_path` finds it by path. The daily driver's two Build
  panes sit in a folder no project covers — this is the door. Pins:
  `dashboard/__tests__/pane-chat-project-v1281.test.tsx`.

- **The Build assist carries the pane, the profile, the project and the
  lessons; dead pane keys are pruned; the job card remembers its target**
  (v1.280.0, /goal surfaces wave). `POST /terminals/{id}/ai` was the one
  model call with no user context (two sentences + skills + other panes'
  output). Now, in the chat lanes' order: `_pane_identity_block(session)`
  (name, folder, CLI), `_profile_section(platform)`, `project_context_block(
  project_for_path(engine, session.cwd))` and `learning.apply_to_prompt` —
  each "" when there is nothing. `projects/locate.py` is the daemon-side
  twin of the dashboard's `projectForCwd` (most specific ACTIVE root at a
  segment boundary, case-folded; never raises). `components/terminal/
  paneKeys.ts::prunePaneStorage(liveIds)` removes `ij.pane.view.<id>`,
  `ij.pane.thread.<id>` and `ij_term_layout` entries for panes the daemon no
  longer offers — called ONLY after a successful `/terminals` answer (a
  booting daemon answers 503, never an empty list). `JobPostCard` opens on
  `ij_agents_last_target` while the roster still lists it (`effectiveTarget`
  falls back to the Team visibly otherwise). Pins:
  `tests/test_build_context_v1280.py`, `pane-keys-v1280.test.ts`,
  `job-target-v1280.test.tsx`.

- **Continue on the next account is DETECTED from Claude Code's own limit
  record, CHOSEN by session id, and COPIED, never moved** (v1.303.0). Evidence
  on this PC (claude 2.1.288): real account limits read "You've hit your
  session limit · resets 3:45pm (America/New_York)", "You've hit your weekly
  limit · …", "You're out of usage credits …" (the binary's classifier prefixes
  "You've hit your", "You've reached your", "You're out of usage credits",
  "Your org is out of usage credits"), and each is also written into the
  session jsonl as `isApiErrorMessage: true, error: "rate_limit"` with
  `sessionId`, `cwd`, `timestamp`, `quotaLimits`. "Context limit reached" is
  the CONTEXT WINDOW, not an account — never a limit (it parked a healthy
  account in the first cut). `terminals/limit_state.py` is an ALLOW-LIST of
  account-limit shapes at line start inside an allow-listed frame; `>`/`❯`
  prompt lines and `⏺`/`●` answer lines are never the hit (they are the
  user's / the model's text); a later answer or working line clears it.
  Iron-Proxy gets ONLY `PaneLimit.signal_text()` (the matched message + a
  normalised reset suffix), never other scrollback. WHICH conversation
  (`terminals/continue_on.py`): every Claude WE start is typed `claude
  --session-id <uuid4>` and the id is recorded on the pane (snapshot; restart
  resumes `claude --resume <id>`); else only a session whose LAST record is a
  rate_limit error with the pane's cwd and a fresh timestamp, exactly one —
  several → fresh + note; NEVER newest-by-mtime (two panes on one account hit
  the same limit at the same moment). Located by glob
  `<home>/projects/*/<id>.jsonl` and copied into the SAME-NAMED folder of the
  next account's home (Claude truncates/hashes long encoded names — never
  recompute them), never overwriting a DIVERGED file, links skipped, confined
  to the home. `--resume` KEEPS the session id (`--fork-session` would change
  it), so a round trip A→B→A meets A's OLDER copy of the same id: a byte-PREFIX
  destination is the same append-only conversation and is replaced (old bytes
  copied to `<home>/trash/<stamp>/claude-carry/…` first, then temp +
  `os.replace`); only a true divergence refuses. A recorded id counts only
  while it is the NEWEST conversation in its folder (the user may /clear into
  a new id): restart Resume falls back to `claude --continue`, continue to the
  unique-limit-record scan. `tzdata` is declared for win32 (zoneinfo named
  zones; pyinstaller-hooks-contrib's hook-zoneinfo/hook-tzdata ship it).
  Every refusal (this PC's login, pane cap 429, Iron-Proxy off/unavailable, no
  other usable account) runs BEFORE any signal or copy. The strip says before
  the press that the conversation is COPIED to the other account (work →
  personal privacy) and Dismiss keys on the limit's `since`, not its text (a
  countdown repaints). Pins: `tests/test_build_continue_v1303.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/continue-next-v1303.test.tsx`.

- **A Build pane and a `claude` child are TOP-LEVEL sessions: a parent Claude
  Code session's markers never cross** (v1.303.1, found by the LIVE end-to-end
  run). A daemon started from inside a Claude Code session (a dev shell, an
  agent) handed `CLAUDECODE`, `CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION`,
  `CLAUDE_CODE_MESSAGING_TOKEN`/`_SOCKET`, `CLAUDE_CODE_SESSION_ID`,
  `CLAUDE_PID`, … to every pane and child; Claude Code in the pane printed
  "Transcript saving is off — inherited CLAUDE_CODE_CHILD_SESSION marker"
  (no conversation file → Continue had nothing to carry) and the parent's
  messaging TOKEN reached shells it was never meant for.
  `core/claude_markers.drop_claude_session_markers` runs in
  `terminals.manager._with_pane_env` and `claude_models.child_env`
  (case-insensitive). The user's own Claude settings (CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR, …)
  are not markers. LIVE-PROVEN the same day (real ConPTY, real claude 2.1.289,
  Claude Max): a pane on the ADOPTED ~/.claude starts `claude --session-id`,
  answers, saves its transcript, has no CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR and keeps other keys;
  the shipped `carry_over` copied that real jsonl byte-identical into a second
  account's home and `claude --resume <id>` there got past lookup to "Not
  logged in" — the only unproven step is a SECOND SIGNED-IN account's API
  accepting it (Anthropic documents thinking signatures as portable across
  platforms). Pin: `tests/test_claude_session_markers_v1303.py`.

- **A carried conversation the next account refuses ends in a HANDOFF, never a
  dead end** (v1.303.2). Whether a second signed-in account accepts the first
  one's transcript cannot be proven without one, so the product does not
  depend on it. The pane opened by Continue records `continued_from`
  (`from_title`, `session_id`, `carried_path` = the copy in the NEW home, `at`).
  ONLY THE FILE raises a problem (`terminals/resume_failed.py`): a new exact
  `isApiErrorMessage` record after `at` in that copy (bounded tail read, only on
  size/mtime change, in the sync activity route) — the SCREEN only clears,
  because tool output under `⎿` ("⎿ API Error: 400 from upstream", "token has
  expired") read as Claude's own error and would /exit a working Claude. A
  failure needs a 4xx other than 429 (limit flow) / 401 (sign-in), or
  invalid_request / "does not have access" / "prompt is too long" / signature
  words — timeouts, "fetch failed", a 408 and billing/server/max-output errors
  (even with a 400) are not; a login expiry ("Login expired ·
  Please run /login", by a 401 OR sign-in words, never the 403
  `authentication_failed` code) is `sign_in_needed` with the account id. `POST /terminals/{id}/start-
  fresh-with-handoff` (`terminals/handoff.py`): refused while the pane is
  working/blocked; a deterministic handoff (first ask ≤ 1,500, last 6 text turns
  ≤ 800, tool_result payloads excluded, ≤ 30 paths, ≤ 8,000 total, secrets
  MASKED with `detections.redact.mask`) at `<home>/handoffs/<sid>/handoff.md`
  (one folder per conversation; others older than 30 days pruned; listed in the
  storage report; included, masked, in backups; never the project); then Ctrl+C (clears Claude Code's
  composer, multi-line drafts included — measured live on 2.1.289; Esc Esc can
  open the rewind menu, Ctrl+U clears one line), `/exit`, Enter, wait for the
  pane's OWN shell prompt (15 s; else a 409 "could not see the shell prompt …
  if Claude has exited, run: <line>"), then ONE line `claude --session-id <new>
  "--add-dir=<that conversation's folder>" "Read the handoff…"`. `--add-dir
  <directories...>` is VARIADIC on 2.1.289 — the two-token form swallows the
  prompt (measured). LIVE-PROVEN on the real signed-in login: the fresh session
  answered a fact only the handoff carried. Also fixed by the live run:
  `session._ANSI_RE` now strips CSI with `<=>!` params and `ESC 7/8/=/>` (Claude
  Code's exit left `>4m<u` before the prompt), and ConPTY glues the prompt onto
  the row it overwrites, so pwsh/cmd prompts are matched at the line END.
  Shared quoting: `routes/iron_proxy.shell_line(shell, argv)`. Pins:
  `tests/test_build_handoff_v13032.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/start-fresh-v1303-2.test.tsx`.

- **The Session tab's CONTEXT drop-down: what fills the conversation, part
  by part, in BOTH coding-session views; a part nobody reported is never
  shown** (v1.415.0; the user, beside Claude Code's own panel). MEASURED on
  this PC, no model call: Claude Code 2.1.316 answers the stream-json control
  request `get_context_usage` (signed out too) with `categories` [{id, name,
  tokens, kind used|buffer|free|deferred}], totalTokens / maxTokens /
  percentage, memoryFiles, mcpTools {name, serverName, tokens, isLoaded},
  skills, autoCompactThreshold, isAutoCompactEnabled, messageBreakdown,
  apiUsage (recorded: `tests/fixtures/claude_context_usage_v14150.json`); Pi
  1.3.0 `get_session_stats` → `contextUsage {tokens, contextWindow, percent}`
  (a total only; answered mid-run; `compact` exists but ABORTS a running
  turn); Codex 0.157.0 `thread/tokenUsage/updated` {last, total,
  modelContextWindow} (a total) and `thread/compact/start {threadId}` (not
  wired: never measured live); OpenCode 1.18.18 ACP `usage_update` {used,
  size} (a total; no compact command). ONE shape, `context/breakdown.py`:
  {used, window, percent, source app|tool, parts [{key, label, tokens, kind}],
  extra (sent but NOT in the percent), lists, auto_compact {enabled, at,
  left, percent}}; `clean()` is what may be stored. CHAT › CODE:
  `chat_turn.harness_context_breakdown` (both lanes, lock-step, harness turns
  ONLY) puts `breakdown` on the context report — counted from the very
  strings appended (`_ctx_pieces`: profile, attachments, page, resources,
  refs, the "/" skill, workspace; plus the grounding's and the harness
  block's own parts; a string not in the prompt is not counted), "System
  prompt" = the rest, messages by `_apply_compaction`'s own rule (content +
  4), so the used parts add up EXACTLY to the report's `tokens` and the
  percent is unchanged; tool specs are `extra` (the compaction figure never
  counted them — do NOT fold them in, the Compact offer keys off that
  percent); `routes/build.context_receipt` keeps it on the reply line. Every
  other report key is unchanged and a non-harness turn gets no `breakdown`.
  "Summarize earlier messages at" is the REAL setting
  `context_compaction.auto_at` (read via GET, saved via PUT /settings/values
  with the rest of the dict kept; choices all above the 70 % offer mark).
  BUILD › CODE: `GET /build/app/sessions/{id}/context` →
  `HostedService.context_view`: asks `Driver.context_breakdown()` only while
  the process runs (never started for it) and — unless the driver says
  `context_while_working` (Pi) — only while no reply runs (`live.busy`;
  answers `waiting`); a reading is kept in memory with its time and shown
  only while no newer seen total contradicts it; else the total the tool
  said after its last reply (`panes.seen_context`). Compact: Claude Code's
  own `/compact` sent as a MESSAGE through the view's own send (idle only);
  Codex/Pi/OpenCode answer `compact.why`. THE 1 % FINDING:
  `seen.claude_context` read `usage.iterations[-1]`; Claude Code's own reader
  (`Ste`, read from the binary) takes the last iteration that is NOT an
  `advisor_message` / `compaction` and IS a `message` / `fallback_message` —
  a side call's small prompt read as the conversation said a long session
  was ~1 % full (pinned with an advisor iteration: (9400, 1M) on the old
  code). Not proven to be the user's exact case (their session's events are
  in the live home, not read). Dashboard: `components/harness/
  ContextBreakdown.tsx` (one component, `prefix` session-rail|code-session;
  a disclosure remembered per window in sessionStorage `ij.session.context.
  open`; stacked bar of the used parts with each label in its accessible
  name and in a row with tokens + percent; `AutoCompactChoice`),
  `lib/contextBreakdown.ts`. Pins: `tests/test_context_breakdown_v14150.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/context-breakdown-v1415-0.test.tsx` (+ fixture
  `context-breakdown-v1415-0.json`, `IJ_REGEN_CONTEXT_BREAKDOWN=1`; the
  Python test compares the SHAPE — numbers move with every prompt wording and
  the date). REVIEW (each red first, `tests/test_context_breakdown_review_
  v14150.py`, `dashboard/__tests__/context-breakdown-review-v1415-0.test.tsx`):
  (1) `get_context_usage` defaults to `detail: "full"`, which (claude
  2.1.316's own schema) "counts each category with the token-count API" —
  the conversation sent to Anthropic per category on every read, through a
  queue Claude Code serialises and waits up to 300 s for at teardown; the
  driver asks `detail: "summary"` (same shape, measured 0.5 s, no network).
  (2) `harness_breakdown` counts a string ONCE (a brain base that cannot be
  used renders `block` == `without_tools()`, both handed in: memory doubled).
  (3) Pi's `tokens: null` right after it compacts is
  `breakdown.NOT_KNOWN_NOW`: the service drops the kept reading and the seen
  total (it showed the pre-compaction 90 % as current). (4) Words: the
  "Sent with each message" heading is Chat › Code's only (Claude Code's
  extras are DEFERRED tools, not sent); Chat › Code's press is
  `COMPACTION_OFFER_BUTTON` ("Summarize", the G75/v1.389.0 word) — "Compact
  session" stays Build › Code's, for Claude Code's own `/compact`.
  OPEN: Claude Code is never asked mid-reply (not measured whether
  it can answer then); Codex/Pi compact not wired; the Chat › Code breakdown
  is from the newest reply (a later setting change shows on the next reply).

- **A step is "running" only while its coding tool runs it** (v1.427.2, a
  USER BUG with a screenshot: six Build › Code steps spun for three days).
  After Claude Code's reply ended on Oct 31 it kept working ON ITS OWN
  (helpers' tests, then a reply it started itself; those events carry no
  `turn`), the app closed mid-way, and the log kept six `tool` rows
  `running`: `reconcile_cut_turns` closed only a reply the user started, and
  nothing closed a step when the process ended with no reply open.
  `hosted/open_steps.py` is the rule: `note()` tracks every emitted step in
  `LiveSession.open_steps` (from `_emit`, after the turn stamp; bounded
  `MAX_OPEN` 500), and `HostedService._close_open_steps` emits a `tool` row of
  the new status `stopped` per open step from `_on_exit`, `_stop_process` and
  `shutdown` (before the turns end), plus ONE notice
  (`W.STEPS_CUT_STOPPED` / `W.STEPS_CUT_RESTART` when `_closing`) only for
  steps with no recorded turn — a reply's own steps close silently, its end
  already says why. The boot reconcile calls `close_in_tail(events)` for every
  session the code host did not keep (rows timed to the record's last `at`,
  never the boot's clock, and carrying no `turn`, so `analyse_tail` and a
  carry-on's `cut_at` are unchanged); `adopt.log_facts` returns `open_steps`
  so an adopted tool's later exit closes them too. Dashboard: `status:
  "stopped"` decoded (an unknown status still reads running), drawn "· did
  not finish" (`STEP_STOPPED_WORDS`, `code-tool-stopped`), never a spinner,
  and never counted as the reply's work (receipt time). Dry-run on a copy of
  the real log: exactly the six steps + one notice. Pins:
  `tests/test_build_app_unfinished_steps_v14272.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-unfinished-steps-v1427-2.test.tsx` (11
  mutations, all red); moved on purpose: `test_code_host_v14220` (log_facts
  gained `open_steps`).

- **A Code-view session whose tool still runs BACKGROUND work is never reaped
  or evicted** (v1.418.0, a USER BUG: seven Claude Code builder teams died
  together twice in one day, each ~15 min after the reply that said "I'll
  report as teams finish"). The pool's idle reaper (`IDLE_S`) looked only at
  whether a reply ran. Claude Code says `system` `task_started` (task_id,
  task_type local_agent | local_bash …) and `task_notification` (completed |
  failed | stopped) — measured live on 2.1.316: the reply's `result` comes
  while the task is open, then the notification, then Claude Code WAKES
  ITSELF with a second reply. `ClaudeDriver.background_tasks` counts the open
  ones; `LiveSession.keeps_working(now)` (open tasks AND the tool heard from
  within `BACKGROUND_SILENT_MAX_S`, 2 h — a forgotten dev server must not
  hold a process forever) keeps the reaper and the cap-6 evictor off it; idle
  is measured from the tool's newest frame of ANY kind (`last_heard`, set in
  the service's `on_message`), so a self-started reply counts. Open tasks
  never make the session `busy` (that refuses a message — Claude Code takes
  one while helpers run). The session row carries `background` (count).
  REVIEW: the reaper was not the only way they died. A restart (effort,
  model back to default, tools) waits while tasks are open (`restart` stays
  set, a notice says so) and runs on the first message after; Open in
  terminal is refused in words (`W.BACKGROUND_OPEN_TERMINAL`); the cap-6
  evictor sorts by `max(last_active, last_heard)` like the reaper (Claude
  Code's self-started reply after helpers finish owns no turn). Archive,
  delete, shutdown and the account move still stop the process as before.
  Pins: `tests/test_build_app_background_tasks_v1417.py` (12 mutations, all red).
  SHOWN, not only kept alive (same release): the session row carries
  `background_helpers` (task types in `claude.HELPER_TASK_TYPES` — agents,
  workflows, team members; never a background command) and `self_reply`
  (the tool is writing a reply no message owns: `_alone`, cleared by its
  result, a new message or exit; stops counting after `SELF_REPLY_QUIET_S`
  10 min of silence). `view()` reads the live flags BEFORE the events, so a
  "nothing is happening" answer always carries the whole report. The Code
  view says ONE quiet line (`backgroundWorkWords`; commands are never promised
  a report), re-reads every 4 s (20 s for commands only) through
  `useVisibleInterval`, ONE read at a time, never while a reply the user
  started runs, a read that was out across a card answer is dropped
  (`answerSeqRef`), then one last read when it ends. Running now lists it
  ("helpers working" / "replying on its own") and the busy counts include
  helpers (keep-awake, install-when-idle), bounded by the 2-h silence rule.
  OPEN: frames a helper writes (`parent_tool_use_id` set) are drawn as Claude
  Code's own. Pins: `tests/test_build_app_background_report_v1417.py`,
  `test_build_app_background_review_v1417.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-background{,-review}-v1417.test.tsx`.

- **Build's CODE view drives the user's REAL Claude Code / Codex with their
  OWN tools ON; Iron Jarvis only starts them, draws them and answers their
  permission questions as cards** (v1.409.0; the user: "a TUI … to control
  any harness they desire … like t3code … only effect the build module").
  NOT Chat's Code view: there Iron Jarvis runs every tool and `claude_native`
  / `codex_native` switch the built-ins OFF — never reuse a tool-off flag
  here. Build's header centre is the shared `components/ViewSwitch`
  ("</> Code | Terminal", `ij.build.view`, `?view=code&code=<id>`); Terminal
  is today's page byte-for-byte; a `?focus=` link always opens Terminal on
  the first frame. DAEMON `src/iron_jarvis/hosted/` + `routes/build_app.py`
  (`/build/app/*`): one long-lived process per open session through
  `core/procs.spawn` with `breakaway_ok=False` (Claude's Bash is Git Bash,
  and MSYS breaks away from a Job otherwise) and a tree kill; the env is a
  Build terminal's (`_with_pane_env`: markers dropped, account home applied,
  the user's own keys KEPT); pool cap 6, idle reaper 15 min, stop at
  shutdown; `stop()` never waits on a grandchild holding a pipe. Claude:
  `claude -p` stream-json both ways + `--permission-prompts host` AND
  `--permission-prompt-tool stdio` (the first alone denied every ask —
  measured) + `--session-id`/`--resume`; asks are control_request
  `can_use_tool`; AskUserQuestion is allowed with `updatedInput.answers
  {question: label}`; a `bypassPermissions` suggestion is never offered, and
  "allow for this session" that changes the mode updates the row and says
  so. Codex: `codex app-server`, NOT ephemeral, NONE of BUILTINS_OFF; on
  Windows `read-only`/`workspace-write` cannot start a command (measured), so
  Ask first = `untrusted` + `danger-full-access` (every command and file
  change asks) and Auto edits / Plan are `hidden_modes` with a reason;
  `acceptForSession` only when that request's `availableDecisions` lists it,
  else the card is `once_only`. Full access needs `confirm: true` at create
  AND patch. ONE CONVERSATION = ONE ROW = ONE PROCESS: a create / turn /
  from-terminal on a `harness_session_id` another row or a live Build
  terminal holds is refused in words; open-terminal stops the process and
  marks the row until that terminal's CLI exits (`can_open_in_code` on
  terminal rows). Sessions live in the tools' OWN stores, so `claude
  --resume` / `codex resume` keep working; continue-on (Claude) copies via
  `continue_on.carry_over` and checks the copy BEFORE telling Iron-Proxy.
  Turns are the daemon's (durable; a rejoin replays from `user`); events
  `<home>/hosted/<id>/events.jsonl`, masked AFTER coalescing, trimmed to 80%
  of the bound only when it is exceeded; a crash-cut turn gets its `done`
  at boot. Asks are claimed before the driver call (one card = one answer).
  The pending listing names `surface: "build_app"` (names only); main.js
  and the bell read it. DASHBOARD `components/buildCode/*` +
  `lib/buildApp.ts` (import-free; the bell reads it): the contract is the
  REAL-parser fixture `dashboard/__tests__/fixtures/build-app-events-v1409.json`
  (`IJ_REGEN_BUILD_APP=1`); Enter never allows a once-only card or anything
  in Full access; Full access is two presses with a repeat guard; streamed
  frames draw once a frame with memo'd rows. Pi below; OpenCode (v1.413.0) is its own bullet. Pins:
  `tests/test_build_app_{,routes_,pool_,review_,title_}v1409.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/build-app-{view,switch,bell,path-pick}-v1409.test.tsx`,
  e2e `dashboard/e2e/build-app-view.spec.ts`. Live-proven on this PC
  (scratch daemon, real claude 2.1.311 + codex 0.157.0): an edit card with
  its diff → Allow once → the edit landed; Open in terminal resumed the same
  conversation and Open in Code view came back to the same row; Codex asked
  once-only before a command and ran it after Allow once.
  PI (v1.411.0, `hosted/pi.py`, pi 1.3.0 measured): `pi --mode rpc
  --session-id <uuid>` (the id minted before the start; the same argv
  creates AND resumes; never `--no-session` or any tool/extension/skill/
  context-off flag — pinned) + `--model provider/id` / `--thinking`, both
  changed LIVE (`set_model`, `set_thinking_level`). Commands carry `id` and
  get ONE `response`; a `prompt` answers at once (`started` | `handled`, an
  extension command — the turn ends after `HANDLED_GRACE_S` if no run begins;
  its answer is read when it comes, never awaited: `/rpc-input` answers only
  after its dialog); `agent_settled` (never `agent_end`) ends a turn — ONLY
  the turn whose message owns the run (`agent_start` while that message
  waits for its run); a run no message owns (an extension started it, or it
  began after the grace) keeps the session BUSY (`LiveSession.working_alone`:
  never reaped or evicted) and a message meanwhile is refused in our words
  (`W.PI_STILL_WORKING`; Pi's own "Agent is already processing" too, never
  "the model answered with an error"). A Stop that lands during prompt
  preflight (Pi's `abort` stops an ACTIVE run only) is sent AGAIN at the
  run's `agent_start`; a Stop on a waiting extension command ends
  "interrupted". A model
  error is an assistant `stopReason: "error"`, a missing sign-in a FAILED
  prompt response. Pi asks NOTHING before a tool: ONE mode `own` ("Pi's own
  rules", not dangerous), the other four `hidden_modes` with the reason (plan
  is an extension that turns itself off — `/plan` stays a `/` command). Only
  an extension asks (`extension_ui_request`): `confirm` → an approval drawn
  as the running tool's card (its diff/command) ONLY when exactly one tool
  runs (Pi's parallel tool mode starts them all first), else the dialog's own
  words; `once_only`, answered `confirmed`; `select`/`input`/`editor` → a
  question (`value` / `cancelled`) — a `select` takes one of ITS choices only
  (`free_text: false`, the card has no typed box, another value is refused);
  a dialog's `timeout` closes the card (`forget_ask`), and so does the end of
  the run it was raised in (every card on an aborted settle);
  `notify` → `notice`; the rest is ignored. Pi signs in by itself: no
  Iron-Proxy account (`W.PROXY` has no `pi`; create with an account → 400,
  continue-on refused), `own_sign_in: true`, `signed_in: null`; its failures
  are `P.failure_event` (sign-in → `/login` in a TERMINAL, never the other
  tools' presses); `/login` / `/logout` typed in the Code view's box is
  answered 400 (`W.PI_LOGIN_HERE`), never sent to the model. Its cost is
  `cost_reported_by: "Pi"`, never `list_price` (Pi prices a subscription and
  a pay-per-use key alike; the RPC says neither). A parent Pi session's `PI_SESSION_*` markers are dropped from the
  child env. Models: what a Pi process listed (`get_available_models`),
  kept in `<home>/hosted/pi_models.json`; never a start to ask. A
  conversation with messages keeps its LAST model and thinking level (Pi
  restores them on reopen, core/sdk.js): the view's `keeps_last` hides "Pi's
  default" and a PATCH back to it is refused (`W.PI_KEEPS_LAST`). Disk rows:
  Pi's own sessions folder (`PI_CODING_AGENT_SESSION_DIR` → `sessionDir`
  setting, the PROJECT's `<folder>/.pi/settings.json` over the global one →
  `<PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR or ~/.pi/agent>/sessions`), read in BOTH layouts
  (FLAT under a sessionDir, `--<folder>--` subfolders only under the
  default), id read from the file NAME and matched, header `cwd` = the
  folder. Open in terminal types
  `pi --session <id>`; from-terminal finds the one session written since the
  pane started. A Pi or Codex conversation is HELD by a live terminal running
  that CLI that the Code view opened it in, or whose screen still shows the
  id (`pi --session <id>` / `codex resume <id>`), like Claude's recorded id
  (`terminal_holds(harness=)`; Pi quits with `/quit`). `binary_for` →
  `ai_clis._find` prefers `.exe/.cmd/.bat/.com` over npm's extensionless sh
  shim, and a program Windows cannot start is one sentence
  (`W.could_not_start`). No new event kind. Pins: `tests/test_build_app_pi_v1411.py`
  (recordings `tests/fixtures/build_app_v1411`, contract
  `dashboard/__tests__/fixtures/build-app-events-v1411.json`,
  `IJ_REGEN_BUILD_APP_PI=1`), `tests/test_build_app_pi_review_v1411.py` (the
  review's twelve; the fake harness takes `sleep` and `await … within/
  otherwise` steps), `dashboard/__tests__/build-app-pi-v1411.test.tsx`.
  Live-proven (scratch home + scratch Pi session folder, real pi 1.3.0 on
  openai-codex/gpt-6-luna): a read, an edit that landed, the user's own
  plan extension's "what next?" answered as a question card, and `pi
  --session <id>` reopening the same conversation.

- **Pi in Build › Code offers what Pi's terminal offers: Pi's OWN model list
  (read in the background before any session), each model's OWN thinking
  levels, and Pi's own `/` commands done through its RPC** (v1.429.0; the
  user: "select a model like i can in the terminal … with related effort and
  … the same things i do in the pi terminal"). WHY IT WAS BARE (measured,
  read-only, on the live install): the list was kept only from a Pi process
  the Code view started (`hosted/pi_models.json`) and that file did not
  exist, so the menu held "Pi's default" alone and the `/` menu nothing; and
  Pi's built-ins typed in the box (`/compact`, `/model`…) reached the MODEL as
  plain text (measured with a stand-in model server). READER
  (`hosted/pi_catalog.py`): `pi --mode rpc --no-session --offline --no-mcp`
  fed `get_state`, `get_commands`, `get_available_models` on stdin answers all
  three and exits at end of input in ~0.8 s (measured on pi 1.3.0): no model
  call, no session file, no network, none of the user's MCP servers; one
  `core.procs.run` (20 s bound) in an empty `<home>/hosted/pi-probe`, on a
  background thread, single-flight; started by `HostedService._models(PI)`
  (off the loop) when the kept read is missing, older than `CATALOG_TTL_S`
  (600 s) or Pi's own `models.json` / `auth.json` / `settings.json` /
  `models-store.json` changed (a stat each, `watch_signature`); after a read
  Pi did not answer the next waits `RETRY_AFTER_FAIL_S` (300 s).
  `AUTO_PROBE` is OFF for the whole suite (`tests/conftest.py`); a test turns
  it on with a fake Pi. The harness row carries `models_reading` (the page
  re-reads the tool list `TOOLS_REREAD_TRIES` 5 times, `TOOLS_REREAD_MS` 2 s
  apart, only while it says so) or `models_note` (`W.PI_NO_MODELS`). The
  kept file is one doc (`P.save_catalog`, merged under a lock, temp +
  replace): models, commands, default, fetched_at, watch. ROWS (`P.model_rows`):
  `{id: provider/id, label: Pi's name, group: provider, efforts, pictures?}`,
  sorted by provider (stable — Pi's own order inside one), `MAX_MODELS` 1000.
  LEVELS (`P.levels_of`) is pi-ai `getSupportedThinkingLevels` ported: no
  `reasoning` → ["off"] → `efforts: []` (chip hides); a `thinkingLevelMap`
  entry of `null` is left out; xhigh/max only when the map names them; no
  `reasoning` key → not known (the tool's whole list). Pi's default (a NEW
  conversation started with no model named, never a resumed one — it
  restores its LAST model) rides the "" row as `default_name` + `efforts`
  (`P.note_default`, `LAST_DEFAULT`, reset per test in conftest); the live
  driver also re-asks `get_available_models` `REREAD_MODELS_S` (16 s) after
  open, because Pi refreshes catalogs in the background at start (main.js,
  15 s). COMMANDS: `P.command_rows` keeps `source` (extension/prompt/skill);
  only USER-scope ones are kept for every folder (`user_only`), a session's
  own list has its project's too. `commands_for(PI)` = `P.builtin_rows()`
  (`BUILTINS`: model, thinking, compact, name, session, export, reload —
  `source: "pi"`, argument hints; then `TERMINAL_ONLY` rows with a `note`),
  then the user's; it reads the file only with `disk=True` (the sync
  harnesses route), memory otherwise. TYPED (`HostedService._pi_typed`, before
  any turn; `P.typed_builtin` — an extension/template/skill of the user's with
  the same name wins): a terminal-only one is a 400 in its own words (the old
  `/login` refusal is part of this table); `/model x` resolves exact
  provider/id, else one unique id or name, and `/thinking x` checks the
  model's own levels — both run through `patch` (`_pi_setting`, no Pi start)
  and say one line; `/reload` sets `live.restart`; compact/session/name/export
  run as a turn of their own through `PiDriver.run_builtin` (Pi's RPC
  `compact` with `customInstructions` and a 600 s bound, `get_session_stats`,
  `set_session_name` + the row's title, `export_html`), a refusal said as
  "Pi said: <Pi's words>", Stop ends a running one at once ("Pi may still
  finish summarizing"). A builtin never titles a session, never rides a note
  (`steer` 400 `W.PI_COMMAND_NOT_A_NOTE`). DASHBOARD: `lib/buildApp`
  `ToolModel.group`, `modelGroups` (optgroups in both model chips),
  `defaultName`, `modelsReading`/`modelsNote`, `ToolCommand.source`/`note`,
  `commandExtras`, `COMMAND_SOURCE_WORDS`, `slashMenuRows` (a note row only
  once its name is typed; picking it shows the note in `code-note` and types
  nothing). OPEN: Pi's `enabledModels` (scoped models) is not read — RPC does
  not say which; a project's own templates/skills appear only once its
  session's Pi runs; compact during a run is not offered (a message waits for
  the reply anyway). Pins: `tests/test_build_app_pi_models_v1429.py` (+ the
  recording `tests/fixtures/build_app_v1429/pi_catalog.json`, a SCRATCH
  config, paths masked; 26 mutations red), contract
  `dashboard/__tests__/fixtures/build-app-pi-models-v1429.json`
  (`IJ_REGEN_BUILD_APP_PI_MODELS=1`), `dashboard/__tests__/build-app-pi-models-v1429.test.tsx`
  (11 mutations red); moved on purpose: `test_build_app_pi_v1411` (gpt-6-astra
  takes no "off") and `test_build_app_start_review_v1412` (a reasoning model's
  levels are known). LIVE-PROVEN on a scratch daemon (8797, scratch root,
  scratch `PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR` + `USERPROFILE`, real pi 1.3.0, a stand-in
  model server on 127.0.0.1): the list filled on a fresh install, the chosen
  model and level reached the server, `/model`, `/thinking`, `/session`,
  `/name`, `/export`, `/compact` (Pi's own refusal) worked, a template and a
  skill were expanded by Pi, an extension's command answered, and the page
  showed the groups, "Pi's default (Think Big)" and only each model's levels.

- **A Pi folder's OWN extensions are loaded only by the user's press, for
  that session (`pi --approve`); Iron Jarvis says when Pi skips them**
  (v1.431.0; the user: "Yes, add that"). Pi 1.3.0 loads a folder's
  protected resources only when the folder is trusted; its terminal asks,
  RPC mode cannot, so with no decision Pi SKIPS them silently (read from
  `dist/core/trust-manager.js`, `project-trust.js`, `main.js`, docs
  `security.md`). `hosted/pi_trust.py` (BLOCKING, read-only, bounded;
  `check` cached `CACHE_S` 10 s) mirrors Pi's rules: needs a decision =
  any of `PROJECT_RESOURCES` under `<folder>/.pi` (the folder ONLY), or
  `.agents/skills` in the folder or ANY parent except the user's own
  `~/.agents/skills` (`HOME`, else the home folder); decision order =
  nearest `trust.json` entry (folder, then parents; keys EXACT strings, as
  Pi's object lookup) -> agent `settings.json` `defaultProjectTrust`
  (always | never | anything else = ask). The folder is spelled as Pi
  spells it (`realpathSync(path.resolve(cwd))`: Node's JS realpath follows
  links and KEEPS case) through `code_editor._walk_links` (one hop at a
  time, never a share: the v1.379.0 rule); a `\\host` folder, a link it
  will not follow, or a trust list Pi could not read is UNKNOWN. States:
  none | trusted | declined (the user's own "do not trust" / `never`:
  NEVER second-guessed) | undecided (OFFERED) | unknown. A user-level
  extension answering Pi's `project_trust` event can decide too; Iron
  Jarvis cannot see that (said in the title). THE PRESS:
  `HostedSessionRecord.load_folder_resources` (additive, NULL = False),
  set ONLY by `PATCH /build/app/sessions/{id} {load_folder_resources}`
  (Pi sessions only; `true` refused in words unless the folder is
  undecided, checked fresh); `_new_driver` copies it to
  `PiDriver.approve_folder`, the ONLY thing that adds `--approve` to
  argv; a running Pi gets `live.restart` (same conversation, before the
  next message) and a notice. Nothing is ever written to Pi's
  `trust.json`; another session of the same folder is not trusted by it.
  View: `pi_folder {state, found, loaded, offer, line, title}` only for an
  undecided folder or a session that loads them. Dashboard
  (`lib/buildApp.piFolderOf`, `PI_FOLDER_LOAD_LABEL` / `_SKIP_LABEL`):
  one quiet line above the box (`code-pi-folder`) with **Load them for
  this session** / **Stop loading them**; only the daemon's own `true`
  offers. PROVEN with the real pi 1.3.0 (scratch agent dir, HOME, folder;
  `--offline --no-session --no-mcp`, no model call): a `.pi/extensions`
  command is absent without `--approve`, listed with it, listed with a
  saved trust, and NOT listed with a saved key in other capitals — the
  check agreed every time. Tests fence every lookup inside their scratch
  folder (`_exists` patched): the temp folder sits under the real home,
  whose own `~/.agents/skills` Pi would count once HOME points elsewhere.
  Pins: `tests/test_build_app_pi_folder_trust_v14310.py` (17 mutations
  red), `dashboard/__tests__/build-app-pi-folder-v1431.test.tsx` (7
  mutations red) + the contract fixture
  `dashboard/__tests__/fixtures/build-app-pi-folder-v1431.json`
  (`IJ_REGEN_BUILD_APP_PI_FOLDER=1`). OPEN: not driven through a scratch
  daemon with the real Pi (the argv is pinned with the fake, Pi's answer
  to the flag with the real Pi); Open in terminal's Pi asks its own
  question there.
  v1.431.0 WAS NOT PUBLISHED (its Release gate went red, Tests green;
  v1.431.1 ships it): `build-code-start-v1412` "the chosen model and effort
  ride the make" picked a model right after the start composer's tool chip
  appeared, but that chip is drawn in the same commit that QUEUES the
  composer's "new tool: reset its choices" effect, which React runs later
  (a passive effect; it flushes before the next discrete update) — so on the
  loaded runner the reset ran inside the model change's update and wiped the
  pick, while the effort chosen after it survived (the gate's exact diff).
  Reproduced by acting inside the DOM observer's microtask, right after that
  commit; `await act(async () => {})` after the wait fixes it. Applied to the
  same shape in `build-code-start-review-v1412`, `build-app-pi-models-v1429`
  and `folder-defaults-v1426-0`. A test that interacts right after a
  `waitFor` on something drawn by the SAME commit that queues an effect must
  let React finish that effect first.

- **Build › Code: "Allow all commands this session" is Iron Jarvis's OWN
  whole-tool session rule; the plan is on its card; a failure names the tool
  and has Retry; cards reach the autonomy metrics** (v1.417.0, fix plan wave
  1: C1.1, C4.1, C1.7 Build half, C4.9, C4.7, C1.11). Live record: 40 command
  cards in one Auto-edits session, "Allow for this session" pressed 24 times —
  Claude Code's `permission_suggestions` are PREFIX rules. CONTRACT
  (additive): `approval` gains `can_all_commands` (Claude Code's Bash /
  PowerShell card, mode not in `claude.ALL_COMMANDS_NOT_IN` = full / auto /
  plan) and `plan` (ExitPlanMode's `input.plan`,
  masked, ≤ `claude.PLAN_CHARS` 20,000; title `words.PLAN_CARD_TITLE`); the
  route's decisions are `service.ANSWER_DECISIONS` = allow / allow_session /
  deny + `allow_commands` (`resolved.decision` says it). Claude Code gets
  `updatedPermissions: [claude.ALL_COMMANDS_RULE]` = `{type: addRules, rules:
  [{toolName: Bash}, {toolName: PowerShell}], behavior: allow, destination:
  session}` (shape read from claude 2.1.316's own validator — `ruleContent` is
  optional; the module docstring says so); its own suggestion stays as "Allow
  similar commands" (`suggestion_words(button=)`). Codex: `allow_commands`
  → `acceptForSession` only on a command request that lists it, else 400
  (`words.ALL_COMMANDS_NOT_OFFERED`); Pi / OpenCode: 400 before anything is
  sent. A deny's `message` reaches Claude Code verbatim; the card's "No, tell
  it what to do instead" (`takesWords`, Claude Code only) sends it. ENTER:
  `lib/buildApp.enterAllowsCodeAsk` = Chat › Code's rule — never in Full
  access, never on a once-only card whose `once_reason` is a SAFETY reason
  (`lib/onceReason`; none from a hosted tool yet), Allow once on every other
  (it was silent on every card without a session allow). FAILURE:
  `words.generic_error` raises `HostedToolError(tool_label)` and
  `failure_words._generic_name` reads `tool_label` off the chain first:
  "<Claude Code|Codex|Pi|OpenCode> couldn't finish this answer" +
  `GENERIC_FIX_BUILD`; `CodeConversation` puts **Retry** (`code-retry`, the
  last `user` item's text + files via `send`) on the NEWEST error (never
  beside `account_limit`, never while running); every `done` carries a
  `receipt` folded from the turn's own events (`foldConversation`: model of
  the newest `session` event, `user`→`done` seconds, steps but not todo,
  edit/write paths that finished) drawn as `code-receipt` ("answered by Opus
  5.5 on Work · 2 m 14 s · 7 actions · 3 files changed", the usage line
  `code-usage` inside; the standalone usage item is `folded`). STARTS:
  `service._say_start` emits a `notice` (`words.start_notice`) when a message
  (re)starts a process — `LiveSession.stopped_why` "idle" (reaper) /
  "evicted" (pool) / restart / resume; `ID_SAVE_WAIT_S` 10 → 2 (the save goes
  on shielded); `claude.HOLD_FOR_NOTE_S` 15 → 5. EVERY turn now opens with
  that notice (moved on purpose: the three goldens and the Pi/OpenCode
  `evs[1]` pins). METRICS: `_ask` → `InterruptNote` (surface `build_app`,
  added to `INTERRUPT_SURFACES`; kind card|question; thread = the hosted
  session, turn = the turn) and every way a card closes answers it once
  (`metric_decision`: once / conversation / deny, answered / skipped;
  stopped on expiry or Stop, timeout on a Pi dialog's own timeout; actor
  "phone" from `news.PhoneWatch`); `eval/autonomy.build_app_tasks` makes one
  "build" task per TURN the asks name (their success is not read, so never
  successful or hands-off). Pins: `tests/test_build_app_{answers,
  phone_actor}_v1417.py` (fixtures `tests/fixtures/build_app_v1409/
  claude_{all_commands,plan,failed}_v1417.json`, built from the recorded
  claude_allow frames; the fake harness's `on` branch reads a control_response:
  `allow+all_commands` = a whole-tool session rule — the fake cannot evaluate
  Claude Code's rules, the scenario plays what it then does),
  `dashboard/__tests__/build-app-answers-v1417.test.tsx` (16 + 11 mutations,
  all red); moved on purpose: the once-only Enter pins in
  `build-app-{view-v1409,pi-v1411}` and the e2e. OPEN: not driven against the
  real Claude Code (whether a whole-tool Bash rule silences EVERY later
  command card, incl. Claude Code's own safety checks, is unproven live);
  Codex shows no "all commands" button (its session allow is per command);
  the plan's live-mode ExitPlanMode shape beyond `plan` was not re-read.
  REVIEW (each red first, mutation-checked): (1) claude 2.1.316 calls a
  whole-tool Bash / PowerShell allow "dangerous … (bypasses classifier)" —
  `stripDangerousPermissionsForAutoMode` removes it on entering Auto and the
  rules matched while the classifier runs leave it out — so the button is
  not offered in Auto (nor Plan), and `prepare_answer` refuses it when the
  mode moved there while the card waited. (2) With the reply's end now
  waiting only 2 s for the id save, "Open in terminal" / "Continue on the
  next account" pressed right after a slower save read "no conversation
  yet" (the v1.414.4 bug back): both wait for the pending save themselves
  (`service._row_with_saved_id`, `ID_SAVE_LATE_S` 15 s). (3) A turn the
  boot reconcile closed got its `done` at the NEXT start, so the receipt
  said "14 h 3 m": a stopped / cut-off turn is timed to its last step
  (never a notice or error), and a changed file is counted by the path the
  step began with. (4) `build_app_tasks` put never-successful tasks into
  every denominator (one card halved a week's hands-off rate):
  `Task.whole=False` — their interruptions count, they are in no task
  count, rate or "per task". (5) The ExitPlanMode card is noted as kind
  `plan` (never avoidable, `by_kind.plan`), not `card`. The literal rule
  shape is pinned (a test comparing the constant with itself could not see
  PowerShell dropped).

- **A coding session STARTS AT THE COMPOSER: no form, no folder until one is
  chosen; Choose… is Windows' own folder picker; a tool with several logins
  asks which account** (v1.412.0; the user: "it should start automatically
  with a no folder chosen in the chat bar with a simple choose selector for
  folder as well as a choose selector for the harness … it should ask which
  account … the generic windows file selector … Effort levels should also be
  displayed"). BUILD › CODE: `components/buildCode/StartComposer.tsx` is the
  empty stage AND New session (the v1.409.0 form is gone): the box, and under
  it ONE row of chips — Folder (`components/folders/FolderChip`), Tool (last
  used `ij.code.lastTool`, else the first that `toolUnavailable` passes),
  Account ONLY when `accountChoices(tool).length > 1` (Iron-Proxy rows; Pi's
  own sign-in never asks; picking such a tool OPENS the account menu at once;
  default `ij.code.lastAccount.<tool>` while listed and usable — REVIEW: with
  none remembered the DEFAULT tool asks too, and Send waits for an answer;
  none usable = Send off and the chip says why, `noUsableAccount`), Mode
  (`components/buildCode/ModeChip.tsx`, lifted out of BuildCodeView, which
  re-exports it and the two-press timings), Model, Effort only when
  `lib/buildApp.effortsFor(tool, model)` is non-empty (REVIEW: the model's
  own levels — `harnesses()` model rows carry `efforts` from
  `claude_models.model_efforts`, `()` for `supportsEffort: false`, and Pi's
  `reasoning: false` rows; an effort the new model does not take is dropped,
  by the composer and by `patch`). Send = `POST /build/app/sessions` (the one
  create path) then the SessionPane sends the words as the first message
  (`firstMessage`, once). A folder heading's "+" starts on that folder; New
  session on NONE (the session in front's folder is only where Choose… opens).
  A 400 from the create is said ON the chip its `field` names (REVIEW:
  `Refused.field`; the create route answers `{detail, field}` —
  `code-start-<field>-problem`; folder → the folder chip), nothing started,
  the words kept. A first message parked for a just-made session is dropped
  when the user opens another session before it went (`firstFor`). DAEMON:
  `AppSessionCreate.folder` may be "" → `HostedSessionRecord.no_folder`
  (additive; NULL = False); a saved conversation (`harness_session_id`) still
  needs its folder (400). `start_turn` makes the OWN folder after the input
  checks (`_own_folder`: `documents/workfolder.make_chat_workfolder` +
  `routes/build._work_folder_title`, `root_problem`-checked, kept by
  `store.set_folder_if_empty` — first wins) and every refusal or exception
  before the turn exists takes it back while EMPTY (`_take_back_folder`, never
  while a turn of that session runs) — REVIEW: also a first message whose
  tool never STARTED inside `_drive` (not installed, Windows could not start
  it); once the tool ran, the folder is the conversation's. ONLY DELETE drops
  an empty own folder inside the chat files folder that no other row uses
  (`_drop_own_folder`); ARCHIVE KEEPS IT (Claude Code and Pi keep a
  conversation by its folder: unarchive must resume at the same path). An own
  folder the user deleted is made AGAIN at the same path at the next message
  (`_remake_own_folder` = `routes/build._make_again`), refused in words
  (`W.NO_FOLDER_GONE`) when the folder above it is gone; a missing folder of
  any session says so (`W.FOLDER_MISSING`, never Windows' "directory name is
  invalid"). Views carry `no_folder` + `work_root`; the list
  answers `work_root` too. The dashboard groups every no-folder session under
  ONE "No folder" heading (its "+" starts another). CHAT › CODE: the sidebar's
  New session (and Ctrl+N) `onStart("")` AT ONCE — `NewSessionForm` is gone;
  `components/harness/HarnessStartFolder` sits under the composer while the
  session has NO messages (not in the one-row toolbar: the v1.400.0 fit is
  untouched) and a chosen folder MOVES the empty session (`moveToFolder`:
  `createSession(folder, carry)` with provider/model/reasoning/approval_mode/
  plan mode/account/`local_only`/`cap`/`team` AT CREATE (`BuildSessionCreate`
  takes `local_only` and `cap`, the cap checked as PATCH checks it) +
  `branch` only when the switch was shown, THEN the old one deleted with
  `DELETE …?only_if_empty=1`, THEN `onMoved(new, draft)` with the box's
  words, files and skill READ AT COMMIT (refs) — a refused folder moves and
  deletes nothing). REVIEW (the move could delete a session the user had
  written in): while the move is in flight nothing sends or attaches
  (`movingRef`, "Moving to <folder>…", Send disabled); a file still uploading
  refuses the move in words (`MOVE_WAITS_FOR_UPLOAD`); `only_if_empty`
  answers 409 `{detail, code: "not_empty"}` for any message, waiting message
  or reply running/starting, and a refused delete keeps the user on the OLD
  session and takes the new one back (`refusedNotEmpty`). ABANDONED New
  sessions: `IjHarnessView` deletes (only-if-empty) a session it made (New
  session, "+", "/clear", the move) when the user leaves it — another session
  opened, New session again, the view closed — unless something was put in
  it (`lib/emptySessions.noteSessionUsed`: words, a file, an "@" file, a
  skill, a reply). A window closed outright cleans nothing up. A repository that can branch asks "Work on a new branch" there
  (v1.375.0's switch, moved). `IjHarnessView.pickFrom` = the folder in front,
  else the last one, else Build's — where Choose… opens. The native model
  menu already asks the account (v1.376.0: account entries before models).
  THE PICKER: `desktop/main.js` `pickFolder(owner, raw)` +
  `ipcMain.handle("dialog:pickFolder")` (sender-checked FIRST, parented to
  `BrowserWindow.fromWebContents(event.sender)`, `openDirectory` only,
  `{path}` | `{cancelled: true}`, never throws), `preload.js`
  `dialog.pickFolder(defaultPath)`, `lib/desktopShell.folderPickerBridge()`;
  no bridge (a browser) → the FolderChip's dialog with the real DirectoryTree
  (`pickTypedPath`; no form, every button `type="button"`). Pins:
  `tests/test_build_app_start_v1412.py`, `tests/test_desktop_pick_folder_v1412.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/{build-code-start,ij-harness-start}-v1412.test.tsx`,
  e2e `dashboard/e2e/build-code-start.spec.ts`; moved on purpose: the
  New-session pins of `build-app-view-v1409`, `build-app-pi-v1411`,
  `harness-folder-picker-v1370-1`, `ij-harness-git-v1375`,
  `ij-harness-new-folder-v1378`, `ij-harness-new-folder-nofolder-review-v1379`,
  `ij-harness-no-folder-v1379`, `ij-harness-phase6-v1377`,
  `ij-harness-phase7-review-v1378`, `ij-harness-sidebar-v1369`,
  `ij-harness-slash-nofolder-review-v1379` and the e2e openers
  (`build-app-view`, `ij-harness`, `ij-harness-attempts`, `ij-harness-git`).
  REVIEW pins: `tests/test_build_app_start_review_v1412.py`,
  `tests/test_build_session_move_v1412.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/{build-code-start-review,ij-harness-start-review,ij-harness-abandoned}-v1412.test.tsx`,
  the 390 px Chat › Code folder-chip case in `e2e/build-code-start.spec.ts`
  (its stub now sends what the real daemon sends); moved on purpose:
  `test_build_app_start_v1412` (archive keeps), `build-code-start-v1412` (a
  remembered account; the daemon names the folder chip), `ij-harness-start-v1412`
  (Local only at create, only-if-empty delete), `build-app-view-v1409` (the
  question open at once).

- **SAME RELEASE (v1.412.0): Build › Code takes PICTURES, changes MODE at
  any time, and a note typed while it works STEERS the running reply** (the user:
  "I cant copy and past images into the build modules </> code … the permission blocker
  does not allow change to auto-- allow all, should be changeable mid session
  and the ability to steer work"). PICTURES: SessionPane and StartComposer
  use the chat's own `components/harness/HarnessAttachments`
  (`useHarnessAttachments`, paste via `pastedFiles` — pasted pictures renamed
  —, drop, `AttachButton`, `AttachmentChips`; a send WAITS for an upload via
  `whenSettled` and reads the box from refs; a picture alone is a message;
  `onStarted(session, words, files)` → SessionPane `firstFiles`); the sent
  message draws `SentAttachments` (in the events log, so a rejoin shows them).
  Each tool gets the picture its own measured way: Claude Code an
  `{"type":"image","source":{"type":"base64",…}}` block (`claude.user_content`,
  png/jpeg/gif/webp ≤ 5 MB, ≤ 8; listed by path too), Codex `localImage`
  (`codex.user_input`), Pi `images` (a model whose `input` lacks "image" —
  `pi.takes_pictures`, model rows `pictures: false` — gets the PATH); a
  picture that cannot go as one is a `notice` (`W.PICTURE_AS_PATH`), never
  silent. MODE — ROOT CAUSE: Claude Code REFUSES a live `set_permission_mode
  bypassPermissions` on a process started without
  `--allow-dangerously-skip-permissions` ("bypass_not_launched", measured on
  2.1.311), so Full access could never be chosen once a session had started;
  every Claude process now starts with that flag (`claude.ALLOW_FULL_FLAG`;
  it only makes Full access AVAILABLE — the mode is still `--permission-mode`'s)
  — and the composer (with its mode chip) was HIDDEN while a card waited:
  `code-ask-mode-row` puts a `ModeChip` beside the card. Claude Code's own
  `auto` ("Auto": its classifier allows/refuses) is offered (`MODE_ORDER`
  ask, edits, plan, auto, full); every mode row carries `about` (plain words;
  `claude.MODE_ABOUT`, `codex.MODE_ABOUT`, `pi.MODE_ABOUT`), the chip shows
  it. A mode changed while a card waits says the card still needs the user
  (`W.MODE_CARD_STILL_OPEN`). Full access keeps its two presses. STEER:
  `POST /build/app/sessions/{id}/steer {text, files}` → `HostedService.steer`
  → `Driver.steer(ref, …)` (409 `code: not_working` when no reply runs — the
  page then sends a message; a note during the tool's start waits
  `STEER_WAIT_S`). Measured mechanisms: Claude Code — a user message written
  mid-turn, with a client `uuid` on EVERY message so it says
  `command_lifecycle` {command_uuid, queued|started|completed|cancelled};
  absorbed at the next tool step, or run right after the turn as a second
  `result` — the driver HOLDS the turn's end while a note is unread
  (`HOLD_FOR_NOTE_S` 15 if never queued), Stop sends `interrupt` with
  `cancel_queued: true`, an older CLI with no lifecycle refuses notes in words
  (`LIFECYCLE_WAIT_S`); Codex — `turn/steer` {threadId, expectedTurnId,
  input, clientUserMessageId}, read when a `userMessage` item echoes that
  `clientId`; Pi — `steer`, read when its `queue_update` steering list
  shrinks, `clear_queue` BEFORE `abort` (Pi's abort RUNS queued notes) with
  `_clearing` so that emptied queue is not read as delivery, and unread notes
  are cleared at the turn's end (they would ride the next message). Event
  `steer` {id, state sent|read|returned, text, files?, how?} (in
  `events.TYPES`, the turn's log, a rejoin replays it); `_end_turn` →
  `_return_steers` hands every unread note back BEFORE the done; the page puts
  a returned note THIS window sent back in the box once (`giveNoteBack`) with
  `STEER_RETURNED_WORDS`. Enter while running = a note (placeholder
  `steerPlaceholder`), the arrow `code-steer-send` beside Stop. Pins:
  `tests/test_build_app_steer_v1412.py` (the fake harness gains `{{uuid:N}}`,
  `{{steer_ref}}` and `IJ_FAKE_CLAUDE_MODES=1` = claude 2.1.311's own answer to
  `set_permission_mode`), `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-pictures-steer-v1412.test.tsx`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/ij-harness-paste-start-v1412.test.tsx` (Chat › Code
  checked: paste already worked at its start and in an open session), e2e
  `dashboard/e2e/build-code-paste.spec.ts` (a real ClipboardEvent, the real
  upload, 1280 × 800 and 390 × 844). Moved on purpose:
  `test_build_app_routes_v1409` (auto in the mode list), `test_build_app_pi_v1411`
  (`about`). Live-proven on a scratch daemon (claude 2.1.311 sonnet/haiku,
  codex 0.157.0, pi 1.3.0 openai-codex/gpt-6-luna): each read ZEBRAFIG drawn
  only in a PNG's pixels; Full access then Auto switched mid-reply ("now"),
  Codex said "from your next message"; a note mid-reply was read and the
  answer ended "BANANA" in all three; a Claude note after its last step ran
  as part of the same reply; Stop with a queued note brought it back. OPEN:
  Auto's classifier answered once "gave no verdict" for an action asked before
  the switch (Claude retried it by itself); a note's pictures on Claude Code
  are read only when the note is absorbed or run; the model and effort chips
  are still disabled while a reply runs.
  REVIEW (each pin red first, `tests/test_build_app_steer_review_v1412.py`
  + one case in the dashboard file; 10 mutations of the review's fixes, all
  red; of 18 mutations of the builder's guarantees 4 survived — three now
  have pins, the fourth (Codex keeping a refused note's ref) is harmless, the
  turn's end clears it): (1) a
  note is written to the tool only while its reply is STILL open after the
  note was prepared (Claude Code's pictures and Pi's are read off the loop):
  a reply that ended during that hop got the note anyway — Claude Code ran it
  as a turn nobody follows, Pi queued it for the NEXT message — while the page
  put the same words back in the box (sent twice); Claude marks the note
  `sent` BEFORE the write so a `result` landing during it holds the reply.
  (2) `_return_steers` keeps a note whose `steer()` has not come back
  (`ended`, with whether the tool READ it); a read one answers `state: read`
  (its sent/read events written after the reply), never `returned`. (3) the
  page puts back a note whose `returned` frame arrived BEFORE its own POST
  answer (`returnedEarlyRef`). (4) `_file_problems(files, folder)` refuses a
  network-share or device spelling from its TEXT before any stat
  (`harness.sessions._network_spelling`; `W.ATTACHED_ON_SHARE`; allowed when
  the session's own folder is on a share) — a stat of a share path is an SMB
  connection (the v1.379.0 lesson). (5) `claude.session_suggestions` refuses
  a card's `setMode auto` like `bypassPermissions` (Auto and Full access only
  from the mode chip). (6) a queued note Claude Code itself `cancelled` ends a
  reply held for it AT ONCE (it waited 15 s; with the mark missing, for ever).
  CHECKED SAFE on claude 2.1.311 (its embedded schema
  and startup code): with `--allow-dangerously-skip-permissions` the process
  still starts in `--permission-mode`'s mode (a CLI mode wins over settings
  and proactivity at startup); bypass is reachable only by the host's
  `set_permission_mode` (PATCH needs `confirm: true`), ExitPlanMode restoring
  a pre-plan mode the user had chosen, or `set_proactivity` (enabled only when
  the host sends `proactivity: true` on initialize, which Iron Jarvis never
  does). NOT FIXED: a note Claude Code never QUEUED within `HOLD_FOR_NOTE_S`
  goes back to the box although it was written to Claude's stdin (no
  `cancel_async_message` is sent).

- **OpenCode is the FOURTH tool in Build › Code: `opencode acp` (the Agent
  Client Protocol, JSON-RPC over stdio), its own tools/agents/plugins/MCP/
  config ON; a mode is OpenCode's OWN permission rules for THAT process, and
  a rule of the user's own is never widened** (v1.413.0, `hosted/opencode.py`,
  opencode 1.18.18 measured). WHY ACP, NOT `opencode serve`: both are
  OpenCode's own long-lived machine-readable ways in; ACP rides the process's
  own stdin/stdout (`hosted.process`, tree kill) and opens no port of ours —
  OpenCode's internal server is started with `--hostname 127.0.0.1 --port 0
  --mdns=false` (`O.ACP_ARGV`; REVIEW: with no flag the user's global
  `server.hostname/port/mdns` decide — measured, `"0.0.0.0"` listened on every
  interface, unauthenticated; port 0 gives 4096 first, then a free one).
  argv is `O.ACP_ARGV` ONLY (the folder is the process cwd: npm's `opencode.cmd` would run through cmd.exe;
  never `--pure`, it switches the user's plugins off — pinned), and
  `process.npm_program` starts `node_modules/opencode-ai/bin/opencode.exe`
  directly when the found program is npm's `.cmd`. USED (the evidence is the
  module docstring + `tests/fixtures/build_app_v1413`, recorded through the
  real service via a logging proxy, masked): `initialize` (clientCapabilities
  fs/terminal FALSE — Iron Jarvis is no editor; OpenCode still sends
  `fs/write_text_file` after writing itself, answered -32601), `session/new`
  {cwd, mcpServers: []} → `ses_…` + `configOptions` (model, effort = the
  current model's variants, mode = agents build/plan), `session/set_config_
  option` (model, effort, mode; live), `session/prompt` (answers only at the
  turn's end, `stopReason` end_turn|cancelled|max_tokens|max_turn_requests|
  refusal; an error answer = the model failed → `O.failure_event`),
  `session/update` (agent_message_chunk/agent_thought_chunk/tool_call/
  tool_call_update/plan/usage_update/available_commands_update),
  `session/request_permission` (options once/always/reject → only an option
  OpenCode OFFERED is ever answered; "always" = allow for this session, kept
  in the process — nothing written to OpenCode's `permission` table),
  `session/cancel` (a notification: the tool ends "Tool execution aborted",
  the prompt answers cancelled), `session/load` (REPLAYS the conversation as
  updates before it answers — `_loading` draws none; keeps the session's last
