  model and effort → `keeps_last` from its DB). MODES (`O.MODES`: preset →
  (posture, agent)): ask = `{"*": "ask"}` + the read-only tools allowed
  (`read` = `O.READ_RULE`: OpenCode's own `.env` ask kept — REVIEW: a plain
  `"read": "allow"` landed after that default and read `.env` files with no
  card, live-proven), edits = + edit allow, plan = ask + OpenCode's plan agent
  + `"edit": "deny"` (REVIEW: OpenCode puts the config's — so the env's —
  rules AFTER the plan agent's own edit deny, so `"*": "ask"` made Plan's
  refusal a card; with a user `edit` key the deny replaces it IN PLACE and no
  `"*"` is used, which would land after it), full = `{"*": "allow"}` (two
  presses), auto hidden. EVERY mode change is a new process (`set_mode`,
  nothing switched live). OpenCode's own default asks NOTHING
  (measured), so a posture is always set: through `OPENCODE_PERMISSION` (JSON)
  in the child env (`O.child_env`, off the loop), merged by OpenCode OVER the
  user's config. MERGE ORDER WIDENS (measured: a project's `"bash": "deny"` +
  env `{"*": "ask"}` ASKED): `O.permission_env` never sets a key the user's
  own rules set (global config via `opencode_config_paths`, `OPENCODE_CONFIG`,
  `OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR`, `OPENCODE_CONFIG_CONTENT`, a pre-existing
  `OPENCODE_PERMISSION`, and `opencode.json(c)` / `.opencode/` in the folder
  and every folder above; REVIEW added the global `config.json`,
  `~/.opencode`, the managed `%ProgramData%\opencode`
  (`OPENCODE_TEST_MANAGED_CONFIG_DIR`) — skipped while
  `IRONJARVIS_OPENCODE_CONFIG_DIR` is set — and the legacy `tools` map
  (`O.legacy_tool_rules`: OpenCode places it BEFORE the permission object) —
  all READ-ONLY), uses `"*"` only when no rule of
  theirs is stricter, else names `ACTING_TOOLS` one by one and says so
  (`W.OPENCODE_RULES_KEPT` / `W.OPENCODE_UNNAMED_UNASKED`: then MCP-server and
  plugin tools run on OpenCode's own default, no card — said in words); one string rule for everything decides alone
  (`W.OPENCODE_RULES_DECIDE`). A posture change is a NEW process before the
  next message (`driver.restart_needed` → `live.restart`; "next"); the agent
  changes live. SAME RELEASE, a pre-existing bug the mode-change pin found:
  `_ensure_running`'s restart called `_stop_process`, which ended the very
  turn that was starting ("Stopped: the coding tool was closed") — so a Claude
  Code effort change or a model back to the default never reached the next
  message; it now stops with `end_turn=False`. NOTES: a SECOND
  `session/prompt` while a turn runs joins it (OpenCode's loop reads it at the
  next step; both answer together) — the turn ends only when EVERY prompt of
  it answered (a late note never runs unfollowed); a note is `read` on its
  answer; one cancelled by Stop is NOT handed back (it is in OpenCode's
  conversation — sent twice otherwise) and `W.OPENCODE_NOTE_KEPT` says so.
  Pictures: ACP `image` blocks (read live). Sign-in is OpenCode's own (`opencode
  auth login`): `OWN_SIGN_IN` = (pi, opencode), no Iron-Proxy account (400),
  `sign_in_hint` on the harness row (the dashboard's `ownSignInTitle`,
  `keepsLastTitle` — never Pi's words for OpenCode). Saved sessions: OpenCode's
  SQLite DB (`O.data_dir`: `IRONJARVIS_OPENCODE_DATA_DIR`, else
  `$XDG_DATA_HOME/opencode`, else `~/.local/share/opencode`), read `mode=ro` +
  `query_only` (top-level, not archived, matched by folder); `opencode
  --session <id>` in a terminal (`typable_sid`: a UUID or `ses_…`). Cost:
  `usage_update.cost` deltas, `cost_reported_by: "OpenCode"`, never list
  price. TESTS NEVER READ THE USER'S OPENCODE DB: `tests/conftest.py`
  `_isolate_opencode_sessions` sets `IRONJARVIS_OPENCODE_DATA_DIR` for the
  session. The fake harness gains `await: notify` and logs
  `OPENCODE_PERMISSION`. LOCAL ONLY: Code-view sessions have no Local only
  setting (none of the four tools) — not added here. OPEN: whether
  `usage_update.cost` is cumulative per session (only free models measured);
  a model's picture support is not known in advance (OpenCode itself tells a
  text-only model); OpenCode's `question` tool never surfaced over ACP (none
  drawn); subagent (`task`) permission requests not measured. Pins:
  `tests/test_build_app_opencode_v1413.py` (recordings `tests/fixtures/
  build_app_v1413`, contract `dashboard/__tests__/fixtures/build-app-events-
  v1413.json`, `IJ_REGEN_BUILD_APP_OPENCODE=1`),
  `dashboard/__tests__/build-app-opencode-v1413.test.tsx`; moved on purpose:
  `test_build_app_routes_v1409` (four tools; the from-terminal words),
  REVIEW pins `tests/test_build_app_opencode_review_v1413.py` (judged by a
  model of OpenCode 1.18.18's OWN merge + `findLast` evaluation read from the
  installed program; the Claude Code / Pi restart-before-the-next-message
  pins live there too),
  `build-code-start-v1412` (the heading). Live-proven on a scratch daemon
  (real opencode 1.18.18, opencode/glm-5.3-flash-free): see the Handbook.

- **Grok is the FIFTH tool in Build › Code: `grok agent --no-leader stdio`
  (ACP, the core OpenCode speaks, LIFTED into `hosted/acp.py`); a mode is the
  session's own `_meta`, ALWAYS stated; Iron Jarvis never picks an option Grok
  SAVES** (v1.416.0, `hosted/grok.py`, grok 1.0.5 -> 1.0.70 measured — the CLI
  UPDATED ITSELF during the first scratch run, its own background updater).
  `AcpDriver` (requests, prompts, cards, the update stream, the turn's end)
  is shared; `OpenCodeDriver` keeps its behaviour on it (its 74 tests
  unchanged, green) and `opencode.py` still re-exports the lifted names.
  START: `G.ARGV` = `agent --no-leader stdio` (Grok's `[cli] use_leader`
  would hand the session to a SHARED leader that outlives the process and that
  no tree kill reaches); no port. POSTURE: Grok's ACP `session/new` /
  `session/resume` take `_meta {yoloMode, autoMode}` (its documented options);
  with no `_meta` the user's own `[ui] permission_mode` decides — on THIS PC
  `always-approve`, and a write ran with no card (measured). So
  `G.session_meta(mode)` is sent on EVERY open and resume: ask = both false,
  auto = Grok's own safety check, full = yolo (two presses). `session/set_mode`
  answers `{}` for ANY id, even a made-up one, and changes nothing (measured);
  the top-level `--permission-mode` / `--allow` are not honoured by `agent
  stdio` (measured) — so Auto edits and Plan are HIDDEN with reasons
  (`G.HIDDEN_MODES`). A mode change is a new process before the next message
  (`restart_needed`, `session/resume` with the new `_meta`). CARDS: an edit
  offers `allow-edits-session` (in memory, per Grok's docs) / `allow-once` /
  `reject-once`; a command offers `always-allow` and `reject-always`, which
  Grok SAVES for the project — `G.SESSION_ONLY_OPTIONS` is the only Allow for
  this session, Deny is `reject-once` only (a card with none answers
  cancelled), so a command card is Allow once only. A `reject-once` ENDS the
  reply (`stopReason cancelled`, `cancellationCategory PermissionRejected`):
  drawn `completed` + `W.GROK_DECLINED_ENDS`, never the user's Stop. STOP:
  `session/cancel` ends the reply and LEAVES THE RUNNING COMMAND RUNNING (a
  `ping` outlived the cancel and Grok itself; `_x.ai/terminal/kill` with the
  tool call id said "terminal not found") — the driver draws each running tool
  failed (`G.STOPPED_TOOL`), sets `close_after_stop` when one was a command,
  and `service._end_turn` stops the process BEFORE the done, with
  `W.GROK_STOP_CLOSED`; the conversation reopens with the next message. THE
  JOB DOES NOT REACH GROK'S COMMANDS: Grok runs each one as `pwsh
  -NoProfile -NonInteractive -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -Command <cmd>` — on this
  PC the Store pwsh (WindowsApps alias), which Windows never puts in our Job
  (the v1.407.1 AppX lesson) — so the `ping` was in NO job and outlived the
  Job's close (measured with `IsProcessInJob`, live). And Grok's cancel kills
  that pwsh, leaving the command with a dead parent, where a tree read at stop
  time no longer finds it. So `HarnessProcess.note_started()` reads the
  descendants (psutil, pid + start time) when the service sends a Stop to a
  driver with `notes_started_on_stop` (Grok only), BEFORE `session/cancel`;
  `stop()` reads them again and `process.kill_left` ends whatever is still
  the SAME process (start time checked — a reused pid is never touched).
  Live-proven: before the fix a ping survived Stop; after, 0 left. REVIEW
  (pins `tests/test_build_app_grok_review_v1416.py`, 7 mutations red): the
  sweep is OPT-IN (`HarnessProcess(ends_left_behind=)`, set from the
  driver's `notes_started_on_stop`) — the other four tools stop exactly as
  before; `descendants` reads a child only when it is not older than ITS OWN
  parent (psutil's recursive walk checks only the top process, so the user's
  process behind a REUSED parent pid was read as Grok's) and is bounded
  (`MAX_DESCENDANTS`); `kill_left` needs the start time EXACTLY and spares a
  visible-window owner or a Windows-GUI program (the keep-windows rule); and
  a Stop with a card open calls `GrokDriver.stopping()` BEFORE the card's
  deny, because Grok ends its reply on that reject before the cancel lands
  (it read "You declined" and `completed`). NOTES: a second `session/prompt` is QUEUED as its own turn
  (measured), so a note is Grok's own `_x.ai/interject` {sessionId, text}
  (answered `{status: queued}`, echoed at once as `_x.ai/session/interjection`
  — read on that echo; registered BEFORE the request, the echo outruns its
  answer). RESUME: `session/resume` (no replay; keeps the last model —
  `keeps_last` from `summary.json` `num_messages`), else `session/load` with
  the replay hidden. MODELS / EFFORT: `models.availableModels` (each with its
  own `reasoningEfforts`), `session/set_model`, `session/set_config_option
  reasoning_effort`; the list is what a Grok process last said
  (`<home>/hosted/grok_models.json`), else Grok's own `models_cache.json`
  (names only). USAGE: the prompt answer's `_meta.usage`, `costUsdTicks /
  10^10` (xAI's unit — NOT cross-checked against a bill), `cost_reported_by:
  "Grok"`, never list price; context = `_meta.totalTokens` over the model's
  `totalContextTokens`. Every other `_x.ai/...` notification is DROPPED unread
  (`_x.ai/mcp/servers_updated` carries the user's MCP config, keys in URLs).
  SIGN-IN / ACCOUNTS: Grok's own `auth.json` read as a FILE (`G.signed_in`,
  never a process); an Iron-Proxy `xai` account is its `GROK_HOME`
  (`W.PROXY`, `BA.proxy_account_rows` on `BA.status_of`, lifted out of
  `row_of`); `continue-on` refuses (`W.GROK_CONTINUE`). SAVED SESSIONS:
  `<home>/sessions/*/<uuid>/summary.json` matched by its `cwd` (the encoded
  folder name is never recomputed); `grok --resume <id>` in a terminal.
  LISTED ONLY WHEN INSTALLED: the row carries `only_when_installed` and the
  dashboard's `decodeCodingTools` drops it when not installed;
  `toolChoiceWords` names the four always plus Grok when listed. TESTS NEVER
  READ THE USER'S ~/.grok: `tests/conftest.py` `_isolate_grok_home` sets
  `IRONJARVIS_GROK_HOME` (`G.HOME_ENV`, Iron Jarvis's reads only — the process
  is never told). OPEN: grok-4.5 did not read an attached picture (it tried
  `read_file`, then a PowerShell search; grok-4.7 read it) — which models
  see pictures is not known in advance; a note interjected in a reply's LAST
  step was not measured (it may run as its own turn); Grok's
  `x.ai/ask_user_question` / `exit_plan_mode` client requests are answered
  "not handled"; Grok's subagent permission requests not measured; Iron
  Jarvis's own MCP tools over Grok's ACP not measured live; the cost tick unit;
  the CLI auto-updates itself (a recording may stop matching a newer Grok).
  Pins: `tests/test_build_app_grok_v1416.py` (recordings
  `tests/fixtures/build_app_v1416`, contract `dashboard/__tests__/fixtures/
  build-app-events-v1416.json`, `IJ_REGEN_BUILD_APP_GROK=1`),
  `dashboard/__tests__/build-app-grok-v1416.test.tsx`; moved on purpose:
  `test_build_app_routes_v1409` (five tools; the from-terminal words).
  Every guarantee mutation-checked (19 daemon + 4 dashboard, all red).
  Live-proven on a scratch daemon (8797, scratch root, real grok 1.0.70,
  grok-4.5 effort low): an answer ("pong", cost reported), an edit card
  allowed (the file written), a command card (Allow once only) allowed then
  Stop (no command left running), and the next message resumed the same
  conversation. NOT driven live: Open in terminal / back (pinned with the
  fake only), an Iron-Proxy account, Iron Jarvis's MCP tools.

- **Build › Code is part of the app: its running work is seen everywhere,
  its card reaches the phone, and the REAL tool may get Iron Jarvis's own MCP
  connection — per process, behind the same five boxes a terminal has, all
  off** (v1.414.0). SEEN: `hosted/news.py` is the one in-memory source —
  `running_rows` (`surface: "build_app"`, the hosted session id as
  `thread_id`, `harness`, `waiting` = a card or question open) rides `GET
  /chat/turns/running` (Running now: `lib/runningNow.buildAppReplyItems`,
  "Coding session · <tool> · 12 min", `buildCodeHref`), `/health.replies.
  build_app` (the orb's `repliesRunning`), `routes/ui.build_replies_running`
  (home line, Status map); `/system/activity` gains `code_replies` +
  `code_tools`: RUNNING counts toward `busy` (the update warning, main.js
  `describeBusyWork` "N coding sessions in Build › Code working (Claude
  Code)"), only the part NOT parked on a card is `working` (keep-awake — the
  v1.391.0 rule). FINISH: `_end_turn` (after the done, never at shutdown,
  never beside an account-limit card, `HostedTurn.started_mono` on the
  service's injectable clock) publishes the SAME `build.turn_finished` with
  `surface: "build_app"`, `harness`, session id `build_app:<id>` for a reply
  > 60 s that ended completed / max_turns (done) or failed — never
  interrupted; `bell_pings` is unchanged (thread_id present), the bell and
  main.js `runOutcomeNotice` link `/terminals?view=code&code=<id>`; the one-
  notice fixture has `build-app-turn-finished`. The Code view's SessionPane
  writes the shared `lib/buildWatching` note (the session id) while mounted
  and focused, so a watched finish stays quiet. PHONE: `_ask` arms a
  `news.PhoneWatch` per approval (`svc.phone_after_s` = `stall.PHONE_AFTER_S`);
  past it, if the card is still open and `phone_may_send` (approval only —
  never a question, never `once_only`, priced or local-only cards, never
  while the row's mode is `full`), it files an entry in `platform.approvals`
  (names only, session `build_app:<id>`) and publishes through
  `stall.PhoneAsk` (`surface: "build"`, the row's title) — the pending
  listing still shows only the session's own `waiting_rows` row; the phone's
  answer resolves the entry and is delivered as `allow` (ONCE) or `deny` via
  `HostedService.answer`; ANY `resolved` event of that card (`_emit`) closes
  the watch (the entry popped, the phone prompt closed) — the watch never
  cancels the task delivering its own answer (`_answering`). JARVIS TOOLS:
  `HostedSessionRecord.capabilities_json` (additive; the pane's five keys,
  `terminals/session.normalise_pane_capabilities`), PATCH `capabilities`
  MERGES; `hosted/jarvis_tools.py`: a token only when a SERVED box (Browser,
  Memory — `mcpserver.CAPABILITY_TOOLS`) is ticked (`wanted`), minted in
  `_start_process` from `HostedTokens` — a `PaneTokenStore` of its OWN
  (`purge_dead` would revoke `code:<sid>` ids in the terminals' store) whose
  lookup reads `live.caps` LIVE (a box off → the next call 403s) and
  `live.running`; revoked in `_stop_process` (before the stop) / `_on_exit` /
  a failed start, with the MCP sessions it opened; `routes/mcpserver._tokens`
  resolves through `_EitherStore` (same `authorize_mcp` door). The first box
  on / last box off sets `live.restart` ("next"); switching between served
  boxes is "now". Per CLI, MEASURED with a logging stand-in server and a
  scratch home (no model call): Claude Code 2.1.311 `--mcp-config
  <home>/hosted/<id>/jarvis-mcp.json` with `Bearer ${IRONJARVIS_MCP_TOKEN}`
  (Claude expands it), NO `--strict-mcp-config`; Codex 0.157.0 `codex
  app-server -c mcp_servers.iron-jarvis-code.url=… -c …bearer_token_env_var=
  "IRONJARVIS_MCP_TOKEN"`; Pi 1.3.0 `-e <home>/hosted/<id>/jarvis-mcp.js`
  (an extension calling `pi.registerMcpServer`, `exposure: "direct"`, header
  `${…}` expanded by Pi); OpenCode 1.18.18 ACP `session/new|load`
  `mcpServers: [{type: "http", name, url, headers: [{name, value}]}]` only
  when `mcpCapabilities.http` (else a notice) — the token on stdin, NOT its
  env. Our two files hold no credential and go with the process. The user's
  own config files are never written. MCP SERVER: `MEMORY_READ_TOOLS` gains
  `brain_search` / `brain_read` (exact names; `brain_save`/`brain_change`
  never); EVERY MEMORY-capability tool's output (memory_search, memory_read,
  ltm_search, brain_*; a terminal's grant too) crosses `/mcp` inside
  `wrap_untrusted` (a browser tool's page text keeps its v1.238.0 shape;
  review: memory_read was left bare by a flag check); a code grant's
  workspace is the session's folder and its `ToolContext.project_id` comes
  from `projects/locate.project_for_path` (terminal grants unchanged). NOT
  ENFORCED HERE: Build › Code has no Local only and no trust posture (none of
  the four tools) — the Memory box says what it reads goes to the tool's
  vendor. PROFILE SHARE: `profile/share.CLIS` gains `pi` (`PI_CODING_AGENT_DIR`
  or `~/.pi/agent`; `pick="exists"`: Pi reads the first of
  AGENTS.override.md / AGENTS.md / CLAUDE.md that EXISTS — an empty override
  still wins) and `opencode` (`home_fn`: `$XDG_CONFIG_HOME/opencode` else
  `~/.config/opencode`, AGENTS.md; `create_guard`: never CREATE it while
  `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` exists and OpenCode's own
  `OPENCODE_DISABLE_CLAUDE_CODE[_PROMPT]` is unset — measured: OpenCode's
  AGENTS.md REPLACES that fallback; the row says `OPENCODE_FALLBACK_ERROR`).
  Both measured by a sentinel reaching a local stand-in endpoint. Config
  `profile_share_pi` / `profile_share_opencode` (off; schema rows ask-tier,
  `put=False`; `settings/writer._SHARE_KEYS` pinned equal to CLIS).
  DASHBOARD: `components/buildCode/JarvisTools.tsx` — `JarvisToolsChip`
  (header; `code-jarvis-tools`, Memory/Browser boxes with
  `lib/buildApp.jarvisToolHint`, PATCHes one key) and `ProfileShareOffer`
  (a new session's empty state; `PUT /profile/share`; "No thanks" =
  localStorage `ij.code.shareOffer.dismissed.<cli>`; hidden on 404, on, not
  installed). OPEN: the MCP-off restart for a running reply is unpinned; Browser over a code grant
  was not driven live; the phone path was not driven with a real phone.
  Pins: `tests/test_build_app_spine{,_desktop}_v1414.py`,
  `tests/test_profile_share_pi_opencode_v1414.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-spine{,-view}-v1414.test.tsx`, the
  one-notice fixture; moved on purpose: `test_harness_gaps_running_v1389`
  (`replies` gains `build_app`). Live-proven (scratch daemon 8901, real
  claude 2.1.311 haiku low, Memory ticked): Running now / activity / health
  listed the reply for 75 s, Claude called `mcp__iron-jarvis__memory_search` (the
  server is `iron-jarvis-code` since the review)
  (ledger row `mcp:code:<id>`), answered from it, and `build.turn_finished`
  (`seconds: 75`) was recorded. REVIEW (each red first; 12 mutations of the fixes and 11 of the first cut's claims, all red):
  a process that ends BY ITSELF also removes our two connection files
  (`_remove_files_if_stopped`: under the pool lock, only while no process
  runs); a Code grant's 403s and instructions name the session and its
  Iron Jarvis tools chip (`no_capability_message`,
  `capability_refused_message(name, pane_id)`), never "this Build
  terminal"; the share offer reads the PUT's answer and says a refused
  write (OpenCode's fallback guard) instead of "shared"; the account-limit
  finish suppression is pinned; the hosted server is named
  `iron-jarvis-code` for all four tools (`hosted/jarvis_tools.SERVER_NAME`),
  never the connected apps' `iron-jarvis`: Codex MERGES `-c mcp_servers.<name>.url`
  into a stdio table of the same name in the user's own settings (both
  `command` and `url`). Since v1.539.0 a Codex terminal with tools uses the
  same `iron-jarvis-code` options and writes no table; Claude's terminal
  server is `iron-jarvis-terminal`.
  Pins:
  `tests/test_build_app_spine_review_v1414.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-spine-review-v1414.test.tsx`.
  SAME RELEASE, TWO DEFECTS A LIVE CHECK FOUND (real codex 0.157.0 / claude
  2.1.311): (1) in Ask first Codex asks before EVERY MCP tool call (the Memory
  box's `iron-jarvis-code` server and the user's own Codex servers) through
  `mcpServer/elicitation/request`, and the driver declined every one, so Codex
  read "user rejected MCP tool call" and the Memory box did nothing for Codex.
  The shape is Codex's own (generate-json-schema + `core/src/mcp_tool_call.rs`
  at `rust-v0.157.0`): `mode: "form"`, an EMPTY `requestedSchema`, `_meta.
  codex_approval_kind == "mcp_tool_call"`, `persist` ("session" / "always" /
  both), `tool_params`; the tool's `mcpToolCall` item starts BEFORE it.
  `codex.mcp_tool_approval` reads it into an ordinary card (kind `tool`,
  `words.mcp_call_title`: Iron Jarvis's own tools in words — `Search Iron
  Jarvis memory for "<query>"` —, any other server "Use <server> › <tool>";
  arguments as "name: value" lines, `words.mcp_args_lines`); Allow once =
  `{"action": "accept"}`, Allow for this session = accept + `_meta: {persist:
  "session"}` (offered ONLY when Codex's `persist` names "session"; else the
  card is `once_only`), Deny / Stop = `{"action": "decline"}`; "always" (a
  write to Codex's own config) is NEVER sent. A server's own form (fields to
  fill, a page to open) is still declined, said with the server's name
  (`words.MCP_FORM_DECLINED`). Full access never asks (Codex auto-approves
  under `never` + full disk). A finished MCP step's output is Codex's own
  error message or the result's text (`codex._mcp_output`), never a Python
  dict. (2) A sign-in failure Retry cannot fix read as the generic "Press
  Retry…": `cli_auth._SIGN_IN_PATTERNS` gains an expired/revoked OAuth login
  and "token/session … could not be refreshed" (the live text: "Failed to
  authenticate: OAuth session expired and could not be refreshed"), OpenCode's
  own regex too (Pi's had it); `words.sign_in_error` titles a lapsed login
  "<Tool> needs to sign in again" and keeps the one Sign in press
  (`fix_kind: "sign_in"`). Claude Code's, OpenCode's and Pi's own MCP asks
  were already cards. Fake: `fake_harness_v1409.py` `await … "on":
  {accept|accept+session|decline|cancel|"*": [steps]}` plays the branch the
  host's answer names. Pins: `tests/test_build_app_codex_mcp_v1414.py`,
  `tests/test_build_app_signin_words_v1414.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/codex-mcp-card-v1414.test.tsx` (+ fixture
  `codex-mcp-card-v1414.json`, `IJ_REGEN_CODEX_MCP=1`).
  SAME RELEASE, THE RIGHT COLUMN (the user: "the icons for changes,
  remaining usage, files and sessions to right just as in the native
  harness"): `components/buildCode/CodePanes.tsx` — Changes · Usage · Files ·
  Session, HarnessPanes' icon-tab look byte for byte (32 px, the tip IS the
  accessible name, `group-hover:delay-300`; `ij.code.pane`); BuildCodeView's
  ONE layout seam: `code-session-row` (side by side from xl, the aside
  `xl:w-[320px] 2xl:w-[400px]`; below xl the row scrolls and the
  conversation keeps the stage's height, `flex-none` — a `flex-1` there is
  the v1.369.0 squeeze). Each pane mounted only on its tab; reads repeat
  every `CODE_PANES_POLL_MS` only while a reply runs (`useVisibleInterval`)
  and once when it ends. DAEMON (new modules only; read-only, off the loop,
  never the tool's binary, a model or a probe): `daemon/routes/
  build_app_panes.py` → `hosted/panes.py`: `GET /build/app/sessions/{id}/
  changes | usage | overview`. CHANGES SOURCE: the real CLI writes outside
  the undo journal, so `git` when the folder is a repository — every file
  differing from the newest FIRST-PARENT commit no later than the row's
  `created_at` MINUS ONE SECOND (git dates are whole seconds and `--before`
  includes its second), `--relative`, untracked via `ls-files --others
  --exclude-standard`, empty tree when no commit predates it; limits SAID in
  `words`: includes the user's / other programs' changes, ignored files not
  listed, rewritten commit dates can land wrong; `by_tool` marks files the
  tool's own edit/write events name. Else `tool`: those events (status done)
  per file, every edit's hunks under ONE header (`_hunks_only` — a second
  header inside a hunk reads as lines); limits said (commands' writes
  unseen). A `\\host` folder is refused from its TEXT first (v1.379.0); no
  Undo (`no_undo` words), `ChangesReview` without `hint`. USAGE: the Iron-
  Proxy CACHED row (`build_accounts.session_account` + `state_of` windows,
  expired ones dropped) + what the TOOL last reported: `hosted/seen.py`
  parses Claude Code's `rate_limit_event.unifiedWindows` (utilization →
  percent; a window with none is left out, never 0%) and `result`'s last
  `usage.iterations` over `modelUsage[*].contextWindow`, Codex's
  `account/rateLimits/updated` (primary/secondary + `planType`) and
  `thread/tokenUsage/updated` (`last.totalTokens` / `modelContextWindow`),
  OpenCode's `usage_update` (`used`/`size`); the driver keeps the newest and
  `seen.attach` puts `windows`/`plan`/`context` on the turn's `usage` event
  (one line each in claude/codex/opencode; Pi reports none — the tab says
  so) — so the 1409 and 1413 goldens moved (usage events only). Totals sum
  the `usage` events; list price is never "spent". SESSION: branch, rule
  files present (CLAUDE.md, AGENTS.md…), context, `psutil` children of the
  LIVE tool process (its MCP servers included, said so; `one_line` masks
  then cuts), nothing while it is not running. `HarnessSessionRail` exports
  `Section`/`Row` (shared look). Pins: `tests/test_build_code_panes_v1414.py`
  (11; 11 mutations red), `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-panes-v1414.test.tsx`
  (13; 10 mutations red), e2e `dashboard/e2e/build-code-panes.spec.ts`
  (1280 × 800, 390 × 844). Live: scratch daemon 8911, real claude 2.1.311
  haiku low, Auto edits, an edit of a scratch repo's `calc.py`: Changes
  listed it from git with its diff and "1 of these was edited by Claude
  Code"; Usage "7-day window 49% left" as Claude Code reported; Session 16%
  of 200k, branch main. OPEN: a stale tool report is shown with its age, not
  refreshed; Codex context counts the last call's total tokens; the git base
  ignores a branch switch during the session; Terminal/Browser tabs not
  added. REVIEW (each red first; 17 + 6 mutations, all red): a program's
  command goes through `harness/process._shown_command` (the v1.404.0 rule:
  the word after `--token`/`--api-key` hidden — `one_line` masked shapes
  only and showed `--token s3cr3t…`); `program_listing` answers `{programs,
  known, more}` → overview `programs_known` (a psutil refusal read "Nothing
  is running") and `programs_more` (cut at `MAX_PROGRAMS`, said); a window
  the TOOL reported whose `resets_at` passed is dropped like Iron-Proxy's
  (`_now()` is the seam; the 1409 recording's windows are judged at their
  own time); Iron-Proxy windows carry their `at` and the account its OLDEST
  (`code-usage-proxy-when` "…, 2 h ago"). GIT THAT DID NOT ANSWER IS NEVER A
  LIST (`GitUnreadable` → `source: none` + `GIT_UNREADABLE` words): a
  timed-out/failed name-status ("No files changed yet"), numstat (every file
  "binary"), ls-files (new files missing), rev-list (only `rev-parse --verify
  -q HEAD` exiting 1 means no commits — else the empty tree listed every file
  "created"), and a rev-parse refusal other than "not a git repository"
  ("dubious ownership" read "not in a git repository"); git missing says so
  (`NO_GIT_WORDS`). ONE git child for every file's diff (`_patches`: the
  combined `git diff -- <paths>` cut at `diff --git` lines, each piece
  checked against the line predicted for its entry, else that file alone;
  past `PATHSPEC_CHARS` per file; a combined read that TIMED OUT is not
  retried per file and every row says `DIFF_UNREAD`) — 50 files: 54 children
  / ~3 s → 6 / ~0.35 s, on every 5 s poll. The page's TIMED reads skip while
  one is out (`usePaneRead` `inFlight`; a press or a reply's end still reads
  at once). e2e adds 1024 × 768, the arrow keys and typing into the box.
  Pins: `tests/test_build_code_panes_review_v1414.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-panes-review-v1414.test.tsx`; moved on
  purpose: the v1414 usage test (a FUTURE reset) and the polling test (one
  tick per act — two ticks in one synchronous jump are one read now).

- **A Build › Code reply ends only after the tool's own session id is on the
  row** (v1.414.4; three CI gates read it empty). `service._on_session_id`
  saves the id in the background (`live.id_save`), and `_end_turn` now waits
  for that save (`ID_SAVE_WAIT_S` 10 s, shielded) before stamping the row and
  emitting `done` — a read right after the reply ("Open in terminal", a
  reopen) used to find "". Pin: `tests/test_build_app_id_save_v14144.py` (the
  save held back 0.8 s; red without the wait).

- **A Build › Code tool signed out on this PC is still offered when an
  Iron-Proxy account can run it** (v1.414.2, a USER BUG: "it states that I'm
  signed out of Claude, but I am signed in"). `signed_in` on GET
  /build/app/harnesses is THIS PC's own login only (`cli_auth` on the default
  home); the user's sessions ran on the Iron-Proxy account TSI. The dashboard's
  `lib/buildApp.toolUnavailable` greyed the tool out on `signedIn === false`
  alone; it now keeps the tool available when any of its Iron-Proxy accounts
  is usable (`accountStatusWords(...).usable`). A start with no pick already
  went to Iron-Proxy's first free account (`service._resolve_account` →
  `pane_accounts.resolve`). Pin:
  `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-signin-accounts-v1414-2.test.ts` (red on the
  old rule).

- **A Build › Code reply ends on the answer to ITS message, never on earlier
  work** (v1.414.0, a USER BUG: "0 in 0 out, it didn't work at all"). The
  live process still held background agents a usage limit had stopped; on the
  user's next message Claude Code first emitted two `result` frames for that
  earlier work (0 tokens, before saying it STARTED on the message), the reply
  ended on the first, and the real answer streamed in after "done". The real
  CLI says `command_lifecycle` queued → started → … → result for a message
  (measured on claude 2.1.311): `ClaudeDriver` keeps the turn's own uuid
  (`_turn_mid`) and ignores a `result` while that message has not
  `started` (only when lifecycle is known and nothing is being interrupted);
  a message `cancelled` before it started ends the reply as interrupted.
  An older CLI with no lifecycle is unchanged. Pin:
  `tests/test_build_app_leftover_result_v1414.py` (2 of 3 red on the old
  code).

- **A coding session's Changes tab is ONE scrolling review, and every file,
  hunk, step and finding opens in VS Code / Cursor at its line — the daemon
  builds the editor's URL, never a client** (v1.405.0; the user: "Live where
  the code is … Claude Code takes one press"). DAEMON: `harness/code_editor.py`
  + `daemon/routes/editor.py` (registered after build_attempts). `GET
  /editor/options` → `{editors: [{id, label, installed}], preferred,
  preferred_label, setting, known, note}`; detection reads
  `HKEY_CLASSES_ROOT\vscode|cursor` (a URL protocol whose open command's
  program EXISTS — a stale key is not installed), cached `CACHE_S` 300, never
  raises, `None` ("not known") off Windows or on an unexpected registry error;
  `preferred`: "auto" = VS Code if installed else Cursor and never a guess
  when nothing is known, a named editor stands unless KNOWN not installed.
  `POST /editor/open {path, line?, column?, editor?}` (sync def: off the loop)
  checks `check_position` (whole numbers, 1..10,000,000 / 1..100,000; a bool
  is not a number), then `check_path`: a network share or device spelling
  (`\\host`, `//host`, `\\?\`, `\\.\`) is refused BEFORE the file policy
  (which resolves paths — an editor opening a model-written UNC path would
  send a sign-in hash out, the v1.379.0 lesson), then absolute, the protected
  roots (`NOT_ALLOWED`), the folder limits (the policy's own sentence), an
  existing FILE (`MISSING` 404 / `A_FOLDER`). REVIEW FIXES: before the file
  policy, `_walk_links` follows every symlink/junction ONE HOP AT A TIME with
  `lstat` + `readlink` (never `resolve`/`realpath`) and refuses a link whose
  target is `\\host`, `UNC\` (also after `\\?\` / `\??\`), a device or a
  non-drive long path (`NETWORK_PATH`), an unreadable, drive-relative or
  rooted-no-drive target or more than `MAX_LINK_HOPS` 40 (`UNREADABLE_LINK`);
  a `*.code-workspace` (by its own name or the name a link leads to) is
  refused (`WORKSPACE_FILE` — the editor would load its settings and tasks);
  `_workspace_name` reads a name the way Windows opens it (a `:stream`
  suffix and trailing dots/spaces dropped) and the check runs on the path's
  OWN name, the link's destination AND its 8.3 long name (`_long_name`,
  `GetLongPathNameW`, after the walk) — a link NAMED `x.code-workspace`
  passed the first review round (second review);
  then `editor_url`:
  `<scheme>://file/<path>:<line>:<col>` — forward slashes, `quote(safe="/:")`
  (space, `#`, `%`, `?`, non-ASCII encoded; the drive colon kept), ALWAYS a
  line and column (1 by default) so a name ending `:<digits>` is never a line;
  launched by module-level `_launch_url` (the `_open_native` pattern — tests
  spy it, nothing here ever opens a real editor). Every refusal is one plain
  sentence (`EditorRefusal`). Setting `code_editor` (auto | vscode | cursor,
  allow tier, a schema row after `build_auto_resume`; Config field tolerant:
  an unknown value reads "auto"), read live. DASHBOARD: `lib/codeEditor.ts`
  (`readEditorOptions` reads FRESH on every `SessionEditorProvider` mount —
  only a read in flight is shared, nothing is kept: a read kept for the
  window's life named VS Code after the setting moved to Cursor —, and every
  press sends `editor: <preferred>` so the label and the editor that opens
  never differ; `EditorContext` —
  `components/harness/OpenInEditor.SessionEditorProvider` wraps
  `HarnessSession` and lends `{folder, label, open}` only when an editor is
  preferred AND the folder is not on a network share (`folderOnShare`: `\\` or
  `//` — the daemon refuses every network path, so the presses would be
  dead), so the CHAT PAGE, a share and a PC with no editor render no press
  anywhere; a press's accessible name STARTS with its visible words
  ("Open at line 12, open cart.py at line 12 in VS Code"); `resolveInFolder` (never a URL/scheme, a share, a device
  path, `C:foo`, a `***` mask; a relative path joined to the folder and never
  `..` out of it; an absolute path kept for a STEP, confined to the folder
  for a FINDING); `stepEditorTarget` (read_file/write_file/edit_file/
  multi_edit/apply_patch/notebook_edit from live args or the saved redacted
  `detail.input` cut at 300 chars — a value whose closing quote was cut, or a
  patch header whose newline was cut, opens nothing; a read's `offset` is the
  line. The daemon makes that possible: `chat_turn._args_line` puts the path
  keys FIRST for `PATH_FIRST_TOOLS` (write_file, edit_file, multi_edit,
  notebook_edit — sorted keys put `path` after `content`/`edits`/`new`/
  `new_source` and the cut lost it), nested values still sorted; every other
  tool's line is byte-identical `sort_keys` and `prior_tool_result_entry`
  uses the same helper; fixture `dashboard/__tests__/fixtures/
  editor-step-inputs-v1405.json` is recorded from the REAL `step_detail`
  (`IJ_REGEN_EDITOR_STEPS=1`)); `findEditorRefs` (a TIGHT `path:line`,
  `path:line:col`, `path#L12` pattern: a separator or a source extension,
  never the tail of a word or a URL; never a product name (`Node.js:18`), a
  last segment with no letter (`2024/10/30:12`), a schemeless web host
  (`github.com/…#L4`), or the TAIL of a path with a space — inside backticks
  or quotes a ref must start the span, in plain text it must not follow a
  word that is itself a path; plain words before a path are not part of it,
  so plain `my project/app.py:12` links `project/app.py:12`)). `components/harness/ChangesReview.tsx` serves BOTH Changes views
  (`HarnessPanes.SessionChanges` with the session's own `undoFor`/`onUndo` —
  the receipt's undo, never a second path — and `hint`; `HarnessBranch`
  with NO undo): every file's diff inline (no press, no dialog), a sticky
  header (status, path, counts, changed since / undone / binary / note, Open
  in <editor> at the first hunk's NEW line — none for a deleted file —, Undo,
  Reviewed), "Open at line N" on each hunk (`hunkChangeLines` over
  `parseUnified`'s rows: the hunk's FIRST CHANGE — an added row's own new
  number, a removal's place in the new file — never the header's start,
  which is up to three context lines above it; `firstNewLine` the same for
  the header press; through `UnifiedRows`/`SplitRows`'s new `hunkAction`;
  DiffView itself draws exactly as before), "N files changed +A −R · M of N reviewed", Collapse
  reviewed / Expand all / Wide view (a `Modal`, side by side from `md`).
  Reviewed marks: `lib/changesReview.ts`, localStorage
  `ij.build.reviewed.<session>` = `path + NUL + diffHash` (FNV-1a over status,
  added, removed, the cut flag, a binary file's `size` and the diff — the text
  alone missed a change past the 200 KB cut and every binary change; both
  daemon views put `size` on a binary row: `chat_changes._one_change`,
  `branches._new_file_change` / `branch_changes`), ≤ `MAX_MARKS` 1000 — a
  file whose change moves is unreviewed by itself; a reviewed file folds;
  each Reviewed box is named "Reviewed: <path>". `ReviewFile` is
  `memo` with plain-value props (the undo match as `undoActionId`/
  `undoable`/`undoReason`) and callbacks made once (`useCallback`; the
  caller's `onUndo`/`onUndone` read through refs), `firstNewLine` in
  `useMemo` — marking one file reviewed redraws that file only. Bounded: `REVIEW_ROWS_PER_FILE` 400 + "Show
  all N lines" (to DiffView's `MAX_DIFF_ROWS`), and from `LAZY_FROM_FILES` 8
  files a file's rows draw only near the screen (IntersectionObserver on the
  nearest scroll box; none → everything draws). WorkLine's `ToolRow`/`StepRow`
  wrap a file step in `work-row-with-open` + `StepOpenInEditor` (hover-shown,
  always on touch) ONLY when the context exists; a running or failed step has
  none. A helper's report (`SettledHelperRow`, the reviewer's findings among
  them) renders through `LinkedRefs`. A wrapping flex header must not use
  `flex-1` (basis 0) for the item that holds a path: the first cut squeezed
  the path and the summary to nothing at 320 px — `grow basis-[14rem]`, and
  the real-browser spec measures it. Moved on purpose:
  `ij-harness-panes-v1369` (the pane shows `changes-review`, not the quiet
  `changed-files` line). Pins: `tests/test_code_editor_v1405.py` (74; 13 +
  6 review mutations, all red), `dashboard/__tests__/review-pane-v1405.test.tsx`
  (65) + `review-pane-memo-v1405.test.tsx` (2; counts draws and parses, never
  times) (23 + 19 review mutations, all red), e2e
  `dashboard/e2e/review-pane.spec.ts` (3: 1280 ×
  800 and 390 × 844, a step and a finding; the editor routes stubbed in the
  browser, never the scratch daemon's). OPEN: an editor
  really opening was never driven (no test may launch one); a binary file
  rewritten at the SAME size keeps its Reviewed mark (only the size is
  hashed); in plain prose (no quotes) the words before a path with a space
  are not part of it, so `my project/app.py:12` links `project/app.py:12`;
  VS Code's own first-time confirmation dialog is not something the app
  can answer (the Handbook says so); the Branch view's
  reviewed marks share the session's key; `notebook_edit` opens the notebook
  file (no cell line).

- **A grant in a coding session is the SESSION's, not one helper's**
  (v1.404.1, a USER BUG: "it keeps asking for approval. it should be one
  approval per command. It stalled out 4 times" — the live record showed four
  helper runs of ONE coding session each parked on its own card for running
  a command, one for 11 hours, each answered "Allow for this run", which was
  kept only on that helper's row). `harness/session_grants.py` is the one
  place: `note(thread, names)` keeps a grant for the session in this process
  (widened by `widen`: `COMMAND_FAMILY` = shell, process_start,
  process_write — running a command is ONE approval), `persist` writes it to
  `setup.granted_tools` through `sessions.add_granted_tools` (read and
  written under `CONVERSATION_WRITE_LOCK`), and `release_helpers` answers the
  session's OTHER live helpers' covered cards with "conversation" (actor
  "grant"), each by its own approval id. Callers: the runtime's
  `_pause_for_approval` "conversation" branch for a HELPER run
  (`_build_helper_thread`: origin `job:build-helper` + the `build_session`
  run option), and the stream lane's "conversation" branch for the session's
  own card (`card_grants` only — a grant never ARMS). Readers:
  `chat_turn.helper_parent_now` (helpers started later; the narrowing after
  it still applies — a deny, Local only, a night task; `always_ask` hands
  down nothing) and `start_build_turn`'s `granted_tools` (later replies). A
  card answered ONCE EVERY TIME (`once_only`, a `cost_note`) is never woken —
  and the runtime's within-run `_release_siblings` now skips them too (it
  woke a once-only sibling by tool name: a `git push` is a `shell` call). The
  helper card says "Allow for this session". NEVER SHARED: app (MCP) tools
  (one `mcp_call` key covers every pack), quarantined tools, once-only and
  priced cards; nothing goes to or from a helper under `always_ask`, Local
  only, low trust or a folder rule that asks (`shareable`). A shell grant in
  the POST lane and `helper_parent_now` arrives as all three command tools
  (`test_harness_helpers_v1372` `COMMAND_GRANT`, moved on purpose). Pins:
  `tests/test_session_grants_v14041.py` (25 mutations, 23 red; the two
  survivors are redundant defences: the origin check in
  `_build_helper_thread` (`shares(child_parent(...))` is False off the live
  registry) and `shareable` inside `_covered` (`release_helpers` already
  filters by it)); moved on purpose: `ij-harness-command-shell-v1381` and
  `ij-harness-helpers-v1372` ("for this session").

- **A coding session's PULL REQUEST is watched; a failed check is fixed BY
  THE SESSION, reviewed by a CLOUD reviewer, and only an approved fix is
  pushed — the ONE push without a card** (v1.407.0 part 3; the user's
  decision of 2026-10-29). `harness/ci_fix.py` (`CiWatchRecord`, a late model
  in `_LATE_MODEL_MODULES`; `read_pr` = `gh pr view <branch> --json
  …headRefOid,baseRefName,statusCheckRollup`; `failed_logs` = `gh run view
  <id> --log-failed`, the END of each run kept, MASKED before the cut, ≤ 3
  runs / 20,000 chars; `fix_note` promptguard-scans and `wrap_untrusted`s it;
  `change_for_review` = `git diff <failing head>` + new files, masked;
  `review_task` = `finish.review_brief` + `VERDICT_ASK`; `pick_reviewer`;
  `commit_fix` = the night shift's SCREENED commit; `move_to_side`,
  `land_side` (`merge --ff-only`, never a reset), `push_refusal` +
  `push_fix`) and `daemon/routes/ci_watch.py` (`ci_loop` =
  `bg_tasks["ci_watch"]`, `_tick("ci_watch")`, `TICK_S` 20 + `wake_ci_loop`;
  one pass at a time per platform, ONE step per session at a time — an
  asyncio lock per thread; `GET /build/sessions/{id}/ci`). STATES watching →
  fix_due → fixing → reviewing → (watching after a push | fix_due on a side
  branch) and the rests passing / gave_up / asking / stopped / closed. A
  check still running is read again after `POLL_FIRST_S` 60 s × 1.5 up to
  `POLL_MAX_S` 600 s, never past `WATCH_MAX_S` 6 h; `READ_FAILS_MAX` 5 failed
  reads stop the watch in words. Candidates: Build threads, not archived,
  `setup.git.pr_url` set, not merged/removed. THE FIX TURN:
  `start_build_turn(continuation=True, started_by=night.BY_CI, model_note=)`
  — the session's own model, posture, account pin, hold, Local only and trust;
  NEVER while the session is busy (`_STARTING`, `_COMPACTING`, a running
  turn) and NOTHING is read from GitHub meanwhile; Emergency stop: nothing is
  read or started (`_check`/`_start_fix`), `start_build_turn` refuses "ci"
  (`UNATTENDED_REFUSAL` names it), a running fix turn is a listed unattended
  turn (`night.stop_unattended_turns`), and `ci_watch.stop_all` (called from
  `Orchestrator.stop_unattended`) cancels a review in flight, which rests
  STOPPED; the push step re-reads the switch right before pushing. THE REVIEW:
  `REVIEWER` (the seam; default `run_review`) = a `helpers.HelperParent(
  review_only=True, own_model=True, tools=REVIEW_TOOLS)` reviewer session
  (origin `HELPER_ORIGIN`, agent "check-fix reviewer"), `pinned`, bounded by
  `finish.REVIEW_DEADLINE_S`, verdict from `team_record.verdict_of` (LAST line;
  a runtime-written report judges nothing). `ci_reviewer` auto = claude-cli /
  codex-cli connected (`model_team.provider_ready`, cached only) and NOT the
  session's own vendor first. Local only, a low-trust fix turn, no reviewer
  connected, or the user's reply started during the review → `asking` (the fix
  stays in the folder, nothing pushed). APPROVED → screened commit, an
  approved side branch fast-forwards the PR branch, `read_pr` again (the PR
  head must still be the failing head), then `branches.push` — refused by
  `push_refusal` unless the branch is the session's own `ij/` branch, the
  open PR's `headRefName`, not `baseRefName` nor the session's base, and the
  worktree's HEAD; the session is HELD (`_STARTING` + `_GIT_ACTING`) under
  `build_git._locked`; `setup.git.pushed_at` recorded. NOT APPROVED → `git
  switch -c ij/<name>-ci-fix-<n>` from the PR branch (its ref never moves; a
  PR branch the fix committed on refuses), screened commit there, never
  pushed; the next hidden turn gets the report (`revise_note`, masked,
  scanned, fenced). `MAX_ROUNDS` 3 per failing STREAK (Iron Jarvis's own
  pushes do not reset it; checks passing or the USER's push — a `pushed_at`
  Iron Jarvis did not write — do). Merge is never automatic. VISIBLE: every
  step is an assistant line with `ci: {kind, round}` (`sessions.LINE_KEYS` and
  `lib/buildSessions` LINE_KEYS) and an event: `build.ci_step` (quiet bell
  row) or `build.ci_done` (`gave_up` | `passing`: rings, counts, a default
  alert = one phone line, `bell_pings`, main.js `RUN_OUTCOME_AUDIT_TYPES` +
  `runOutcomeNotice`, "This PC" quiet; fixture cases `ci-gave-up`,
  `ci-passing`; a Notifications page box, `test_notification_events_v14003`;
  `ALERT_PAYLOADS` in `test_integrate_wave4_v13540`); a Local only session's
  news carries no words. Settings
  `ci_autofix` (default on; ON from chat is ask-floor, `floor_when=(True,)`)
  and `ci_reviewer` (ask). OPEN: a fix turn a restart cut off is carried on
  by the resume pass under a new turn id, so the watch stops ("stopped before
  it finished") rather than reviewing it; the reviewer's account is its
  provider's normal pick (account groups are not applied); a gh log is read
  whole into memory before its tail is kept; human review comments are out
  of scope; not driven against the real GitHub or in the packaged app. Pins:
  `tests/test_ci_fix_v1406.py` (real git + a bare remote, a fake `gh` on
  PATH, real build turns and one real review run; 15 mutations, all red:
  'bad' or no verdict pushing, a verdict not on the last line, the base-branch
  guard, a force push, Local only, Emergency stop ×4, both 3-round caps, the
  side branch, the busy check, `started_by`), `dashboard/__tests__/
  ci-fix-bell-v1406.test.ts` (a step row quiet — its mutation red). The
  push step's own Emergency-stop re-read is defence in depth behind the
  post-review check (no test can reach it alone). REVIEW (15 pins red on the
  first cut, `tests/test_ci_fix_review_v1406.py`): WHAT IS PUSHED IS WHAT WAS
  READ — `ci_fix.change_fingerprint` (every file differing from the failing
  head, by its BYTES, committed or not) and HEAD are taken in the same locked
  read as the review's diff (`ReviewJob.fingerprint/head`) and must still match
  right before AND right after the commit (`changed_since_review`;
  `CHANGED_SINCE_REVIEW`): a program the fix turn left running, an editor, the
  user's Commit press or a reply that ended during the review used to be
  committed and pushed unreviewed. The verdict follows the v1.401.0 rules in
  full: never from a reviewer run that ended at LOW trust (`MT._low_trust` of
  its session) nor from a report `detect_injection` flags. `read_pr` reads
  `gh pr view <pr_url>` (by branch it could answer a fork's PR of the same
  head name) and `push_refusal` refuses an answer whose url/number is not the
  session's, or whose `headRepository`/`headRepositoryOwner` is not the
  repository the branch's remote names (fetch address, and the push address
  when it is a GitHub one; `PUSH_OTHER_REPO`). The push step re-reads the
  session: archived/removed (`GONE_WORDS`), Local only turned on, `ci_autofix`
  turned off (`OFF_WORDS`) → nothing pushed. A failed check's log (or a
  reviewer's report fed to the next round) that promptguard flags starts the
  fix turn at LOW trust (`fix_note(flagged=)`, `CiWatchRecord.due_low_trust`)
  → its fix waits for the user. A USER message after the fix turn's reply →
  `USER_REPLY_WORDS`, asking. FOLLOW-UPS (the five items the review left
  open, each closed the SAFE way — asking, never a push; 11 pins in
  `tests/test_ci_fix_followups_v1406.py`, all red on 2ca30be8, 13 mutations,
  all red): (1) THE FIX TURN'S POSTURE is the night shift's guarded one
  whatever the session's (yolo and Auto included): `start_build_turn(
  started_by="ci")` applies `night.posture_setup` for THAT turn only
  (nothing stored; "Ask before everything" keeps its stricter posture and no
  command grant) and arms a `NightTurn(task_id="", on_note=)` — the night
  allow-list (`RUNS_UNASKED`) runs unasked, everything else is carded,
  bounded by `night_card_wait_minutes` and then declined; `ci_watch.
  _note_for(turn_id)` hears each decline (`NightTurn.on_note`, a new field)
  and a declined or denied step (`deniedTools`) puts the watch in asking,
  naming it (`DECLINED_WORDS`). (2) ONLY IRON JARVIS'S OWN COMMITS between
  the failing head and what is pushed: `CiWatchRecord.own_commits_json` keeps
  every screened commit of the streak (the side-branch rounds too);
  `foreign_commits` after the land → any other commit (or a list git could
  not read) is `CI.AskUser` → asking, each named (`FOREIGN_COMMITS_WORDS`).
  (3) THE PUSH ADDRESS (`push_address_refusal`, in `push_fix`): the URL git
  REALLY pushes to (`resolved_push_url` = `git remote get-url --push`,
  `insteadOf`/`pushInsteadOf` applied — measured; THE SEAM the bare-remote
  tests replace) must be a GitHub URL on the PR's own host EXACTLY (an ssh
  alias such as `github-work`, a look-alike host, a local folder, another
  host refused) of the PR's `headRepositoryOwner/headRepository` (missing =
  `PUSH_ADDRESS_UNKNOWN`) → else asking. (4) HOOKS AND CANCEL: `CI.Kill` is
  the push step's hold — `commit_fix(on_spawn=)` (bounded `COMMIT_S`) and
  `branches.push(on_spawn=)` (new kwarg; `None` = exactly as before) publish
  their child, the async supervisor (`STOP_POLL_S` 0.25) kills it the moment
  Emergency stop is on, and a cancel (`stop_all`) kills, waits for the
  thread, then `_settle_push` writes what REALLY happened: a push that had
  begun is READ BACK (`remote_branch_sha` = `git ls-remote <push url>
  refs/heads/<branch>`) — equal to the fix's tip → recorded as pushed ("had
  already gone out"), else "stopped the push: the PR's branch still has
  commit X … not pushed", unreadable → said so; the outer cancel handler
  writes "stopped" ONLY while the record still says reviewing (never over
  the truth). (5) BINARIES: `binary_changes` (`git diff --numstat` "-",
  a new file holding a NUL; None = git could not say) — any → asking before
  the review (`BINARY_WORDS`), re-checked before the push. OPEN: a fix turn
  cut off by a restart is carried on under a new turn id by the resume pass
  without the guarded posture (its record then stops the watch); the read-back
  trusts the remote's ref name (a remote that rewrites refs is not modelled).

- **The NEXT-STEP BOX under the newest settled reply is DERIVED, never
  saved; every button is an existing action; the user's own decisions are
  only named** (v1.407.0 part 4; the user: like Claude Code desktop, a short
  "here's where we are" and one or two buttons for the obvious next move).
  `lib/nextStep.ts` (pure, table-tested): `codeNextStep(codeReplyFacts(m),
  gitFacts(view.git, branchStatus))` and `chatNextStep(chatReplyFacts(m),
  {connected})` → `{recap, actions ≤ MAX_NEXT_ACTIONS (2)}` or null. Recap from
  the record only: `documents` count, the finish record (tests via
  `finishWarn`, the team review's verdict, `unmet`), `stepLimit` / `capMet`,
  the branch (`GET /build/sessions/{id}/git`, read by HarnessSession only for
  a LIVE branch and only shown after a reply that changed something), the
  closing offer. NO box for a plain answer, a failed / account-limit /
  declined reply (its own line is the next step) — never filler. Buttons, in
  order: Keep going (the step-limit / cap line's OWN press moved here — the
  line keeps its words, the button keeps its old test id
  `harness-keep-going` / `harness-cap-keep-going`; never drawn twice), Fix
  the failing tests, Fix what the reviewer found, Yes, do it (sends
  `YES_TEXT` "Yes, go ahead." through the page's own send), the user-owned
  offer (`fill_offer`: fills the box with "Yes, <action>."; a commit / PR
  offer on a live branch maps to the Commit / Create PR press), Commit
  (uncommitted), Open a pull request (committed, no PR). A "send" is the
  host's ordinary send (HarnessSession `send({text})`, the chat page's
  `send(text)`), so every card / grant / once-only rule applies to the turn
  it starts; Commit / PR go HarnessSession `branchPress` → HarnessPanes
  (opens Changes, holds `pendingPress` until made) → ChangesPane (Branch
  scope) → BranchView `press` → its own `startCommit` / `startPr` (the form
  waits; nothing is committed by the box), made once per `n` (`onPressed`
  clears it so a remount never presses twice). THE OFFER READING is the
  daemon's: `harness/closing_offer.offer_kind` ("work" | "user" | "") and
  `offered_action` (new, `ends_on_offer` = `offer_kind == "work"`, unchanged)
  with a TS twin `lib/closingOffer.ts` whose PATTERNS block is GENERATED from
  the compiled Python patterns (`scripts/gen_closing_offer_ts.py`, `--check`
  in `tests/test_closing_offer_twin_v1406.py`) and held equal on every v1.402.0
  table row via `dashboard/__tests__/fixtures/closing-offer-v1406.json`
  (`IJ_REGEN_CLOSING_OFFER=1`). An outward action asked with no offer shape
  ("Should I deploy it?") reads "" — no button, the safe side. COMPONENT:
  `components/chat/NextStepBox.tsx` (calm: the 13px quiet line + the
  step-limit line's pills, no box style); the recap and buttons step aside
  while the user types (`hidden`; harness `input`, chat `ChatNextStepBox` →
  `useComposerEmpty` — the PAGE never subscribes to the composer), and the
  model follow-ups (`chat_followups` chips / the harness `harness-next-steps`
  strip) render INSIDE it as `children` and stay while typing, as before; the
  chat's ConnectedElsewhere moved out of MessageRow into the box (its own
  words and Continue). The chat box renders at page level after the
  transcript for `messages[last]` only (never for runResult / agent / panel /
  phone-thread replies, never while `failedTurn` — the failure line's Retry
  is that next step). Pins: `dashboard/__tests__/next-step-v1406.test.tsx`
  (the state table on real recorded lines — the Timeline fixture's saved
  line, the team seams' finish — plus the harness screen),
  `next-step-chat-v1406.test.tsx`, `closing-offer-twin-v1406.test.ts`,
  `tests/test_closing_offer_twin_v1406.py` (21 mutations, all red). REVIEW:
  the daemon's `user_owns` was written for the NUDGE (a miss there only lets
  the model carry on), and it reads "git push --force", "transfer the
  funds", "rm -rf …", "wipe the logs", "run the deploy script", "cancel the
  subscription" as plain work — on the box that was ONE press of "Yes, do
  it". `nextStep.asksFirst` (ASKS_FIRST_ANYWHERE / ASKS_FIRST_VERB, over the
  offer's last sentence, dashboard only — the daemon's reading and its nudge
  are unchanged) turns those into the user's call (named, fill); a false hit
  costs one press. Also: "Yes, do it" pressed mid-upload puts its words in
  the box first (the queued send reads the box; the file went out alone),
  and the coding box is not drawn while `view.running` / `view.planRun` (an
  approved plan's act turn about to start). OPEN: the
  chat's own Continue on a cut-off reply and RunResultCard's Keep going stay
  where they were (not in the box); a failed chat turn shows no box (its
  Retry line is the one place); an older reply's offer is never read.

- **The coding sessions are the CHAT page's Code view; Build is the
  terminals; the sidebar is five rooms** (v1.407.0 part 1 + 5; the user's
  decision 2026-10-29: "the chat will contain the box at the top for chat vs

  `chat-view-code`, arrow keys. CHAT stays MOUNTED behind Code (`hidden` +

  gives up the sidebar slot (`chatSlot = surfaceHidden ? null : …`);
  `DockAsk`'s window key handler ignores a card under `[inert]` (Enter in
  Code must never press a hidden chat's Allow). CODE
  (`components/harness/CodeView.tsx`, dynamic, browser only) MOUNTS when

  survives through `lib/turnCache`, a session's terminal through `paneHost`.
  CodeView lends what Build lent: `/terminals` + the activity poll for the

  `&night=<date>` (`/` reads the same query); the address wins, then this
  WINDOW's memory (module state for the first frame + sessionStorage
  `ij.chat.view`, written only for Code), then Chat; any chat link param
  (`CHAT_LINK_PARAMS`: thread, ask, new, skill, persona, model, about,
  project) means Chat. `lib/buildLinks.buildSessionHref` / `lastNightHref`
  are THE producers (bell, Running now, Safety checks, Time travel,
  missionLinks); desktop/main.js spells the same address (ask watcher, the

  `?session=` / `?night=` on with `addressMove.replace` (a plain location
  replace — the page has no router, and ~30 Build tests mock no
  next/navigation) and draws `build-moved` meanwhile. Same-page links:
  `announceBuildSessionLink` fires on CHAT paths now (never Build),
  `announceChatViewLink` (Ctrl K's "Code" row, `CHAT_VIEW_EVENT`),
  `NEW_CHAT_EVENT` brings Chat to the front. BUILD has no switch; a saved
  `ij.build.shape` of anything opens Terminals. WORDS (`lib/buildWords`):
  `CHAT_VIEW_NAME`/`CODE_VIEW_NAME` "Chat"/"Code", `CODING_VIEW_NAME` =
  "Code", `CODING_SESSIONS_LIST` "Coding sessions" (the sidebar heading in

  "Chat › Code", `BUILD_BLURB` the terminals only, `CHAT_BLURB`,
  `CODE_BLURB`; "Your coding session finished", "A coding session is
  waiting for your OK" etc. replace "Build session" in the bell, main.js and

  equal); the coding aliases moved from Build to Chat in `lib/nav`; Ctrl K
  has a "Code" row (`lib/surfaces` EXTRA entry, `primary`, never on

  Everything, Settings (the T1 cap is five now); `SIDEBAR_EXCLUDED` names
  the automation/admin rooms that must stay off it (each still on

  `dashboard/__tests__/{chat-code-switch,chat-code-reply-survives,sidebar-rooms}-v1406.test.tsx`,
  e2e `dashboard/e2e/chat-code-switch.spec.ts` (1280 × 800, 390 × 844);

  `ux-wave3-build-files-terminals-v1315`, `ij-harness-sidebar-v1369`,

  a page-wide file drop (still `preventDefault`, so the browser never opens
  the file — it attached files dropped on a coding session to the hidden

- **Two coding sessions SIDE BY SIDE: one shared pane column, keys stay in
  the focused column, both are looked at** (v1.407.0 part 7; the user: "two
  sessions side by side", like Claude Code desktop's split view).
  `lib/codeSplit.ts` is the ONE place for the rules (pure, import-free):
  `CodeSplit {left, right, focus}`; `openSession` (one of the two → FOCUS it,
  else replace the FOCUSED column's), `openBeside` (right column, focused),
  `closeSide` (back to one, the other kept), `dropSession`; at most two.
  ADDRESS `/chat?view=code&session=<a>&beside=<b>` (`splitFromSearch`,
  `writeSplitToAddress`; a `beside` counts only with the session the page
  was opened with; `writeViewToAddress("chat")` drops it too); WINDOW MEMORY
  module state + sessionStorage `ij.code.split`, written ONLY for a split
  (one column keeps the old `LAST_SESSION_KEY` path byte for byte —
  `__tests__/setup.ts` resets it like turnCache, and a pin checks a return to
  Code WITHOUT that reset). VIEW (`IjHarnessView`): ONE structure for one or
  two columns, each column div keyed by its SESSION id (opening beside never
  remounts the first; closing the left keeps the right's instance), carrying
  `data-split-column="left|right"` + `data-focused` only when there are two;
  focus = the last pointerdown / focusin in a column (`focusSide` returns
  the same object when unchanged, so a click re-renders nothing). PANES: one
  shared column (`harness-split-panes`, 300/360 px, "Views for <name>"):
  `HarnessSession.panesInto` — absent = its own aside as before, null =
  none, an element = `createPortal` there; only the focused column passes
  the element (two pane sets at 1440 px left each conversation ~250 px).
  NARROW (`SPLIT_MIN_WIDTH` 1280 = the harness's own xl, where a single
  session already stacks its panes): one column + `harness-split-tabs`; the
  other column stays MOUNTED, `hidden` + `inert`, `onScreen={false}` (its
  reply streams on, it is not "looked at", no panes). KEYS: `ownsKeys(el,
  target)` — a key belongs to its target's column, else the
  `data-focused="true"` one; a column under `[inert]` owns none; an element
  in no column owns every key exactly as before. `useHarnessKeys(handlers,
  state, owner)` (HarnessSession passes its root) and DockAsk's window
  handler both ask it — two sessions both registered Esc/Ctrl+L/Ctrl+K and
  both docks answered Enter before. LOOKING: `lib/buildWatching` holds a SET
  (`here`), the note carries `threads` (and `thread` = the first, for an
  older window), `stopLooking(thread)` takes back only that session's note
  (no argument = all) — a column's cleanup must never clear the other's.
  WAYS IN: the row's `harness-open-beside-<id>` (hover / focus / touch),
  Shift+Enter on a row, dragging a row (`SESSION_DRAG_TYPE`) onto
  `harness-drop-beside` (wide windows only). Pins:
  `dashboard/__tests__/code-split-v1406.test.tsx` (rules + the view with a
  marker session), `code-split-live-v1406.test.tsx` (two REAL sessions: two
  streams, 60 words re-render the view ≤ 1 time, Esc / Enter scoped, both
  looked at, narrow tab looked at), e2e `dashboard/e2e/code-split.spec.ts`
  (1600 × 900, 1280 × 800, 390 × 844); 24 mutations, all red. Moved on
  purpose: `ij-harness-phone-unit-v1369` (the panes are `panesEl`).
  REVIEW (each red first): (1) a card in the OTHER column took the caret
  out of the box the user was typing in (DockAsk's "the composer had it"
  matched ANY `[data-dock-composer]`), so the next letter picked its choice
  and Enter its Allow — the grab and the give-back now ask `ownsKeys`
  (`columnRef` keeps the column after the card goes). (2) The chat kept
  behind Code still answered its WINDOW listeners (part 1): a file dropped on
  a session's box was ALSO uploaded into the hidden chat (and made it a work
  folder), and "Switch model" (`ij:open-switcher`, Ctrl K / the demo strip)
  opened the hidden chat's menu portaled over Code — `surfaceHiddenRef`
  gates both; the focused session's own menu opens instead (HarnessSession
  listens, `ownsKeys`). (3) The narrow tabs name their panels
  (`aria-controls` ⇄ `role="tabpanel"`). (4) e2e: the account-limit rejoin
  stub matched `/stream` while the page asks `/stream?ids=1` (since
  v1.384.0; a string route matches the query too) — not a product bug; the
  model-menu e2e scopes "Switch model" to `harness-session` (the hidden
  chat's chip stays in the DOM). Pins: `code-split-live-v1406` (+3),
  `chat-code-hidden-window-v1406`.

- **A coding session's SESSION tab reads what the app already knows; its one
  new read is the session's background programs** (v1.404.0; the user, beside
  a screenshot of Claude Code's session panel: "add a sessions rail … next to
  the other icons … and have it display when selected information like the
  last attachment"). `HarnessPanes.PANE_TABS` gains `session` (SquareMenu)
  after Files; `components/harness/HarnessSessionRail.tsx` is mounted only on
  its tab. Readings live in `lib/sessionRail.ts` (pure): `lastAttachment` =
  the NEWEST user message (never a steer note or the daemon's `continuation`)
  carrying `attachmentPaths` / `filePaths` (a `/` skill ALONE is not an
  attachment — it buried the upload), read from the SHOWN conversation
  HarnessSession passes (`messages={shown}`: the question being sent counts at
  once); `earlierAttachments` lists older uploads each once; `openablePath` =
  `openWayFor`'s rule (a local file, never an .svg) and the daemon's
  `/documents/open` still decides. Context is `latestContext` (the daemon's
  reading; nothing before the first reply), HIDDEN when a Compact summary is
  newer (ContextMeter's rule), warning tone = `offersCompact`; the changes
  count reuses `fetchTurnChangeSet` with the session's `build:<id>` journal,
  re-read every `CHANGES_POLL_MS` while busy, and "See changes" opens the
  Changes pane's "This session" scope (`initialScope`, cleared by any tab
  press). DAEMON: `GET /build/sessions/{id}/programs` →
  `harness/process.program_rows` (the session's `process_start` programs,
  newest first, keys `{id, command, running, left_running, stopped,
  exit_code, ran_s, started_s}` — never output, never a pid; the command
  split quote-aware (`shlex.split(posix=False)`) and through
  `mcp/secrets.split_args` (the value after `--api-key`/`--token`) then
  `failure_words.mask_failure_text`, BEFORE the cut to one line of
  `SHOWN_COMMAND_CHARS`, cached on `ManagedProcess.shown`) plus `others` =
  `procs.kept_running` (kept jobs with a live WINDOWLESS member — exactly what
  `end_session` stops; a window a command opened is spared and not counted).
  `left_running`: the command ended but a member of its Job still runs
  (`start /b node server.js`) — `running` stays true and Stop reaches it
  (`_left_running`; the review found the first cut said "Ended (exit 0)" with
  no Stop and the route did nothing). Members within `LEFT_GRACE_S` (1 s) of
  the command's end do not count: conhost.exe stays a member for some 10-40 ms
  after EVERY command (measured, 19 runs in 20). `POST …/programs/{pid}/stop`
  → `stop_program` (the tool's own `_stop`, off the loop via the sync route)
  and the program STAYS LISTED as stopped — `process_stop` removes it, but the
  user's press must not, or the model's next `process_read` says "no
  program". With NO Job and the command gone, a tree kill walks from a dead
  pid: if the pipe is still held after `_stop`, it raises `CouldNotStop` → 409
  in words, never a claimed stop (a Job's terminate is trusted). `_reader` stamps `ended_at` at EOF (bounded wait), so an unread
  program's "ran for" is true. A helper's programs run under the helper's run
  and are not listed. The tab re-reads programs every `PROGRAMS_POLL_MS` only
  while something runs or the session works (`useVisibleInterval`); a read
  that fails (not a 404) is SAID, never "nothing running"; a failed Stop keeps
  its own error. Pins: `tests/test_session_rail_programs_v1404.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/session-rail-v1404.test.tsx`, e2e
  `dashboard/e2e/session-rail.spec.ts` (1280 × 800, 390 × 844: a real picture
  loads, nothing wider than the pane); moved on purpose: the tab lists in
  `ij-harness-panes-v1369`, `harness-timeline-live-v1390`,
  `plan-row-effort-pane-icons-v1401-2` and its e2e.

- **A CODING SESSION CAN HAVE A TEAM: the lead plans and checks and NEVER
  edits; workers do the code; the reviewer checks every change; a lead that
  had to do a piece itself SAYS SO** (v1.403.0; the user's decision after a
  Try on run felt like "models unsure of their duties" — and the bench's 0 of
  108 showed asking does not move a planner, so the roles are ENFORCED).
  DATA: `setup.team {workers: [member names] (1..2), reviewer}` (members are
  model-team members; `POST /team/members/from-model {provider, model, role
  worker|reviewer}` makes or REUSES one, never clobbering it — a worker gets
  code+test+read, a reviewer review), validated by `harness/team.validate`
  (unknown, > 2, no Writing code, no Reviewing, the same member twice, a
  COORDINATOR as a worker — the router never hands it a piece), folder
  default `BuildFolderRecord.team_json` + `GET|PUT /build/folders/team`
  (`folder=` empty → 200 null; keyed on the PICKED folder, never the
  worktree), the view's `team` from CACHED states only. THE LOCK:
  `start_build_turn` arms `harness/team.SESSION_TEAM`; ONE shared builder
  (`chat_turn._build_harness`, both lanes) resolves it and `mode.build(lead=
  True)` arms the lead with `team.lead_tools` (no file-changing or running
  tool, app tools only when declared read-only; plan mode and text-only picks
  never lock; no helper door → never locked); the dialect shows exactly what
  is armed. ROUTING: role pieces from delegate / spawn_agent / the dialects'
  helper tools go ONLY to the session's workers (code/test/read — any worker
  takes any of the three: `team_takes_role` via `helpers.team_route_kwargs`)
  or its reviewer (review); a name off the team is refused in one sentence;
  workers run on their own model with `worker_rules()`. THE EXCEPTION: a
  ROUTER REFUSAL of a worker piece (never a failed run, a review, a low-trust
  turn) unlocks the coding tools for the REST OF THAT REPLY
  (`apply_team_unlock`), the refusal carries `UNLOCK_NOTE`, and the record's
  `lead_did` keeps the router's reason; a team with NO worker left runs
  unlocked and records `NO_WORKERS_LEFT` (`team.gone_record`,
  `turn.team_gone`) — never a silent solo. Codex: a parked thread whose tool
  names differ is a miss (`codex_native.adapter._same_tools`), so an
  unlocked lead gets its new tools. THE REVIEWER: the finish review runs for a
  session with a reviewer WITHOUT `team_first`, routed only to it, and once
  more after the lead's workers fixed what it found (`finish.review.rounds`
  1|2, never 3); finish tests come from this reply's workers' `shell` runs,
  and `round_gate` lets ONLY the check's own unsettled carrier calls (and its
  pending review) through the lead's gate. RECORD: `team {lead, workers,
  reviewer, pieces, lead_did}` LAST on the done frame / POST answer / reply
  line (`LINE_KEYS`). SCREEN: `components/harness/HarnessTeam.tsx` (Team chip
  in `HARNESS_FIT_ORDER` after tryon; panel picks from the Try on provider
  groups; folder default; 404 hides it), `lib/sessionTeam.ts` (decoders,
  `helperTeamName` "Worker …"/"Reviewer …" only in a team turn, the turn's
  team captured when the screen starts it), `TeamReplyLine` (names via
  `friendlyModelName`, never ids; `lead_did` in the warn tone, never folded).
  Try on runs each model ALONE (it never erases `setup.team`). BENCH: a team
  file's `[planner] team = true` runs a locked lead ("lead (locked)"). SEAM:
  `tests/test_session_team_seams_v1403.py` records the real daemon's answers
  to `dashboard/__tests__/fixtures/session-team-seams-v1403.json`
  (`IJ_REGEN_TEAM_SEAMS=1`) and `session-team-seams-v1403.test.tsx` decodes
  them. OPEN: the router's burst guard and 80% new-work mark also count as
  "no worker could take it"; PATCH does not check cloud members against a
  Local only session (the turn refuses); a reply resumed after a restart is
  locked again; Codex's write_stdin / Claude's BashOutput are shown to the lead
  but only read; a team helper row names its model twice. Pins:
  `tests/test_session_team{,_routes,_bench,_seams,_gone}_v1403.py` (+ the part
  D review pins), `dashboard/__tests__/session-team{,-seams}-v1403.test.tsx`.

- **TEAM REVIEW AT THE FINISH: Iron Jarvis hands the change to the team's
  reviewer ITSELF; the reviewer only reads, is bounded, and its verdict is
  the report's last line** (v1.401.0; the user's decision after a Sonnet
  planner told "work as a team" handed the team 0 pieces in 108 bench runs —
  soft words do not move it). With `team_first` on, a member holding the
  Review role, `delegate` in the turn's gate, not low trust and the folder's
  `finish.toml` not saying `review = false`, `FinishCheck.prepare` adds ONE
  synthetic `delegate` call (`REVIEW_CALL_ID` "ij-finish-review", role review,
  `called_as` "team review", in `HARNESS_CALLED_AS`) AFTER the test calls of
  its round, so the team router, the helper row, Stop and MAX_HELPERS all
  apply. The brief (`_review_brief`, off the loop): request + the coder's
  draft + `change_diff` (journal before-bytes vs disk, per-file 8k / total
  24k, files past the cap named, command-changed files by name), MASKED
  BEFORE every cut and promptguard-scanned. READ-ONLY IS A GUARANTEE:
  `HelperParent.review_only` → `helper_definition` gives only the turn's
  `REVIEW_TOOLS` (read_file/glob/grep/list_files) whatever the member's own
  roster or base; run option `review_only` → the runtime arms no notebook and
  never decomposes. BOTH lanes (MIRROR NOTE) pass `review_only` and
  `deadline_s=review_deadline(...)` (`REVIEW_DEADLINE_S` 600; past it the
  call is cancelled as Stop cancels it, `helpers._bounded` answers
  `data.state` `timed_out`, only on the bound's own expiry) for that call
  only, and keep it out of `tools_used` / `last_tool_output`. The verdict is
  `team_record.verdict_of` (last line only), never from a low-trust child, a
  runtime-written report, or a report the injection detector flags (that one
  is withheld by the fence and its verdict cleared). Record `finish.review
  {state done|failed|not_run|stopped, member, model, verdict
  approved|changes_needed|"", why?}` whitelisted by `saved_review` and the
  dashboard's `decodeFinishReview`; the work line says "reviewed by X: …" /
  "review failed|stopped|not run". The bench splits `planner_handed` from
  `finish_reviewed` (`FINISH_REVIEW_ID` == `REVIEW_CALL_ID`), so "The planner
  handed …" stays true. OPEN: a folder "ask" rule on `delegate` cards the
  whole ~30k brief; ~30k chars per reviewed reply land in the ledger args and
  the child's task; a reviewer's account limit voids a bench run. Pins:
  `tests/test_team_review_finish{,_review,_ship}_v1401.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/team-review-finish-v1401.test.tsx`.

- **The bench's TEAM tier: six tasks that split into pieces; `--tier` takes
  a comma list** (v1.400.0; a doer + a reviewer that found 5 more defects,
  three of them hidden checks failing a correct answer). `harness_bench/
  tasks/team-*`: audit-events (rubric, 75 files / 120 writes),
  port-js (expected values from node, pinned; the check never runs node),
  spec-modules, rename-option (deprecated aliases warn once), review-patch
  (a format-patch against an older copy: 4 files need a hand merge, 3 edits
  do not match its description) and test-triage (13 bugs, 10 packages; still
  quick — measures what handing out costs). Each has a solution, a decoy,
  more decoys and ≥ 2 alternatives under `tests/fixtures/`, and
  `TEAM_HASHES` pins the content. CHECKS MUST ACCEPT WHAT A WINDOWS AGENT
  WRITES: an `answer.json` saved by PowerShell 5.1 is UTF-16 LE with a BOM
  (`json.loads` on the RAW BYTES reads UTF-8/16/32); an argparse
  `SystemExit(2)` is an exit code; a CHANGELOG names a rename in any
  spelling. `frontier-legacy-callers` and core `run_rubric` still read UTF-8
  only (a fix changes the frontier hash — the user's call). `catalog.TIERS`
  gains "team"; `catalog.tier_names` / `canonical_tier` (`team,frontier` →
  `frontier,team`; `all` beside another refused); a tier never enters a
  setup's identity (pinned), the refine key spells a multi-tier selection
  one way and keeps single tiers and `all` unchanged; `--tasks` beside
  `--tier` keeps working (the first cut reused a variable and emptied it —
  81 red). The audit repo, the patch and its fixtures regenerate
  byte-identical from `scripts/bench_task_generators/` (not shipped, not
  collected). `edge_installed()` compiles the check in memory (it left a
  `.pyc` of a hidden check in the source tree with bytecode on). Pins:
  `tests/test_harness_bench_{team_tasks,team_review,tiers,team_generators}
  _v1400.py`.
  v1.400.1 (test-only; v1.400.0's Tests AND Release gates both went red,
  nothing was published): (1) the patch pin compared the files `git apply`
  wrote byte for byte with LF copies, but GitHub's Windows runners set a
  GLOBAL `core.autocrlf=true`, so `git apply` wrote its NEW files with CRLF
  — the pin tested the runner's git, not the patch. It now runs git as an
  LF checkout (`GIT_LF = ["git", "-c", "core.autocrlf=false"]`); the CRLF
  checkout keeps its own pin. REPRODUCE a runner's git here with
  `GIT_CONFIG_GLOBAL=<a file holding "[core] autocrlf = true">` (this PC's
  global is `input`). (2) `approval-mode-v1188`'s window from the chip's
  `value=` to `markSetupChanged()` was 694 chars on LF once the one-row
  toolbar added props, past 700 on CI's CRLF checkout (the v1.232.1 trap);
  it reads every source through one LF-normalising `read` and the windows
  are 300 / 1200. Both reproduced on this PC with the old shape (CI's exact
  errors) and green on the new one; a mutation of each fix goes red.
  v1.400.2 (benchmark only): (1) an answer file is read in the encoding the
  agent wrote it in — `grading._answer_text` (`json.detect_encoding`: UTF-8,
  UTF-8 BOM, the UTF-16 Windows PowerShell 5.1's `Out-File`/`>` writes; an
  undecodable file falls back to UTF-8 with replacement, as before) for every
  `[rubric]`, and frontier-legacy-callers' check reads the BYTES like the team
  checks (its hash moved). (2) `task.toml` takes `rounds` (1..1000,
  `catalog.MAX_TASK_ROUNDS` == `chat_turn.MAX_TURN_ROUNDS` ==
  `mode.BUILD_SAFETY_ROUNDS`, pinned); `catalog.rounds_for(task, budget)` =
  max(runner budget, task rounds) — a task RAISES the budget, never lowers it;
  the Jarvis runner passes it to the lane AND judges `cut_off` against it. The
  six team tasks ask for 1000 (the product's own Build ceiling, so the
  40-minute time limit decides; last night's fastest round was 3.6 s, so 60
  could cut off a lone planner on the audit task); every other tier keeps 60
  (its most rounds ever measured: 16). Members' step budgets are unchanged
  (a piece is part of a task). The identity's `round_budget` stays the
  runner's; the task's own value is in its content hash. Pins:
  `tests/test_harness_bench_answers_and_rounds_v14002.py` (6 mutations red).
  Same release (test-only): the full suite failed
  `test_harness_bench_team_review_v1396::test_a_dry_run_says_what_it_checked_
  and_what_it_cannot_know` once — an earlier test on its worker left a
  "claude" refusal in `cli_auth.DEFAULT_PROBE` (the v1.396.0 shape, another
  test); it now starts from an empty `_cache`/`_inflight`. A plugin that
  plants that verdict before the test reproduced it on the old shape. EVERY
