  test that asserts the shared probe's state must start from an empty one.

- **NIGHT SHIFT N2: Iron Jarvis commits each ended night task to ITS OWN
  branch (screened), and ONE summary per night is announced ONCE** (v1.399.0,
  phase N2 of `docs/NIGHT-SHIFT-PLAN.md`, decision 2; a doer + a reviewer
  that found 12 more defects, each red first). COMMIT
  (`build_git.commit_for_night`, never raises; `harness/night_commit.py`):
  at settle, through the Commit button's own `branches.commit` under the
  session's git lock, message from the task's words (masked, control chars
  dropped, via stdin `-F -`, a night trailer), never a push or merge; done,
  needs-you and failed are all committed; nothing changed = no commit + a
  note; refused = a note in words, work left in the folder; NO commit under
  Emergency stop (a watcher kills the commit's tree; git runs
  `breakaway_ok=False`; bounded 120 s). `night_commit.screen` runs AFTER
  staging for the NIGHT's commit only (a person's Commit press is unchanged)
  and unstages secret-named files (`.env*` except example/sample/template,
  private keys, `.netrc`, `.npmrc`, credentials, secrets.*), ADDED text
  holding a secret shape (the mask shapes + private-key block + long
  key=value + URL password; in a tracked file only values the old copy did
  not hold), files > 50 MB, new files in dependency/cache folders; left-out
  files stay uncommitted and are NAMED on the note and the row;
  `check_commit` judges what really went in (vs `HEAD~1`) and undoes Iron
  Jarvis's own seconds-old unpushed commit (soft reset) when a hook re-staged
  a secret. The commit refuses in words when the worktree's HEAD is on
  another branch, detached or gone. The N1 main-folder guard is read AGAIN
  after the commit (a post-commit hook writing into main is caught; the
  night's own `ij/` commit never counts) and now snapshots `.git/hooks`
  (names/sizes/times, never contents) + a digest of `.git/config` — shared
  with main, so a planted hook is a main change; an older snapshot without
  them is never read as a change. SUMMARY (`harness/night_morning.py`,
  `NightSummaryRecord` late model keyed by `night` = the local date the
  window OPENED; `NightTaskRecord.night/commit_sha/morning_json` additive):
  built only from records (task notes, reply lines' finish + usage,
  `branches.branch_summary`, the guard), ready when the night's last task
  ends or the window closes, whichever first; rows re-derived when stored
  state differs (`rows_differ`); a stopped row leads with its reason; last
  14 kept; a pruned task keeps its row. NOTICE: event `build.night_summary`
  (no session id; words written ONCE by `notice_words`), a default alert;
  `bell_pings`/`bell_attention` true (one-notice fixture case
  `night-summary`), main.js `RUN_OUTCOME_AUDIT_TYPES` + `runOutcomeNotice`;
  the CLAIM (`notified_at`) is written before the publish and GIVEN BACK if
  the publish fails, and every pass announces any recent unannounced
  summary (a crash exactly between claim and publish loses that one notice;
  the view still has it); only tonight's/last night's is announced. LOCAL
  ONLY is recorded at task end and re-read at send: such a task's words and
  branch name never reach the phone, a webhook or the log ("A Local only
  task: open Iron Jarvis on this PC to see it"). A night turn raises no
  `build.turn_finished`. VIEW: `LastNightList.tsx` (Coding sessions, under
  Tonight; `/terminals?night=<date>`; failed, needs you, running, done,
  stopped; Open / Changes (the session's Changes pane via `choosePaneNext`) /
  Push / Open a pull request / Throw away = the existing Discard, two
  presses, branch kept); `GET /build/night/summaries`. OPEN: the phone
  message can come at ~1 AM (announced when the last task ends, not at
  window close — the user's call); a task after the summary updates its row
  with no second notice; secrets in unknown shapes are still committed;
  attempts' own branches are not committed; a user with an explicit
  `comm.event_types` list gets no phone message; per-task stall and
  account-limit notices still fire at night; a GPG-signing commit may pop a
  passphrase window (bounded 120 s); not driven in the packaged app, with a
  real phone or real GitHub. Pins: `tests/test_night_morning{,_run,
  _review}_v1399.py`, `dashboard/__tests__/last-night-v1399.test.tsx` (+
  fixture `last-night-v1399.json`), e2e `dashboard/e2e/ij-harness-last-night.spec.ts`;
  moved on purpose: `test_one_notice_v13600` (audit types),
  `test_integrate_wave4_v13540` (default-alert table).
  v1.400.3: the Notifications page's per-destination boxes
  (`NotificationsPage.EVENT_LABELS`, "Send this destination") had no
  `build.night_summary`, so ticking ANY smaller set saved an `events` list
  without it and the phone's one message a night stopped silently. Added
  ("The night shift's summary (one message a night)"), and
  `tests/test_notification_events_v14003.py` pins the page's keys EQUAL to
  `notifier.DEFAULT_ALERT_EVENTS` (a new default alert with no box fails it)
  and drives a narrowed save through `POST /comm/channels`; all three red on
  the old page.
  v1.399.1 (test-only; v1.399.0's Release gate went red, Tests green, nothing
  published): `test_profile_share_v1306::test_profile_change_rerenders_on_
  the_share_thread_never_the_loop` made two profile changes inside a 0.4 s
  debounce, but on the loaded runner a PUT took longer than the window, so
  the first timer fired between them and two writes landed. The test now
  holds the debounce timer at its start (a `threading.Timer` subclass whose
  `run` waits on a gate) until both changes are in, and also COUNTS the
  re-renders: a poke that stopped cancelling re-renders twice while still
  writing once (the second render's text is the same), which the write
  count alone never caught, in the old test or the new. A profile save held
  0.5 s reproduced CI's failure on the old shape; the new shape passes with
  it held, and the no-cancel mutant goes red.

- **NIGHT SHIFT N1: a queue of coding tasks that run overnight on their own
  branches; file edits and commands run unasked, EVERYTHING else is carded
  and declined; the main folder is WATCHED** (v1.398.0, phase N1 of
  `docs/NIGHT-SHIFT-PLAN.md`, decisions 1, 3, 4 accepted 2026-10-26; a doer +
  a reviewer that found 16 more defects, each red first). `harness/night.py`
  (`NightTaskRecord`, a late model; states queued|running|done|failed|
  needs_you|stopped; notes `{kind, text, at}` written under `_RECORD_LOCK`),
  `harness/night_window.py`, `harness/night_guard.py`, `harness/main_env.py`,
  `routes/night.py` (`GET/POST /build/night`, PATCH/DELETE, `/move`,
  `/cancel`), `TonightList.tsx`, `lib/nightShift.ts`. Settings (Agents &
  automation › Night shift): `night_window_start/end` "23:00"/"07:00",
  `night_max_at_once` 2, `night_task_minutes` 90 — these three ASK from chat
  (they widen the night) — and `night_card_wait_minutes` 15 (allow). THE
  LOOP (`bg_tasks`, `_tick("night_shift")`, a per-platform pass LOCK so two
  passes never exceed N or share a folder) starts tasks only inside the
  window, each a NEW `ij/` branch session through the lifted create path and
  `start_build_turn(started_by="night")` on the MAIN loop; a task is held to
  its ACCUMULATED `ran_s` (the cap is what is left), and a carry-on (restart
  resume, Resume when ready) is refused outside the window or with no time
  left. THE POSTURE (decision 4) is `approve_for_me` + the night's own
  allow-list `RUNS_UNASKED` (file read/edit/patch, glob/grep/list,
  shell/process_*, plan_update, skills, the helpers) applied PER TURN, never
  stored on the session; the stream lane cards EVERYTHING else
  (`routes/chat._night_asks`/`_night_card`) past tab grants, standing grants
  and a free spend verdict, helpers get the same as "ask" rules, and the
  model is told the night's rule. The first cut used yolo, and `web_fetch`/
  `web_search` RAN unasked — pinned through the REAL `registry.invoke`.
  CARDS (decision 1): a card waits `night_card_wait_minutes`, goes to the
  phone AT ONCE when one is set up (`stall.after_s/expires_s`; safety and
  once-only cards never), then that call is DECLINED with "left for the
  user: …" and the task ends needs_you. A project's own command lines still
  card once-only and are declined; folder checks and project checkers run
  under the command grant. Low trust carries over to the task's later turns
  and ends needs_you; an account limit ends needs_you. MAIN FOLDER GUARD
  (`night_guard.snapshot/changes`, before and after each task): HEAD, branch,
  porcelain status, a hash of `diff HEAD`, the stash, non-`ij/` branches and
  tags, the `.venv` package list — a change ends needs_you with
  `main_changed` shown un-folded; every record says what is NOT confined
  (`NOT_CONFINED`: commands run as the user; outside-repo writes, untracked
  contents, other ignored files and the network are unseen). ENV (decision
  3): `main_env` puts the main checkout's `.venv` and `node_modules/.bin`
  on the shell, the finish check and the checkers — never a junction or
  symlink in the worktree; `editable_redirects` reads `.pth` / editable
  finders (parsed, never run) / `direct_url.json` and puts the branch's import
  roots first on PYTHONPATH, else "NOT TRUSTED" + needs_you (proven with a
  real offline `uv pip install -e`). EMERGENCY STOP now covers every coding
  turn Iron Jarvis started by itself (`started_by` night | wait | restart,
  account moves, night-started attempts) — never a person's — at each
  automatic start AND the top of each resume pass, and stops the running
  STEP (a hung command no longer runs to its 10 min deadline);
  `stop_unattended` returns `turn:<id>` entries; a stop during a start
  records STOPPED. Background programs end at settle
  (`S.end_session_programs`). Night tasks hold a "runs at once" slot
  (`Orchestrator.try_hold_slot/release_slot`). PAY: `pay_problem`/`pay_room`
  — no start at a $0 or spent limit; with room the reply's dollar cap is
  what is left. KEEP-AWAKE: `/system/activity` gains `night_shift` (running,
  or queued from `AWAKE_BEFORE_S` 6 h before the window opens); `working`
  keeps its meaning. The phone's 5-minute refusal of an answer to a card the
  app still holds open is fixed (an EXPIRED agent card is still refused).
  OPEN: a night task's starting account ignores the 95% floor and group
  spreading; the team's pay ledger does not count the night session's own
  spend; `ask_user` questions do not go to the phone; Cancel of a queued task
  is one press; not driven in the packaged app or with a real phone. NOT
  YET (N2): committing finished tasks, the morning summary. Pins:
  `tests/test_night_shift{,_run,_waits,_review,_outside,_editable,_window,
  _limits,_attempts}_v1398.py`, `dashboard/__tests__/tonight{,-review}-v1398.test.tsx`, e2e
  `dashboard/e2e/ij-harness-tonight.spec.ts`.
  v1.398.1 (test-only): v1.398.0's Tests gate went red (Release passed and
  published) on `model-team-v1394` "says its team roles, and edits them" —
  the mocked PATCH is recorded when it is SENT, but SetupCard closes the
  editor (and the Edit button comes back) only after it ANSWERS, and the
  test pressed Edit straight after the record. It now waits for the button
  itself (`findByTitle`). Holding the mocked save 300 ms reproduced CI's
  exact "Unable to find an element with the title" on the old shape; the new
  shape passes with it held.

- **TRY ON SEVERAL MODELS: up to three attempts, each its own branch, worktree,
  model and account; opt-in by the user's words or the Try on… chip only; Keep
  lands one** (v1.397.0, Phase 5 Part B of `docs/LONG-JOBS-AND-MODEL-TEAM-PLAN.md`
  = part 3's Phase 8, part 3 decision 8; a doer + a reviewer that found 10 more
  defects, each red first). `harness/attempts.py` + `routes/build_attempts.py`
  (`POST /build/sessions/{id}/attempts/keep {turn_id, attempt}` and
  `/attempts/discard`). OPT-IN: `parse_request` — only the FIRST or LAST
  sentence can ask, fenced blocks and quoted lines are ignored, the words must
  name TWO or more models (one model = the chip, `BuildTurnBody.try_on
  [{provider, model}]`), an unknown name refuses only when it looks like a
  model, a count phrase must be the whole name list, "both" is no count,
  "later" means not now, the task keeps its line breaks; a table of 16 asking
  + 31 ordinary rows runs through the real route (13 of 31 went wrong before
  the review). When unsure it is an ordinary turn. No tool can start
  attempts. EACH ATTEMPT is a harness turn of the stream lane (not an
  agent-run helper, so the finish check, the dialect and the coding tools
  apply) under session `build:<thread>~try<n>`, turn `<turn>.try<n>`, with its
  own hold, pin (`member_account` with the session's pin + `work_group`),
  branch (`ij/try-…`, a worktree beside the repo) and a tool ceiling without
  delegate / spawn_agent / list_agents / assign_work / consult /
  delegate_remote / ask_user / exit_plan_mode / the exits. Refused before
  anything starts, in words: > `MAX_ATTEMPTS` 3, unknown/ambiguous/twin
  names, cloud under Local only, provider not connected, the 95% floor, the
  group, the pay limit (the session's own model exempt), not a git repo
  (`NEEDS_GIT`), plan mode, files/skill/command with the request. UNCOMMITTED
  WORK is carried: `snapshot_commit` makes a commit object of the session's
  current files (new ones too) through a throwaway index — the session's own
  index, files and branch untouched — and every attempt starts from it
  (`StartPoint.carried`). KEEP: a clean start commits the attempt (Iron
  Jarvis's own commit, like the Commit button; never pushes) and merges it
  into the session's branch; a CARRIED start lands the result in the session
  folder as UNCOMMITTED changes (`land`: two-tree `read-tree -m -u` through a
  throwaway index) only while the folder still holds exactly what the
  attempts started from (`KEEP_CHANGED_SINCE`); a no-branch session ADOPTS
  the kept branch (`S.adopt_branch`) so the user's main checkout is never
  touched (proven byte-identical). Keep refuses while any attempt runs
  (`KEEP_RUNNING`), a second time, and on a card COPIED by Branch from here
  (`KEEP_COPIED`; the copy names `from_session`, holds no git facts).
  Discard all (two presses) trashes the folders; every branch is kept;
  deleting a session trashes its attempts' folders (refused in words while
  one is in use); a rewind past a try turn with live folders is refused
  ("keep one or Discard all first"). SPEND: `harness/attempt_spend.
  AttemptSpendRecord` (late model, in `_LATE_MODEL_MODULES`), written once per
  attempt at its end (a cut-off too), is added to `paid_spend_this_month`;
  `SharedCap` shares the session's `setup.cap` across the attempts as ONE
  reply. RECORD: `attempt` SSE frames (each attempt's first frame carries the
  card's own facts, so a card rebuilt after Stop or a crash still knows
  adopt/base/carried); the card rides `done` and the reply line (`attempts`,
  in `harness/sessions.LINE_KEYS` and the dashboard's); a saved card never
  has `ask` and never says running. RESTART: a cut-off card says
  `CUT_OFF_LINE` (nothing restarts on its own; a finished one can still be
  kept) and the page offers NO Retry on a reply with an attempts card (a
  Retry would start every attempt again). Dashboard: `lib/buildAttempts.ts`,
  `components/harness/HarnessAttempts.tsx` + `HarnessTryOn.tsx` (portaled,
  fixed, inside the window on a phone); a queued message keeps its picks
  (`QueuedItem.tryOn`). OPEN: an attempt's ask never goes to the phone, and
  "Stop the step", the mid-reply summary and the Timeline do not cover
  attempts (the lane keys them on `reply_record`); a crash between making the
  worktrees and writing the question line leaves worktrees nothing knows
  about; a landed Keep is not in the undo journal; concurrent attempts all
  pass the pay check at start; Emergency stop does not reach attempts (like
  every Build turn until night shift N1). Pins:
  `tests/test_build_attempts{,_review}_v1397.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/build-attempts{,-review}-v1397.test.tsx`, e2e
  `dashboard/e2e/ij-harness-attempts.spec.ts` (390 px, 1280 px, keyboard).
  v1.400.7 (a USER REPORT, a screenshot of one flat list of every model):
  the "Try on…" list is GROUPED BY PROVIDER (`lib/tryOnMenu.tryOnGroups`):
  one row per provider, local first (ModelMenu `PROVIDER_RANK`), each with a
  `KIND_BADGE` word ("local + cloud", `MIXED_BADGE`, for a gateway that
  sends some names out), each opening a `FitPanel` pop-out (`toward="right"`:
  the chip sits at the box's left; under its row below 640 px) where every
  model says "local" / "cloud" from its OWN `on_this_network` (true = local,
  else ModelMenu `rowKind`, lifted out of `modelProvidersOf` unchanged). NO
  TWINS: the keyless-API fold is ONE rule, `harnessModelMenu.foldKeylessApis`
  (lifted out of `companyMenu`, which calls it); a config slot and the fleet
  node the registry SEEDS from it (`custom`/`fleet-custom`,
  `ollama`/`fleet-ollama` — `routable_nodes()` includes the seeds, so GET
  /models lists the user's gateway twice) are ONE group named by
  `machineName("fleet-<slot>")` else "Your model server"; a name in a twin
  row's `aliases` is no second entry; the "fleet" alias is left out when its
  server lists a model on the network. A PICK SENDS THE CATALOG PAIR
  (`FoldedModels.origin`): `attempts.resolve` refuses a pair GET /models does
  not list, so a model folded under Claude Code still sends `anthropic::…`,
  and a model both twins list sends the slot's. A find box, the picks listed
  on top (each with ×), Escape closes the pop-out and then the list. The list
  has NO backdrop blur: a `backdrop-filter` makes a box the containing block
  of the FIXED pop-outs inside it and would clip them. The chat's menu is
  byte-identical (`chat-model-menu-v1376.json`). Pins:
  `dashboard/__tests__/try-on-grouped-v1400-7.test.tsx`, e2e
  `dashboard/e2e/try-on-grouped-v1400-7.spec.ts` (1280 × 720, 390 × 844);
  the v1397 pins open a provider first. OPEN: an attempt's account is chosen
  by the daemon, never in the list.
  v1.400.8 (the user, after v1.400.7): Tonight's "Try it on several models"
  is the SAME list. `components/harness/TryOnModels.tsx` is the one list
  (picks on top, find box, provider rows, models) and both callers render
  it: HarnessTryOn is only the menu around it (portal, chip, header, Done;
  it keeps the open provider itself, `openGroup`/`onOpenGroup`, so Escape
  from its own Done/Clear closes the provider first), and TonightList passes
  `layout="inline"` (a provider opens UNDER its row — the form sits in a
  scrolling column, so a pop-out fixed to the window would have to follow
  every scroll). Enter in the find box is prevented (it would send the
  form); every button is `type="button"`. Tonight's picks are labelled by
  `tryOnMenu.pickEntry` (the list's words) and a saved pick the list no
  longer shows (a `fleet-custom` pair from before v1.400.7) stays on top,
  removable, counting toward three. The demo model is never offered there
  now (it was). Pins: `dashboard/__tests__/tonight-try-on-v1400-8.test.tsx`
  (15 mutations red with the v1397/v1398/v1400-7 pins), e2e
  `dashboard/e2e/tonight-try-on-v1400-8.spec.ts` (1280 × 800, 390 × 844);
  the user's catalog is `__tests__/helpers/tryOnCatalog.ts`. OPEN: the
  form's "Runs on" select still lists every model flat. LESSON: on this PC
  Python's `Path.write_text` and the Write tool write CRLF; git's
  `autocrlf=input` hides it, but an anchored edit (a mutation harness, a
  multi-line replace) then misses every match — read and write BYTES.
  v1.397.1 (test-only): v1.397.0's Tests gate went red (Release passed and
  published) on `test_iron_proxy_routes_v1301::test_the_real_bundle_starts_
  answers_and_stops` — the account list read `[]` right after a 201 create.
  Every Iron-Proxy route asks `/iron/health` with a 2 s wait
  (`HEALTH_TIMEOUT_S`, bound as `_health`'s DEFAULT, so patching the constant
  alone changes nothing), and on the loaded runner one answer came later, so
  the bundle read as not running and the view listed nothing. The real-bundle
  tests and the stand-in fixture now set a 30 s health wait (both the
  constant and `_health.__defaults__`) and the view assertion prints the
  status. Reproduced with a plugin that makes every health answer take 2.5 s
  (the old shape fails, the new passes). A wait bound as a DEFAULT argument
  is patched through `__defaults__`.
  v1.397.2 (test-only): v1.397.1 was NOT published — three OTHER load races
  on its gates, each reproduced by holding back the one async step it
  depends on (scratch plugin), old shape red with CI's exact words, new
  green: (1) `test_voice_offloop_v1333` closed the socket the moment the
  final text arrived, but the route builds the NEXT recognizer after that
  send, so a loaded runner shut the app down first (`1 == 2`) — it now waits
  for the second build with the socket open; (2) the real Windows OCR tests
  ran a page past its 20 s `PAGE_DEADLINE_S` (`None is not None`) — they set
  120 s, and the deadline is a KEYWORD-ONLY default, so it is patched through
  `__kwdefaults__` (not `__defaults__`); (3) `test_phone_apps_agent_v1387`
  sent "approve" once a text naming the tool reached the fake phone, but the
  general alert can arrive before the answerable question is recorded, so
  "approve" read as a new chat (`['chat_escalated'] == ['approval_answered']`)
  — it now waits until `poller._fresh_open_prompt` sees the question. Wait
  for the state the next step needs, never for a message that only
  usually comes after it.

- **The bench MEASURES a team: one command runs the team, then the planner
  alone, and refuses the night before it starts** (v1.396.0, Phase 7 of
  `docs/LONG-JOBS-AND-MODEL-TEAM-PLAN.md`; a doer + a reviewer that found 10
  more defects, each red first). `harness_bench/team.py`: `--runner
  jarvis-team --team <toml>` (planner + members: name, provider, model,
  roles, optional account / `effort` / `max_steps` 1–200). Members are made
  as custom agents in the `--root` home before the first task and removed at
  the end; the single-model runner removes the bench's own leftovers and
  refuses members someone else made, so the alone row never sees a team.
  `--with-alone` runs the planner alone AFTER, in series (neither measured
  span holds the other's use). Members get the file's `effort` else
  `--effort`, sent only on that member's calls and only where
  `providers.reasoning` offers a level (`counting_router(reasoning_for=)`),
  and `max_steps` else the planner's round budget (60) — the home's
  `max_agent_steps` 12 starved them; both are recorded per part and in the
  identity (`member_effort`, `member_steps`, `team`, router constants,
  member prompt, `model_team`/`team_record` sources — a changed router is a
  new setup, so a live run is started from a frozen checkout). PREFLIGHT
  (`preflight.check_models`, dry run AND real run, before any task):
  unknown provider, a CLI the CACHED answer calls signed out or missing (it
  never starts a CLI), a model server that does not answer / wants a key
  the home lacks / refuses it / does not list the model (one bounded 5 s
  `_probe_endpoint_models`, the home's own key, no redirects), a bad Claude
  id — one sentence each; what it cannot check is a printed note. RECORDS:
  `parts {who: provider, model, vendor, pieces, calls, tokens, metered and
  list-price cost, effort_passed, steps}`, `accounts {<provider>:<account>:
  windows {first, last, one_period, why}}` read from the vendors' own extras
  (claude-cli relay `ratelimit_headers`, Codex `rateLimits` stamped with the
  snapshot's own `observedAt`; parsed as Iron-Proxy parses them),
  `helpers` with role + reason, `team_records` (+ `usage_numbers`). The ledger
  counts helper receipts (a single-model run with a helper used to read
  `ledger_ok: False` forever and re-run every time) and an idle member counts
  0 roundings. FAILURES: `failures.helper_infra` reads a failed helper in both
  voices (raw `ProviderError` or the run's plain summary) — a member lost to
  its account or sign-in makes the run DID-NOT-RUN (`member_infra`), never a
  team result; a crash in the bench's own step is `bench_failed` and the
  night goes on; a plan whose grading or work folder would pass 248 chars is
  refused before any task (Windows refuses a 260+ char working folder:
  WinError 267). REPORT (`team_report.py`): the VERDICT first ("No: the team
  passed fewer tasks." / "Yes on these runs: …" only when both hold), pass
  counts, Claude list price over runs BOTH made and scored, then "Beyond
  Claude, the team also used $X of Codex list-price; N tokens … on local
  models"; measured windows ("not comparable" across a reset, "not known"
  with no reading, other use of the account inside the number); says when
  the router had no usage numbers (Iron-Proxy off in the scratch home, so
  the 80% rule never applied); a per member/role table to tune from. OPEN: no
  wait for a reset (limit runs are did-not-run; re-run the command later);
  Codex's model name is not checked in advance; a `~/.ironjarvis/config.toml`
  would layer into the scratch home. Pins:
  `tests/test_harness_bench_{team,helpers_ledger,team_review}_v1396.py` (+
  `tests/_fakes/bench_{team,tonight}_v1396.py`).
  v1.396.0 WAS NOT PUBLISHED (Tests and Release both red on
  `test_team_router_v1395::test_routing_never_starts_a_sign_in_probe`,
  `[] == ['claude']`): `cli_auth.DEFAULT_PROBE` is ONE object per test
  process, and once v1.396.0's new test files moved that test to another CI
  shard, an earlier test on its worker had left a fresh "claude" verdict or
  "claude" in `_inflight` (a patched `_start` never runs the refresh that
  clears it), so the real refresh was never due. Both probe tests now start
  from an empty `_cache` and `_inflight` (monkeypatched, restored after);
  each leftover reproduced CI's exact error on the old shape. A test that
  asserts the shared probe DOES start a check must start from an empty one.
  THE FIRST LIVE RUN (2026-10-26, v1.396.1 frozen, 12 frontier tasks x 3,
  Sonnet planner + glm + Codex): team 35/36, Sonnet alone 35/36, team 2%
  less Claude list price — and ZERO pieces handed to a member in 36 runs.
  The planner WAS shown both the team list and Claude Code's `Agent` tool
  with the role:<id> words (a scripted run recorded its system prompt and
  tools); the words are permissive ("Helpers you can start…", the Agent
  tool opens "on this session's model and account") and on one-minute
  tasks Sonnet does the work itself. v1.399.2: `team_report.answer` gives
  `NO_MEMBER_WORK` ("No answer: … the planner working alone") BEFORE any
  other verdict when no compared run handed a piece to a member
  (`handed_to_a_member`: a member part with a call or a piece, or a helper
  row naming a member — a refused piece still went to the team), and says
  "Team members worked in M of the N runs" when only some did. The old
  report's "Yes on these runs" was the planner's own variation read as a
  verdict. Pins: `tests/test_team_report_no_member_work_v13992.py` (8 of 9
  red on the old code; 5 mutations red); moved on purpose: the memberless
  teams in `test_harness_bench_team_v1396` and `…_review_v1396` got a
  member, keeping what each tested.

- **An account limit is WAITED OUT by itself; a move to another account
  happens only WITHIN a group the user made, same vendor** (v1.393.0, Phase 4
  of `docs/LONG-JOBS-AND-MODEL-TEAM-PLAN.md`; a doer + a reviewer that found
  10 more defects). AUTO WAIT: `routes/build.auto_wait` arms the v1.368.0
  wait (`wait_record`, `resume_due`, `take_wait`) the moment a coding reply
  ends on a resumable account-limit card. The setting `build_auto_resume`
  (automation, allow tier, default on); OFF = NOTHING carries on by itself,
  a move included — the card waits for the press, and the press is the
  consent. GROUPS: `Config.account_groups {name: [ids]}`, a schema row in
  connections, tier **ask-floor** (always carded from chat, no grant lifts
  it: work ↔ personal is a privacy decision; `redact_args` names the
  accounts, never Iron-Proxy's ids). Checked on save (names 1–40, one group
  per account, ≤ 20 × 20; ADDED ids must be Claude Code / Codex accounts in the
  cached snapshot) and cleaned quietly at load (never refuses boot). MOVE:
  the wait loop hands the reply to an account of the SAME group and SAME
  vendor that is on, signed in (`needs_sign_in`), not parked and below
  `TAKEOVER_MAX_PERCENT` 95 of every window. Known-low accounts first, then
  accounts with NO numbers in Iron-Proxy's order; numbers only arrive after a
  call, so unknown is never "0%". It goes through `account_patch` +
  `start_build_turn(continuation=True)`, with the patch applied INSIDE
  `take_wait(then=)`. `setup.account_moves` caps moves in a row at
  `MAX_GROUP_MOVES` 4 and resets when a turn served (a billed call or words)
  or the user chose an account. `S.amend_wait` writes only if the wait is the
  one the loop checked and never on an archived session (a Cancel during a
  slow check was re-armed). The loop clears its wake event BEFORE a pass. Line
  key `accountMove {from, fromName, to, toName, group}` (also on the
  crash-rebuilt line); the card's `account_wait.group` is the LIVE group.
  NOTICES: `build.account_limit` gains `waiting`, and `build.resumed` names
  both accounts on a move. Both ping the bell uncounted and quiet while the
  user looks at that session; "This PC", the phone and the tray stay quiet.
  `notifier.bell_pings` knows both types (one-notice fixture rows
  `build-limit-waiting`, `build-moved-within-group`). USAGE: the chip and the
  Timeline header read the cached snapshot ("63% of the 5-hour window, resets
  3:40 PM" / "usage not known yet"). OPEN: the tray says nothing for a limit
  waiting for the press (pre-existing since v1.369.0); usage shows one turn
  late (the snapshot refreshes every 30 s); the Settings JSON row and the
  card's Group choice are last-writer-wins; arrow keys on the closed Group
  select save each step. Pins: `tests/test_build_limits{,_review}_v1393.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/{limits-without-a-press,account-groups,
  account-groups-review,limits-bell-review}-v1393.test.tsx`, the one-notice
  fixture.

- **A coding reply NEVER STALLS SILENTLY: a stall notice with Stop the step, a
  question to the phone, a summary mid-reply, an optional cap** (v1.392.0,
  Phase 3 of `docs/LONG-JOBS-AND-MODEL-TEAM-PLAN.md`; a doer + a reviewer that
  found 7 more defects). STALL: `harness/stall.StallWatch` in
  `routes/build.recorded_turn` (`STALL_AFTER_S` 600, `TICK_S` 5, injectable
  `CLOCK`). Progress = any frame, or a command's output through
  `core/progress`'s ContextVar mark (`core/procs` uses its moving readers only
  while that mark is armed). `core/events._drop_build_turn_scope` drops the
  mark too, or a handler's own output reset the reply's clock. The watch names
  the turn's HELD model (no frame says it). A step whose own registry deadline
  ends it at the window is not announced (`step_deadline`,
  `DEADLINE_GRACE_S` 60): the default `tool_call_timeout_s` IS 600. Frame
  `stall {words, minutes, step?}` / `{over}`; event `build.turn_stalled` (the
  bell, plus the tray with no window; `one-notice-v13600` fixture row);
  `POST /build/sessions/{id}/stop-step {step_id, turn_id?, reply?}`. Each tool
  call runs as its own task keyed by (turn, call id); the registry's cancel
  path writes "stopped by you" and the model gets `stall.stopped_result`,
  keeping the unit rule. PHONE: a coding session's card unanswered for
  `PHONE_AFTER_S` (300) publishes `approval.requested` `{surface: "build",
  thread_id, title, count?}` (session_id None) through the EXISTING notifier +
  `comm/prompts` path; the phone's approve maps to ONCE; an in-app answer
  closes the phone prompt. `stall.phone_may_ask` never sends a once-only card,
  the cloud card, a priced card, or ANY card of a Local only turn
  (`egress.current_scope().on` at the call site; decided at integration: a
  phone channel is a cloud service). Words "Your coding session …"
  (`notifier.CODING_SESSION`, the twin of `lib/buildWords`). SUMMARY:
  `harness/reply_summary` reuses `AgentRuntime._maybe_compact(complete=,
  ledger=, report=)` on the reply's pinned model through `router.complete(
  pin=True)` under the hold and Local only. The ledger window starts at the
  original question; billed to the reply's tally; raced against Stop
  (`_prep_step`); the demo model writes none; `RETRY_ROUNDS` 3. Frame
  `summarized`, `model_call` kind "summary", line key `summarized`. A restart
  loses the summary and rewrites it at the resumed reply's first round. CAP:
  `setup.cap {minutes?, usd?}` via PATCH, saved EXACTLY as sent or 400
  `CAP_REFUSED` (`reply_cap.strict`). At the cap the reply ends the step-limit
  way: done `cap`, line `capMet`, Keep going. A carried-on reply counts its
  cut parts' RUNNING time (`ran_since`, never the hours the app was off) and
  their spend. Off = byte-identical. OPEN: the cost cap ignores helpers'
  spend; a futile summary turns summaries off for the rest of the reply; a
  pack's progress counts only when the turn draws MCP cards; the composer's
  plain Stop still waits for a hung step; a notice can fire right after the
  PC wakes; not driven in the packaged app or with a real phone. Pins:
  `tests/test_build_stall{,_review}_v1392.py`,
  `test_build_phone_ask{,_review}_v1392.py`,
  `test_build_phone_local_only_v1392.py`, `test_build_reply_summary_v1392.py`,
  `test_build_reply_cap{,_review}_v1392.py`, `dashboard/__tests__/
  build-long-jobs-v1392.test.tsx`, `build-stall-words-review-v1392.test.ts`,
  the one-notice fixture. SAME RELEASE (test-only):
  `test_bash_dialect_review_v1386`'s background-server test reads the
  server's pid file only after waiting for it — the model's own `sleep 1` is
  no promise Python has started (a 3 s slow start reproduces the full suite's
  `FileNotFoundError` on the old read).

- **A coding reply SURVIVES sleep and restarts: awake while WORKING, its own
  record kept per round, carried on by itself exactly once** (v1.391.0, Phase 2
  of `docs/LONG-JOBS-AND-MODEL-TEAM-PLAN.md`; a doer + a reviewer that found
  10 more defects). KEEP AWAKE: `desktop/main.js` holds ONE `powerSaveBlocker`
  "prevent-app-suspension" while `GET /system/activity`'s `working` is true —
  NOT `busy`, which also counts work parked on the user and is what the update
  warning reads. `working` = replies not waiting, sessions except an attended
  one on a pending ask, workflow runs not waiting, and terminals whose state is
  `working`. Released after 3 missed checks and on quit; the tray menu is
  rebuilt only on a change; setting `keep_awake` (allow tier, default on) is
  read live from `/system/activity`. SAVING: `harness/reply_record.ReplyRecord`
  → `RunTranscript` (`run_id "build-turn:<turn>"`, `session_id
  "build:<thread>"`, a nullable `waiting` column the reconciler adds), awaited
  at every round top and before tools run (one writer thread, ≤ 10 s),
  deleted at a clean end. A list past 4M chars keeps the question plus the
  newest WHOLE rounds (`_fit`, `LEFT_OUT_NOTE`; a round is never split);
  `KEEP_DAYS` 7. Only `start_build_turn` passes the stream lane's
  `reply_record=` keyword (pinned). `recorded_turn` sets `waiting` from ask /
  answer frames AND from a helper frame's ask. RESUME: the lifespan task
  `bg_tasks["build_resume"]` runs `resume_after_restart`. `claim(turn,
  to_turn)` MOVES the row to the new turn's key in one UPDATE guarded by
  `waiting` (a delete lost a reply cut again before the carry-on's first save).
  It refuses waiting / archived / gone / account-wait / a newer user message,
  re-checked at the start. `_resume_root` follows `resumedFrom` back to the
  ORIGINAL question (its files, skill and history end; a second cut sent the
  question twice). `load_for_resume(ledger_run_id=, since=)` closes cut-off
  calls by their REAL tool. The finish check treats a resumed turn as a
  carry-on of the original question (`resumed=`) and notes once
  (`FinishCheck.checked_before`). Line key `resumedFrom`; bell
  `build.resumed` reason "restart". `TurnLog._flush_later` writes the last
  interval's frames, for every turn. `replayed_reply_line` rebuilds a
  crash-cut reply's Timeline from its stored frames. `/sessions/interrupted`
  never lists a coding session's helper (`HELPER_ORIGIN`). The update
  rehearsal drives a coding reply through `/shutdown` and a hard kill and
  checks it carries on once without re-running a step. OPEN: a call that had
  finished inside a cut round is told "cut off"; a resumed turn's meter leaves
  out the carried tail; a resume that fails at once (an account parked) loses
  its kept rounds; a cloud model picked while the app was down receives the cut
  part's tool outputs; two full rewrites of the kept list per round (≤ 4 MB
  each); not driven in the packaged app. Pins:
  `tests/test_build_resume{,_review}_v1391.py`,
  `test_build_cutoff_timeline_v1391.py`, `test_keep_awake_v1391.py`,
  `test_interrupted_helpers_v1391.py`, `test_update_rehearsal_v1338.py`
  (+ `fixtures/fake_coding_model_v1391.py`), `dashboard/__tests__/
  build-resume-v1391.test.tsx`, fixture `build-cutoff-timeline-v1391.json`
  (`IJ_REGEN_CUTOFF_TIMELINE=1`).

- **A coding reply's TIMELINE: one precise clock for LENGTHS, the wall clock
  for WINDOWS** (v1.390.0, Phase 1 of `docs/LONG-JOBS-AND-MODEL-TEAM-PLAN.md`;
  a doer + a reviewer that found 5 more defects). FRAMES (harness turns ONLY;
  chat, POST, sidebar, bench and the Build pane chat byte-identical, pinned):
  a `model_call` frame `{ms, provider, model, usage, kind?}` at the three
  tally sites (tool loop, final-answer nudge, language rewrite), its cost from
  the receipt's own `UsageTally.step_cost` so the calls add up to the receipt;
  `tool_call` finished frames gain `ms` (the tool's own run time);
  `routes/build.recorded_turn` stamps offsets through `harness/timeline.
  ReplyClock` (perf_counter): `start` on round / tool_call / model_call,
  start/end on helper; `check: finish | project` (`timeline.check_kind`, from
  the call id) marks Iron Jarvis's own check runs, never the model's work.
  REPLY LINE: `calls` (≤ 2,000, plus an in-flight `{state}` call),
  `steps[].start`, `helper: true` on a step that started a helper, `helpers[]`
  start/end/provider/model/usage/work (≤ 20). `timing.startedAt /
  firstTokenAt / endedAt` STAY WALL-CLOCK — `paneTurnWindow` sends them to
  `POST /chat/changes`, which compares them with wall-stamped journal rows,
  and putting them on perf_counter dropped a reply's changes after a clock
  step — while the precise length rides beside them as `timing.ms` (the axis
  and `WorkLine.workDuration` read it; a chat reply has none). On Python 3.12
  Windows `time.time`/`monotonic` tick every 15.6 ms: never put a start from
  one clock with a length from another. DASHBOARD: `lib/buildTimeline.ts`,
  `components/harness/HarnessTimeline.tsx` (the second tab after Plan; a
  header with the helpers apart and "list price"; windowed rows; a card on
  press or focus, never hover; the live read once a second and only while
  visible; `noTimelineReason` early | cut | before); `lib/turnCache` keeps it;
  `useModels` returns STABLE lists (a fresh `[]` / `filter` per render redrew
  every memo on each streamed word). OPEN: a helper's own model calls are one
  bar; waits for the user show as gaps; the session screen still re-renders
  per word through `useChatStream`'s `setLastEventAt` (v1.246.0); a reply a
  crash cut off has no timeline (Phase 2 can rebuild it from the stored
  frames); whether perf_counter counts system sleep is unverified. Pins:
  `tests/test_build_timeline{,_review}_v1390.py`, `dashboard/__tests__/
  harness-timeline{,-live,-return,-review}-v1390.test.tsx`,
  `use-models-stable-v1390.test.ts`, fixture `build-timeline-v1390.json`
  (`IJ_REGEN_BUILD_TIMELINE=1`).
  v1.390.1 (test-only): v1.390.0's Tests gate went red on
  `test_reads_that_ran_together_overlap_on_the_axis` — the runner measured
  the 0.2 s read at 535 ms (a step's length includes its bookkeeping, which
  waited on a loaded disk) against a "< 400 ms" bound, a bound on the
  hardware. The test now reads the lane's clock as an injected stand-in
  (`_StepClock`, each read moves it to its own end), so the lengths are
  exact; dropping the lane's own-length measurement still turns it red.
  v1.399.3 (test-only): v1.399.2's Tests gate went red on the same test
  (`ms` 397, not 400). The LANE's clock was injected, but the REPLY clock
  (`harness/timeline.ReplyClock`, bound to its module's `time.perf_counter`
  at construction) still read the real one, and `step_finished` caps a
  step's length at the span IT saw — on Windows `asyncio.sleep(0.4)` can
  end a few ms early (the v1.266.1 rule), so 400 became 397. The test now
  injects the same clock into `timeline.time` too. A read that ends 20 ms
  early reproduced CI's failure on the old shape (394 ms); the new shape
  passes with it, and dropping the lane's own length still goes red. When a
  test injects a clock, inject it into EVERY clock that bounds the number.
  v1.477.2 (test-only): GitHub run 43225505243 passed the same test only on
  its retry (the log keeps no failure text for a retried test). The lane
  reads a step's end AFTER the step's bookkeeping (`registry.invoke` awaits
  the ledger row), and the test let each read set the ONE shared clock to
  its own end after a real sleep: when the shorter read's ledger write took
  longer than the 0.2 s between the two ends, the longer read moved the
  clock first and both reads were measured at the pair's length. Now nothing
  sleeps: both reads wait until both are running, the shorter moves the
  clock to its end, and the longer moves it on only once the LANE has read
  the shorter one's end (`_LaneClock`: the lane's own view of the clock, so
  a read of it during a round IS a step being measured; the reply clock
  keeps the plain `_StepClock`). A wait that is only ever spent when
  something is broken (`ORDER_WAIT_S`) fails the test in words ("did not
  run together", "the lane never read its clock…"). Reproduced on an idle PC
  by holding that one ledger write 0.35 s (a scratch plugin wrapping
  `ToolRegistry._ledger_executed`): the old shape reads 399 ms twice, the
  new one passes with it held 2 s; 8 mutations, all red (six of the lane /
  reply clock / round, two of the test's own gate). A fake clock that two
  steps running together both move is moved in the order the code under
  test READS it, never in the order sleeps end.

- **A coding-session reply is a reply the whole app can see; a failed one
  has a press; the view's name lives in ONE constant** (v1.389.0, the v1.388.0
  re-audit's harness gaps; a doer + a reviewer that found 9 more defects).
  RUNNING: `durable_turns.running_replies` / `reply_counts` / `reply_titles`
  read MEMORY (titles: one primary-key read of the title column); `TurnLog.
  open_asks` COPIES `_asks` first — `GET /chat/turns/running` is a sync route,
  so it reads the loop's registry from a worker thread, and a live dict raised
  mid-iteration and the listing came back EMPTY. `/health.replies {chat,
  build}` carries counts only, never titles; the orb counts them only while
  online (the last /health survives an outage); `ui.status_line` and
  `system_map._status` count a Build reply, never as a chat; Running now
  lists it ("Coding session · 12 min", "· Waiting on you") linking
  `/terminals?session=<id>`. No Session rows. FAILURE: the reply line keeps
  `errorTitle`, `errorDetail` (≤ 2,000, through `failure_words.
  mask_failure_text`, which adds a bare Bearer value and AIza / xai- / gsk_ /
  hf_ keys to the shared mask — the live frame of BOTH chat lanes reads it
  too), `errorKind` sign_in | key | refusal and `errorProvider`
  (`harness/sessions.LINE_KEYS` + the decoder). `HarnessFailureLine` + `lib/
  buildRetry`: Retry on the NEWEST failed or cut-off reply only, never while
  a reply runs, on an archived session, on a refusal, or beside a limit card
  or a wait; `QueuedItem.resend` carries a Claude Code command BY NAME and
  skips the "/" reading (a "/model…" skill question opened the menu; a
  command was lost when the list could not be read). `BUILD_TERMINAL_EVENT`
  makes a same-page "Finish signing in there" link re-read Build's terminal
  list. `failure_words.this_place` / `GENERIC_FIX_BUILD`: a Build turn's fix
  says "this session". RECEIPT: `lib/receiptFacts` decodes approved / refit /
  adapted / remembered / folderRules / threadRefs for the coding session AND
  PaneChat (which saves them on its reply). NAME: `lib/buildWords.ts`
  `CODING_VIEW_NAME` "Coding sessions" / `CODING_SESSION_LABEL` /
  `BUILD_BLURB`; every reader imports them and a pin refuses the words spelled
  elsewhere — a different name is a one-line change. "harness" is a retired
  word for the scans; internal ids, routes, test ids, `ij.build.shape`
  "harness" and the `harness/` package are unchanged, and "IJ harness" is a
  search alias. An ALLOW / PENDING piece that is only a retired word is
  refused (one silenced a whole file). "Summarize" replaced "Compact";
  `/compact` keeps Claude Code's name. PLAN TAB: `HarnessPanes.keptPlan` —
  the live plan, else the newest reply's saved `plan` through `replyPlan` (the
  reply's own reading), else the step detail (cut at 300 chars — the cause of
  "No plan yet" beside an 11-step plan), else the approved plan; a reply's
  plan beats an approved one only when written at or after `approvedAt`.
  OPEN: "Build session" in the bell, desktop notices and daemon sentences (the
  one-notice fixture holds them equal — a separate sweep); the orb says
  Working while a session is parked on the user; a retried question is saved
  twice; the shared `detections.redact.mask` still misses the bare Bearer
  and the four key shapes; never driven in the packaged app. Pins:
  `tests/test_harness_gaps_{running,failure_receipt,names,review}_v1389.py`,
  `tests/test_build_fix_words_v1389.py`, `dashboard/__tests__/{harness-gaps-
  running,harness-gaps-failure-receipt,pane-chat-receipt,harness-gaps-plan-
  pane,harness-gaps-names,harness-gaps-review,harness-gaps-review-orb,
  harness-gaps-review-map}-v1389.test.tsx`.

- **The finish check sees files a reply changed THROUGH COMMANDS: a folder
  snapshot beside the journal** (v1.387.1; found by the Phase 2 benchmark —
  both of Jarvis-on-Claude's remaining failures wrote `calc.py` with a bash
  heredoc, so the journal saw no change and the check never ran; a doer + a
  reviewer that found 6 more defects). `harness/folder_changes.py`: `take`
  records (size, mtime_ns) under the session folder (list_files' pruning +
  caps, 5,000 files; the project map's 3 s deadline; `walk_bounded` waits ≤
  deadline + 2 s then falls back, the reason said; never follows links /
  junctions; `SOURCE_DOT_DIRS` like `.github` ARE walked; EVERY nested
  `.gitignore` read via `ignore_for` — GitIgnore's 64-folder cap had made the
  user's own checkout fall back every time); taken in `finish.start` off the
  loop after the ledger mark, before any tool; a second walk only after an
  unjournaled writer ran (`UNJOURNALED_TOOLS`; `_MUTATING_TOOLS` is split
  between it and `JOURNALED_TOOLS`, pinned); created/changed/deleted, moves
  paired; caches, coverage, `*.tsbuildinfo`, `.eslintcache`, gitignored never
  count; `HARNESS_CALLED_AS` ("finish check", "project check") never the
  reply's work. Command-made files are listed APART with keep words ("…leave
  these as they are; remove one only if you wrote it yourself as a scratch
  file…"), files the request names never listed, a "gone" section; the
  description file is read when the reply BEGINS; a carry-on compares with the
  kept first snapshot (≤ 4 sessions, in memory) or says why not; `removed`/
  `kept` still count file-tool files only. TEST PART: `finish.strip_heredocs`
  removes heredoc BODIES under bash before the length check and the split (a
  body line `pytest -q` was read as a test); `_drop_comment` (`cd x # note &&
  pytest` ran only the cd and reported "passed"). HONESTY: a folder snapshot
  can prove a write, NEVER its absence (ignored / pruned / outside-folder
  writes are unseen), so both lanes' creation-honesty and claimed-write notes
  stay SILENT after any tool that may have written a local file without a file
  tool (`chat_turn._writes_local_files_unnamed`, `ran_local_writer` reads the
  live registry's read-only flags; a Build turn also `may_have_written()`);
  change claims keep the v1.351.0 rule byte for byte. OPEN: whether real
  models follow the keep / remove words; a server left writing un-ignored logs
  makes later command replies "changed"; the project map's own walk has no
  stall bound. Pins: `tests/test_{folder_changes,finish_shell_writes,
  finish_shell_replay,finish_shell_writes_review}_v1387.py` (+
  `fixtures/finish_shell_replays_v1387.json`); moved on purpose:
  `test_chat_lanes_v13510`, `test_file_claim_honesty_v11532`,
  `test_finish_check_review_v1386`, `test_bash_dialect_v1386`.

- **A Build turn starts with a PROJECT MAP; the project's OWN checkers run
  after an edit as GATED calls that need a person; a made-up tool name is a
  refusal, not a dead turn** (v1.387.0, IJ harness part 3 Phase 3; a doer +
  a reviewer that found 6 more defects). THE COUNT that decided the scope
  (Phase 2 trajectories, 126 pairs): Jarvis on Claude 5.32 calls/run vs
  Claude Code 5.02 (+5.8%; 0.23 are the finish check's) — the plan's "within
  20%" was already met; left: listing 0.57/run, re-reads of a 12,000-cut
  result. `harness/project_map.py`: tree to 3 levels + top-level names (Python
  `ast`, JS/TS a bounded line scan that says it can miss names), never runs
  project code, never follows links/junctions, heavy + hidden folders pruned,
  3 s walk deadline + entry cap said, 2% of the window at 4 chars/token ≤
  8,000 chars (~2,560 unknown window), shrinks depth then entries and says
  so; names read only at levels a nameless tree could fit
  (`_names_depth`) within `NAMES_DEADLINE_S` 1.5 s (a 4,800-file tree took
  88 s before the review), an unfinished map never cached; promptguard; built
  ONCE per user message via to_thread, appended LAST in `HarnessTurn.block()`
  (before the planner), NOT in the fingerprint; `route.harness.map` receipt.
  `harness/checkers.py`: ruff / mypy / eslint / tsc ONLY when the project's
  config AND program exist (`.venv`/`venv`, `node_modules/.bin`, else the
  FULL PATH `shutil.which` found — a bare name let cmd.exe run a planted
  `ruff.bat` from the folder; a path with cmd metacharacters is not used),
  never installs, ≤ 50 changed files, safe names only, 60 s, findings ≤ 20
  lines / 2,000 chars attached to the edit's tool message; run as a call of
  the turn's command tool (`shell`, `process_start` under gpt) through the
  REAL `registry.invoke`, ledgered `called_as "project check"`, ALWAYS in
  cmd (never Git bash); `ProjectCheckers.needs_a_person` (the v1.386.1 rule)
  → a card even under yolo unless a grant wider than one call covers the
  tool; ONE batch card per round under Ask first; Local only once-only;
  never under low trust / plan mode / plain chat / after a failed edit;
  `apply_patch` read from `patch` (Codex sessions never got a checker
  before). `read_file(paths=[…])` ≤ 20 files / 30,000 chars, each through
  `safe_path` + `fs_read_ok`, left-out line FIRST, never cached, a single
  `path` byte-identical, shown only for Jarvis's own read_file (generic, gpt;
  Claude's `Read` is one path); `registry` `required_alternatives` (a null
  `paths` is "missing required: path"); `detections/ledger.to_events` emits
  one event per file (a multi-read of `.env` was invisible). Harness tool
  results keep max(12,000, min(32,000, window×4×0.25)) chars, head 2/3 +
  tail 1/3 with a note; plain chat keeps [:12000] byte for byte (both lanes).
  UNKNOWN TOOLS: `claude_native` `assemble` no longer raises; the call goes
  to the lane (registry name if offered, else the model's own name minus
  `mcp__ij__`) and the registry refuses it ("unknown tool" / "not armed");
  `tool_use`/`tool_result` stay paired for the next request; harness turns
  add "(The tools you can call in this reply: …)"; Codex's answer lists its
  tools. OPEN: cmd.exe's current-folder lookup also affects the model's own
  commands and folder checks (`NoDefaultCurrentDirectoryInExePath` is not set
  by the app — a decision for the user: setting it breaks `script.bat` typed
  without `.\`); a denied `shell` alone still leaves `process_start`; tsc
  checks the whole project (≤ 60 s per editing round); worktree sessions do
  not see the main checkout's `.venv`/`node_modules`; whether models use the
  map and `paths` is unmeasured. Pins:
  `tests/test_harness_{unknown_tool,multi_read,project_map,checkers,replay,phase3_review}_v1387.py`
  (+ `tests/_fakes/harness_lane.py`, `fixtures/harness_replay_v1387.json`).

- **The claude dialect's `Bash` RUNS IN BASH on Windows; wherever cmd.exe
  still runs a model's command, a `;` guard refuses the destructive case**
  (v1.386.2; found by the part 3 Phase 1 benchmark: 7 of Jarvis-on-Claude's 11
  failed runs, incl. `rm -f server.port; python server.py & …` deleting
  `server.py` in 2 of 3 repeats — cmd does not split on `;`, so Git's `rm` got
  the rest of the line as file names; a doer + a reviewer that found 6 more
  defects). `sandbox/git_bash.py` finds Git for Windows' bash
  (`CLAUDE_CODE_GIT_BASH_PATH`, else Git's own folder via `git.exe`, else
  Program Files / x86 / `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\Git` / scoop incl.
  SCOOP/SCOOP_GLOBAL/ProgramData); NEVER System32 / SysWOW64 / Sysnative /
  WindowsApps / `wsl.exe` (path and realpath), NEVER a bare PATH `bash`;
  verified once (`--version` says GNU bash, bounded), cached, a miss rechecked
  after 60 s, Rescan forgets. The command rides env `IJ_BASH_COMMAND` with the
  constant line `export -n IJ_BASH_COMMAND; eval "$IJ_BASH_COMMAND"` (MSYS's
  argv parser mangles `\\` and globs; a 40 M-char command arrived byte-exact;
  cap 1,000,000; a NUL is refused in words). ONLY `ToolContext.shell_syntax`
  ("bash", set by `HarnessTurn.ctx_for` for `DialectTool.shell_syntax`, i.e.
  Claude's `Bash` → shell/process_start) takes the bash path
  (`NativeSandbox.run(bash=)`, same Build env / cwd / timeout / keep_windows
  Job / `_as_text` / hidden console); plain chat, agents, schedules, helpers
  (a decision: their words say cmd), the generic / gpt / qwen dialects and a
  folder's own `[[checks]]` keep cmd BYTE-FOR-BYTE (pinned). Bash runs with
  `procs` `breakaway_ok=False` (MSYS2 starts every Windows program with
  CREATE_BREAKAWAY_FROM_JOB, so a bash command's python escaped the tree
  kill) and through `procs.run_to_files` (output to private delete-on-close
  temp files, the call waits for bash, not the pipe — Claude Code captures the
  same way), so `server &` returns at once and the server is owned by the
  session until `end_session`; every other caller keeps `procs.run`. The
  finish check keeps each command's syntax and re-runs a Bash test command in
  bash (a resumed reply reads it back from `called_as`). `sandbox/cmd_guard.py`
  judges the FIRST line (cmd /c runs only that), split at `& && | ||`, and
  refuses an unquoted `;` inside a non-last word of a guarded program (Git's
  file commands, sed/perl `-i`, del/copy/move/md/rd/ren, xcopy/robocopy, git
  rm/mv/checkout/restore/clean/reset/stash, installers, formatters and
  `--fix`/`-w`/`-i` linters, through xargs/sudo/env/start/call/`cmd /c`,
  npx/uvx/`… run`/`python -m`); a `;` ending a redirect's file name counts too;
  ordinary cmd lines run (`set PATH=…;…`, quoted `;`, `python -c "a; b"`).
  The git guard joins `\`/`^` line continuations (`git \⏎push -f` force-pushed
  uncarded on the old code). Hints tell the truth: the POSIX hint reads the
  PATH the line ran with (it told a model Git's `cp` did not exist right after
  it ran, and the model copied twice), cmd habits in bash (`type`, `findstr
  /i`, `cmd /c`) get one hint, `start /b` in bash is refused (it opened an
  error window). Bench identity gains `command_shell`. OPEN: after a daemon
  CRASH a kept `&` job keeps running (the v1.333.0 keep-windows rule); a kept
  job's later output grows a temp file until it ends; the guard reads text
  only (`for` bodies, scripts, variables unseen); Codex on Windows uses
  PowerShell and the gpt dialect still runs cmd (proposal, needs a benchmark);
  not driven in the packaged app or on a PC without Git (stubbed). Pins:
  `tests/test_{bash_dialect,bash_dialect_review,cmd_semicolon_guard,git_bash}_v1386.py`;
  moved on purpose: `test_build_command_shell_ask_v1381` (`cmd //c` in bash).

- **A Build reply that changed files CHECKS ITS OWN WORK once before it ends,
  through the same gates as any call** (v1.386.0, IJ harness part 3 Phase 2,
  `docs/IJ-HARNESS-PLAN-3.md`, the user's decision 6; a doer + a reviewer that
  found 17 more defects, each red first and mutation-checked).
  `harness/finish.py` (`FinishCheck`): a harness component in the DEFAULT
  layer, `.ironjarvis/harness/finish.toml` (`enabled`, `tests`, `new_files`,
  `request`; all on when absent; a voided layer = default on). WHEN: a harness
  turn about to end on its final text whose CHANGES come from the ledger (a
  rowid mark at turn start; helper sessions' writes via
  `options.build_session`; a carry-on turn counts the whole reply from the
  question it continues), not cut for length, with ≥ 2 rounds left; never on
  error / refusal / Stop / step limit / plain chat / a pane chat; at most once.
  ONE note, ONE extra round: (a) TESTS = the folder's `[[checks]]` for the
  changed files, else the TEST PART of the last command the model ran that
  STARTED (`_never_ran` skips a refused one; segments split at unquoted
  `&&`/`||`/`;`/lone `&`/newlines, only before an unquoted `|`; `$(…)`,
  backticks, `<(…)` never re-run; `python -c` ends the read; `--version`,
  `--collect-only`, `--no-run`, `-DskipTests`, watch modes are NOT test
  runs; a docs-named command must say test/check/spec/e2e/verify and not
  remove/fetch/ship), run as a synthetic `ij-finish-N` call of the turn's
  command tool (`shell`, `process_start` under GPT) through the lane's own
  round and the REAL `registry.invoke` (`called_as "finish check"`); a folder
  check (or a test only the folder names) is judged as approve-for-me even
  under yolo (`needs_a_person`: a card unless a grant wider than one call
  covers the tool); low trust = "not run: low trust"; verdict ONLY from the
  exit status, a timeout = failed; `after` ("then fixed") only from a later
  `&&`-chained, pipe-free run that covers the check's own tests
  (`status_is_tests`, `_covers`). (b) NEW FILES = the journal's created
  entries still present, not named in the request, not ignored (`GitIgnore`:
  nested `.gitignore` + `.git/info/exclude`; global excludes not read); a
  reply that ran a command is told commands may have made other files. (c)
  THE REQUEST as points + the points of a written description it names (read
  as it was BEFORE the reply, promptguard-scanned). The note rides after the
  round; the stream lane sends a "\n\n" token; the answer's "N. met:" lines
  are dropped from the SAVED reply (unmet lines and the closing sentence
  stay). The check's failures never count toward the repeated-call limit.
  RECORD `finish {tests, command, removed, kept, points, unmet, after[, why]}`
  LAST on the done frame / POST answer and the Build reply line
  (`harness/sessions.LINE_KEYS`; `chat_append.LINE_KEYS` is unchanged — it
  refuses Build threads), dashboard `lib/stepWords.finishWords` →
  `work-line-finish`. CODEX: `providers/turn_scope.py` — both lanes open a
  scope while a note can still be sent; inside it `CodexNativeAdapter` keeps a
  session whose turn ENDED, keyed by the exact conversation + tools (never a
  helper's run), and the note continues the same thread with `turn/start`
  (measured with the fake app-server: 2 process starts + 10,354 chars
  replayed → 1 process, the note alone); outside a scope nothing changes.
  The bench records `extra.finish` and hashes `finish.py` into its identity.
  OPEN: the Codex continuation not yet seen live (a refusal falls back to the
  replay); Stop waits for a running check command; the card does not say
  "finish check"; shell-only writes are not changes (a git-status snapshot is
  the later fix); after-edit plugin checks never fire for `write_file`;
  denying `shell` alone leaves `process_start`. Pins:
  `tests/test_finish_{check,lanes,replay,check_review,claude_shape,codex_continue}_v1386.py`
  (+ `fixtures/finish_replays_v1386.json`, built from the two lost runs'
  trajectories), `dashboard/__tests__/finish-check-v1386.test.tsx`; moved on
  purpose: `test_build_turns_v1367`, `test_harness_rewind_v1370`,
  `test_harness_bench_v1363`, `test_build_command_shell_review_v1381`,
  `test_build_checks_v1367`, `test_chat_mcp_error_fence_v1342`.
  v1.386.0 WAS NOT PUBLISHED (Tests and Release both red on
  `test_finish_lanes_v1386::test_failed_tests_keep_the_reply_working...`,
  `after` read "failed"): the scripted model wrote `X = 1`, the tests ran,
  and its fix to `X = 2` landed in the SAME SECOND with the SAME SIZE, so
  Python reused the stale `.pyc` (it checks only the whole-second mtime and
  the size) and the re-run still failed. THIS PC SETS
  `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1` in its environment and CI does not, so a
  stale-bytecode failure can never show here: reproduce a CI-only Python
  failure with `env -u PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE`. The test's wrong value is
  now `X = 11` (a different size); a copy holding one fixed mtime across
  every run failed on the old shape and passes on the new. v1.386.1 ships
  it all.

- **A Build reply works until it is DONE: no fixed step limit, never stopped
  for being unwatched, resumed from where the page was, the plan on the
  right** (v1.385.0; a USER BUG: a reply ran 117 minutes — Opus 5.5 high,
  60 rounds, 606,863 output tokens, 120 tool calls, nearly all reads — hit
  the 60-round `build_round_budget` and answered "Nothing is built yet", and
  a return mid-reply replayed the whole turn and jumped; a doer + a reviewer
  that found 6 more defects, each red first and mutation-checked).
  - **STEPS.** `config.build_round_budget` defaults to 0 = no limit
    (`routes/build.build_step_limit()` → (rounds, no_limit)), with a safety
    stop at `harness/mode.BUILD_SAFETY_ROUNDS` = 1000 (the agent run's own
    `max_steps` ceiling). The schema range is 0..1000, and
    `chat_turn.MAX_TURN_ROUNDS` is 1000 (the validator only): plain chat,
    POST, agents and the bench keep their budgets, pinned. At a limit, a
    HARNESS turn gets `OUT_OF_STEPS_BUILD_INSTRUCTION` (a progress report,
    never "nothing was done") and no "_Note: stopped after…_". `step_limit`
    rides the frame and answer only when the caller set a budget and the turn
    was cut. The reply line keeps `stepLimit` (`chat_append.LINE_KEYS`), and
    the page shows the line plus **Keep going** (sends "continue"; newest
    reply only, only when nothing runs). The bench and refine proposer get
    the same Build wording at their limit ("Keep going" is untrue there;
    grading unaffected).
  - **NEVER ORPHANED.** A Build turn's `should_stop` is `_never_orphaned`;
    chat turns keep `durable_turns.stop_predicate`'s 1-hour guard (both
    pinned). A turn parked on an unanswered card waits forever. It holds no
    account lease (leases are per model call), no helper slot and no
    process, but it counts as in flight, so "Install when idle" never sees
    the app idle.
  - **RESUME.** `GET /chat/turns/{id}/stream?ids=1&after=<i>:<c>` (no params
    = byte-identical): `TurnLog.add` stores a frame, then tags it
    `id: <index>:<text length>`. `frames_after` returns the rest of frame i
    from char c plus later frames, or None (→ whole replay). `resume
    {resumed, running, started_at}` … `caught_up` bracket the stored part,
    and a record replay is always whole. The property is pinned at every cut
    of real recorded logs, on both the daemon and the page. A long record is
    persisted every max(1 s, size ÷ 64 KB/s): rewriting a 2–7 MB
    `frames_json` every second was about 8 GB an hour.
  - **THE PAGE.** `lib/turnCache.ts` (bounded, ≤ 8 kept replies) holds the
    running reply's seed, folds, last position and the question
    (`page.pending`), plus the session view, scroll, folder list and Build
    view. It paints at mount and resumes once the session read confirms the
    same turn runs. Frames between `resume` and `caught_up` are folded
    undrawn, then drawn once. A run-generation guard and follow-once guard
    apply. The list drops kept replies of sessions not running.
    `useChatStream` takes `ids`/`resume`/`seed` and a rejoin's real
    `started_at`.
  - **STEPS ON THE LINE.** `_ReplyWatch` keeps the first 60 steps with detail
    and the rest compact, up to `MAX_COUNTED_STEPS` = 2000.
  - **PLAN.** `LivePlan` is `xl:hidden`; the pane's live plan is
    `hidden xl:block`, with a note below xl. The Plan tab dot (sr-only "(a new
    plan)") never switches tabs.
  - **GUIDANCE.** The DEFAULT layer adds one change at a time (checked before
    reading for the next) and independent reads in one round. `compose`
    drops the change sentence in plan mode, and no dialect is told reads run
    "at the same time" (GPT's do not). `CONCURRENT_READ_TOOLS` gains `grep`
    and `glob`. Measured with sonnet medium: no measurable speed change. Each
    round already sends the turn's earlier signed thinking (evidence pin, the
    real adapter on a fake CLI).
  - **OPEN.**
    - Mid-turn, `_fit_turn_transcript` drops the oldest rounds silently and
      no model-written compaction runs, so a very long reply may re-read.
    - The context meter measures the history at the turn's start.
    - Round texts are glued with no space; a plan never updated reads "2 of
      4 done" under a finished answer.
  - **Pins:** `tests/test_build_{long_replies,resume,faster_rounds,thinking_continuity,long_replies_review}_v1384.py`,
    `dashboard/__tests__/{build-resume,build-resume-kinds,ij-harness-long-replies,ij-harness-long-replies-review,ij-harness-turn-cache-drop-review}-v1384.test.ts(x)`
    (+ fixtures `build-resume*-v1384.json`).
  - **v1.385.1, a defect the BENCHMARK found.** On v1.385.0, Jarvis on Codex
    (gpt-6-astra, medium) failed 26 of 65 scored runs, and all 26 ended "The
    working folder is read-only in this session, so I couldn't apply the
    fix". Codex shows the model the thread's sandbox, and
    `codex_native.protocol` starts every thread `sandbox: read-only` +
    `approvalPolicy: untrusted` on purpose: that posture keeps Codex's OWN
    built-ins from acting (and `start_problem` still refuses a thread that
    does not confirm it). `thread_start_params` now appends
    `protocol.SANDBOX_NOTE` to `baseInstructions` whenever the turn HAS
    dynamic tools: the posture covers only Codex's switched-off built-ins,
    Iron Jarvis carries out the function tools (they really change files),
    and Iron Jarvis asks the user itself. A text-only turn gets no note, and
    the posture is unchanged. Pin: `tests/test_codex_sandbox_note_v13851.py`
    (`test_codex_native_v1365`'s exact-instructions assert moved on purpose).
    Not yet seen live: the ChatGPT account on this PC hit its Codex usage
    limit during that run (8 runs said so; 61 never started under the
    breaker's cooldown).

- **The benchmark can RANK: repeats with a range, the exact model, a
  `frontier` tier, and every run worked ALONE** (v1.384.0, IJ harness part 3
  Phase 1, `docs/IJ-HARNESS-PLAN-3.md`; a doer + a reviewer whose 4 hunters
  wrote 54 more decoys — 38 PASSED the checks as first shipped — and 19
  correct alternatives, 4 of which the checks wrongly FAILED; all fixed and
  pinned. The pin files say v1383: the target moved when the lighthouse splash
  took v1.383.0). REPEATS: `--repeats N` (1–10, `bench.check_repeats`); every
  task's repeat 1 before any repeat 2; repeat 1 keeps `runs/<id>`, the old
  fingerprint and NO `repeat` key (a one-run results folder is reused), repeat
  k≥2 is `runs/<id>.r<k>` with `fingerprint(task, k)`; `ledger.run_key`/
  `record_key`, `verify_plan` per run; the report gives each repeat its own
  denominator, the mean, the range and passed every time / some / never /
  never scored, and counts only repeats with a scored run ("over 1 of 2
  repeats (1 had no scored run)"); each invocation writes `plan.json`, and a
  task's runs from repeats beyond the latest plan are `stale`, left out of
  every number, and SAID. MODEL: `--model` is REQUIRED (Jarvis also
  `--provider`), and `default`/`subscription`/`auto` are refused
  (`MODEL_NOT_A_MODEL` — the Claude adapter reads them as the CLI's own
  default); `--effort` (medium) is passed to every runner (Claude
  `--model/--effort`, Codex `-m` + `-c model_reasoning_effort="<e>"` as TOML,
  Jarvis only when `providers.reasoning.supports`, else "not offered"); records
  carry `model_passed`, `effort_passed`, `model_reported` (Claude Code's
  `system/init`; Jarvis's route ALIAS; Codex 0.157.0 reports none) and
  `effort_reported`; `report.model_matches` treats an alias word, a `[1m]`
  suffix, a provider prefix, `-latest` and 4.5/4-5 as one model and two
  snapshot DATES as two; "default" is never written as a model. The identity
  gains effort only when set, so a v1.382.1 folder run without an effort is
  reused, and one run through the CLI (default effort medium) is not —
  accepted: the old run used the user's own effort setting. FRONTIER: 12 tasks
  (`tasks/frontier-*`, ≤ 200 files each, offline), each with a solution and
  a decoy; `[setup]` in `task.toml` runs `python -I <script> <work> <task
  seed>` through `procs.run` with a scrubbed env and isolated git config
  (`setup_step.py`), a failure is "did not run" (`setup_failed` =
  infrastructure); the browser task's check uses headless Edge via Playwright
  and exits 3 (unscored) when no browser OR the driver cannot start; core and
  hard content hashes are pinned. GRADING: `grading.remove_hidden_check` also
  removes the COMPILED check (`python -I` ignores PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE;
  the reviewer decoded the whole legacy-callers answer key from a `.pyc` left
  in `runs/<task>/grade/`). ISOLATION: the agent works in
  `<system temp>/ij-bench-runs/<16 hex>/run/work` (`agent_runs_root`; the
  extra `run/` level keeps a concurrent bench's run out of `../..` too); after
  the run `work/` is MOVED into `runs/<key>/work` and grading, the marker
  hash, reuse and the tripwire run there as before; the outside snapshot
  covers the agent folder; `owner.json` (pid + start time, never a path) lets
  `sweep_agent_roots` remove only folders whose owner is gone; `HIDDEN_PATHS`
  + `hide_paths` + `hidden_environ()` keep the bench root out of a CLI child's
  env (PWD/OLDPWD dropped) and a Jarvis turn's Build env and prompt. A task id
  ending `.r<n>` is refused. Known: an absolute bench root under %TEMP% is
  still reachable by a determined agent; a `uv run` venv path names the source
  tree; the price task does not force debugging the running server; the Codex
  effort flag has not yet been seen taking effect. Pins:
  `tests/test_harness_bench_{repeats_model,frontier,review,isolation}_v1383.py`,
  fixtures `harness_bench_decoys_more/`, `harness_bench_alternatives/`. SAME
  RELEASE (test-only): `test_mcp_stderr_tail_v1342`'s chattering child gets a
  5 s transport deadline, not 0.5 s — a Python child on a loaded PC took
  1.2–2.1 s to print its first line, so the kept tail was empty every busy run.
  v1.384.1 (v1.384.0's Tests AND Release gates both failed; nothing was
  published): GitHub's Windows runners set TEMP to an 8.3 SHORT name
  (`C:\Users\RUNNER~1\...`), so the agent's prompt named its folder by the
  short form while `Path.resolve()` gave the long one —
  `test_the_claude_runner_runs_headless_in_the_copy` failed there and passed
  here (this PC's TEMP is long). `bench.agent_runs_root` now RESOLVES its
  parent, so the agent sees one name. Pin: `tests/test_harness_bench_short_
  temp_v13841.py` makes a short-named temp folder on this PC (skips where a
  volume makes no 8.3 names) and reproduced CI's exact failure on the old
  code. RULE: a path the agent is TOLD and a path a test RESOLVES must come
  from the same normalisation; on Windows TEMP may be an 8.3 name.
  v1.384.2 (test-only; v1.384.1's gates failed, nothing published): the fix
  broke `test_the_default_agent_folder_is_under_the_system_temp`, which still
  compared with the temp folder AS NAMED. To reproduce the runners before a
  push, run the touched files with TMP/TEMP set to the short name of a
  long-named folder (`GetShortPathNameW`); all 382 benchmark tests pass so.
  v1.384.3 (test-only; v1.384.2's Release gate failed, Tests passed, nothing
  published): `test_approval_words_v13520`'s `_ask_and_list` read the pending
  listing ONCE after its publish spy saw the ask, but the spy records BEFORE
  the real publish writes the event row the listing reads (the v1.373.1
  shape) — IndexError on the loaded runner. It now polls until the ask is
  listed (15 s bound). Holding the real publish 0.5 s reproduced it: old
  helper 3 failed, new 3 passed. 18 test files read
  `/chat/approvals/pending`; any that reads it once right after an ask is
  the same risk.
  v1.399.4 (test-only, THE SWEEP): v1.399.3's Tests gate went red on the
  same shape in `test_plan_contract_v1350` (`AssertionError: []`). All 22
  files that read the listing were run with the `approval.requested` row's
