  that stays empty; `lib/emptySessions` is still written (pinned by
  `ij-harness-start-review-v1412`) but nothing reads it.

- **One approval means one approval in Chat › Code: a session grant reaches
  the RUNNING reply; read-only commands, Iron Jarvis's own checks under
  "Don't ask", and a test that already passed after the last edit never
  card; every card says who asks and why** (v1.417.0, fix plan C1.2-C1.6,
  C1.7/C2.4 daemon halves; the user's decisions D1, D2). (1) GRANTS:
  `harness/session_grants.attach_turn` registers a coding session's reply
  with its OWN `armed_grant` / `card_grants` (the very sets every card
  decision reads; `routes/chat._attach_session_turn`, detached in the
  stream's `finally`) and an `accept` (never low trust / tainted, never
  `always_ask`, never a name a folder rule asks about or denies); `note()`
  reaches attached replies, `release_helpers` wakes the reply's ORDINARY
  card it covers (`watch_ask`; never once-only, priced, Local only, night)
  with "conversation", actor "grant" (not listed in `approved`). Grants made
  BEFORE the reply arrive through its `granted_tools` (attach adds nothing —
  a test's earlier grant must not leak). A lead's "conversation" on `shell`
  also widens `armed_grant` by `COMMAND_FAMILY` (minus folder-ruled names).
  (2) `core/auto_approve.read_only_command` / `read_only_call` +
  `READ_ONLY_COMMANDS` (git status/diff/log/show/branch listing/rev-parse,
  ls, dir, cat, type, rg, grep, find, head, tail, wc, pwd, which, where,
  echo, `node|python|python3|py|uv|git --version`): both shells' readings, `_UNSAFE_TEST_CHARS`,
  no `<>;&(){}`/newline, a `|` only into another listed program, no escape,
  no assignment, no path leaving the folder (a `cwd` too), git never
  `--output`/`--ext-diff`/a branch name, rg never `--pre`/`-z`, find never
  `-exec`/`-delete`, and NO `<prog><any PATHEXT ext>` in the folder
  (cmd.exe runs it first). PASS rule `read_only_command` in `_classify`
  for `ASK_FIRST` ("approve_for_me", decided for nothing else), Accept edits
  and Auto (before the judge); the stream lane's pre-pass runs for Ask first
  too; the POST lane gets it through `classify` under Accept edits / Auto
  only (under Ask first it arms no ask-tier tool). (3) The judge: one round's
  judgements `asyncio.gather`ed, `JUDGE_TIMEOUT_S` 3, `judge_prompt(first=,
  reply=)` (byte-identical without them; `first_request_of`,
  `last_reply_of`). (4) FINISH: `ProjectCheckers.needs_a_person(call,
  posture=)` is False under "yolo"; the finish check's folder/non-builtin
  test keeps its card. `_test_calls` does not re-run the model's last test
  command when it ran after the last possible change (`_may_change_files`:
  journaled edits, non-test non-read-only commands, unjournaled writers) and
  exited 0 with its own status its tests' (`_reusable`) — record `reused:
  true` (only then; `RECORD_KEYS`, `saved_record`), note "did not run it
  again". (5) `unwatched_mode(pressed=)`: a person's press (the plan run —
  `start_build_turn(pressed=True)` from `run_approved_plan` — or a
  `resume_of` wait without `auto`) keeps Accept edits / Auto for
  `PRESS_STARTERS` (person, wait); night, CI, restart still fall back.
  (6) Every stream-lane `approval` frame carries `asker` ("model" |
  "iron_jarvis" — the finish check's or a checker's own call) and `why`
  (`auto_approve.card_why`, ≤ 400: own call → plan door → a spend check's
  sentence → once-only reason → Local only / night → the eased rule kept
  (`_auto_kept`, incl. `JUDGE_ASKED_RULE`) → strict → `NOT_GRANTED_WHY`).
  Every round / token / thinking / tool_call / reset frame from the check
  starting until the model's answer to the note has streamed carries
  `check: "finish"` (`_ev`, `_fin_live`; `FinishCheck.answer_heard`); the
  model's fixes after its answer are untagged. MOVED ON PURPOSE: the yolo
  checker pins in `test_harness_phase3_review_v1387` (now Ask first, plus a
  yolo-runs-it pin), five finish scenarios (test before the edit, so the
  re-run still happens) in `test_finish_lanes_v1386`,
  `test_finish_shell_writes_v1387`, `test_team_review_finish_v1401`, and
  "ls -la" → "npm run build" as the judged command in the v14060 auto
  pins, and every stream-lane pin that used `echo …` as "a cheap command
  that cards" now runs `python -c "print(…)"` (`echo` is read-only now:
  chat_approvals_v1187 (`_shell_call_stream`'s default), approvals_office,
  standing_grants, session_grants, build_turns, night_shift, build_review,
  build_stall, build_phone_ask(+review), build_long_replies_review,
  wave4_chat_recovery, chat_lanes, chat_waiting, chat_receipts,
  autonomy_metrics(+review)); a NEW pin that needs a card uses a command
  that is not read-only. Pins: `tests/test_{one_approval_turn,read_only_commands,
  auto_judge_together,finish_skip_rerun,checker_card_why,
  posture_kept_on_press}_v1417.py`. REVIEW (each red first, mutation-checked;
  `tests/test_{read_only,one_approval}_review_v1417.py`): the pass let a
  command RUN a program with no card under the DEFAULT posture — (a) rg
  `--hostname-bin=PROG` (ripgrep 14 runs it): every `--hostname*` refused
  beside `--pre*`/`-z`; (b) `npm|npx|pnpm|yarn --version` are corepack shims
  that fetch and run the version a `package.json` names: off the
  version list; (c) cmd.exe tries EVERY PATHEXT extension in the current
  folder (`.vbs`, `.js`, `.wsf`…), not only exe/bat/cmd/com: the planted check
  reads the default list plus `PATHEXT`; (d) a `\host\share` cwd was
  RESOLVED (an SMB connection, the v1.379.0 lesson): `_leaves_folder(cwd)`
  first, lexically; (e) git in a SUB-folder `cwd` may meet another
  repository whose config runs programs (fsmonitor, diff/textconv drivers):
  only in the session folder itself (`git_ok`); (f) under Ask first a folder's
  own "ask" rule on the tool kept no card: the lane skips the pass for it;
  (g) no workspace known ⇒ never read-only; (h) a Local only reply attaches
  no session grant (`_attach_session_turn`). Survivors, on purpose: adding
  `rm` to the list (`command_destructive` still refuses it) and dropping the
  lane's own `armed_grant` family widen (the attached turn's `_reach` makes
  the same widen). KNOWN, not changed: an existing symlink/junction inside the
  folder is followed by `cat`/`type` (reads are broad, as `read_file`); `dir
  /s` under Git's bash lists drive S:; `tail -f` runs until its timeout;
  identical calls in one Auto round are judged twice (cost only); the judge
  now reads the assistant's own last words (a prompt-injected reply can argue
  its case — the request is still the person's); helpers (agent runtime)
  under Ask first get no read-only pass.

- **The project's checkers run ONCE per reply, at the finish check, inside
  one 15 s budget; tsc only on a small TypeScript project** (v1.420.0, fix
  plan C3.3, the user's decision D1). Before, after EACH round that edited a
  .py/.js/.ts file the checkers ran inside that round (60 s each, mypy
  following imports, tsc over the whole project, a card per round). Now
  `harness/checkers.ProjectCheckers.schedule_finish(changed, low_trust=)` is
  called by `FinishCheck.prepare` (`_checker_calls`, after the test calls,
  BEFORE the team review) with `_changed_rels` (the journal's and
  `folder_changes`' files — the union of the reply's change); both lanes'
  per-round `checkers.schedule` block is GONE (`schedule`, `_edited`,
  `_patch_files` and the per-edit bookkeeping were deleted; `note()` /
  `target()` stay as no-ops because the lanes still call them, so
  `attach_check` does nothing for a checker). ONCE: `scheduled` flag; never
  in low trust. BUDGET: `CHECKERS_BUDGET_S` 15 shared — the clock
  (`checkers._clock`, a test seam) starts at the first checker's `prepare`,
  each run's `timeout_s` (or `wait_ms`) is what is LEFT, a run with less than
  `MIN_RUN_S` left is not started; a cut run (`timed_out` / still running)
  and an unstarted one are both "late" and NAMED in the note ("Check (ruff)
  not finished in time: the project's checkers share 15 s, so it was
  stopped."), never a silent miss. TSC: only when a changed file is
  TypeScript AND `_count_typescript` (the map's pruning: `_SKIP_DIRS`,
  dot-folders, no links; off the loop; `TSC_COUNT_DEADLINE_S` 1.5) finds at
  most `TSC_MAX_FILES` 400, else skipped with `TSC_TOO_LARGE` "project too
  large for a quick check" (or `TSC_NOT_COUNTED`). MYPY:
  `--follow-imports=silent`, changed files only. FINDINGS go into the finish
  note's `CHECKERS_HEAD` "## The project's checkers" section (each head line
  Iron Jarvis's words, the lines under it through the lane's own fence —
  `note_text(fence)`) plus `CHECKERS_ASK`; the record gains `checkers {ran,
  skipped: [{checker, why}], late}` ONLY when they had something to do
  (`RECORD_KEYS` puts it BEFORE `review`, which stays last;
  `saved_checkers` types and caps it for the reply line). CARDS (Wave 1,
  unchanged): Don't ask or a session grant on the carrier → none; else ONE
  card for the finish round's own calls (test re-run + checkers), asker
  `iron_jarvis`. A folder's own `plugins.toml` checks keep their per-round
  behaviour and cards. A folder whose `finish.toml` says `enabled = false`
  now runs NO checker (they are part of the check); a reply cut off, at its
  last two rounds, or that changed nothing runs none either. Pins:
  `tests/test_checkers_once_v1420.py` (17 mutations, all red); moved on
  purpose: `test_harness_checkers_v1387` (the fixture turns the finish check
  on with its other parts off — `checkers_only_finish` — and the pins read
  `finish_note` where they read the edit's result; "an edit that did not
  apply" hands out no step; the on-loop pin drives `schedule_finish`),
  `test_harness_phase3_review_v1387` ("the next round's checker" became "a
  two-round editing reply runs its checker once behind one card"),
  `test_checker_card_why_v1417`.

- **A scroll box is the POSITIONING box for what it holds** (v1.420.2, a USER
  BUG with two screenshots: in Chat › Code the window's own scrollbar
  scrolled the whole view away into ~10 screens of nothing). Measured on a
  read-only copy of the user's session in a real browser: ONE `sr-only`
  label (Tailwind's is `position:absolute`; a live plan's step words) at the
  end of a long transcript made `<main>` 9,094 px tall in a 950 px window.
  `harness-transcript` was not positioned, so the label's containing block
  was `ij-harness-view` (`relative`): the transcript neither clipped nor
  scrolled it, and it stretched the page to the conversation's length. Now
  `relative`: `harness-transcript` (the fix), `harness-no-session`, the
  Tonight / Last night boxes, `code-transcript` (Build › Code was NOT
  affected — the layout's `relative overflow-hidden` div above `<main>`
  clipped it — a guard) and each PlanChecklist row (a guard on every screen
  that shows a plan). The other Code-view scroll boxes (HarnessSidebar,
  FilesPanel, HarnessTimeline, the panes bodies) hold no unparented
  absolutes. A NEW scroll box that can hold an `absolute`/`sr-only`
  descendant takes `relative` too. The fixed height is xl-only; below xl the
  view stacks and the page scrolls by design. `e2e/code-page-height.spec.ts`
  (1913 × 990, 1280 × 800; both Code views; Chat › Code's Plan, Timeline,
  Changes, Session and Files panes) checks THE RULE — no drawn absolute or
  fixed element inside a scroll box has its containing block (walked:
  position, transform, filter, backdrop-filter, perspective, contain — never
  offsetParent, which SVG lacks) outside it — and THE SYMPTOM (a 6,000 px
  transcript ending in an `sr-only` label keeps `<main>` within 1 px of the
  window); e2e does not run in CI, so
  `dashboard/__tests__/code-page-height-v1420-2.test.tsx` pins the classes.
  SAME RELEASE: the shell tool's note TO THE MODEL about running outside the
  sandbox (`_COMPACT_ADVISORY`, `[warning] ` + `_NO_CONFINEMENT_WARNING`,
  always the FRONT of the output) led every command row's preview and the
  session page's tools table. `lib/stepDetail.withoutRuntimeNotes` takes it
  off the first non-empty line ONLY (the same words further down are the
  command's own — a `cat` of a log — and never mark it unconfined);
  `RUNTIME_NOTE_LINES` is pinned word-for-word against the Python constants
  by `tests/test_runtime_note_words_v14202.py`. WorkLine's `firstLine`,
  `StepDetailPanel` (says `UNCONFINED_WORDS` once) and the session page use
  it. The model's output is unchanged. Reword a note in
  `sandbox/shell_tool.py` and that pin fails until the dashboard list
  matches. Not done: the daemon's structured `data["confinement"]` is not on
  the tool_call frame, so the screen still reads the prose.

- **Build › Code shows the work AS IT HAPPENS: a row from the first written
  character, helpers nested, the tool's own words, the rest of a long output
  kept in the LOG** (v1.418.0, fix plan C4.2-C4.5, lane F). STREAMING
  (`hosted/claude.py`): a `tool_use` `content_block_start` emits the running
  row at once with `partial: true`; `input_json_delta` keeps a 4,000-char
  HEAD (fields read by `partial_input`: path, command, description …, for the
  title) and a 2,000-char TAIL (`events.clip_tail`, the row's `input`) and
  re-emits at most every `PARTIAL_EVERY_S` (0.25 s, by the clock — the golden
  test sets it to 3600 so only the start row is pinned); the block's end
  sends what came since unless the whole input already arrived (the
  assistant frame comes BEFORE `content_block_stop`). Codex
  (`hosted/codex.py`): `item/commandExecution/outputDelta` {itemId, delta}
  (READ from codex 0.157.0's `generate-json-schema`) updates the running row's
  `output` (tail, `partial`, `OUTPUT_EVERY_S`); Pi's running output rows carry
  `partial` too. NESTING: every frame's `parent_tool_use_id` → `parent` on
  `text`/`text_done`/`thinking`/`tool` (`_with_parent`); stream state is per
  parent (`_streams`); a helper's TodoWrite is no plan. MEASURED in the ONE
  real run (claude 2.1.316, haiku, Full access, `tests/fixtures/build_app_
  v1409/claude_full_mode_edit_v1418.json`, masked): Claude Code names the tool
  `Agent`, runs it in the BACKGROUND (its tool_result is "Async agent
  launched" at once), and the helper's messages come WHOLE (no stream deltas)
  with `parent_tool_use_id` — so a helper's thinking is said from the whole
  message only when that message id was never streamed (`_streamed`). A
  helper's title is `Helper: <its description>`. DASHBOARD
  (`lib/buildApp.foldConversation(events, extra, root)`): a `parent`-tagged
  event folds into its top helper's row as `children` (recursive fold; a
  missing helper row gets a placeholder `task` row), the child edits count
  toward the receipt's files; `CodeConversation` draws a helper closed with
  `code-helper-count` + `code-helper-final` (its last text) and opens onto the
  nested rows. SYSTEM (C4.3): `--include-hook-events` is passed (READ from
  2.1.316's bundle; without it only SessionStart/Setup hooks are emitted;
  the flag is in the 2.1.310 and 2.1.311 bundles too and hook events streamed
  headless by 2.1.204 per Claude Code's changelog, while `--permission-prompts`
  (already passed) came in 2.1.259 — a CLI too old for the hook flag is
  refused by that one first, so it adds no new way to fail to start; pinned
  as riding together); `claude.system_notice` maps
  hook_response (silent when it succeeded and said nothing), stop_hook_summary
  (only when it prevented continuation), task_started (background only, no
  tool_use_id), task_notification, notification, informational/local_command/
  model_*fallback, permission_denied, api_error to a `notice` in plain words
  (`words.SYS_*`); `status: compacting` (sent every 30 s while it lasts) is
  said ONCE (`_compacting`), a failed compaction says why; every other
  control request is refused AND said (`words.CONTROL_REFUSED`). Thinking
  items carry `live` (the newest thing a running turn said) and `seconds`
  (to the next event). KEPT TEXTS (C4.4): `events.with_full` cuts a finished
  step's `input`/`output` for the screen (300 / 1,500) and, when cut, adds
  `more`, `input_chars`/`output_chars` and the texts (≤ 20,000, masked
  BEFORE the cut) under `_full`; `EventLog.append` POPS `_full` off the
  caller's dict IN PLACE (the service hands the same dict to the watchers
  right after — `_emit` calls `live.log.append` BEFORE `turn.add`; keep that
  order) and writes it as `full`; `EventLog.read` and `coalesce_into` drop
  it; `EventLog.item_text(item)` reads it back (`{item, input, output}`; the
  newest event of the step that kept texts) for `GET /build/app/sessions/
  {id}/events/{item}` (lane G's route); `CodeConversation`'s `onExpand(item)`
  prop (BuildCodeView supplies it) is pressed from "Show all N characters".
  A command's title is Claude Code's own `description` (`tool_title`), the
  command its `subtitle`; a card whose description equals its title shows no
  detail twice. DIFFS (C4.5): the live log held 619 of 619 Full-access edits
  with NO diff, yet the one direct run had `tool_use_result.structuredPatch`
  on the main Edit — the cause under Iron Jarvis's own flags is NOT known; a
  finished edit/write with no diff from the meta gets one from its own input
  (`_edit_diff`; a Write's line is 1) and an Edit's line read off the file
  OFF THE LOOP, ≤ `LINE_READ_BYTES` 2 MB (`line_of`), sent as a second `tool`
  event with `line`. Pins: `tests/test_build_app_stream_rows_v1418.py` (20
  mutations, all red), `dashboard/__tests__/build-app-stream-rows-v1418.
  test.tsx` (18 mutations, all red), the v1409 golden's
  `claude_full_mode_helper` and `claude_hooks_and_long_output` cases
  (`claude_hooks_v1418.json` is built from recorded frame shapes). Moved on
  purpose: the v1409/v1411 goldens (start rows, titles from descriptions,
  `partial`). REVIEW (independent, each red first): (1) `line_of` read a
  model-written path as given — `\\host\share\x`, or a link that leads there,
  is an SMB connection (the v1.379.0 lesson): it refuses the spelling
  (`code_editor._network_or_device`) before any lstat/open and walks links
  one hop at a time (`code_editor._walk_links`); (2) the line read's task was
  held by nobody (asyncio keeps tasks weakly): `ClaudeDriver._line_tasks`;
  (3) every update of a step being written went into the LOG (~4 a second for
  as long as a long Write takes) and the turn's replay: `EventLog.append`
  writes a step's FIRST `partial` row only (a crash still shows the step) and
  then its finished row (`_partial_logged`), and `coalesce_into` lets a newer
  row of the same step replace a trailing `partial` one
  (`_replaces_partial`); followers still get every update; (4)
  `reuseItems` now reuses an open helper's unchanged `children` (they drew
  again on every frame).

- **An ended task is worded by what it WAS; a foreground command's end says
  nothing** (v1.477.2, a USER REPORT: a wall of grey "A helper finished: cd
  "C:/…" && grep …" lines buried the one line that mattered). `words.
  SYS_TASK_DONE` said "A helper …" for EVERY `task_notification`. READ from
  the 2.1.322 program on this PC (read-only): the notification carries NO
  `task_type` and NO `is_backgrounded` (`{task_id, tool_use_id, status,
  summary, output_file, usage}`), so both come from the task's `task_started`;
  and a Bash / PowerShell command that runs in the FOREGROUND for 2 s
  (`UTo=2000`) is registered as a `local_bash` task (`is_backgrounded:
  false`, description = the command's description, else the command line)
  whose end is announced with that description as the `summary`. In the
  reported session 121 of 185 "A helper finished" lines were such commands,
  13 were real background commands and 19 were helpers. A command sent to the
  background later says `task_updated {patch: {is_backgrounded: true}}`; an
  ended background command's summary is Claude Code's own sentence
  (`Background command "…" completed (exit code 0)` / `failed with exit code
  N`), and a STOPPED one sends only its description. THE RULE
  (`claude.task_ended_words(obj, task_type=, background=)`, called by
  `system_notice`): a helper (`is_helper_task`) keeps "A helper finished /
  failed / was stopped: …"; a background command is "A background command …",
  or Claude Code's own sentence as it is when the summary starts with
  `words.SYS_TASK_OWN_SENTENCE` (never for a helper, whose answer may start
  with those words); a foreground command WITH a step of its own
  (`tool_use_id`) says NOTHING (its step row shows how it ended); a command
  whose place was never said, or with no step, is "A command …"; any other
  type is "A background task …" / "A task …"; a task whose start was never
  seen is "A task …" (never a helper, never quiet). Words: `words.
  SYS_TASK_SUBJECT` / `SYS_TASK_ENDED` / `SYS_TASK_DONE`. THE DRIVER keeps
  `ClaudeDriver._task_place` (task_id → True background / False foreground;
  NO entry = Claude Code never said, e.g. a state kept by an older version —
  read as not known, so such a command's end is SAID, never kept quiet),
  written only by `_note_task_place` (a real bool, an open task) from
  `task_started.is_backgrounded`, `task_updated.patch.is_backgrounded` and
  membership in `background_tasks_changed`; type and place are read BEFORE
  the pop at `task_notification`; cleared in `on_exit`; kept across a restart
  by `state()`. Unchanged on purpose: the open-task count, `background_
  helpers`, the Session tab's list and Stop, and `_alone` (still set by every
  notification with no reply open, a quiet one's too).
  The recorded helper run and the dashboard contract fixture did not move.
  Lines already in a session's log keep their old words. Pins:
  `tests/test_build_app_task_ended_words_v1477.py`; moved on purpose:
  `test_build_app_stream_rows_v1418` (the pure function, told nothing, says
  "A task finished").
  v1.481.1 (the owner: "address the risk"): OLD lines are read at SERVING
  time, never rewritten on disk. `hosted/old_task_lines.reword(events,
  context)` (pure; copies a changed line; a list with no old line comes back
  as the same object) takes a notice that fully matches the v1.418.0 ..
  v1.477.1 template `A helper (finished|failed|was stopped): …` and: says
  Claude Code's own `Background command "` sentence as it is; makes its
  `Monitor "` sentence "A background task …"; matches the summary to the
  NEAREST command step BEFORE it by title (the description, cut at 90 with
  "…": prefix match), subtitle (the command) or a "Run <cmd>" title — then
  the step's own result in one of Claude Code's five background sentences
  (`BACKGROUND_RESULT`, read from its program) or its input's
  `"run_in_background": true` makes it "A background command …", an ending
  row (done/failed/stopped) in the record HIDES it, no ending row makes it
  "A command …"; anything else is left exactly as written. MEASURED on the
  owner's three logs (618 old lines): 523 hidden, 24 own sentences, 2
  background, 1 monitor, 68 left — every one of those a helper's report or
  Claude Code's own agent words. `history.file_view`, `read_page` and
  `TailLog.served` call it with up to `REWORD_CONTEXT` (400) events from just
  before what they serve (`TailLog.seed` keeps them in `self.context`; a
  memory window whose in-process events overflowed takes no seed context),
  and subtract hidden lines from `events_total` (no phantom "earlier
  steps"); cursors are computed on the raw events first. Without the context
  4 of 311 lines in a view kept the old words; with it 0. `adopt._turn_back`
  rewords a rejoined turn's replay. A NEW reader of a hosted log that shows
  notices must go through these readers. Pins:
  `tests/test_build_app_old_task_lines_v1480.py`.
  SAME RELEASE (test-only): the branch's first GitHub run went red in shard
  10, all eight `test_code_handoff_v1474` tests reading "Claude Code is not
  signed in on this PC" (and again on retry); alone they passed. Adding a test
  file moved which files shared the shard, and an earlier test on the worker
  had left a "claude is signed out" answer in `cli_auth.DEFAULT_PROBE` (one
  object per process; real code writes it when a stand-in CLI refuses a
  sign-in). The Build › Code tool list reads it through `harnesses()`.
  `tests/conftest._sign_in_answers_do_not_cross_tests` now gives EVERY test an
  empty `_cache` / `_inflight` and puts the old ones back after (the v1.396.1
  lesson, now suite-wide). Reproduced by a plugin that marks claude signed out
  at `pytest_configure`: the old conftest gives CI's exact 8 failures, the new
  one 14 passed.

- **Build › Code's history STAYS (paged by a cursor, the tool's own
  transcript merged in), a view is served from MEMORY, and a message sent
  while it works WAITS** (v1.418.0, fix plan C4.6/C4.8/C4.11, lane G).
  MEMORY (C4.11): `hosted/history.TailLog` (an `EventLog` SUBCLASS — never
  edit `events.py` for this) is every live session's log (`_live`); each
  append stamps `seq` (`next_seq`: microseconds since 1970, strictly
  increasing, so larger than any earlier run's) on the LOG's copy only —
  the caller's dict (which `turn.add` sends to followers) is untouched,
  EXCEPT that `FULL_KEY` comes off it in place (the v1.418.0 C4.4 rule:
  watchers never see the kept texts) — and keeps the newest 2,000 coalesced
  entries in memory. `seed()` is the ONE file read: only the lines BEFORE
  the one carrying this process's first seq EXACTLY (a batch's first event
  opens its first line; never "seq >= first" — a seq is the wall clock and
  after the clock moved back an earlier run's newer lines vanished), so the
  writer thread's batching can never double an event. `service.view` → `_view_events`:
  `served(limit)` from memory, else the file (`history.file_view`, which
  also serves sessions this daemon never drove); a read past what memory
  holds falls back to the file. The view's events never carry `seq` or the
  log's `full` (`history.public`). CURSOR (C4.6): a view and a page answer
  `older` = `s<seq>` (an event with a seq — survives the memory merging
  deltas the file kept apart; `_cut_of` finds that seq EXACTLY, the first
  larger one only when no line carries it) or `i<index>:<dropped>` (an older event; a
  later trim moves it back); `GET /build/app/sessions/{id}/events?before=
  <cursor>&limit=` (`events_page`, ≤ 2,000, 400 `BAD_CURSOR` in words).
  `GET …/events/{item}` = `EventLog.item_text` (Lane F's, C4.4), else
  `history.item_text`; BuildCodeView hands CodeConversation `onExpand`
  (`expandStep` → `decodeItemText`). TRANSCRIPT (C4.6): `history.transcript_events` reads
  the tool's OWN saved conversation READ-ONLY — Claude Code's
  `projects/*/<id>.jsonl` (the `history` package's bounded `_records` +
  `claude.tool_kind/tool_title`), Codex's rollout (`history.codex.
  map_events`), Pi's session file, OpenCode's DB `mode=ro` (`part` JOIN
  `message`, newest 6,000 parts) — as `user`/`text_done`/`tool` events
  (`imported: true`, masked, newest 2,000), cached by (size, mtime_ns; +
  the `-wal` for OpenCode). Read ONLY for a session whose log is empty or
  that `mark_outside` named (`<home>/hosted/<id>/outside.json`, written by
  create-with-`harness_session_id`, `open_terminal`, `from_terminal`) —
  never for a session that lived only in the view. The file is FOUND once
  (`_SOURCES`, then a stat per view); a Pi sessionDir spelled as a network
  path (a project's `.pi/settings.json` can name one) is refused by its TEXT
  unless the session's folder is itself on a share (the v1.379.0 rule).
  `merge_transcript`:
  entries inside a log turn's window (user → done, ±`EDGE_S` 2 s; an open
  turn runs on) are that turn's and dropped; the rest merged by time; with
  the view cut (`older`), entries older than its oldest are dropped. QUEUE
  (C4.8): `hosted/queued.SessionQueue` (memory first, `<home>/hosted/<id>/
  queue.json` behind it, newest write wins, ≤ 5 items); `POST …/queue`
  (409 `code: not_working` when no reply runs or it is ending — checked
  AFTER the awaits), `POST …/queue/{qid}/remove`, `POST …/queue/take`;
  `_end_turn` → `_after_turn_queue`: `completed` and no account-limit card →
  `_send_queued` starts the first as the next turn (a refusal puts it back,
  holds the queue, says `QUEUE_NOT_SENT`); anything else HOLDS. The view
  says `queued` + `queue_held` = the queue's OWN hold (never "nothing is
  running": between a clean end and the next waiting message's turn nothing
  runs, and the page pulled the rest back into the box); a queue read back
  from disk is an EARLIER run's and is HELD at load (`_queue_of`; a shutdown
  returns before writing the hold), so it is never sent behind a new reply.
  The waiting message goes with the session's CURRENT model and mode. `commands_for` now keeps `argument_hint`. DASHBOARD
  (`BuildCodeView`, words in `lib/buildAppHistory.ts`): `earlierEvents` +
  `older` (`code-earlier-count` "N earlier steps", `code-load-earlier`,
  scroll kept); `allEvents` feeds the fold and the panes. Enter while
  running QUEUES (`queueIt`; `code-queued`, × `code-queued-remove-<id>`);
  Shift+Enter and the arrow send a NOTE (`sendNote`, waits for uploads) —
  the v1412 steer pins moved to Shift+Enter on purpose; a held queue is
  taken back into the box on load (`takeBack`, by `sessionId`); after a turn
  ends, a running turn the window has NOT followed (`followedTurnsRef`) is
  followed — the daemon's queued turn — never in a loop. Esc stops when no
  menu is open; Shift+Tab → `nextMode` (ordinary modes only, never Full
  access). `/` menu: `slashAt(text, caret)` (start of ANY line), `SLASH_ROWS`
  20, hints (`commandHints`). Chips while running: model open (its default
  row held for Claude Code, Pi, OpenCode — a new start), effort held only for
  Claude Code (`effortWhileRunning`), titles say why. OPEN: Codex's own `/`
  prompts are not listed (needs a driver change); a page loaded earlier is
  dropped on a rejoin; "Load earlier" pages the log only (transcript entries
  older than a cut view are not shown). Pins: `tests/test_build_app_{history,
  queue}_v1418.py` (14 mutations, all red), `dashboard/__tests__/build-app-
  history-keys-v1418.test.tsx` (14 mutations, all red), e2e `dashboard/e2e/
  build-code-keys.spec.ts` (not run here). REVIEW (each red first,
  `tests/test_build_app_{history,queue}_v1418.py`): the restart queue held,
  `queue_held` no longer "nothing running", the exact-seq cursor and seed,
  the Pi share, the transcript file found once. Known (older than this
  wave): `service._conversation_files` lists a Pi sessionDir a project names
  even when it is a share.

- **Build › Code's OpenCode never CHANGES a rule of the user's (looser or
  tighter); Codex on Windows has no "edits free, commands ask"** (v1.420.0,
  C1.9 + C1.10). OPENCODE (`hosted/opencode.permission_env`): a key merged
  from `OPENCODE_PERMISSION` lands AFTER the config's and the last match
  wins, so a key of ours over a tool the user names overrides THEIR rule — a
  `"bash": "allow"` became a card per command, and a user `"*": "deny"` had
  the built-in tools named `ask` after it. Now a tool is named only when no
  user key matches it (`_users`: OpenCode's own `Wildcard.match`, `si` flags,
  read from the 1.18.18 program — `"web*"` counts), `"*"` only when every
  user rule says EXACTLY what it would (`_equal_to`, every pattern), and a
  user `"*"` names every tool (env `{}` → no `OPENCODE_PERMISSION`,
  `OPENCODE_RULES_DECIDE`; Plan: `OPENCODE_RULES_DECIDE_PLAN`). Plan's edit
  refusal goes under a key the user does NOT have (`_fresh_key`: `edit`,
  else `EDIT`/`Edit`/… — names match case-blind, a key they have keeps its
  PLACE), so it is the last edit rule: a user `"edit": …` followed by
  `"*": "allow"` used to undo it (Plan edited). `task` is `allow` in Ask
  first and Auto edits (the helper's session gets only the parent's folder
  rules and refusals; its steps meet the same config rules and still ask);
  Plan keeps OpenCode's own. "ALLOW FOR THIS SESSION" ACROSS A MODE CHANGE:
  OpenCode's reply "always" pushes `{permission, pattern, allow}` per the
  request's `always` patterns onto an in-PROCESS list (opencode.db's
  `permission` table is a newer mechanism and stays empty), and ACP neither
  sends those patterns nor takes rules. So `OpenCodeDriver.answered` keeps
  each allow_session in `SESSION_ALWAYS[ses_id]` (memory, ≤ 64 × 200) and
  the NEW process's `_ask` answers the same request "always" itself (one
  notice, `OPENCODE_ALWAYS_CARRIED`): the whole tool for
  `ALWAYS_COVERS_ALL` (edit/write/patch, webfetch, websearch, task, glob,
  grep, lsp, todowrite — each `always: ["*"]` in OpenCode's source), the
  EXACT command for bash (OpenCode's own is the command's prefix, never
  sent; a command with `*`/`?` is never kept — a wildcard), nothing for any
  other tool; matched only by the tool's FIRST record (a request whose title
  is the command is a card). Never via `OPENCODE_PERMISSION` (it would sit
  in the user's rules' place). CODEX (`hosted/codex.py` docstring, from
  0.157.0's `generate-json-schema --experimental` + `--help`, no model
  call): `approvalPolicy` untrusted | on-request | never | `{granular}`
  (switches whole prompt KINDS off), sandbox/permission profiles choose what
  may be touched, never when to ask; on Windows only full disk access runs a
  command, where on-request asks nothing and untrusted asks before every file
  change AND command — no such posture, the mode table is unchanged, and
  `HIDDEN_MODES` says the cause in plain words. Codex DOES have a plan mode
  (`collaborationMode` {mode: plan}, EXPERIMENTAL): the Plan reason says
  so. NOT BUILT (the user's call): answering
  `item/fileChange/requestApproval` ourselves under untrusted would give
  "edits free, commands ask" by Iron Jarvis's own rule (needs Chat's
  `sensitive_kind` checks). Pins: `tests/test_build_app_{opencode_rules,
  codex_posture}_v1420.py` (14 mutations, all red); moved on purpose:
  `test_build_app_opencode_v1413` (task granted; a user `"bash": "allow"`
  kept) and `test_build_app_opencode_review_v1413` (a user edit rule equal
  to the mode no longer keeps `"*"` out in Plan).

- **A coding session's keys work from its PORTALED list; a long question's
  title is the first reply's words; the panes poll, open where the work is
  and collapse; Plan and the account are offered ONCE** (v1.420.0, fix plan
  C2.9-C2.11 and C4.10). KEYS: `lib/harnessKeys` — the session list
  (HarnessSidebar's nav) carries `HARNESS_LIST_ATTR`
  (`data-harness-keys-root`) and `pressIsInside` / the pointer watcher read
  `inHarness(node, root)` = the view's root OR a marked list (it is portaled
  into the app's sidebar, outside `ij-harness-view`, so Esc / Ctrl+N /
  Ctrl+L / Ctrl+K died after a click on a row); rows carry
  `HARNESS_ROW_ATTR` and ↑/↓ call `moveRowFocus` (no modifier; Enter is the
  row button's own click). TITLE: `harness/sessions.session_title(current,
  before, after)` is THE rule `append_lines` calls (a separate function; Lane
  J edits `history_of` in the same file): "New session" takes the question
  cut at 48 + "…"; when the question was cut AND the title is still exactly
  that cut question AND this append brings the FIRST reply (no assistant line
  before it, not an error), the reply's first sentence (fences, list marks,
  links, `*`/backticks and word-edge `_` dropped; cut at `.!?:;` + space)
  names it: 3-6 words, ≤ 48 chars at a word boundary, else the cut question
  stays. Deterministic, no model call; a typed title is never touched. PANES
  (`HarnessPanes`): Changes re-reads every `CHANGES_POLL_MS` (5 s) while
  `busy` (`useVisibleInterval`), plus the turn's end `refreshKey`; the first
  tab, when none is remembered, is `defaultPaneTab` = Changes when the newest
  reply's `documents` is non-empty (`lib/buildSessions.replyChangedFiles`),
  else Session — and the remembered tab is read in the FIRST render (an
  effect switching it mounted the Session tab for a frame and fired its
  reads); `PANES_LAYOUT_KEY` (`ij.harness.panes`, `{collapsed, wide}`):
  `harness-panes-toggle` collapses to a strip (the views unmount; a terminal
  parks), `harness-panes-width` picks 320/400 or 480/560 px from xl —
  the session's own aside only (`onLayout` absent in a split's shared
  column). TOOLBAR: the "Plan first" chip is gone (`harness-plan-mode`;
  `HARNESS_FIT_ORDER` lost "plan", the step stays in `FIT_STEPS`); Plan is
  PermissionChip's row. The header's `AccountChip` shows only with NO
  account ("Choose an account"); on an account the model chip names it and
  the header keeps quiet `account-chip-status` / `account-chip-usage` spans;
  "Make it the default" moved to the Session tab
  (`session-rail-make-default`, `onMakeDefault` through the memoised panes).
  UNDO: no `window.confirm` — the row's Undo (ChangesReview, not this lane's
  file) awaits a page question (`harness-undo-confirm`: "Undo it" /
  "Keep"); a second Undo answers the first "keep". BUILD › CODE
  (`BuildCodeView`): the row's ⋯ gains Rename (inline input, PATCH
  `{title}`, trimmed, once — `renameOpenRef`) and Delete (ConfirmButton, the
  DELETE route; its 409 for a running reply is said in `code-row-note`, the
  row stays); `code-session-search` filters the held list by title + folder,
  every word. Pins: `tests/test_build_session_title_v1420.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/{ij-harness-keys-list,ij-harness-panes-toolbar,
  build-app-sessions}-v1420.test.tsx` (20 mutations, 19 red; the survivor is
  the rename blur guard, which jsdom cannot reach: it fires no blur on an
  element's removal). Moved on purpose: `composer-one-row-v1400` (no plan
  chip/step), `ij-harness-model-accounts{,-review}-v1376`,
  `ij-harness-model-fold-v1376`, `ij-harness-session-v1369`,
  `limits-without-a-press-v1393` (the account is the model chip's),
  `ij-harness-panes-v1369`, `ij-harness-long-replies-v1384`,
  `harness-gaps-plan-pane-v1389`, `session-rail-v1404` (Plan is no longer
  the first tab), e2e `ij-harness.spec.ts`. Same lane: HarnessSession's two
  `vocabulary.test.ts` ALLOW lines were reworded ("its own copy of the
  folder", "this change can't be undone") and their entries deleted.

- **A team that works: a Build session's HELPERS run bash and see the
  session's standard, every member piece is recorded, the reviewer verifies
  with read-only git, and an agent run plans at its real window** (v1.421.0,
  Wave 1 of `docs/HARNESS-BUILD-AUDIT-2026-11-01.md`; the owner's decisions
  A1/A4 of 2026-11-01; three builders + three reviewers + a fixer).
  A1 (reverses v1.386.2's "helpers keep cmd"; a glm worker failed 182 of 742
  Linux-shaped commands in cmd.exe in one day): for `HELPER_ORIGIN` +
  `is_build_call`, the runtime decides ONCE per run, off the loop, through
  `command_env.helper_command_facts` (the one source of syntax and words):
  Windows + `git_bash.find()` → `self._shell_syntax[sid] = "bash"`, carried on
  every step's `ToolContext.shell_syntax`; `shell` gets bash's words with
  `native.HELPER_BACKGROUND_WORDS` (process_start, never
  `run_in_background`), `host_os_line(bash=True)`. No bash, another OS, any
  non-helper run, a chat's own helper: byte-identical cmd.
  `ToolInvocation.shell_syntax` (additive; the update rehearsal drops it on
  its older-table copy) records a command call's language for
  shell/process_start only (registry `_COMMAND_TOOLS`), and
  `finish.helper_commands` reads it so a worker's bash test re-runs in bash.
  The runtime is the ONE other writer of `shell_syntax=` beside
  `HarnessTurn.ctx_for` (pinned in `test_bash_dialect_v1386`). A test that
  drives a helper's `shell` pins the native runtime (`SandboxManager.get` →
  `NativeSandbox`): with Docker reachable the default policy runs it in a
  Linux container — run shell-touching files here with
  `DOCKER_HOST=tcp://127.0.0.1:9` (CI's state; `test_harness_helpers_v1372`'s
  two Stop tests and `test_finish_replay_v1386` fail with Docker up).
  A3: `harness/standard.HelperStandard` is built once per reply at the end of
  `_build_harness` (Build session, folder, act mode, a helper door in the
  gate): the lead's own rules text (never re-read), floor + folder
  guardrails, accepted lessons, the scanned matching `sub_agents` brief
  (member → asked agent → role/role-noun → type, ≤ 3,000 chars) and the
  finish check's items; `STANDARD_CAP` 16,000, and `for_helper(names,
  provider=, model=)` composes to `window_cap(window)` = 14% of the helper
  model's KNOWN window (a brief ≤ a quarter, a part under `MIN_PART_CHARS`
  200 left out, `WINDOW_CUT_LINE` says so; unknown or 32k+ windows keep the
  full section byte for byte; a budget ≤ 0 leaves parts out — `_shares`
  answers "no cap" there). It rides `helper_base(standard=)` in BOTH lanes
  into `HelperParent.standard`; `helper_definition` appends it BEFORE the
  worker rules, only with a folder and a `build:` session. The section rides
  the system prompt, which the planner cannot shrink (the old 16k section
  clipped a 4,096-window worker's piece to 400 chars). A7:
  `ComponentTree.helpers` picks `SUB_AGENTS_HAND_OUT` vs `SUB_AGENTS_SOLO`;
  a folder with no briefs renders identically (goldens unmoved). The
  benchmark's `harness_sha256` includes `harness/standard` and
  `harness/command_env`.
  A2: both helper doors call `model_team.piece_role(record, asked=, team=,
  base_type=)` → `(role, asked)`: an asked role the member holds, else no
  row; a session's reviewer `review`; a worker `read` for a base type in
  `helpers.READ_ONLY_TYPES`, else `code` if held, else its first role.
  `TeamRoleRun` gains additive `asked` (role|name) and `files_json`
  (`team_record.piece_files` from `outcome.session_result`, masked, ≤ 40;
  an older table gains both through `open_db`, pinned in a fresh
  interpreter). A named piece's task is unchanged (no `VERDICT_ASK`).
  `credit_review` never takes a named review row as its cut-off, and SKIPS a
  named piece whose session wrote no file (one "changes needed" counted two
  failed code pieces for a worker that only answered a question); role
  pieces are judged as before.
  A4 (the owner: the reviewer gets read-only commands AND names exactly
  what to fix for the lead to redistribute): `harness/git_read.py`
  (`git_read`; allow tier, read-only, untrusted output, masked, ≤ 30,000)
  is armed ONLY through `helpers.REVIEW_ALWAYS` in `helper_definition`'s
  reviewer-only branch (on no roster, not in `CODING_TOOLS`; permission row,
  run words, `gen_tool_words` + both fixtures done). IT IS A BLOCKLIST over
  git's huge option/config surface, and the review found nine more holes
  against real git 2.50 (each pinned with a trapped repo whose control run
  proves the trap fires, `tests/test_git_read_review_v14210.py`): `status
  -v` ran textconv; a filter name holding `=` cannot be emptied by `-c`
  (such a folder is refused; settings read with `-z`); `format.pretty` /
  `pretty.<alias>` with `%G?` ran gpg (every `gpg.*program` set empty); a
  short-option cluster like `-nO<prog>` ran the pager (git reads clusters
  LETTER BY LETTER); a submodule's `diff.external` via `diff.submodule=diff`
  or `--submodule` (`diff.submodule=short`, `submodule.recurse=false`
  forced); `core.worktree` outside the folder (the working folder must
  contain the session folder; a network path refused by its text);
  Iron Jarvis's protected folders inside the repository's top
  (`_place_problem`); `blame.ignoreRevsFile` echoing an outside file;
  `--remerge-diff` / `log.diffMerges=remerge` ran the merge driver
  (`log.diffMerges=separate` forced). `describe --dirty` writes the index
  (refused). Before widening git_read, re-run that trapped-repo harness.
  KNOWN: a junction inside the folder pointing out is followed by `blame
  --contents` / `grep --untracked`; a `.git` file's `gitdir:` elsewhere is
  read; `core.attributesFile` outside can leak invalid attribute names on a
  failing call's stderr; a user's global filter (Git LFS) still runs; a home
  folder that is itself a repository refuses git_read everywhere under it.
  `finish.review_brief(…, who)` ends with `REVIEW_FINDINGS_ASK` (`R<n>.
  <path>:<line> — … — …` above the UNCHANGED `VERDICT_ASK`; `FINDING_RX` is
  the stable prefix; `verdict_of` still reads the last line) and lists
  `who_changed_what` (file → member). The lead's note uses
  `report_for_lead`: a CLEAN report has its prose cut, findings and verdict
  whole; a FLAGGED report goes to the fence WHOLE (cutting first moved an
  instruction out of the scanned text, so the reply stayed at full trust);
  `TEAM_REVIEW_ASK` routes findings by name. E6: `core/approvals.run_place`
  reads the bare `build_session` helpers write (every helper card was
  surface `agent`, no thread); `eval/autonomy.compute` places OLD helper asks
  by the run's `options.build_session` and attempt asks by `_bare_thread`
  (the live DB's last week: 0 placed / 12 unattributed → 11 / 1, the
  sidebar's).
  A5: `agents/runtime.perceive_act` answers a tool call written as TEXT
  (`mode.named_text_tool_call`, only for a tool the step OFFERED) with the
  reply + `mode.TEXT_TOOL_CALL_NUDGE` as plain turns and goes on, never
  executing the text; with a step left, never twice in a row, ≤
  `TEXT_CALL_NUDGES_PER_RUN` (2) a run, counted as max(the breaker-state key
  "text_call_nudges", notes in the run's messages) so it spans both flat
  allotments, decomposed mini-loops and a resume. A call is read only from
  bare text or a fenced block that ENDS the reply: inline code and fences
  with text after them are quotations (`_call_text`; inline spans paired by
  index in `_blank_inline_code` — never a backtracking regex on a reply, it
  can be 200,000 chars and is read on the loop; one took 30 s on 80k).
  `core/nudges.TEXT_TOOL_CALL` is published by the runtime and both chat
  lanes (`chat_turn.note_text_tool_call`, lock-step); a new `NUDGE_KINDS`
  entry needs a `dashboard/lib/autonomyMetrics` SYSTEM_WORDS row. NEVER
  `import logging` inside a runtime method: it makes the name local to the
  whole method, and the brain block's warning raised `UnboundLocalError` and
  failed runs (`tests/test_run_body_logging_v14210.py`).
  D3: the window ladder's "endpoint" rung reads `fleet/windows.probed_window`
  off `platform.fleet_sampler` — vLLM `max_model_len`, else Ollama's loaded
  `context_length`, a LiteLLM alias followed to its deployment — below the
  gateway-stated window; unknown stays unknown; it never makes a request. A
  test of a fleet window builds the REAL registry + sampler
  (`tests/_fakes/fleet_window.py`), never a `nodes()[].models` fake (that

- **Never lose work: a coding reply waits out a passing outage and keeps its
  rounds when it fails; Stop stops the running step; Build › Code's tools live
  in a CODE HOST that outlives a restart; a cut or limited reply carries on by
  itself; a dev server survives the idle stop; a new terminal is never the
  Store pwsh** (v1.422.0, Wave 2 of `docs/HARNESS-BUILD-AUDIT-2026-11-01.md`:
  B1-B6 and C4.7; the owner's decision B3 = the large option; four builders +
  four reviewers + the coordinator's integration).
  B1 PATIENCE (Chat › Code, harness turns only; `harness/patience.py`): the
  stream lane's round call is `round_frames(_harness is not None, ...)`, the
  POST lane `patient_call` (lock-step); plain chat gets the generator itself,
  byte for byte. `why_waited` waits on overloaded, a 429 that is not the
  account's own limit, 5xx, timeout, interrupted, unreachable (never a `-cli`)
  and a breaker cooldown; never on a refusal, 401/403, an account limit
  (`_ACCOUNT_LIMIT_RX`: usage limit, session/weekly limit, "you've hit your",
  out of credits, `insufficient_quota`; never a bare "quota exceeded" —
  Gemini's per-minute quota is waited out), overflow, `no_failover`, other
  4xx, Stop. `WAITS_S` 5/15/30/60, Retry-After capped at 120 s, 240 s a round,
  same provider/model/account (the hold and pin ride the pump). A reply's
  FIRST model call (`first_call=completions == 0`, both lanes) is never waited
  out on the router's local not-answering refusal
  (`patience.local_not_answering`: unreachable/timeout/cooldown for a local
  provider) — v1.228.0's "refuses at once" holds; a later round waits a box
  that went away. Between tries: `reset {reason: "retry", drop: N}` (CODE
  POINTS; the page takes it back with `lib/buildPatience.dropCodePoints`, hook
  and session tap) then `provider_wait {state, words, attempt, of}`; no retry
  after the `final` frame (tools only run after it). Tries after the first are
  marked by `providers/retry_round`; the router puts `retry: n` on their
  `provider.failed` and the notifier says only the first. `tried_words` rides
  `error_payload`'s fix. THE SUITE TURNS PATIENCE OFF
  (`conftest._round_patience_off_for_bare_tests`, `WAITS_S = ()`); a pin opts
  in with `DEFAULT_WAITS_S`. A failed reply KEEPS ITS ROUNDS
  (`ReplyRecord.keep_failed` → key `build-failed:<turn>`, never read by the
  boot resume; not for a refusal, a limit card, attempts, or nothing past the
  question); the line gets `canContinue`; `BuildTurnBody.continue_turn` →
  `start_build_turn(resume_turn=, resume_failed=True, continuation=True,
  pressed=True)` (`FAILED_RESUME_NOTE`, `continuedFrom`); kept rounds belong to
  the session that kept them (`reply_record.load/claim(session_id=)`); Branch
  from here drops `canContinue`; every started turn and a session delete
  `drop_failed`. TRUST: a low-trust reply writes `RunTranscript.low_trust`
  with its rounds (`ReplyRecord.note_trust` before every save; additive
  nullable column) and both the restart's carry-on (`RESTART_LOW_TRUST`) and
  Continue (`CONTINUE_LOW_TRUST`, `chat_turn.failed_trust_keys` on both lanes'
  errors) start low. B2 STOP: `stall.run_step` races the turn's
  `TurnHandle.wait_stopped`; after `STOP_GRACE_S` (3 s) the step is cancelled
  (`_STOPPED[task] = STOP_TURN`; Stop the step's own reason wins), the
  registry writes `stop_note()`, the model gets `turn_stopped_result`; a step
  of a reply already stopped is never started (`run_step(not_started=)`,
  `not_started_result`, no ledger row); `race=False` for `deadline_exempt`
  tools (ask_user, delegate, spawn_agent); `process_start(until_exit)` kills
  its program on cancel, a background program survives. Plain chat's Stop
  still does not kill a tool (only harness steps run through `run_step`).
  B3 CODE HOST (`src/iron_jarvis/codehost.py`, reached only through
  `hosted/hostlink.py` — `RemoteProcess` has `HarnessProcess`'s shape;
  `hosted/adopt.py`: `state_blob` / `adopt_all` / `at_boot` /
  `close_unadopted` / `shutdown(keep=)`; `Driver.state()/restore()/
  after_restore()` re-arm what cannot be kept: ACP prompts, Pi's prompt,
  Codex's turn id, Claude's held end). A launcher runs `codehost --launch` so
  the host is never in the daemon's tree; the host holds the launcher's
  handle (`held_pid` via GetProcessId, logged "holding its launcher (pid N)")
  so its pid is never reused. NEVER `DETACHED_PROCESS`: `HOST_FLAGS =
  CREATE_NO_WINDOW | CREATE_NEW_PROCESS_GROUP` (a console-less host gave its
  child a visible Windows Terminal window, and closing it killed the host,
  0xC000013A). Tools start through `core.procs.spawn(breakaway_ok=False)` in
  a kill-on-close Job the host owns. One host per home (a named mutex, never
  a file lock in the home); `<home>/hosted/codehost.json` {pid, port, token,
  version, protocol}, loopback only, token checked, the hello's payload at
  most `MAX_HELLO_PAYLOAD` (4 KB) BEFORE the token is read; a host of another
  version is told to quit. Replay without duplicates: lines numbered, the
  event log's copy stamped `htool`/`hseq` (stripped from every view, page and
  stream); on re-attach lines ≤ shown are silent and writes ≤ written are
  dropped; card ids seeded per line (`ASK_SEED`); a user-caused write carries
  the state in the same frame. BOOT: `keep_ids` → `reconcile_cut_turns(home,
  keep)` → `adopt.at_boot(platform, keep)` (bounded by `BOOT_ADOPT_WAIT_S`
  60 s); `close_unadopted` stops the tool and closes in words every kept
  session not carried on, and (integration) `adopt.note_cut` records it as a
  restart's cut so the carry-on pass below takes it (the overflow fallback
  `_give_up` too). A link that BREAKS (`HostLink.deliberate` false) records
  its tools in `_LOST` and a fresh background link stops them; a start the
  host answered too late is told to stop. QUIT vs RESTART: the lifespan's end
  is a QUIT unless `POST /shutdown {"restart": true}` set
  `platform.code_host_keep` (nothing in production sends it; a watchdog or
  tray Restart KILLS the daemon, which keeps the host by itself); Quit and
  every update path end with the unspared `sweepOrphanDaemons()` +
  `installer.nsh`'s `taskkill /IM ironjarvis.exe`, so the installer is never
  blocked; the desktop's `quitCodeHost` asks first; only the watchdog's
  adopted-daemon sweep spares the host's pid. Orphan bounds: 60 s empty /
  10 min idle / 2 h busy-quiet / 3 h hard; 64 MiB per tool, then overflow →
  graceful stop + the CLI's resume. TESTS: `IRONJARVIS_CODE_HOST=off` is the
  suite default (`IJ_TEST_CODE_HOST=on` for parity; the session fixture quits
  every host at teardown); the host-only paths (RemoteProcess I/O, line-seeded
  card ids, snapshots, stamps, launcher/mutex/orphan rules, boot keep/adopt,
  MCP token restore) are covered ONLY by `tests/test_code_host_*_v14220.py`
  (`tests/_code_host_helpers_v14220.host_session`, pid-checked). KNOWN: the
  host handles start/attach on its one connection thread (a hung process
  creation would stall writes for every session); ~0.25 s of events can be
  missing at a crash and a card answered just before it can reappear.
  RELEASE CHECK: `"<install>\resources\daemon\ironjarvis.exe" codehost
  --launch <scratch home>` writes `<scratch>\hosted\codehost.json` naming a
  live `ironjarvis.exe` pid, `codehost.log` says "holding its launcher (pid
  N)", no window appears; end it with `taskkill /PID <pid> /T /F`.
  B3 FALLBACK + B5 (`hosted/carry_on.py`, `hosted/codex_move.py`,
  `routes/build_app_carry.py`): the boot `reconcile_cut_turns(home, keep=())`
  writes `cut.json` beside the log (turn, `waiting`, `carried`, `limit`,
  `cut_at` = the newest time on the turn's OWN events) and clears a kept
  session's undecided record (`forget_undecided`). `carry_on_after_restart`
  (first pass of `bg_tasks["build_app_carry"]`) carries each cut on ONCE
  through the tool's own resume (`CONTINUE_AFTER_RESTART`, `carry=`), only
  when `cut_at` is within `CUT_KEEP_DAYS` (7 == `reply_record.KEEP_DAYS`) AND
  at or after `<home>/hosted/carry_on_since.json` (written at the loop's first
  start; every cut v1.421.0 and earlier left, the update to v1.422.0
  included, is only offered: `WHY_BEFORE`); never a turn the USER stopped
  (`note_stopped`, written by `service.interrupt` before the tool is told),
  one that ended, a card/question open, a newer message, archived, unsaved,
  beside a limit, a session alive at pass time, Emergency stop (re-read at
  every start; `Orchestrator.stop_unattended` → `stop_unattended_turns` stops
  a running carry-on Iron Jarvis started), past the pool (every busy session
  counts); otherwise offered with `WHY_*` via `POST …/continue`; Retry of a
  carry-on that failed re-runs it hidden (`failed_carry`). LIMITS:
  `limits.json` {card, wait, chain}; a wait is armed only while
  `build_auto_resume` is on (off ends armed waits); at the reset it carries on
  (`CONTINUE_AFTER_RESET`) or moves within the account group (same vendor,

  refused move is not retried) through `continue_on(..., note=)`;
  `MAX_AUTO_IN_A_ROW` 4 counted before each start; `MAX_TRIES` refused starts
  → `GAVE_UP_NOTE`; Cancel `POST …/wait/cancel`; the page re-reads every
  `CODE_WAIT_REREAD_MS` (15 s) while a wait shows. Codex moves by copying its
  rollout file into the other `CODEX_HOME` (measured on codex 0.157.0 scratch
  homes; another ChatGPT account accepting the reasoning items is UNPROVEN);
  Grok's limit is the card and it only waits; Pi/OpenCode limits stay plain
  errors. B4 + C4.7 (`hosted/programs.py`): a program counts when it is in the
  tool's OWN tree (Job members ∪ `process.descendants`, minus the root; never
  by name), holds a TCP LISTEN socket and was born ≥ the CURRENT process's
  FIRST `tool` event − 2 s ("MCP servers never listen" is FALSE: the
  QuickBooks pack listens on 8765; the birth rule keeps such servers out).
  Every read on its own daemon thread (`_read_on_own_thread`, never the
  loop's shared pool), one at a time (reaper 5 s; the activity read 1.5 s,
  inside the desktop's 3 s). The reaper reads only sessions past 15 min and
  looks again after the read; a serving program keeps the session until 8 h
  with no turn and no word; the session looked at (a view read or the Code
  view's `POST …/seen` beat within 3 min; `CODE_SEEN_BEAT_MS` == `BEAT_S`)
  gets 60 min; the pool picks serving sessions last, looked-at next.
  `_said_stopped` names the programs a stop ended (masked; `short_argv` quotes
  a spaced word and hides a secret-named key's value whole) in the
  conversation, the row's `stopped_note` and the next start — at the idle
  stop, the pool, a restart, Open in terminal and Continue on another account
  (not archive, delete or Grok's Stop). `POST …/close` stops the tool and its
  programs, refused while it works on its own. `GET /system/activity` adds
  `code_serving`/`code_serving_tools`: `busy` only, never `working`; main.js
  `describeBusyWork` names them. B6 SHELLS: `terminals/shells.available_shells`
  puts a real (non-packaged, `app_alias.is_packaged`) PowerShell 7 from
  absolute local PATH folders or Program Files first — never `shutil.which`,
  which reads shares and the current folder — else `powershell`; the Store
  copy is listed after it, labelled "PowerShell 7 (Microsoft Store copy)";
  names unchanged, so restored panes keep their argv; `/terminals/shells`
  answers `default` and `label`. RISK: Windows PowerShell 5.1's default
  execution policy on client PCs is Restricted (npm `.ps1` shims would fail);
  this PC's LocalMachine policy is RemoteSigned — a decision for the owner.

  v1.422.3 (the v1.422.2 review's open junction item, closed for git_read;
  author + reviewer): `git_read._linked_folder` lists the tracked paths

  when a folder holding a tracked path is a LINK — a symlink, a junction or
  any NAME-SURROGATE reparse point (bit 0x20000000, Python's own rule;
  OneDrive placeholders and ProjFS folders are NOT links, or every repo in
  OneDrive would be refused). Entries git tracks as symlinks (mode 120000)
  are skipped; folders are checked SHALLOWEST FIRST and the check stops at
  the first link, so nothing is ever looked up THROUGH one (an lstat follows

  the listing and the loop; an unfinished or refused listing runs nothing
  (`LINK_CHECK_UNSURE`, fail-closed); a tracked name Windows cannot hold
  (WinError 123/161, a Linux repo) reads as "not a link", any other lstat

  app's broad-read policy and older than this: the reviewer's `grep`,
  `glob` and `list_files` walk INTO junctions (`_walk_files` / `os.walk`),
  and `ls-files -o` / `status -uall` list the NAMES inside an untracked

  comes fails the new wait (not vacuous). Another instance of the waitFor
  rule: a button whose LABEL changes while it works is not gone.

- **A member's CONFIRMED findings become working habits the owner accepts;
  a habit is MEASURED, never removed by a measure; the fix request reads the
  team's findings; a coding session's words to its model are true; the bench
  runs the product path; the finish snapshot is read BESIDE the first model
  call** (v1.425.0, Wave 3 rounds 2-3 + Wave 5 of
  `docs/HARNESS-BUILD-AUDIT-2026-11-01.md`; builders + independent reviewers
  per lane; test files keep their lane tags v14230 / v14250).
  TEAM NOTES PHASE 2 (`agents/team_notes.py`, read its docstring): a member
  with ≥ `MIN_FINDINGS` 4 confirmed (`asked` and `fixed`) findings across ≥
  `MIN_SESSIONS` 2 sessions, none cited by a pending/accepted/recently
  declined proposal (`SUPPRESS_DAYS` 14, from the REMOVAL for a removed
  habit), none from a low-trust review, at most one distill per
  `DISTILL_EVERY_S` (the attempt is CLAIMED in one step before the call and
  holds when the save fails), on the library's idle pacing (never near an
  account's limit, never on the loop; `library.AUTO_REFLECT` off in bare
  tests). ONE `router.complete(pin=True)` on the reviewer's own model, held,
  on its account, inside `local_only_scope` when a cited finding was Local
  only (a reviewer model off the network SKIPS); the demo model distills
  nothing. The answer is read LINEARLY (rfind/split, each line masked and
  bounded first): a habit citing unknown/too few (`MIN_CITED` 2) `F<n>` is
  dropped, scanned, masked, ≤ `MAX_HABIT_CHARS`, de-duplicated by
  `word_overlap` ≥ 0.5; at most `MAX_HABITS` 3. The proposal is the COACH's
  row (kind `team_note`, `coach.proposal` with `kind` → a quiet bell row).
  Accept renders the member's `TEAM-NOTES.md` (agent folder, atomic, a
  revision copy) only while it is exactly what Iron Jarvis last wrote
  (`TeamNoteState.written_sha`), read bounded (256 KiB), never through a
  link, and RE-READ right before `os.replace` (`_replace_unless_changed`): a
  save that landed mid-render is kept, no temp left. `habits_for_run`
  injects only into that member's TEAM pieces (`HELPER_ORIGIN`,
  `MISSION_MEMBER_ORIGIN`), ≤ `INJECT_CHARS`, scanned, once per reply; off or
  none = byte-identical prompt. `role_edit_for`: one habit on two members of
  a role → `library.propose_role_brief_edit` (refused when `team_notes` off,
  when `build_reflect` off — `REASON_OFF` —, for a Local only habit, through
  a link); `library._reflect` and `team_notes.distill` re-read their switch
  AFTER the model call (billed, nothing minted); `_write_temp` removes a
  half-written temp file. Setting `team_notes` (Models › Model team, on). PHASE 3
  (`agents/team_measure.measure(engine, member, accepted_at, removed_at)`):
  AFTER = acceptance → removal/now; BEFORE = the same span capped at
  `BEFORE_CAP_DAYS` 30, ending 1 µs before acceptance; per side code/test
  pieces a check or review judged, first-pass via `team_findings.
  member_findings(..., strict=True)`, confirmed ASKED findings per piece,
  check pass rate; a whole REPLY (scope, turn) sits on the side where its
  member's first piece STARTED (the helper Session's `created_at`, else the
  record's) — Accept does not clear `habits_for_run`'s per-reply cache,
  Remove does (`forget_member`), so a piece or finding that started after a
  removal is left out of AFTER; findings go with their reply;
  `windows(not_before=)` / `not_before_for` stop BEFORE at the end of an
  earlier copy of the same habit (`same_habit`; history unreadable =
  nothing measured); `attach` counts habits TRIED (`MAX_MEASURED`), 2
  queries each; with no reply across the boundary the numbers equal
  `member_findings` (pinned); < `MIN_PIECES` 5 = no numbers; `worse()` on exact
  `Fraction`s (`FIRST_PASS_FALL`/`CHECK_FALL` 1/5, `FINDINGS_RISE_RATIO`
  3/2 and `FINDINGS_RISE_MIN` 1/2 — Phase 7's to tune). `routes/team.
  team_view` attaches `measure {before, after, worse}` (sync route); an
  unreadable measure has NO key (`findings_for(strict=)` raises, never
  zeros). The card shows `measureWords` / the amber `HABIT_WORSE_WORDS`;
  nothing calls `team_notes.remove` on a measure (pinned both sides). FIX
  REQUEST (`agents/fix_request._gather_findings`): `TeamFinding` rows with
  `asked = 1` (ORDER BY created_at DESC, rowid DESC); a finding belongs to
  the reply line whose timing window holds it (no line = not judged); kinds
  `finding_did_not_apply`, `finding_unanswered` (only when the reply ended
  as its answer), `finding_said_fixed`, `finding_repeated` (≥ 2 sessions);
  FIXED is never a problem. `clean()` fails closed
  (`mask_failure_text(strict=True)`, `scan_context(strict=True)` raise;
  every other caller keeps the never-raises default). A reply whose
  `ChatTurnRecord` still says running is not read (`running_left_out`,
  said). A `build:<id>~try<n>` attempt is read only when its card row (the
  span `since`/`ended` holds the call) says trust "full"
  (`harness/attempts.Attempt.trust` from the done frame; an error frame only
  when it says low); low/unknown are left out and counted
  (`attempts_low_left_out` / `attempts_unknown_left_out`). STOP LINES:
  `_ReplyWatch`'s stop branch writes `trust`/`trustReason` from
  `ReplyRecord.trust_now()` (the lane hands its trust over via
  `watch_trust`), and keeps the line when a call is on the record (steps or
  one still running) at ANY trust — a full-trust line has no trust key, so
  the report reads its calls (the integrator moved both controls on
  purpose); a reply stopped before a word or a call keeps none.
  `sessions.reconcile_interrupted_turns` adds trust from
  `reply_record.low_trust_of`. HARNESS TRUTH (Wave 5 lane B, C4-C6):
  `HarnessTurn.translate_calls(serving=)` (both lanes) gives a dialect's
  picture read `ToolContext.picture_read`; `view_image._picture_read`
  returns `ToolResult.images` when `carries_tool_pictures` (Anthropic,
  claude-cli, Codex native), fitted by `fit_picture` (non PNG/JPEG/GIF/WebP
  re-encoded, unreadable refused), after `tools/images.
  scan_picture_text_bounded` (the local OCR worker, off the loop,
  `PICTURE_SCAN_WAIT_S` 6, fails open, `local_enabled` honoured; a flagged
  excerpt lowers trust through `_fence_tool_output`, the picture still
  sent); else a description + `PICTURE_DESCRIBED_NOTE`. `mode.ask_sentence`
  is one TRUE sentence per posture (Auto "may run without asking" + the
  never-routine list; Auto + low trust = Ask-first wording; Local only names
  gate ∩ `egress.reaches_outside`; `_yolo_still_asks(gate)`).
  `dialects.DIALECT_COMMAND_TIMEOUT_S` 120 (the finish check re-runs a Bash
  test with its own 120 s); a windowed `read_file` gets `max_chars =
  tool_message_chars - READ_NOTE_ROOM`; `mode.unreachable_cut` trims words
  naming a tool outside the gate (never the first sentence, never after
  e.g./i.e.); `Dialect.hints` only for shown tools; `NO_BACKGROUND_NOTE`
  when `process_start` is not armed; `history_of(last_work=True)` attaches
  to the newest shown reply whose note is non-empty. Golden
  `team_first_off_golden_v1400.json` regenerated; harness_off untouched.
  BENCH PRODUCT PATH (Wave 5 lane C, C2): `bench-harness run --path
  product` (the CLI default) makes a real coding session per task
  (`routes/build.create_session_row` + `start_build_turn`), "Ask first",
  every card "Allow once" (actor bench), questions skipped, the session's
  own step limit unless `--rounds`, the time limit as the user's Stop;
  `extra.coding_session` records steps, finish and step limit; `--path lane`
  keeps the bare lane (`--dialect` is lane-only). The path is in the
  identity; the setup key includes `__version__`, so results are reusable
  within one version only. `resolve_bench_home` refuses
  `global_config_path().parent` (`~/.ironjarvis`); a cut or cancelled
  product turn's reply record is dropped
  (`runners.forget_reply_record_when_done`, a done callback queued on
  `reply_record._writer()`); a fault in the bench's own reading stops the
  turn (`_end_turn_now`). FINISH SNAPSHOT BESIDE THE FIRST CALL (Wave 5 lane A, C1;
  the reviewer found 6 more): in a git repository `harness/folder_changes.
  take` lists the folder with `git -c core.fsmonitor=false ls-files -z
  --cached --others --exclude-standard` (`_git_names`, `GIT_OPTIONAL_LOCKS=0`;
  ls-files runs no hooks or filters, fsmonitor is the one program it would
  start) and stats only the listed folders with `os.scandir` (the walk's
  pruning, no link followed; folders cut at the FOLDED key's "/" — "İ"
  lower-cases longer); submodules and inner repositories are walked;
  `GIT_MAX_FILES` 200,000. The WALK is kept (old cap) with no `.git`, a repo
  top that is the home folder or a drive, git missing or refusing, a folder
  the enclosing repository IGNORES (`check-ignore -q -- ./`, exit 0 or any
  refusal), or a `.git` FILE whose `gitdir:` is spelled as a share while the
  folder is local (`_git_folder_on_a_share`, git never started).
  `Snapshot.how` makes the end read the same way (`take(how=)`).
  `finish.start` STARTS the first read as a task (`_FIRST_READS`); BOTH lanes
  await `FinishCheck.ready()` right before `_run_tool_round` (lock-step; the
  stream lane races it against Stop with `_prep_step`); `prepare` and
  `_folder_changes` await it too; a reply with no tool never waits. KEPT
  INDEX (C3): `hosted/panes.snapshot_tree(keep=kept_index(home, sid))` →
  `workspaces/code-index/<sid>.index` (out of backups, deleted with the
  session by `forget_kept_index`, `.rules` too): the user's index copied
  once, then `git add -A -v -- .`; copied AGAIN when git refuses it, the add
  reports a `.gitignore` (`_rules_touched`), or the size/mtime fingerprint of
  `info/exclude` and every `.gitignore` from the folder's parent up to the
  repo top changed (`_rules_print`, `<index>.rules`) — `add -A` alone keeps a
  file that later became ignored forever; a stale `<index>.lock` (git killed
  at `QUICK_S`) is removed under `_kept_lock`. Not followed: global excludes,
  the user's own `rm --cached`/`add -f`. POOL (B7): `HostedService.
  _reserve_slot` holds the pool lock only to decide; `LiveSession.starting`
  holds a place and is never a victim (`_stoppable` = running, not starting,
  not busy, no background work); with every place unstoppable a message
  WAITS (`W.POOL_WAITING` once, woken by `_room_changed`, re-checked every
  `POOL_RECHECK_S` 5, refused `W.POOL_FULL` after `POOL_WAIT_S` 15 min);
  `HostedTurn.unsent` (from `_drive`'s start to `send_user`) makes
  `interrupt` end the turn itself, so a Stop during the wait, the tree read
  or the tool's handshake never reaches the tool. Moved on purpose:
  `test_build_app_pool_v1409`, `test_build_app_background_tasks_v1417`
  (`POOL_WAIT_S` short), `test_build_code_revert_review_v1420`. Pins:
  `tests/test_team_notes{,_review,_leftovers}_v14230.py`,
  `tests/test_team_measure_v14230.py`, `tests/test_lane_e_review_v14230.py`, `tests/test_fix_request_findings{,
  _review}_v14230.py`, `tests/test_fix_request_attempts_v14230.py`,
  `tests/test_build_stop_trust_v14230.py`, `tests/test_harness_truth{,
  _review}_v14250.py`, `tests/test_harness_bench_product_path{,_review}
  _v14250.py`, `tests/test_finish_snapshot_v14250.py`, `tests/test_build_code_turn_tree_v14250.py`,
  `tests/test_build_app_start_lock_v14250.py`, `tests/test_wave5_lane_a_review_v14250.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/{team-notes,habit-measure}-v1423-0.test.tsx`.

- **What a coding session's model is told is VISIBLE; the chat's coding door
  carries the request; one writer per conversation between a terminal and
  the Code view; the real CLI's own setup is visible; a folder keeps its
  defaults; the subscription CLIs live in ONE table** (v1.426.0, Wave 6 of
  `docs/HARNESS-BUILD-AUDIT-2026-11-01.md`, E1-E5; two builders + two
  reviewers who found 11 more defects, each red first and mutation-checked).
  E1 (`daemon/routes/build_harness.py`): `GET /build/sessions/{id}/harness`
  = `instructions_view(platform, row)`, the component tree put together THE
  SAME WAY a turn does (`chat_turn._build_harness`: layer read + validated,
  rule files, accepted lessons, the family's dialect, plan mode, a team
  lead's tools, Local only, the posture) and rendered by
  `harness/components.render`; READ-ONLY, NEVER STORED, masked BEFORE the
  cut to `SHOW_CAP` 80,000; answers `{family, dialect, dialect_label,
  layers, note, mode, rule_files, text, chars, cut, fingerprint}`; a
  failure is `SHOW_FAILED` with no text, never a 500; it reaches no model
  (a Local only session's view pinned with a router that raises). A flagged
  folder text is the `[BLOCKED…]` placeholder; a flagged check command voids
  the layer and `note` says why. Per-turn parts (date, project map, skill
  listing, a picked skill, team brief, summary) are not in the tree. The
  Session tab (`HarnessSessionRail`, `lib/sessionRail`) shows the newest
  reply's route facts and the folder note in the warning tone. E2
  (`tools/autoselect.CODING_OFFER_HREF` == `lib/codingStart.
  CODING_OFFER_HREF` "/chat?view=code"): the chat's coding door (both lanes)
  opens Chat › Code; the press stages `{text, folder, localOnly?, at}` in
  THIS TAB's sessionStorage (`CODING_START_KEY` "ij.code.startRequest",
  `localOnly` present only when true) — never the address, never the
  daemon; `IjHarnessView` takes it once, within 5 minutes, seeds the start
  composer (Local only chip ON for a Local only chat); nothing is made or
  sent before Send; an old saved door `/terminals?view=code` is a plain
  link to Build › Code's empty start. E3 (`hosted/service`): `claude_later`
  finds the conversation a `/clear` moved a terminal to — ONLY one a
  `/clear` STARTED (`started_by_clear`: its first non-meta user record is
  Claude Code's own `<command-name>/clear</command-name>`, `/reset` or
  `/new`, measured on 2.1.280 and 2.1.296; a bounded 64 KB / 40-line head
  read), in the same folder, written since the pane started and after the
  recorded one, held by no Code-view row or other live terminal; several →
  `W.CANNOT_TELL`; a newer ordinary conversation is the user's own Claude
  outside Iron Jarvis and is never handed over or held. The pane's home is
  `continue_on.claude_home` (a "this PC" pane reads `~/.claude`, never the
  daemon's `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`). `_handover()` (an asyncio lock; every walk
  under it via `to_thread`) makes two hand-overs one row; Open in terminal
  claims (`W.CLAIM_TERMINAL`) and refuses a second press
  (`W.ALREADY_IN_TERMINAL`). `/resume` and a fresh `claude` typed in the
  terminal are not read (the recorded conversation is handed over). E4
  (`hosted/tool_setup.py`): frames the driver ALREADY receives become a
  `setup` event `{mcp_servers: [{name, state, error?, source?}]}`
  (`events.TYPES`), emitted only when the list changes (`note_servers`;
  None = not said) — Claude Code's `system` `init` `mcp_servers`
  (connected | pending | failed | needs-auth | disabled, 2.1.316), Codex's
  `mcpServer/startupStatus/updated` (starting|ready|failed|cancelled; "not
  logged in / codex mcp login" or `reauthenticationRequired` =
  `needs_sign_in`); names and errors masked before the cut; SCREEN ONLY,
  never a prompt; the overview gains `mcp {known, servers [{name, state,
  words, error?, sign_in?}], at, note}` (`panes.session_view`, CodePanes
  `ToolMcpSection`). Codex's sign-in words say `/exit` first (Open in
  terminal opens Codex's own screen). `CodexDriver.open` WRITES `skills/list
  {cwds}` before any turn's request (the newest-id rule of `after_restore`)
  and takes the answer in a background task (`SKILLS_WAIT_S` 15, never
  awaited by the start); rows: compact + enabled skills with `prefix: "$"`;
  a message EXACTLY `/compact` (no files) → `thread/compact/start` (answers
  `{}`, then its own turn; BUSY while a reply runs); `turn/started` names
  the turn for Stop only for THIS thread; `/context` offers compact for
  Codex. Pi/OpenCode/Grok compact NOT wired. E5 FOLDER DEFAULTS
  (`harness/folder_defaults.py`, `BuildFolderRecord.defaults_json`,
  additive): `GET|PUT /build/folders/defaults` (`routes/
  build_folder_defaults.py`), code `{provider, model, reasoning,
  approval_mode}` | tool `{harness, model, effort, mode}`; yolo and `full`
  refused on save AND dropped on read; a bad row reads as none; used ONLY by
  the two start composers (an untouched chip follows the folder, a touched
  one wins; the create routes are unchanged) and never a model that cannot
  answer, a tool that cannot start, a model the tool no longer offers, or a
  cloud model under Local only — each said in words; `lib/folderDefaults`
  is one cache per window whose saves every reader hears
  (`onFolderDefaultsSaved`); `rename_folder` keeps a project root's row
  when it holds a team OR defaults. ONE TABLE (`providers/
  subscription_clis.py`: provider, vendor, proxy, home var/dir, harness,
  binary, probed, names) read by iron_proxy/accounts, iron_proxy/service,
  hosted/words, model_team `_CLI_LABELS`, `providers/billing.PLAN_CLIS`
  (integrated: v1.424.0 had moved the plan set there), failure_words,
  cli_auth and the manager/connections CLI checks, each pinned to its OLD
  value; `dashboard/lib/subscriptionClis.ts` is GENERATED
  (`scripts/gen_subscription_clis.py --check`). NOT in the table (routing or
  a different list): `router._FAILOVER_ORDER`, `manager._AUTO_DEFAULT_ORDER`,
  `egress._FRIENDLY`, `cli_auth.SIGN_IN_FIX`, `terminals/ai_clis`,
  `connections._WATCHED`, `attempts.PROVIDER_WORDS`, `lib/cliSignIn`,
  ConnectDoors, desktop/main.js. OPEN: `LAST_COMMANDS[codex]` is global;
  `iron-jarvis-code` shows in the tool's own list; Codex `turn/completed`
  has no thread check (older); not driven against the real CLIs. Pins:
  `tests/test_build_harness_view{,_review}_v14260.py`,
  `tests/test_coding_offer_start_v14260.py`,
  `tests/test_build_app_handover{,_review}_v14260.py`,
  `tests/test_build_app_tool_setup_v14260.py`,
  `tests/test_folder_defaults_v14260.py`,
  `tests/test_subscription_clis_v14260.py`, `tests/test_lane_b_review_v14260.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/{session-instructions,coding-offer-page,coding-offer-start,build-code-tool-setup,folder-defaults,folder-defaults-review}-v1426-0.test.tsx`;
  moved on purpose: `test_matcher_tables_v13610`, `test_lane_j_review_v1420`
  (the door's address), `test_build_app_routes_v1409`,
  `test_build_app_pi_v1411` (Codex's compact listed),
  `test_integrate_wave4_v13540` (FRIENDLY reads the twin).
  v1.426.1 (test-only; v1.426.0's master Tests went red on
  `test_build_phone_ask_review_v1392`, "the first card never reached the
  phone"): the stream lane YIELDS a coding session's card before it makes
  the card's phone watcher (`stall.PhoneAsk`, whose five minutes start at
  `asked_at = now()`), so a test that moved its fake clock as soon as the
  card was on the record could move it before the watcher existed — the
  watcher then started after the jump and never became due. Every such
  test now waits for the watcher's first look (`_armed(ticks)` in
  `tests/test_build_phone_ask_v1392.py`) before it moves the clock.
  Reproduced by pulling the frame after the card 0.5 s late (a scratch
  plugin wrapping `routes/build.recorded_turn`): old shape CI's exact error
  plus two siblings, new shape green. In the product the window starts a
  moment after the card shows, which changes nothing a person can see.
  v1.426.2 (test-only; v1.426.1's Release gate went red, nothing published):
  `build-app-grok-v1416` "an edit card offers Allow for this session" waited
  for the Build › Code card with testing-library's DEFAULT 1 s
  (`findByTestId("code-ask")`), and the Release gate's smaller machine took
  longer to read the session and draw it. All 31 such waits across the seven
  Build › Code view files now give the card 10 s. Reproduced by holding the
  mocked reads 1.2 s (old shape: CI's exact "Unable to find
  [data-testid=\"code-ask\"]"; new shape green). A `findBy…` on a surface
  that needs a read and a stream first must name its own timeout.

- **Chat › Code's new-session box is the session composer's own row**
  (v1.437.0, a USER REPORT: Try on, Team, effort and the account existed only
  once a session did). `HarnessStartComposer` runs
  `useToolbarFit(HARNESS_FIT_ORDER)` with the session's classes and holds:
  PermissionChip (Plan → `mode: "plan"`), `TryOnControl` (`unavailable` =
  `tryOnAvailability`: needs a folder in a git repository that can branch, not
  Plan; a press shows the reason), `TeamControl draft` (a change goes to the
