  box, never a PATCH; untouched = the folder's saved team, which the create
  applies; "No team" = `team: null`; a worker pick still makes or reuses a
  model-team member before Send, as in a session; `StartRequest.teamMembers`
  keeps what each pick said and is never sent), `ReasoningChip` (only where the
  model offers levels; a touched level wins over the folder's; a level the next
  model lacks is dropped; a level is saved as a folder default only together
  with a model) and `ModelMenu companies` (`companyMenu`, never a second list;
  the account rides only with a model). ONE create (`startCreateBody` + account,
  mode, team, reasoning); `try_on` rides the FIRST message
  (`FirstMessage.tryOn`), never the create. A refused first message reseeds the
  box with every chip (model + account, effort, Plan, team, Local only) and the
  next Send repeats the identical create. Under Local only every list offers
  only models whose catalog row stays on the network (`staysOnThisNetwork`, never
  the Try on list, which leaves out down boxes), and turning it on takes back a
  cloud model, cloud picks and cloud team members, in words. A failed accounts
  read is retried when the model menu opens (only a 404 means no list). Pins:
  `tests/test_start_box_try_on_v14370.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/ij-harness-start-box{,-review}-v1437.test.tsx`, e2e
  `dashboard/e2e/start-box-v1437.spec.ts` (scratch ports; its daemon reads the
  real `~/.claude` for skills, so do not run it casually); moved on purpose:
  `folder-defaults{,-review}-v1426-0`, `ij-harness-start-composer-v1418` (the
  model chip is no longer a select). OPEN: turning Plan on drops Try on picks
  for good; under Local only with no model picked the chip still names a cloud
  default (the first message then fails in the session); an untouched folder
  team shows members by name and its cloud members are not taken back (the
  team router keeps each piece local instead).
  v1.437.0 WAS NOT PUBLISHED (its Release gate went red, Tests green;
  v1.437.1 ships it): `wave1-device-settings-appearance-v1433` clicked the
  Settings "Appearance" group button with `getByRole` right after
  `settings-groups` appeared, but that strip is drawn BEFORE the schema
  arrives and the group buttons come with it. Both such tests (it and
  `settings-update-channel-v14050`) now `findByRole` the button itself;
  holding the mocked `/settings/schema` 300 ms reproduced the gate's exact
  "Unable to find … Appearance" on the old wait.

- **Antigravity is the SIXTH tool in Build › Code; an account's usage is READ
  from its own CLI on a press or while it is in use; the parity table is
  written down** (v1.439.0; four lanes, each a builder + an independent
  mutation-checking reviewer; the reviewers found 16 more defects).
  - **ANTIGRAVITY (`hosted/antigravity.py`, on `hosted/acp.AcpDriver`).**
    Every fact was MEASURED on Google's signed `agy` 1.5.2, run from a
    scratch home against a local stand-in Gemini API
    (`GOOGLE_GEMINI_BASE_URL`); nothing reached Google, and nothing was
    signed in. See the module docstring and
    `tests/fixtures/build_app_v1439/a_*`.
    - Start: argv `agy acp --disable-telemetry --log-file
      <home>/hosted/<sid>/agy-acp.log`. The log is trimmed to its last
      256 KB (`trim_log`, off the loop) when it passes 2 MB.
    - Install and sign-in: listed only when installed (`ONLY_WHEN_INSTALLED`;
      found on PATH, else `%LOCALAPPDATA%\agy\bin\agy.exe`). It signs in on
      its own (`OWN_SIGN_IN`; no Iron-Proxy accounts), and it is NOT a
      `subscription_clis` row.
    - NEVER call ACP `authenticate`: agy KEEPS the chosen method in
      `cache/acp-auth-state.json` for later starts, which would change the
      user's own sign-in. A `-32000 "Authentication required"` says to run
      `agy` and sign in.
    - Modes: agy `default` is "Antigravity's own rules", and it asks before
      COMMANDS ONLY. Measured: with no settings file, writes ran uncarded
      inside and outside the folder; only the user's own `toolPermission:
      "strict"` asks before writes. `accept-edits` is Auto edits; `plan` is
      Plan, which is NOT read-only. Full access is
      `--dangerously-skip-permissions`, a new process with two presses. Ask
      first and Auto are hidden, with reasons. Iron Jarvis never writes
      `~/.gemini/antigravity-cli/settings.json`.
    - Cards are Allow once / Deny only. A Deny ends agy's reply.
    - Questions: `elicitation/create` forms (`qN` with oneOf const +
      `qN_other`) are answered `{action: accept, content}` or decline. The
      client MUST declare `elicitation`, or agy prints the question and
      ends the turn.
    - STEER differs from OpenCode: a second `session/prompt` makes the FIRST
      prompt answer `end_turn` at once, and the latest prompt's answer ends
      the turn. A note is read on its own `user_message_chunk` echo (never
      on the message's echo).
    - FAILURES ARE TEXT: "⚠ Agent execution terminated due to error." fails
      the reply, with the reason taken from the session's own log;
      "⚠ API error (attempt N)" is a notice.
    - Models: the base ids, with thinking levels per model (from
      `<base>-<level>` ids), set live through `session/set_config_option`
      and kept in `<home>/hosted/antigravity_models.json`.
    - Saved conversations: `conversation_summaries.db` read-only
      (`nesting_depth` 0, `file:///` workspace URIs). Resume with
      `session/resume`, else `session/load` with the replay hidden. "Open in
      terminal" runs `agy --conversation <id>`.
    - Tests read only `IRONJARVIS_ANTIGRAVITY_HOME` (a conftest session
      fixture).
    - Shared fix: a tool whose `open()` failed now ends FAILED with its
      refusal (`_stop_process(end_turn=False)`), never "the coding tool was
      closed".
  - **USAGE (`iron_proxy/usage_read.py`, route `POST
    /build/accounts/{id}/usage/refresh`).** Before this, nothing ever asked a
    vendor; windows only arrived with finished calls.
    - Sources, measured, neither a model call: Claude Code's control request
      `get_usage {skip_behaviors: true}` after `initialize` (0.15 s), and
      Codex's `account/rateLimits/read` on `codex app-server` (0.7 s). The
      Claude request is EXPERIMENTAL in Claude Code's own schema, and it
      sometimes answers `rate_limits: null`; that is said as "try again in a
      minute" and nothing is stored.
    - How the CLI runs: as the account (`make_lease`), with `child_env`,
      `--setting-sources "" --strict-mcp-config`, a private empty folder,
      `core/procs`, 15 s, a 3 s grace to exit, then a tree kill.
    - It is REFUSED for: an account with no home (it would read this PC's
      login into the wrong account), signed out, off, Grok/other vendors, and
      Iron-Proxy off.
    - The reading is told to Iron-Proxy as `/signal` `status: 200` + headers
      / `rateLimits`, which STORES the windows (`recordLimits`) without
      parking and without counting a request. This needs the `plan-usage`
      feature and is pinned against the real vendored bundle. The snapshot
      is then refreshed, so menus, router floors and the Usage tab see it.
    - Limits: 20 s per account; "as of" = the last read that WORKED.
    - Loop `account_usage`: every 60 s, it re-reads only accounts IN USE (a
      Build › Code live turn running, or a Chat › Code running turn's
      `setup.account`) whose last read is over 180 s old. Nothing in use =
      no read. Iron-Proxy off = the row is popped (`on_off`).
    - Screen: one component, `components/accounts/UsageRefresh.tsx`, on the
      Connections Iron-Proxy card (which now also shows "N% of the 7-day
      window used"), Chat › Code's account cards and Build › Code's Usage
      tab. Model menu rows get no button.
  - **PARITY (`docs/BUILD-CODE-PARITY-2026-11-06.md`: 207 rows, what
    changed, what is left).**
    - New `hosted/typed.py`: `/model x` and `/effort x` typed for Claude Code
      or Grok go through the service's patch. Claude Code's terminal-only
      built-ins become note rows, ONLY for names missing from its own
      `initialize` list, which was measured.
    - Grok's `/always-approve` and `/auto` are dropped and refused typed.
    - ACP command argument hints are kept. OpenCode's command list is kept
      per folder; it is read through a copy, because the sync route races
      the loop. OpenCode model rows are grouped.
    - Codex warnings and failed hooks show as one masked notice each. The
      deprecation about Iron Jarvis's own resume call is logged only.
    - Rename reaches the tool (`rename_session`, `thread/name/set`) only
      AFTER the PATCH is saved, in the background.
    - A Grok sign-in failure gets the Sign in press.
    - Copy icon on each finished reply. "Export as Markdown" in the row's ⋯
      menu (events already loaded; no tool output).
    - Claude Code's plan card names its buttons by the mode Claude Code
      returns to (`pre_plan_mode` → `then_mode`/`sets_mode`). The row and
      chip follow that mode after approval: Claude Code restores its
      pre-Plan mode, Full access included, without a new two-press.
  - **OPEN:**
    - Never driven with a real Google sign-in, and never in the packaged
      app.
    - `rename_session` in headless mode was not tried live.
    - A picture-only Antigravity note can be handed back after agy read it.
    - Antigravity's transcript and usage windows are not read.
    - The left-for-later list is in the parity doc.
  - **Pins:** `tests/test_build_app_antigravity{,_review,_live}_v1439.py`,
    `tests/test_account_usage_{read,routes}_v1439.py`,
    `tests/test_build_app_parity{,_review}_v1439.py`,
    `tests/test_build_code_plan_card{,_review}_v1439.py`, and
    `dashboard/__tests__/{build-app-antigravity,usage-refresh,build-app-parity,build-code-parity,build-code-parity-review}-v1439.test.tsx`.

- **Build › Code catches up with each tool's own terminal** (v1.441.0; six
  lanes, each a builder + an independent mutation-checking reviewer who found
  31 more defects, each pinned red first; every fact MEASURED on the real CLI
  from a scratch home with a stand-in model server, recordings in
  `tests/fixtures/build_app_v14410/`; table in
  `docs/BUILD-CODE-PARITY-2026-11-06.md` "What v1.441.0 changed").
  - REVIEW FIXES. Antigravity: open card/question ids in `_asking`; `steer`
    then refuses with `W.ANTIGRAVITY_NOTE_WHILE_ASKING`, a plain 409, NEVER
    code `not_working` (the page would send the words as a new message); a
    note prompt agy refuses anyway goes to `_note_refused` (the reply goes on
    under the prompt before it; the note is handed back). Multi-select forms
    (`type: array`, `items.anyOf`) answer a LIST of consts (+ `qN_other`), the
    page's ", "-joined answer matched greedily; an unshowable form is
    declined AND said; `jarvis_tools.takes()`/`note_takes` give
    `jarvis_tools: false` (no chip, token or restart; a handshake flipping to
    http with a box wanted sets `live.restart`); a dropped effort leaves the
    row (`store.clear_effort_if`). Claude Code: the plan's way back is read
    AT THE ANSWER (`plan_exit_mode`, only while still in plan, as 2.1.316's
    `ExitPlanModeV2Tool`) and the card follows a live mode change
    (`_plan_then` is the one rule; `refresh_ask` + `_refresh_asks` re-send it
    under the SAME `request_id`, the fold keeps the newest) — Allow could put
    the session back in Full access; `/model opus|sonnet|haiku` via
    `claude_models.resolve_alias` (cached only), an unresolved name is SENT,
    never refused; `FOLDER_COMMANDS` gains claude-code (the bare key = its
    `builtin: true` rows) and the terminal-only check waits for the folder's
    own list, so a user's own `status`/`export` reaches Claude Code; a rename
    made while stopped (`_renames`) is told after the next `open`; a typed
    `/export` names the ⋯ menu; all six tools named in every list.
  - USAGE: `_row_and_lease` refuses (409) an Iron-Proxy without `plan-usage`
    or not answering BEFORE any CLI starts; `can_refresh(row, svc)`.
    `in_use_accounts` = Build › Code turns with NO open card, plus Build
    terminals running Claude Code/Codex on that provider's account, `working`
    AND printing within `IN_USE_EVERY_S` (`last_output_at`: a frozen screen
    is not use); Chat › Code is not read (its adapters report each step).
    `NO_PLAN_WORDS` for `rate_limits_available: false`; empty answers back
    off per account to `EMPTY_BACKOFF_MAX_S`; `USAGE_READ_STRIP`;
    `claude_markers` drops a parent's `CLAUDE_CODE_ACCOUNT_UUID`/
    `_USER_EMAIL`/`_ORGANIZATION_UUID`. Page: Export reads every earlier page
    (`collectAllEvents`, `exportGapWords`); Enter never approves a plan that
    lands in Full access (`codeAskKeyHint`); Copy = `replyCopyTexts`.
  - SESSION CONTROLS (`hosted/session_controls.py`): `Driver.
    background_work()` (None = no list, no key), `stop_background`,
    `mcp_actions`/`mcp_action`; routes `…/background/{work_id}/stop`,
    `…/mcp/{server}`; rows carry only the actions that fit. Claude Code:
    `apply_flag_settings {effortLevel}` read back via `get_settings`
    (`effort_live`; the RUNNING reply keeps its effort, so mid-reply it says
    `EFFORT_NEXT_WORDS`), `stop_task`, `mcp_reconnect`/`mcp_toggle` (off
    persists in `.claude.json`, Claude Code's own write). Codex:
    `thread/backgroundTerminals/*` feed `background_tasks` (the v1.418.0
    keep-alive rule extended), read after a turn and every
    `TERMINALS_POLL_S`, never mid-turn, our own reads `quiet_frame`
    (`heard_from`, both `on_message` paths) or `last_heard` never ages;
    `mcpServer/oauth/login` → `safe_sign_in_url`, opened by the page.
  - BRANCH/REWIND (`hosted/branching.py`, `POST …/fork {at?}`, `…/rewind
    {to}`): a message = its `user` event's `at`, the tool's ref in
    `refs.json` (Claude uuid; Codex turn id; Pi by text against
    `get_fork_messages`), noted only after `send_user` returns, awaited at
    the reply's end (`ref_save`). Claude: `--resume H --fork-session
    --session-id N --resume-session-at <parentUuid>`, pending until its first
    message (`pending_rewind`: no Open in terminal). Codex `thread/fork`/
    `revert` and Pi `fork`/`clone` then STOP the process (writer lock /
    switched process). OpenCode whole fork only; Grok, Antigravity none. A
    rewind trashes the log BEFORE the tool acts (`NOT_KEPT`), writes
    `rewound.json` (`without_rewound`); no answer = `NO_ANSWER` 409. Never
    while a reply or background work runs, archived or in a terminal; files
    are never touched.
  - CODEX PLAN (SUPERSEDES v1.420.0's hidden Plan): `_collaboration()` sends
    `collaborationMode {mode: plan, settings: {model, reasoning_effort: pick
    or the thread's own}}`, `default` once on leaving, none if never planned;
    file changes declined (`_decline_in_plan`), commands ask; `_ask_plan`
    raises the plan card BY ID (survives the code host); Allow starts
    `IMPLEMENT_TEXT` in the same reply. Auto review, Auto edits hidden.
  - OPENCODE: `hosted/opencode_catalog.py` (`opencode models --verbose`,
    background, single-flight, `OPENCODE_DISABLE_MODELS_FETCH=1`, off in
    conftest); primary agents are modes `agent:<name>` (`LAST_AGENTS`, Ask
    first + the agent's rules, which win; Shift+Tab's `nextMode` never steps
    into one, as it never steps into Full access); `task` is DENY outside Full
    access unless the user has a `task` rule (SUPERSEDES v1.420.0's "task
    allow": a helper's question never reaches ACP and waited for ever;
    `NO_HELPERS`); `OPENCODE_ENABLE_QUESTION_TOOL` dropped (never crosses
    ACP).
  - GROK, PI, AGY: `history.transcript_source` imports Grok's
    `chat_history.jsonl` (times from `events.jsonl` turn/tool fields ONLY —
    it holds MCP URLs with keys) and agy's `transcript.jsonl`; Grok's MCP
    list reads ONLY name/status/reason (`grok_servers`, `reported_mcp`;
    SUPERSEDES v1.416.0's "dropped unread"); `CLIS["grok"]` share; Pi
    `!`/`!!` through RPC `bash` (`_run_shell`, `abort_bash`, `_rearm_shell`,
    a cut `!` turn never carried on: `WHY_COMMAND`). FOUND: Pi has no MCP
    list over RPC; agy reports no usage window over ACP.
  - Codex Plan with no model known (a session the code host kept from
    v1.440.0, none picked) asks `thread/read` for the thread's model first
    (`_know_thread_model`, written before `turn/start`; measured on 0.157.0);
    none there = a notice (`W.CODEX_PLAN_NO_MODEL` /
    `CODEX_LEAVE_PLAN_NO_MODEL`), never silence.
  - OPEN: an allowed command in Codex Plan can write; Grok also reads
    `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` (profile twice, no guard by decision); agy terminal
    sign-in may not reach ACP; `/model sonnet[1m]` loses `[1m]`; the bare
    Claude list is empty after a restart; renames while stopped live in
    memory; Claude `mcp_authenticate` not wired; a held queue survives a
    rewind; per-message actions only for messages sent from v1.441.0; not
    driven in the packaged app.
  - Pins: every `tests/test_*_v14410.py` and `dashboard/__tests__/
    *-v1441-0.test.tsx` (the parity doc maps each gap to its file); moved on
    purpose: `test_build_app_{parity_v1439,routes_v1409,codex_posture_v1420,
    opencode_v1413,opencode_rules_v1420,grok_v1416,tool_setup_v14260}`,
    `test_account_usage_read_v1439`, `build-code-parity-v1439`.

- **Antigravity's model list and sign-in are READ by `agy models` in the
  background, only once its own sign-in finished; a chat picks a FOLDER with
  Build › Code's own picker** (v1.443.0; the user: "it doesn't have the
  ability to select the specific models. The login is not being recognized
  in the connections" and "what if i dont want it to be a specific project -
  rather just work in a specific folder"). MEASURED on agy 1.5.3 (scratch
  home): `agy --log-file F models` prints `<id>	<name>` (no session, no
  model call, ~2 s); an ACP session's `configOptions.model` groups ids that
  share a base and end in a level (`gemini-3.8-flash-{high,medium,low}` -> one
  model, levels low/medium/high) and leaves a lone suffixed id whole
  (`gpt-oss-120b-medium`) — `hosted/antigravity_catalog.parse_models` follows
  that rule. ITS SIGN-IN IS IN WINDOWS' CREDENTIAL STORE ("keyringAuth: loaded
  token"): a scratch USERPROFILE still reads the real account, so never run
  the real `agy` in a test (conftest turns `AUTO_PROBE` off; the reader's pins
  use `tests/fixtures/fake_agy_models_v14430.py`). What it does SIGNED OUT is
  not measured (a sign-in prompt is possible), so the read runs only when
  `<agy home>/cache/onboarding.json` says onboarding finished (`onboarded`).
  EVERY agy run starts its updater; `AGY_CLI_DISABLE_AUTO_UPDATE=true` (NOT
  `1`) turns it off for the read and for Code-view sessions (`NO_UPDATE_ENV`,
  set in `_start_process`), because the kill-on-close Job would cut an update
  off. Kept in `<home>/hosted/antigravity_read.json` (TTL 600 s, watch =
  onboarding/auth-state/program stats, a failure waits 120 s, `force` for
  `GET /build/app/harnesses?recheck=antigravity`), merged under a live
  session's own list (`merged`: the session wins). The tool row: `signed_in`
  True from a read that listed models, False only from a read whose words say
  sign-in, NEVER False from the onboarding file (False greys the tool out in
  the Code view); `signin_finished` (that file) is a hint for Connections;
  `models_reading`/`models_note` as for Pi. CONNECTIONS:
  `components/connections/AntigravityRow.tsx` in the plans card (signed in /
  not signed in + Sign in = `launchAppSignIn("agy")` / Not detected + Google's
  page / not checked yet + Check again; Look again rechecks it); renders
  nothing unless the answer holds a `harnesses` list. CHAT FOLDER:
  `FolderChip`'s picking half is `useFolderPicker` (Windows' picker via
  `folderPickerBridge`, else the tree in a dialog, the tree a `next/dynamic`
  import so it stays off /chat); the chat page holds ONE picker for the hero
  chip (beside "No project"), the toolbar's `ChatFolderChip` (name and × on
  the project's fit step), "+ Choose a working folder" and the drawer's
  Change. A pick outside the active project leaves it
  (`lib/chatFolder.pickLeavesProject`; inside keeps it), arms
  `PROJECT_FILE_TOOLS` over an empty set, drops the conversation's own folder,
  then `POST /documents/folder-check` (`root_problem` off the loop; a network
  spelling never looked at) — a refusal shown only while about the folder on
  screen. OPEN: Antigravity's signed-out answer; the chat chip not tried in
  the packaged app or at 390 px in a browser; a reopened chat's saved folder
  is not checked; a pick still writes the sticky default. Pins:
  `tests/test_build_app_antigravity_models_v14430.py` (9 mutations red),
  `dashboard/__tests__/antigravity-connections-v1443.test.tsx` (5 red),
  `dashboard/__tests__/chat-folder-chip-v1443.test.tsx` and
  `tests/test_chat_folder_check_v1443.py` (18 red); moved on purpose:
  `drawer-folder-calm-v1329` (Change opens the shared picker).
  v1.443.0 WAS NOT PUBLISHED (its Release gate went red on one shard, Tests
  green; v1.443.1 ships it): the real-Edge
  `test_build_browser_review_v1376::test_real_edge_local_only_reaches_no_public_address_at_all`
  slept a FIXED 1 s before checking that the page's requests reached the
  public stand-in, and on the loaded runner /cdn.js and /api had not landed.
  It now waits (≤ 20 s) for those requests themselves.

- **Build › Code, every tool again; a calm sweep** (v1.447.0; three lanes,
  measured on agy 1.5.3, Claude Code 2.1.319, codex 0.157.0, grok 1.0.70;
  table in `docs/BUILD-CODE-PARITY-2026-11-06.md` "What v1.447.0 changed").
  ANTIGRAVITY: `hosted/antigravity_agents.py` reads `agy agents` (names only,
  no model call) in the background → modes `agent:<name>` started with
  `--agent`; a conversation keeps its agent (a change after it exists is a
  409, a saved one opened under another agent refused by its own
  `agent_name`); `--process-per-session` ONLY when the Iron Jarvis tools are
  handed over (that start says `mcpCapabilities.http`; only such a start may
  record "takes none"); `agy mcp list` at each start, NAME + STATUS columns
  only (its COMMAND/URL holds keys) → tool_setup states `on`/`off`; profile
  share `antigravity` → `~/.gemini/config/AGENTS.md` (setting
  `profile_share_antigravity`). Sign-in: `agy acp` signs in silently from the
  keyring `agy` writes, so the press keeps opening `agy`. OTHERS:
  `mcp_authenticate` = "Sign in…" on a Claude Code server that needs it (list
  re-read every 5 s, ≤ 36); `/model sonnet[1m]` keeps `[1m]`;
  `hosted/claude_catalog.py` reads a folder's `/` list with one short
  `claude -p` answering `initialize` (`--strict-mcp-config`, settings file
  `{"disableAllHooks": true}` — without it a SessionStart hook RAN; off in the
  suite, conftest); `session_writes_lost` said; Codex `config/mcpServer/reload`
  then `mcpServerStatus/list` (`POST …/mcp-reload`, refused mid-reply); Codex
  and Grok (`_x.ai/mcp/list`) list turned-off servers. CALM: new
  `components/IconButton.tsx` (aria-label = the old words, tip after 400 ms or
  on keyboard focus, portaled only while shown), `ConfirmButton icon` (trash
  first press, worded second); row Edit/Delete/Refresh/Copy-beside-a-path/
  strip Dismiss became icons; 134 uppercase form labels and `Field`/
  `SectionLabel`/`Stat` are sentence case. Icons only for universal actions;
  approval cards, PermissionChip, decisions and primary actions keep words.
  `service.py` must not contain the word "ACP" (`test_build_app_v1409`).
  Pins: `tests/test_build_app_antigravity{,_live}_v14470.py`,
  `tests/test_build_app_parity_v14471.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/{build-app-antigravity-v1447-0,build-code-parity-v1447-1,calm-sweep-v1447-2}.test.ts(x)`.

- **Archiving a Build › Code session writes a short Markdown summary from
  its OWN record, never a model** (v1.453.0; the owner: "a general summary
  … in markdown (to avoid bloat) … what project was worked on … what state
  the project was in before … and after"). `hosted/archive_summary.py`
  (BLOCKING): `build_summary(row, events, dropped=, engine=)` reads the
  events log (helper-tagged `parent` events left out) and git in the folder
  through `hosted/panes` (`_base_commit` = the newest first-parent commit
  before `created_at` − 1 s, `git_changes(base=)`, plus `log`, `status
  --porcelain=v1 -z`, `@{u}` ahead count; all `_g` reads, bounded); a
  share / gone folder / no git / not a repo / git refusing is SAID, never
  guessed. Sections: who/where (folder, project via
  `projects/locate.project_for_path`, tool, model, account), Before (base
  commit + first ask quoted), What was done (asks, files differing from the
  start, commits after it, test runs via `harness/finish.status_is_tests`,
  CI checks (`gh run …` / `gh pr checks`) and `git push` from the tool's own
  command steps — "finished without an error" / "ended with an error",
  NEVER "passed"), After (branch + commit, uncommitted count, unpushed
  commits, plan steps not done, the end of the last reply QUOTED). Every
  string masked before its cut AND the whole text masked again (both locks
  needed: either alone survives a mutation); ≤ `MAX_CHARS` 8,000. Written
  to `<home>/hosted/<id>/summary.md` (temp + replace) — it goes to the
  trash with a delete, stays on unarchive, is rewritten by a later archive.
  `HostedService.archive`: refuses `W.ARCHIVE_BACKGROUND` while the running
  tool `keeps_working` / `working_alone` / `replying_alone` (BEFORE the
  claim; a running reply is still `_claim`'s `W.RUNNING`), stops the tool,
  then writes the summary from `live.log.read(EV.MAX_LINES)` +
  `_with_transcript`; a summary that fails NEVER stops the archive (answer
  key `archive_summary_error`, else `archive_summary {path, at}`). The view
  (`GET /build/app/sessions/{id}`) carries `archive_summary` when one is
  kept (one stat). Routes `GET …/summary` (`{text, path, at}`, 404 none)
  and `POST …/summary/open` (Iron Jarvis's own path through
  `documents._open_native`, never a caller's). Dashboard: the row's ⋯ gains
  **Archive** (`code-row-archive-<id>`, not on archived or disk rows,
  disabled while running; a refusal and a summary error go to
  `code-row-note`), the header's Archive says the same title;
  `components/buildCode/ArchiveSummary.tsx` (`code-archive-summary`, open
  for an archived session with Copy / Open file / Bring back, folded once
  brought back; read only when shown), words in `lib/buildArchive.ts`. Pins:
  `tests/test_build_app_archive_summary_v1453.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-archive-v1453.test.tsx` (16 mutations,
  all red). OPEN: the branch at the START is not known (only the commit);
  "files that differ" counts the user's own edits too (said in the text);
  a Build session whose log was trimmed shows only what the log kept.

- **A streamed word in Build › Code costs only its own work** (v1.472.0;
  first wave of the t3code comparison, 2026-11-11). `lib/buildApp.ts`
  `foldConversationFrom(memo, events, extra)` RESUMES the fold
  (`FoldState`, `foldStep`, `foldResult`; `foldConversation` is the same
  steps over a whole list): it folds only events after the ones `memo`
  holds, and folds whole when the list is not the old list plus more (a
  read again, earlier steps loaded, the echo that replaces the optimistic
  question) or when a helper's own helper became known after its events
  (`FoldShared.broken`; a whole fold reads every step's helper first).
  Child (helper) folds share `parentOf` and their rows are cached per
  helper version. `applyEvents` copies the list ONCE per frame. A text item
  not yet done renders `LiveReplyText` (`lib/streamSplit.settledSplit`:
  the settled part through MemoMarkdown, the tail through Markdown, one
  MediaGroup). `CodePanes` is `memo` and gets ONE event (the newest with
  files, the same array while it does not change) and a stable send.
  Daemon: `HostedTurn.follow` → `_gather_live` merges a live viewer's
  pieces the replay's way (`coalesce_into`, masked again) for up to
  `LIVE_MERGE_S` (0.04) after a streamed piece; any other event is sent
  with what is already queued. NOT done (why): virtualizing the list or
  `content-visibility` (paint containment would clip and re-anchor the
  rows' menus and fixed popovers); the timed re-reads (fallbacks, not the
  streaming path). Pins: `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-speed-v1472.test.tsx`
  (event-by-event == whole fold after every event of all six tools'
  recordings; one fold, each event once), `build-code-live-text-v1472.test.tsx`
  (parse counts), the v1.472.0 case in `build-app-view-v1409` (panes draw 0
  times for 8 streamed frames), `tests/test_build_app_live_merge_v1472.py`
  (7 mutations, all red).

- **A long Build › Code session draws its newest rows only** (v1.473.0;
  second wave of the t3code comparison). `lib/codeWindow.ts`:
  `windowStart(items, end, size)` = `ROW_WINDOW` (120) rows before `end`,
  moved back to the start of a message (a `user` row that is not a carry)
  within `SNAP_ROWS` (40); nothing folded below `MIN_HIDDEN` (30).
  `BuildCodeView` keeps `drawn {session, from}`: set on a session's first
  rows; cut back to `windowStart` only when the items GREW, the reader is at
  the bottom and more than `REWINDOW_AT` (240) rows are drawn (never after a
  Show earlier press); "Show earlier" (`code-show-earlier`) draws one more
  window and keeps the scroll position; the daemon's "Load earlier" shows
  only once `from` is 0. `CodeConversation` takes `from` and draws
  `items.slice(from)`; the last error and each reply's Copy are still
  computed over ALL items, and Copy's text is joined only when pressed
  (`replyEndKeys` marks the rows `replyCopyTexts` keys; `copyFor` reads the
  items through a ref). This REPLACED the planned separate store for live
  words: with ~120 rows drawn, a streamed frame costs 13 ms at 600 turns
  (measured; 0 long tasks), and a hand-off between two stores risks lost or
  doubled words. Known: browser Find sees drawn rows only. Bench: a stand-in
  server that answers the Code view's routes with a 250/600-turn history and
  streams 3,001 pieces — a stream that SENDS NOTHING while it waits is cut
  by the page's 60 s stall bound, so it sends the turn so far at once and a
  keep-alive every 10 s, as the daemon does. Pins:
  `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-window-v1473.test.tsx`, the v1.473.0
  describe in `build-app-view-v1409` (8 mutations, all red; one HUNG — a
  mutation that draws every row of a 600-turn session in jsdom runs for hours,
  so a mutation harness must bound each run).

- **A Build › Code session's coding tool can hand a piece of work to another
  coding tool** (v1.474.0, "Hand off"; third wave of the t3code comparison:
  its orchestrator `delegate_task`, built our way). `hosted/handoff.py`: the
  `handoff` box (Build › Code ONLY: `jarvis_tools.normalise` = the pane's five
  + `handoff`; a terminal's reader keeps five) unlocks four MCP tools through
  `mcpserver/server.CAPABILITY_TOOLS["handoff"]` (an exact-name set):
  `code_handoff_start` (answers at once with the helper's id),
  `code_handoff_check` (waits ≤ `CHECK_WAIT_MAX_S` 25 s — every CLI caps one
  MCP call; Codex's is about a minute), `code_handoff_stop`,
  `code_handoff_list`; permission rows allow (the box + the CLI's own MCP card
  are the consent); on no roster (`_OFF_ROSTER_BY_DESIGN`). The caller is
  `parent_of(ctx)` = `mcp:code:<sid>`; anything else is refused. A HELPER is a
  real Build › Code session of the chosen tool in the SAME folder
  (`HostedSessionRecord.handed_from`, nullable, reconciler-added;
  `store.helpers_of`), titled "Helper: …"; it takes the parent's mode only when
  `check_mode(..., confirm=False)` accepts it (never Full access); the same
  tool keeps the parent's account, another tool gets `helper_account` — an
  account of the SAME account group (`build_accounts.group_of`, `usable_now`,
  cached reads) or a refusal, never another group; a helper can never hand
  off again (PATCH refuses the box, the tool refuses); at most `MAX_RUNNING`
  (3) working, counted under a per-session lock (`_start_lock`; parallel
  calls); a helper whose first piece never started is deleted. Emergency stop
  refuses starts and `carry_on.stop_unattended_turns` stops helper turns
  (`svc.handoff_turns`, memory only). The user's Stop on the parent's reply,
  archive and delete stop running helpers (`stop_helpers_soon`). What comes
  back (`summarize`: the turn's own finished text, the paths its finished
  edits/writes named, steps, error) goes out in ONE `wrap_untrusted` fence,
  `promptguard.defang`ed first. Views: the parent's carries `helpers`
  [{id, title, label, state}], a helper's `handed_from` {id, title, label}
  (dashboard `HelperLines`, `decodeHelpers`, `jarvisToolKeysFor` hides the box
  in a helper). Cards: `words.JARVIS_TOOL_TITLES` names the four tools in
  words for Claude Code and Codex alike. LIVE-PROVEN (scratch daemon, real
  claude 2.1.x haiku, Auto edits): the parent called start, a Claude Code
  helper wrote the file, one check brought the report back. A scratch daemon
  must be started with `IJ_DAEMON_PORT=<its port>` or coding tools are handed
  the 8787 address (`platform.own_mcp_url`). OPEN: each CLI's own MCP call
  limit was not measured for Pi, OpenCode, Grok, Antigravity; the helper list
  is read when the parent's view is read (no live push); a helper cut off by
  a restart reads "done" in the list (check reads the turn's own stop).
  Pins: `tests/test_code_handoff{,_review,_rules}_v1474.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/code-handoff-v1474.test.tsx` (18 + 5 mutations, all
  red); moved on purpose: `test_build_app_spine_v1414`, `build-code-spine-v1414`
  (a sixth box), `test_roster_coverage_v1178`, the tool-word tables.

- **Build › Code's tool, model and effort are ONE picker** (v1.475.0; the
  user, after the t3code comparison: "a similar flow in the build > code
  area"). `components/buildCode/ModelPicker.tsx`: a chip "Tool · Model ·
  Effort" (`pickerWords`; a "" model reads the page's `defaultModelLabel`)
  opening one panel — the tools (`toolUnavailable` greys one with its reason;
  `toolExtra` is the page's Sign in press), the tool's models
  (`modelRows`: the default first when offered, `modelGroups` groups, a kept
  model the tool no longer lists marked "not in the list"; a search box
  `matchesModel` over name, id and group) and the model's OWN levels
  (`effortRows` = `effortsFor`, "Default" first, a kept level kept). Picking
  a model focuses its levels (a model with none closes the panel); Enter in
  the search picks the highlighted row; Ctrl/Cmd+Shift+M
  (`isPickerShortcut`, both places pass `shortcut`). Test ids from the
  `testId` prefix (`<p>-tool-menu`, `<p>-tool-<id>`, `<p>-tool-why-<id>`,
  `<p>-model-<id|default>`, `<p>-effort-<level|default>`,
  `<p>-model-search`); the chip carries `data-tool/-model/-effort`. The
  start box: prefix "code-start" (chip `code-start-tool`, opens DOWN like its
  account menu), `closeAfterTool` = `asksForAccount` (the account question
  comes next); a new session starts from the folder's own default for the
  tool, else `lastPick(tool)` (`LAST_PICK_KEY` + tool, written at a
  successful start; a model the tool no longer offers takes its effort with
  it), else the tool's default. The session: prefix "code-pick", chip
  `code-model-chip`, `lockTool`; keepsLast hides the default rows,
  `modelWhileRunning`/`effortWhileRunning` hold rows with their reason
  (`defaultModelHeld`, `defaultEffortHeld`, `effortHeld`);
  `defaultModelWords(tool, keepsLast)` no longer repeats the tool's name.
  Opening either re-reads `/build/app/harnesses` at most every
  `PICKER_REREAD_MS` (60 s; the page's first read counts). DAEMON:
  `HostedService._say_model_change` emits ONE `notice` ("Model changed:
  Sonnet 5.5 → Opus 5.5; effort: Default → High, from your next message",
  `words.model_change_words`) after a PATCH that changed the model or effort
  of a session that has a conversation (`harness_session_id` or a live
  turn); never raises. TEST HELPER `__tests__/helpers/modelPicker.ts`
  (`START`, `SESSION`, `chooseModel`, `chooseEffort`, `chosen`,
  `effortLevels`, `modelRowsShown`, `groupsShown`, `effortButtons`): NEVER
  call one inside `waitFor` — opening the panel changes the page, so waitFor
  re-runs it forever (a test hung that way). Pins:
  `dashboard/__tests__/model-picker-v1475.test.tsx`, the v1.475.0 cases in
  `folder-defaults-v1426-0` and `build-app-view-v1409`,
  `tests/test_build_app_model_change_v1475.py`; moved on purpose: the eight
  files that set the model or effort through the old selects. REVIEW (each
  red first): the start box's tool reset is keyed on `tool?.id` (reading the
  list again on opening made new objects and wiped the account, mode and
  model); a session's chip names its tool and model from the session while
  the list is not read (`pickerTools`, a plain value: it sits past the pane's
  early return, so no hook); a model picked in a session shows its own
  levels at once but they wait ("Changing the model…") until the daemon's
  answer makes it the chosen one (`pending`); Tab closes the panel (it is
  portaled to the end of the page); the shortcut opens nothing with no tools;
  the daemon line says the model's and the effort's own timing when they
  differ (`effort_when`). v1.483.1: a change made while a reply runs keeps
  the page's own "applies from your next message" note only UNTIL a new
  "Model/Effort changed" line is drawn (`noteUntilLine` + `changeLines`,
  counted against what was drawn or waiting for the next frame at the
  press): said once, and still said when the reply ended while the change
  was out (the line then goes to the record only). Only THAT note is taken
  away (matched by its text), the watch ends once anything else is shown in
  its place (a returned steer note, a rewind note, an account move), and a
  line that already arrived means no note at all. NO extra session read for
  it: an extra read at a reply's end raced the next WAITING message's follow
  and drew its question twice (three review rounds found a new race each
  time). A re-read that keeps what is drawn (the live pieces outnumber the
  saved record) still adds every notice newer than anything drawn
  (`lib/buildApp.withNewerNotices`): stamps compared as `stampKey`s (the
  daemon's `isoformat()` drops the fraction of a whole-second stamp, and
  "…:07Z" sorts after "…:07.3Z" as text), a notice stamped AT the newest
  drawn time added unless the same notice (stamp + text) is drawn.
  `EmergencyStopStrip`'s server snapshot is ONE constant (a new object per
  call is React's "getServerSnapshot should be cached" warning, checked
  during HYDRATION — a `renderToString` alone never warns; pin
  `__tests__/server-snapshot-v1483-1.test.tsx`). Checked and dropped: the
  panel's placement does not need re-measuring when its list changes — its
  height cap is the ROOM on its side, not its first height.

- **A Store program is never asked into the pane job, and starts one at a
  time; a frozen loop says where it is stuck** (v1.407.1, a USER BUG on
  v1.407.0: Build stuck at "Attaching to sessions…", then "Iron Jarvis isn't
  responding", across restarts). The panes run the Store pwsh
  (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Microsoft\WindowsApps\pwsh.EXE`, an App Execution Alias,
  reparse tag 0x8000001B). v1.407.0 stopped the BOOT waiting on stuck pane
  restores, but ~90 s later NOTHING answered (even the 25 s audit polls) until
  the desktop watchdog killed it: a stuck process creation is not contained
  by a bounded wait. THE REVIEW CORRECTED THE FIRST CUT: starting the real
  `C:\Program Files\WindowsApps\<pkg>\pwsh.exe` goes through the SAME package
  activation as the alias (AppModel 8001/219/211/201, same identity); every
  hang the boot watch caught was in `ConPtyBackend.start`'s FIRST `_spawn`,
  the one with `PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_JOB_LIST`, which Windows always refuses
  for a packaged program (AppModel 212/208, 0x80070005 "Cannot add process to
  Desktop AppX container") before the no-job retry — two activations per pane,
  two panes at once, and these panes have had NO job since v1.333.0 anyway.
  Now `start` resolves argv once (`terminals/app_alias.direct_argv`: reads the
  alias's reparse data, desktop app type "0" only, target must be an existing
  absolute `.exe`; the saved argv keeps the alias), never creates a job when
  `is_packaged(argv[0])` (an alias, or a path under `\WindowsApps\`), and
  holds `_PACKAGED_SPAWN` (one packaged spawn at a time); pywinpty and pipe
  backends get `direct_argv` too. `daemon/boot_watch.LoopWatch` (armed in the
  lifespan beside the v1.245.0 loop-lag task, which calls `beat()` each
  second; stopped at shutdown) reports from its own thread at 20/45/75 s of
  a frozen loop where the loop thread and busy threads wait — the loop-lag
  line is written by the loop AFTER a stall ends, so a stall that never ended
  left no trace. A NEXT FREEZE NAMES ITS PLACE: read daemon.log for "event
  loop frozen for". OPEN: the Windows-side cause is still not reproduced on
  demand; other spawns of Store programs (`core/procs`) keep their job
  attempt. Pins: `tests/test_app_alias_v14071.py` (15 mutations, 14 red; the
  survivor — handing a packaged spawn `self._job` — is equivalent, the job is
  `None` there), including real Store-pwsh and cmd ConPTY spawns.
  v1.409.1 (a USER BUG, the SAME freeze on the first start after the v1.409.0
  update): both panes were still not started at the 8 s mark, ~1 s after the
  dashboard opened every request stopped, and the daemon wrote NOTHING for
  94 s, LoopWatch included. Windows' AppModel log shows no pwsh activation in
  that window, so both CreateProcessW calls never reached activation. A scratch
  daemon whose pane spawns never return (ctypes releases the GIL) stayed fully
  responsive, so a stuck spawn ALONE does not freeze it: the silence means the
  interpreter lock, a log handler's lock, or every request worker was taken.
  THE CAUSE IS NOT FOUND. LoopWatch now (a) arms `faulthandler.
  dump_traceback_later(TRACE_AFTER_S=30, repeat=True)` into
  `<userData>/logs/daemon-freeze.txt` (`trace_path_for`; never inside the
  state home), re-armed from its own thread every TRACE_REARM_S=10 while
  Python runs — the C watchdog needs neither lock; ONLY a frozen build or
  `IRONJARVIS_FREEZE_TRACE=1` arms it (the process has one such dump), and
  `stop()` cancels it; (b) writes its reports to that file BEFORE the log;
  (c) reports at 20/45/75 s when anyio's default limiter has every token
  borrowed (`pool_full`: loop alive, /health — a sync route — waiting).
  READ `logs/daemon-freeze.txt` after the next "isn't responding"; it names
  the place. Pins: `tests/test_freeze_trace_v14091.py` (a real held GIL via
  `PyDLL("kernel32").Sleep` in a child; 7 mutations, all red).
  v1.419.1 (a USER BUG: Build › Terminal said "Reopening 3 terminals…" for
  4+ hours; `GET /terminals` held `restoring: [3 ids]` and the three Store
  pwsh restores never returned). NOTHING WAITS ON A RESTORE FOREVER:
  `manager.RESTORE_GIVE_UP_S` (60 s from the boot's restore start) GIVES UP a
  restore still starting — decided when the state is READ
  (`_settle_restores` in `restoring_ids`/`restore_failed`/
  `snapshot_if_changed`), never by a timer thread (a stuck process creation
  can hold the loader lock, and `Thread.start` then hangs too). A given-up
  pane moves to `_restore_failed`, its `_reserved` slot is released (a late
  `_finish_late_restore` never releases it twice), its snapshot entry is
  KEPT (`_index_text` writes both tables) so the next start tries again, and
  a shell that starts later still registers and clears the note. `GET
  /terminals` adds `restore_failed: {count, ids, reason}` only when non-empty;
  the Build page (`lib/restoreFailed`) drops "Reopening…", says "N terminals
  from before the restart couldn't be reopened: their shell didn't start. Open
  a new terminal, or restart Iron Jarvis to try again." in the stage (beside
  the normal New terminal door) and the rail, keeps their saved keys, and
  keeps re-reading while one could still open. `_PACKAGED_SPAWN` is acquired
  with `PACKAGED_SPAWN_WAIT_S` (15 s): past it a Store shell raises
  `ShellStartStuck` (a RuntimeError, never `ConPtyUnavailable`, so ConPTY is
  not marked broken) — `POST /terminals` and `/terminals/launch` answer 503
  with its sentence; a RESTORE refused that way is given up with its entry
  kept. `_conpty_api` builds under `_CONPTY_API_LOCK` (double-checked) and
  `rehydrate` calls `warm_conpty()` on the boot thread before any restore
  thread starts (the 15:36 freeze: two restore threads in `ctypes.WinDLL` of
  conpty.dll while the boot thread sat in `Thread.start`). THE WINDOWS-SIDE
  CAUSE IS STILL NOT KNOWN. Pins: `tests/test_restore_give_up_v14181.py`
  (time through `TerminalManager._now`, never a sleep; 14 mutations, all
  red), `dashboard/__tests__/build-restore-failed-v14181.test.tsx` (7
  mutations, all red); moved on purpose: `pane-keys-v1280`,
  `terminal-host-v1243` (the kept set gained the given-up ids).
  REVIEW (2 defects, each red first): (1) `kill_all` sets `_closing`, lets go
  of `_lock`, then reads the tables for the shutdown snapshot; a late shell
  starting in that gap made `_finish_late_restore` POP the pane (reopening,
  given up, or refused behind a stuck shell) before closing it, so the
  shutdown snapshot lost it. Once `_closing`, the finisher now only closes a
  started shell and leaves both tables as they are. (2) panes given up at
  different moments (refused at 15 s, the stuck one at the minute) were saved
  and reported in give-up order; `_restore_rank` (snapshot order, set by
  `rehydrate`) sorts the pending entries and `restore_failed.ids`. Pins:
  `tests/test_restore_give_up_review_v14181.py` (drives the REAL `kill_all`;
  checks the file was written by the shutdown, never read off the boot's own
  snapshot); moved on purpose: `test_boot_pane_restore_bound_v1406::
  test_a_pane_that_starts_after_shutdown_began_is_closed_not_registered`
  (waits for the finisher, not for `restoring` to empty). OPEN: a pane that
  opens after the give-up is registered without a cap check (at most the
  number given up past the cap).

- **Build (`app/terminals/page.tsx`)**: since v1.409.0 a "</> Code | Terminal"
  switch (Code = `components/buildCode/*`, see the v1.409.0 hard rule). The
  Terminal view below was the TERMINALS only page (v1.407.0
  part 1; it had two views from v1.369.0, and the free-form react-rnd
  CANVAS of v1.218.0 is long gone — a saved `ij.build.shape` of "harness"
  or "canvas" now just opens Terminals): `components/terminal/PaneRail.tsx`
  (every live pane in a column with its state, one focused beside it);
  `?focus=<id>` opens a terminal (`BUILD_TERMINAL_EVENT` for a same-page
  link), and an old `?session=` / `?night=` is `router.replace`d to the Code
  view (`lib/buildLinks.codeHrefForOldBuildSearch`). **The coding sessions**
  (`components/harness/*`, IjHarnessView: folder groups + a Terminals group,
  the conversation built from the chat page's own pieces, the right panes)
  are the CHAT page's **Code** view: `components/chat/ChatCodeSurface.tsx`
  (the Chat | Code switch, default export of `app/chat/page.tsx`) +
  `components/harness/CodeView.tsx` (lends the terminal list / terminal tab
  Build used to lend); address `/chat?view=code&session=<id>` /
  `&night=<date>` (`lib/chatSurface`, `lib/buildLinks.buildSessionHref`);
  same-page links go through `BUILD_SESSION_EVENT` / `BUILD_NIGHT_EVENT` /
  `CHAT_VIEW_EVENT` because Next keeps page state on a query-only change.
  In the rail every pane still RENDERS — same box, all
  mounted, `visibility` toggled — because the v1.190.0 rule stands: a
  terminal whose holder has no size wraps its replay into a buffer no later
  fit can re-wrap, so a hidden pane must keep a real box. Never
  `display:none`, never an unmount. The rail needs a fallback focus
  (`activeId`). The TerminalPane component itself CAN unmount (leaving Build,
  Chat | Code on the chat page) — since v1.243.0 its xterm and socket live in
  `components/terminal/paneHost.ts` and are parked, not destroyed (see the
  hard rule).

- **Build › Code's "working" sign: ONE ring in a slot that is always there; the
  light hops in steps, never turns smoothly** (v1.477.0; the owner: the plain line
  "5 helpers are still working in the background…" was too easy to miss).
  `components/buildCode/WorkingSign.tsx` draws, `lib/workingSign.ts` decides
  (pure). States, in `backgroundWorkWords`' own order: `reply` (a reply the
  user started; steps aside for a card), `helpers` (one segment per helper,
  the working count in the middle, finished ones first, thin and steady; past
  `RING_SLOTS_MAX` 12 the ring shares 12 out and the number stays exact), `own`
  (the tool's own reply), `commands` (a STILL ring + the old sentence; a dev
  server is not work for the user). "N finished" is `noteHelpers`: the drops in
  `background_helpers` between two reads of the session, kept in the component
  (a pane is mounted per session), started again when no helper is left. It is
  what THIS WINDOW saw, never more than really ended; no daemon change.
  - THE OLD IDS LIVE INSIDE IT: `code-working` (the reply state's `<p>`) and
    `code-background` (`role="status"`, the unchanged `backgroundWorkWords`
    sentence: `sr-only` beside the short words, visible for `commands`). The
    v1417 pins are green and unmoved. The short words are `aria-hidden`.
  - THE SLOT (`code-sign-slot`) is always rendered, `h-8`, so nothing moves
    when the sign comes or goes (it adds ~30 px above the message box in every
    session). `sticky -bottom-2`: Chromium measures a sticky box's `bottom`
    from INSIDE the scroll box's padding (the transcript's `py-4`), so
    `-bottom-2` is 8 px above the transcript's edge; `bottom-2` pushed the sign
    14 px up onto the last line (measured; the e2e holds the 8 px both ways).
    `pointer-events-none` on the slot, `-auto` on the sign. `floating` (the
    pane's new `away` state, set in `onScroll`) adds the pill; `compact`
    (`jump`) drops the second words, and the first under `sm`.
  - MOTION (`globals.css` `.wsign-hop` / `.wsign-tick`): `steps()` only, one
    new frame per hop (`BEAT_BACKGROUND_MS` 900, `BEAT_REPLY_MS` 600), transform
    and opacity only; `--wsign-m` / `--wsign-sweep` / `--wsign-beat` are set
    inline (`steps(var(--wsign-m))` works in Chromium). Off under
    `prefers-reduced-motion` and forced colours (the stylesheet), and while the
    window is hidden or the setting is read in script (`data-motion="off"`, the
    classes are not even added). MEASURED (headless Edge, 1440 x 900, 3 rounds x 20 s,
    `SystemInfo.getProcessInfo`): standing still 1.56% of one core (graphics
    process) / 1.29% (page); as shipped 1.90% / 1.61%; the same light turning
    smoothly 23.37% / 3.52%. Do not put a smooth timing function
    back (a unit test reads the stylesheet for it).
  - Pins: `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-working-sign.test.tsx` (20 tests; 20 mutations, all red),
    e2e `dashboard/e2e/build-code-working-sign.spec.ts` (1900 / 1280 / 390 px,
    dark and light: rests 8 px under the last row, nothing moves when it goes,
    the light is seen only at its step places, scrolled-up pill, reduce motion,
    a made theme's variables; `IJ_SIGN_OUT=<folder>` writes pictures,
    `IJ_SIGN_MEASURE=1` runs the cost measurement). OPEN: Chat › Code keeps its
    own "Working for…" line.
  - SAME RELEASE, A TIMER ONE TEST LEFT FOR THE NEXT (the branch's first full
    run on GitHub went red in `chat-waiting-where-you-are-v1387` "leaveTurn …
    tells the bell only when no chat page here follows the turn again", in the
    run and in its retry; a file this change never touched). The tests before
    it unmount a chat page mid-turn, and `lib/chatAway.leaveTurn` then arms its
    bell announcement `ANNOUNCE_AFTER_MS` (1.5 s) later, on a real timer that
    outlives the test. On that run the tests in between took about 1.35 s
    (341 + 366 + 20 + 621 ms on the log), so two of those timers went off
    inside the later test, whose listener counts EVERY announcement on the
    window. It had passed for weeks only because the gap was usually longer;
    a new test file moved the shard's mix. `__resetChatAway()` (tests only,
    every `beforeEach`) now cancels the armed announcements (`announcing`) and
    moves `generation`, which also drops a `leaveTurn` whose POST was still out
    when the reset ran. Nothing else moves `generation`: the app is unchanged.
    Reproduced on an idle PC by adding one test that leaves a page mid-turn and
    then waits `ANNOUNCE_AFTER_MS - 30` ms: the old code gives GitHub's exact
    error, the new one passes; 4 mutations of the fix, all red. THE RULE: a
    module that arms a real timer needs a test reset that takes the timer
    back, and a test that counts events on `window` is exposed to every timer
    an earlier test in its file left.

- **A coding reply is CHECKED AGAIN until its check passes or a limit ends
  it; the line under an unfinished reply is Iron Jarvis's own; a reply that
  ends on "can't" is sent back once** (v1.493.0; the owner: "i need some form
  of loop so it doesnt just stop and so it doesn't claim it cant be done …
  and dont lie about being done if you are not"). Chat › Code only (harness
  turns with no team); plain chat, agent runs and Build › Code are unchanged.
  - **THE LOOP (`harness/finish.py`, `FinishCheck`).** The v1.386.0 finish
    check ran once. Now, after its note, every time the reply is about to
    end on words `prepare()` goes to `_check_again`: `[]` + a check-again
    note (`_again_note`: "FINISH CHECK. Check again (n of at most 5)…", what
    is still open and nothing else), or new `ij-finish-N` calls that re-run
    the tests when a file may have changed since the last run (`_dirty`;
    the same gates, cards and ledger mark as the first check's calls), or
    None (the reply ends). Both lanes call `_finish.settled()` once after a
    round of the check's OWN calls: True = the re-run passed and nothing else
    is open, so the reply ends with NO extra model round (MIRROR NOTE,
    lock-step; the stream lane turns `_fin_live` off first).
  - **WHAT IS OPEN (`_open_work`).** Tests whose newest verdict is FAILED
    (not stale, not called outside the request); points the model answered
    "N. not met" and has not settled; files the request lists that are not on
    disk (`deliverables/scope.required_files`: reading only, listed
    deliverables or two or more named files, inside the folder by text, never
    `//host` or `..`), asked for once. A point asked about and then left
    unconfirmed after a change is dropped from the count (the model fixed it
    and did not say so); a point answered "N. blocked: <what stops it, what
    the user must do>" (`blocked_points`, only after a check-again note) is
    never asked again and stays listed as not done.
  - **LIMITS, each an ending in the record (`AGAIN_ENDS`).** `done`,
    `blocked`, `unchecked` (the tests failed, a file changed since, and they
    could not be run again, e.g. the re-run card was declined), `limit`
    (`AGAIN_MAX_ROUNDS` 5), `stuck` (`AGAIN_IDLE_MAX` 2 rounds in a row with
    no progress), `steps` (`round_index >= rounds - 2`), `outside`. Stop, the
    reply's time or cost cap and the step limit end the reply as before.
    Never for a cut reply, low trust, a team's reply, or with `again = false`
    in `.ironjarvis/harness/finish.toml` (`FinishConfig.again`).
  - **PROGRESS IS A CERTAIN CHANGE.** `_moved` is set only by a journaled
    file edit (`folder_changes.JOURNALED_TOOLS`) or by a test re-run whose
    `run_signature` (verdict, exit code, output with timings and addresses
    removed) differs from the last one. A command that only MAY have changed
    a file makes the verdict stale (so the tests are run again) but is not
    progress: seen live, a model that re-ran `python -m unittest | tail`
    each round was pushed three times.
  - **THE USER'S REQUEST IS THE STANDARD (`OUTSIDE_FORM` "Outside the
    request:").** Seen live: "only fix add(), leave the rest to me" left
    tests failing by design and the first cut pushed the model three times.
    The first note (when its tests failed) and every check-again note offer
    the form; `says_outside` (line start, linear) sets `_outside`, the tests
    are then never pushed, the ending is `outside`, and the verdict line says
    "the tests still fail; the reply says those failures are outside what you
    asked for." with no "keep going" sentence.
  - **THE VERDICT (`verdict_note()`, both lanes, appended after
    `_deliv.final()`).** "_Checked by Iron Jarvis: …_" built only from the
    check's record: the tests' newest verdict (or "failed when they were
    last run, and were not run again after the last change"), points not
    done, listed files not there, how the loop ended, then "Tell it to keep
    going and it will carry on." "this is not finished." leads only when a
    point is not done. "" when everything it could check is fine. `combine()`
    keeps each round's closing words once and drops "N. met" bookkeeping
    lines from any final answer.
  - **THE RECORD.** `finish.again {rounds, ended, open}` only when the loop
    was in play (`RECORD_KEYS` before checkers/review, `saved_again`).
    Dashboard: `lib/buildSessions.decodeFinish` whitelists it;
    `lib/stepWords` `againWords` ("sent back to work twice, stopped: two
    tries changed nothing"), `finishNotDone`, `testsFailing` (the old
    `finishWarn` body), `finishWarn = testsFailing || finishNotDone`;
    `lib/nextStep` offers "Fix the failing tests" only when `testsFailing`
    and says "N point(s) of the request is/are not done." from `again.open`.
  - **GIVE-UP (`harness/give_up.py`, `HarnessTurn.give_up`).** `gives_up`
    (deterministic, English, linear): first-person inability + a work verb,
    "this can't be done", "unable to <verb>", "you'll need to do this
    yourself", read in the last paragraph of prose, or the first sentence
    unless the last paragraph says success; never a reply ending on a
    question or holding a "Blocked:" line, never inside code or a quote.
    Once per reply `GIVE_UP_NUDGE` goes through the closing offer's own seam
    (`chat_turn.closing_offer_wanted` asks the offer first, then
    `give.wants`; `take_closing_offer` returns `give.take()`;
    `note_closing_offer(..., harness)` publishes nudge kind
    `core/nudges.GAVE_UP`, dashboard `SYSTEM_WORDS.gave_up`). Never after
    the finish check ran, with tool calls, cut, low trust or plan mode. A
    wrong reading costs one round; the nudge authorises nothing.
  - **REGEXES OVER MODEL TEXT.** `_ANSWER_RX` (older) was quadratic (2 s on
    16,000 spaces): never two whitespace runs side by side. `_BLOCKED_RX`,
    `_OUTSIDE_RX`, `give_up._BLOCKED_LINE_RX` follow the same shape; a
    child-process pin feeds hostile text.
  - **LIVE** (Haiku through claude-cli, scratch daemons 8881 old / 8891 new,
    eight small tasks, one run each): four ordinary tasks identical in result
    and time (the loop added nothing); a command on a host that does not
    exist: old stopped after one failed try, new was sent back, tried four
    more ways and ended on a "Blocked:" line (21 s vs 5 s); "only fix add()":
    ended `outside`, no push, the honest line under the reply; a plain
    question: no nudge. The benchmark's `harness_sha256` includes
    `harness_give_up`.
  - **OPEN:** no loop for a team's reply, Build › Code, plain chat or agent
    runs; with no known test command only points and listed files are
    checked; a model can misuse "Outside the request:" (the line attributes
    the claim); `gives_up` is English only; one model tried live, single
    runs; not driven in the packaged app.
  - **Pins:** `tests/test_until_done_v1493.py` (both lanes with a scripted
    model; tables both ways for `gives_up`),
    `dashboard/__tests__/until-done-v1493.test.ts`; moved on purpose:
    `test_finish_lanes_v1386` (failed tests: 2 notes, `again`),
    `test_finish_shell_writes_review_v1387` (the commented `cd`: 2 notes,
    `stuck`, the verdict line), `test_finish_shell_replay_v1387` (a confirm
    step).

- **A coding session is archived or deleted from its ROW, not only from
  inside it** (v1.500.1; the owner: Chat › Code's list in the app sidebar had
  "no way to archive, or delete a session"). `HarnessSidebar` takes
  `onArchive(id, stopFirst)` and `onDelete(id)` (both absent: no menu); each
  live row gets a ⋯ (`harness-row-menu-<id>`) with Archive ("Stop and
  archive" while it works, sent with `stop: true`) and Delete (a
  `ConfirmButton`, two presses); an Archived row gets a trash-can
  `ConfirmButton` beside Bring back. `IjHarnessView` wires them to the same
  `archiveSession` / `deleteSession` routes the open session's own ⋯ menu
  uses, then drops the session from the split and reads both lists again, as
  `onGone` does. Pins: `dashboard/__tests__/harness-row-archive-delete-v1500-1.test.tsx`
  (4 mutations, all red).

- **A failed tool call says what to do next, and whose failure it was**
  (v1.508.0; read off the v1.500.2 ledger). Edits: `harness/textedit` shows
  the file's lines around the closest one and, for text that differs only in
  spacing, the exact text there, and still REFUSES (strict parity);
  `harness/edit_memory` remembers per (session, file) what the session last
  read or wrote: a refused edit says "this session has not read …" or "…
  changed after this session last read it", and after `EDIT_FAIL_GATE` (3)
  refused edits of one file the next is not tried until a read or write.
  Shell: `git_bash.bash_refusal` (both command doors) refuses `rmdir /s /q`
  and PowerShell's `$_` / `$env:` inside a double-quoted `-Command` before
  anything runs. `git_read` outside a repository answers `NOT_A_REPO` (data
  `not_a_repo`), and the finish check's review brief drops git there
  (`finish.review_lead`). In a team turn `list_agents` leads with the
  session's team and marks every other agent refused; `off_team` says what to
  tell a user who asked for an off-team model. The agent loop notes every 3rd
  failure in a row of one tool on one target with different arguments
  (`runtime.target_streak_note`). `harness/failure_kind` classifies a failed
  call (harness / model / environment / probe / policy / unclassified);
  "Copy as a fix request" leaves out probes and model mistakes a later call
  on the same target put right (counted), and lists the rest as
  `model_mistake` / `environment_mismatch`, never as app defects; the daily
  "Find out why X keeps failing" scan (`improvement/engine`) counts neither
  those nor environment mismatches. Pins:
  `tests/test_harness_recovery_v1508.py` (9 mutations, all red).

- **A coding session never loses a reply, a plan approval, a file's replaced version or a helper's change without a copy and a word** (v1.510.0, second review H5, H6, S2, S8, S11, S12, T3).
  Changes tab: `hosted/panes.revert_file` copies the file git will write
  over into `<home>/trash/<stamp>/code-revert/<sid>/` first (`_keep_copy`),
  refuses if no copy can be made, and the route notes the revert in the
  session's own log and waits while the tool, its helpers or a hand-off
  helper work in the folder. Session log: `hosted/events.EventLog.flush`
  cuts off a partial write, puts the rows back at the FRONT of the queue
  (bounded by `MAX_LINES` / `MAX_BYTES`, oldest dropped and counted) and
  says so once per run of failures; never drop a batch on `OSError`. Build
  reply: `routes/build._record_turn_end` reads the write's `ok`, tries once
  more (`REPLY_SAVE_RETRY_S`, injected `_pause_before_retry`), and when it
  still fails keeps the reply record and puts `not_saved` on the last
  frame; `sessions.reconcile_unsaved_replies` (run at the end of
  `reconcile_interrupted_turns`) writes the line at the next start, and the
  page keeps the live reply with the daemon's sentence until a read holds
  it (`lib/buildSessions.withUnsavedReplies`). An approved plan leaves
  `approved_plans` only after `S.update` succeeds. A refused delete says
  `PROGRAMS_STOPPED`; a refused Choose account + continue restores the old
  account fields. `library.accept` writes a `write_file` Undo record before
  it writes the file. The 200-line cap cuts at a turn start
  (`sessions._capped`). Rewind: `rewind._rows_since` reads the session's
  rows AND its helpers' (sessions whose `options.build_session` is the
  thread; one level, helpers start no helpers) as one newest-first
  sequence; a local hand-off is not listed as a command when helper rows
  are present; a failed or stopped write that kept its Undo is named in the
  note (`stopped`), never skipped in silence. Left open: a page reload
  drops a kept unsaved reply until the next start; a hand-off row is not
  linked to its exact helper session.
  Pins: `tests/test_second_review_{hosted_kept,build_record,rewind_helpers}_v1510.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/build-reply-not-saved-v1510.test.tsx`.

- **Consent in a coding session is exactly what the card said, in both kinds of session and in every coding tool** (v1.511.0, second review T2, T8, D5, S6, A2, A5, A12, H7, H9).
  Git guard: `routes/build.start_build_turn` arms `git_guard.TURN_FOLDER`
  (from `folder_facts`, found off the loop) beside the branch. In the user's
  own folder (a repository) every kind the guard reads except a commit asks
  ONCE, EVERY TIME (a session grant, "Don't ask" and auto-approve never
  cover it); on a session's own branch its refusals stand. Decision E3 as
  built is wider than written: it also covers a rewrite, a settings change,
  a stash pop and GitHub changes (the filter is `a.kind != "commit"` in
  `guard_check`). A command is cut with quotes respected and both shells'
  escapes, failing closed on an unclosed quote; `remote set-url` / `add` /
  `remove` / `rename` are a setting, `reset <ref>` moves the branch,
  `checkout <file>` is a discard, `stash pop` / `apply` card. Finish check:
  a Maven / Gradle / tox / nox line is "the tests" only when every goal,
  task, environment or session is a test one (`_jvm_test_run`,
  `TEST_ENV_WORDS`). Page browser: `page_type` with `submit` gets the click
  rule of the button it would press (`_SUBMIT_WORDS`,
  `submit_needs_asking`) and is refused with `SUBMIT_NOT_ASKED` unless the
  check showed those exact words. Coding tools: Claude Code's
  `permission_suggestions` go back as copies with `destination: "session"`
  and unknown types are dropped (`claude.session_suggestions`);
  `handoff.MODE_STRICTNESS` (reads only / asks / edits freely / does not
  ask) decides a helper's mode in `helper_mode`, refused in words otherwise,
  and the card names the tool; `branching.branch_consent` turns Full access
  into the tool's default and does not carry Pi's folder press;
  `service._pi_folder_at_start` reads Pi's folder trust afresh before each
  start; `service.patch` checks every field before `set_mode` and puts the
  mode back on a later live refusal. Left open: the guard's one
  `os.path.lexists` for a reset / checkout word runs on the event loop (group
  16 moves the guard's file checks off it); handing MORE work to an existing
  helper does not re-check its mode; "Deploy" is not in the click vocabulary.
  Pins: `tests/test_second_review_{git_guard,test_rerun,page_submit,tool_consent}_v1511.py`.

- **Stop means stopped in Build, and a stop never races a start** (v1.512.0, second review H1, H2, H3, H8, H13, H14, T1, A11, D6, D9, and the open shutdown item).
  Code view (`hosted/service.py`): every stop of a session's process runs
  under `live.lock` (`_stop_process`; `lock_held=True` only where the
  caller holds it, asserted); it reads the turn it stops BEFORE waiting and
  ends only that turn. `_claim` refuses while a start is in flight
  (`starting` / `opening`); the reaper and the pool skip a locked or
  starting session, never wait for it. A start re-checks after each wait
  and after the spawn (`_start_unwanted`, `_undo_unwanted_start`): a
  removed session or a closing Iron Jarvis means it stops what it started;
  `shutdown` waits, bounded, for starts in flight (`_stop_at_shutdown`). A
  turn that is ENDING is no longer wanted (`_drive`, `_reserve_slot`). A
  Stop the tool ignores is closed by a second Stop (after
  `INTERRUPT_WAIT_S`), by "Close it now", or after `STOP_FORCE_AFTER_S`
  (60 s) with one notice (`W.STOP_NOT_HONOURED`); the first Stop answers
  `stop_pending` with `words`, which the page shows as sent. Around it: the
  kept state's `turn.carry` comes back on an adopted turn
  (`adopt._turn_back`), Emergency stop finds a helper's piece by its
  `handoff-` id plus the row's `handed_from` and stops a turn with no task
  on the service's loop (`carry_on._loop_for`); a tool counts as stopped
  only when the host confirmed it (`RemoteProcess.stop_confirmed`), else it
  stays on `_LOST`; a Codex Stop with no turn id is remembered and sent by
  `_named`; a rewind or branch whose log ends on a cut reply writes a
  decided `auto: "offered"` record (`carry_on.note_rewritten`). Night: a
  task re-reads its stop note right before and right after its turn starts,
  and the settle pass stops an unattended turn whose task carries one.
  Finish check: each `process_read` is raced against the turn's
  `TurnHandle` (`finish._unless_stopped`), then `process_stop`, recorded
  not run, "stopped by you". CI watch: a gone worktree ends the watch
  stopped in words; an unexpected error after a review rests it in "gave
  up" once. Left open: none of this was driven on the packaged build or
  through the code host with a real tool (fakes only); the `handoff-`
  prefix lives in two files; a start refused after its connection files
  were written leaves them, as the failed-spawn path already did.
  Pins: `tests/test_second_review_{tool_stop,tool_stops_more,night_cancel,ci_watch_ends,finish_stop}_v1512.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/build-code-stop-pending-v1512.test.tsx`.

- **A coding session, the night shift and the pull-request auto-fix never say more than they proved** (v1.513.0, second review D1, D2, D3, D4, D7, D8, D11, T5, T6, T7, T10, S3, S5).
  Finish check: `FinishCheck.keep_command` is the only writer of
  `_commands` / `_cmd_meta`; a cut part's or a worker's command is never
  reused and `_reusable` refuses lists that differ in length. A restart
  resume with the note in its tail goes through `carry_past_note`: no
  second note, and the cut part's tests are re-run through the check-again
  loop (`_carried_runs`) under its own exclusions. Review states add
  `not_counted` (low trust, a withheld report); a silent reviewer is
  `failed`, "it ended without a report". A folder `finish.toml` with
  `enabled = false` gives an off check whose record is `{"off": "folder"}`
  ("Not checked: ...", and `finish_off` in `/build/sessions/{id}/harness`).
  ONE reading of a finish record on the daemon side, in `harness/night.py`:
  `finish_tests` (the tests as LAST seen: `after`, else `tests`, as the
  screen's `testsFailing`), `finish_unmet` (`again.open` when the block is
  there, else `unmet`), `finish_loop_unfinished`, and `finish_needs_you`,
  shared by the night, the day's finished notice and the auto-fix. Never
  read `fin["tests"]` alone again. Night: "unmet" and "did_nothing" are in
  `NOTE_KINDS` (any other kind is filed "info" and the morning row loses
  its reason); a task with nothing to commit, an empty branch and no passed
  check ends "Needs you" (never a try-on task); the window sentence says
  tasks START until its end. Auto-fix: `ci_fix.commit_fix(left_out=)`
  collects what the screen left out (a "nothing to commit" refusal too);
  any left-out file, a tip still at `base_sha`, or a tip that is not this
  streak's own commit rests the watch asking, before `push_fix`;
  `_after_fix` rests asking before any paid review when
  `night.finish_needs_you(line["finish"])`. Checkers: a file ESLint ignored
  is "not checked"; a run whose settings left out every file is `excluded`
  and listed under `skipped`. An attempt cut at the step limit is "Stopped
  at its step limit (N steps)". History: `sessions.reply_end_note` adds one
  fixed-word `[How that reply ended ...]` note in `history_of` and in the
  summarizer's `_line_text` / `pairs_of`; a failed reply with no words
  keeps it as a stand-in (follow-up suggestions treat that as nothing to
  suggest for). Left open: a night task in a folder that turned the check
  off still reads "Done" (it reads as "no check", like a folder with no
  tests); points that come from a description file are re-read only at the
  end of a carried reply.
  Pins: `tests/test_second_review_{finish_truth,ci_push_truth,night_verdict,coding_reports}_v1513.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/finish-truth-v1513.test.tsx`.

- **What a coding tool's session reports is read from its own record, never assumed** (v1.514.0, second review H4, H10, A7, A9, A13).
  A helper whose turn left memory reads its last `handoff-` piece's `done`
  from its own log (`handoff.logged_piece_state`, off the loop, cached by
  size and time); no `done` means "stopped", never "done". The archive
  summary counts `carry` messages apart from the user's asks. Codex: a
  frame that ends the reply or writes its words, plan or model
  (`_THREAD_FRAMES`: the text / plan / thinking deltas, `turn/plan/updated`,
  `error`, `turn/completed`, `model/rerouted`) is read only for THIS
  thread, and an end or error only for this turn; a sub-agent thread's
  STEPS (`item/started` / `item/completed` of a command, a file change, a
  tool call) are still drawn into the reply, its own message is not. A
  reroute names `toModel` and emits a `session` event; the next turn
  re-emits the chosen model. An unknown ACP `stopReason` is "failed" in
  words; an unreported Antigravity step is "stopped" and is not a changed
  file; `_set_model` keeps the model the tool answered. Left open: a
  sub-agent thread's token use is still added to the reply.
  Pin: `tests/test_second_review_tool_reports_v1514.py`.

- **A page the agent looks at is judged whole, its pictures belong to its session, and a turn never starts on an account nobody chose** (v1.515.0, second review S1, S7, S9, S10, H12).
  `page_browser._route` judges EVERY navigation, a frame's included, with
  `_guard`; a refused frame is aborted, noted (`frame-<verdict>`) and left
  empty, and no screenshot is taken with a refused frame in it. A one-shot
  `page_screenshot` goes through a throwaway `EdgeBackend`
  (`AgentBrowsers.one_shot`: the route, redirect, arrival and frame guards,
  the Local only proxy, the Job) and gives no picture of a page that moved;
  the bare `--screenshot` command line is gone, because it could not say
  where the page ended. It therefore needs Playwright's driver, as the
  agent's browser does. Pictures live in `page-screenshots/<thread id>/`
  and are moved to the trash with a deleted session. A library lesson, edit
  or habit that the secret mask would change is DROPPED, never kept masked
  (`library._quotes_a_secret`). `routes/build.turn_account` refuses with
  `BA.NOT_READ_NOTE` when the manager is on, the provider is one it
  manages, and the accounts list is still not known. `hosted/panes.where_view`
  never says "on this network" for a tool's own `ollama/...`
  (`on_this_network: null` plus the daemon's sentence, which
  `lib/codePanes.whereLine` shows as sent). Left open: the one-shot path
  was not driven on the packaged build; a one-shot in flight is not tracked
  by `AgentBrowsers.shutdown` (its Job ends with the daemon); archiving a
  session does not move its pictures; the secret mask also drops a lesson
  that merely contains words like `token: str`.
  Pins: `tests/test_second_review_session_pages_v1515.py`,
  `dashboard/__tests__/code-where-not-known-v1515.test.ts`.

- **A search pattern that could run away is matched by its own program, killed at the limit; an ordinary one stays in the daemon, bounded by line length** (v1.516.0, second review S4).
  Python's `re` holds the interpreter lock for a whole match, so a worker
  thread does not protect the event loop: `(a+)+$` on one line froze the
  daemon. `harness/search.in_process_line_limit(pattern)` decides: `-1` a
  plain pattern (no repeat, no group: any line); a positive number for a
  pattern with NO group and at most `IN_PROCESS_MAX_REPEATS` repeats (it can
  only cost a polynomial of the line's length, kept under
  `IN_PROCESS_STEPS`: about 7,000 characters with one repeat, 368 with two;
  the time limit is read every 64 lines); `0` for a group, a multiline
  search or more repeats. A `0`, or the first line longer than the limit
  (`LineTooLong`), sends the WHOLE search to `search-worker` through
  `core/procs.run` (frozen: `ironjarvis.exe search-worker`, served by
  `packaging/ironjarvis_entry._fast_path`; from source `python -m
  iron_jarvis.harness.search`), killed at the limit plus `SEARCH_START_S`.
  What it found before the kill is kept and the note names the stuck file
  (`stuck_words`). Both forms of `grep` use it (`builtins._plain_grep`,
  `search.plain_grep_bounded`). Do NOT route every repeat to the program: it
  costs about half a second per search (measured 0.5 to 0.9 s against 0.03 s)
  and most patterns a model writes carry a repeat. A glob becomes a `re`
  pattern too: `globmatch.normalize_pattern` folds a run of `**/` into one
  and refuses more than `MAX_DEEP_PARTS` `**` or `MAX_STARS_IN_A_NAME` `*`
  in one name. NOT verified on the packaged build. KNOWN: `finish.py` compiles
  an ignore file's lines with `compile_glob` without those counts.
  Pin: `tests/test_second_review_no_stall_build_v1516.py`.

- **A coding tool's git check and a program's environment are worked out off the event loop, bounded, and fail closed** (v1.516.0, second review T9 and the guard's file checks left open by group 11).
  The command tools await `git_guard.spend_check_off_loop` and
  `refusal_off_loop` (a worker thread, `CHECK_TIMEOUT_S`; a command that
  names no git or gh program is still judged inline). Past the bound the
  card asks once, every time, and the run refuses, in words.
  `process_start` works out its environment through `to_thread` under
  `ENV_TIMEOUT_S`; past it nothing is started.
  Pin: `tests/test_second_review_no_stall_build_v1516.py`.

- **A coding tool's, helper's or attempt's failure is a plain sentence; a sentence the code or the tool wrote is kept** (v1.516.0, second review H11, D10).
  The "did not start" title (`hosted/service._start_failed_words`), the
  helper row's crash text (`helpers.HELPER_CRASHED` through
  `core/failures.plain_failure`) and an attempt's error `detail`
  (`attempts._run_one`) never carry an exception's name or a bare key; the
  raw text is logged. Raw text may show only in the error card's
  `technical` field, under Details.
  Pin: `tests/test_second_review_build_words_v1516.py`.

- **A test of "this never waits for that" asserts the order, never a number of seconds** (v1.516.0).
  `test_finish_shell_writes_review_v1387::test_a_stalled_walk_never_holds_the_reply_and_is_never_started_twice`
  passed only on the retry in v1.515.0's release run: its stall ended by
  itself after 8 seconds, so two slow replies let a second walk start, and
  its "within 3 seconds" bound timed the reply's start, not the stall. The
  stall now lasts until the test releases it, and the test asserts that
  each reply ENDED while the walk was still stalled.
